Just as darkness is not suitable for light, it is impossible for mercy to manifest since it is impossible for his heart to be contaminated by the darkness of suffering. Thus the suffering of the materialists in this world does not exist in the Lord who, existing at all times, is capable of doing, not doing or doing otherwise. Thus, only mercy of the devotees remains. Though the devotees are also not touched by the suffering of the world, they sometimes remember it like remembering the suffering in dreams after awaking. Thus they show mercy to the people of this world. In this way Nārada showed mercy Nalakūvara and Maṇigrīva.

It is not because of experiencing the sufferings of this world that the Lord shows mercy. He shows mercy because of bhakti filled with humility in the devotee, who thinks, “The Lord is my shelter.” This was the case with Gajendra. The Lord however does not show mercy to persons who did not show surrender, who think the deity is stone etc. (nārakī).

Bhaktihas been described and will later be described (Prīti Sandarbha) as the Lord’s special śakti which enters the devotee and softens him. It begins to operate nicely in combination with dainya (humility). Thus, the Lord’s mercy which exists in the Lord’s devotees has devotee association as its vehicle or it has the devotee’s mercy as its vehicle and thus passes into other jīvas. The Lord’s mercy does not operate on its own, independently. Therefore it is said:

*svayaṁ samuttīrya sudustaraṁ dyuman*  
*bhavārṇavaṁ bhīmam adabhra-sauhṛdāḥ*  
*bhavat-padāmbhoruha-nāvam atra te*  
*nidhāya yātāḥ sad-anugraho bhavān*

**O Lord, shining brilliantly! The merciful devotees, easily crossing the insurmountable and terrifying ocean of material life, attain their goal using the boat of your lotus feet (bhakti). You show your mercy through your devotees. SB 10.2.31**

O self-manifesting Lord*(dyuman*)! The devoteestake the boat of your lotus feet,the means to cross the material world, in order cross this ocean. This means they take the process of bhakti which you manifest in various devotees. “Why does bhakti not manifest on its own? How can it be dependent on devotees?” You give mercy to others through the devotees (*sad-anugrahaḥ*). Or your mercy is the devotees. Your mercy which operates in this world, operates in this form--the devotees--and not in any other way. Thus it is said in Śiva’s song:

*athānaghāṅghres tava kīrti-tīrthayor*  
*antar-bahiḥ-snāna-vidhūta-pāpmanām*  
*bhūteṣv anukrośa-susattva-śīlināṁ*  
*syāt saṅgamo 'nugraha eṣa nas tava*

**From this, we will attain association with persons who have been purified externally and internally by the famous Gaṅgā arising from your lotus foot which destroys sin, and with person who have proper conduct, pure hearts and show mercy to all beings. This is your mercy to us. SB 4.24.58**

*Sad-anugrahaḥ* can also mean “theLord whose mercy manifests in the devotees.” This means his mercy does not manifest in the non-devotees. Thus from this understanding it follows that mercy manifests only through devotees.

It is said:

*jāyamānaṁ hi puruṣaṁ yaṁ paśyen madhusūdanaḥ \|*  
*sāttvikas tu sa vijñeyo bhaven mokṣe ca niścitaḥ \|\|*

**One should know that the sāttvika person, born as a human, whom the Lord sees, attains liberation. Mahā-bhārata 12.336.68**

It is understood in this verse that the person is must have had devotee association in a previous birth.The devatās speak to the Lord.

## Anuccheda 181

The cause of devotee association is the independent nature of the devotees and nothing else. Thus it is said:

*ta ekadā nimeḥ satram upajagmur yadṛcchayā*  
*vitāyamānam ṛṣibhir ajanābhe mahātmanaḥ*

**Once in Ajanābha (Bhārata-varṣa), the Navayogenrdasby their own will came upon the sacrificial performance of the great soul Mahārāja Nimi, which was being carried out under the direction of sages. SB 11.2.24**

The Navayogendras, by their free will (*yadṛcchayā*), came to the sacrifice. There was no other reason for their coming. *Yaḍrcchā* means independently according to Amara-koṣa. Though the Lord is ultimate doer for the devotees, he makes them act them according to *their* desires.

*asyāpi deva vapuṣo mad-anugrahasya*  
*svecchā-mayasya tanu-bhūta-mayasya ko ’pi*  
*neśe mahi tv avasituṁ manasāntareṇa*  
*sākṣāt tavaiva kim utātma-sukhānubhūteḥ*

**Even by controlling the mind, no one can understand the greatness of this form of the Lord of the universe, which manifested in many four-armed forms, but which is actually you, the one form of Svayam Bhagavān, who gave memercy by you own will, who spread through the universe in many spiritual forms and possess bliss which can be understood only by yourself. SB 10.14.2**

*ahaṁ bhakta-parādhīno hy asvatantra iva dvija*  
*sādhubhir grasta-hṛdayo bhaktair bhakta-jana-priyaḥ*

**O brāhmaṇa! I am completely under the control of my devotees. I am not at all independent. My heart is controlled by the pure devotees. What to speak of my devotee, even those who are devotees of my devotee are very dear to me. SB 9.4.63**

Nārada speaks.

## Anuccheda 182

*tasyaikadā tu bhavanam aṅgirā bhagavān ṛṣiḥ*  
*lokān anucarann etān upāgacchad yadṛcchayā*

**Once upon a time, the powerful sage named Aṅgirā, while traveling all over the universe, came to the palace of King Citraketu by his own will. SB 6.14.14**

Citraketu met the devotee Aṅgirā at his house at that time. Later he appeared again. Aṅgirā gives instructions to him, starting in SB 6.15.17. Śukadeva speaks.

## Anuccheda 183

The mercy of devotees arises just from seeing a miserable situation. It does not depend on the person worshipping the devotee. This was the case of Nārada with Nalakūvara and Maṇigrīva. It is said:

*bhajanti ye yathā devān devā api tathaiva tā.*  
*chāyeva karma-sacivāḥ sādhavo dīna-vatsalāḥ*

**Those who worship the devatās receive reciprocation from the devatās in a way corresponding to the offering. The devatās are attendants of karma, like a person's shadow, but devotees are affectionate to the fallen. SB 11.2.6**

The meaning is clear. Vasudeva speaks.

## Anuccheda 184

Since association is most purifying its bestowal does not depend on a person’s prior purity:

*na hy am-mayāni tīrthāni na devā mṛc-chilā-mayāḥ*  
*te punanty uru-kālena darśanād eva sādhavaḥ*

**Mere bodies of water are not the real sacred places of pilgrimage, nor are mere images of earth and stone the true worshipable deities. These purify one only after a long time, but saintly sages purify one immediately upon being seen. SB 10.84.11**

Other things are not respected since they are secondary causes of purification. The Lord speaks to sages.

## Anuccheda 185

Thus the cause of becoming favorable to the Lord is devotee association. This is expressed negatively:

*jñānaṁ viśuddhaṁ paramārtham ekam*  
*anantaraṁ tv abahir brahma satyam*  
*pratyak praśāntaṁ bhagavac-chabda-saṁjñaṁ*  
*yad vāsudevaṁ kavayo vadanti*

**The wise say that the truth is beyond the guṇas, contains everything including liberation, is one without a second, is all-pervading, is Brahman, Paramātmā and Bhagavān, and is Vāsudeva.**

*rahūgaṇaitat tapasā na yāti*  
*na cejyayā nirvapaṇād gṛhād vā*  
*na cchandasā naiva jalāgni-sūryair*  
*vinā mahat-pāda-rajo-'bhiṣekam*

**Without bathing in the dust from the feet of great devotees, one cannot realize the Lord through concentration of the mind, performance of sacrifices, distributing food, building shelters for the destitute, studying the Vedas, or performing austerities in the water, fire or the sun. SB 5.12.11-12**

The commentary says, “What is truth? Knowledge is truth. Conventional truth is discarded: it is spiritual (*paramārtham).* Six descriptive terms are given distinguish this knowledge from sense knowledge. It is pure, whereas convention knowledge has ignorance. It is one, whereas conventional knowledge has various forms. It is without interior or exterior (*anantaram tv abahiḥ)*. Conventional knowledge is the opposite. It is Brahman, complete, where as conventional knowledge is limited. It is inward or self-manifesting (*pratyak*) whereas conventional knowledge has material objects as its focus. It is without change whereas conventional knowledge changes. Thus this knowledge is truth. What is it? It is called Bhagavān, since the Lord possesses six qualities. They call it Vāsudeva.

One cannot attain it without service to the great devotees. That is expressed in the second verse. O Rahūgaṇa! This knowledge is not attained by austerity, by Vedic actions *(ījyayā*), by distributing food, by building houses for the poor, by studying the Vedas *(chandasā*) or by worshipping with water or fire.”

By mentioning Brahman (all-pervading) and Bhagavān (possessor of all qualities), knowledge of the jīva with its qualities of smallness is discarded as truth. The brāhmaṇa Jaḍa-bharata speaks to Rahūgaṇa.

## Anuccheda 186

Thus association is the means to become favorable to the Lord. It is accepted that those who are peaceful (santa) are favorable to the Lord (in his form as Brahman). They are not just persons dedicated to Vedic conduct, as that is unsuitable. According to the type of association one develops a particular inclination to theLord. To express this, two types of mahats are described.

*mahat-sevāṁ dvāram āhur vimuktes*  
*tamo-dvāraṁ yoṣitāṁ saṅgi-saṅgam*  
*mahāntas te sama-cittāḥ praśāntā*  
*vimanyavaḥ suhṛdaḥ sādhavo ye*

**Service to saintly persons is the cause of two types of liberation, whereas association with men who associate with women is the cause of bondage. The impersonalist sees no differences and is peaceful devoid of anger, is friendly to all and does not see faults in others.**

*ye vā mayīśe kṛta-sauhṛdārthā*  
*janeṣu dehambhara-vārtikeṣu*  
*gṛheṣu jāyātmaja-rātimatsu*  
*na prīti-yuktā yāvad-arthāś ca loke*

**The devotees have made their goal of life affection for me, the Lord, and do not show affection for persons who enjoy talks about people attached to eating and drinking and who have houses, wives, sons and wealth. The devotees with prema realize the Lord according to their worship. SB 5.5.2-3**

The qualities of persons fixed in impersonal Brahman (*sama-cittāḥ*) are described. They are peaceful etc. Then the other type of mahat is described (*ye vā*). *Vā* indicates an alternative. Since this type is mentioned later it is superior. Their puruṣārtha is prema (*sauhṛdārthāḥ*) fixed in me. *Ye* indicates that they are also mahats mentioned in the previous verse. Because their goal is prema for me, they are not fond of people absorbed in material enjoyment (*dehambhara-vārtikeṣu*), possessing houses, wives, sons and relatives. According to the way they worship the Lord, they attain the goal*(yāvad arthaḥ).*

Both these types are called mahat because one has great knowledge and the other has great devotion. Having the same name does not mean that they are equal, for it is said *muktānām api siddhānāṁ nārāyaṇa-parāyaṇaḥ:* among the liberated and the yogīs the devotees are best. (SB 6.14.50) On the path of jñāna, the mahat realizes Brahman and on the path of bhakti the mahat attains prema for the Lord. Ṛṣabha speaks to his sons.

## Anuccheda 187

The following should be considered. Two types of accomplished mahats on their respective paths were described. One type is accomplished in jñāna.

*dehaṁ ca naśvaram avasthitam utthitaṁ vā*  
*siddho na paśyati yato ’dhyagamat svarūpam*  
*daivād apetam atha daiva-vaśād upetaṁ*  
*vāso yathā parikṛtaṁ madirā-madāndhaḥ*

**The perfected sage is not aware whether he is seated or standing while situated in the temporary body, since he has realized Brahman. He is like a drunken man who is not aware of receiving, putting on or taking off his clothing. SB 11.13.36**

Those who are accomplished in bhaktiare of three types: those who have attained a body as an associate of the Lord; those who have been completely cleansed of contamination (nirdhūta-kaṣāya) and those with faint contamination (mūrcchita-kaṣāya). The example of the first is Nārada. An example of the second type is Śukadeva. An example of the third type is Nārada in his previous life.

*prayujyamāne mayi tāṁ śuddhāṁ bhāgavatīṁ tanum \|*  
*ārabdha-karma-nirvāṇo nyapatat pāñca-bhautikaḥ \|\|*

**Having been awarded a transcendental body befitting an associate of the Lord, Nārada’s body made of five material elements, with karmas relating to the present body, fell away. SB 1.6.28**

*sva-sukha-nibhṛta-cetās tad-vyudastānya-bhāvo*  
*'py ajita-rucira-līlākṛṣṭa-sāras tadīyam*  
*vyatanuta kṛpayā yas tattva-dīpaṁ purāṇaṁ*  
*tam akhila-vṛjina-ghnaṁ vyāsa-sūnuṁ nato 'smi*

**Let me offer my respectful obeisances unto the son of Vyāsadeva, Śukadeva Gosvāmī, who destroyed all contamination, and who, though fixed in his own bliss with no distractions, became attracted to the pastimes of Kṛṣṇa and mercifully spread the** ***Bhāgavatam*** **which describes Kṛṣṇa and reveals the rasaof his pastimes. SB 12.12.69**

*hantāsmin janmani bhavān mā māṁ draṣṭum ihārhati \|*  
*avipakva-kaṣāyāṇāṁ durdarśo ’haṁ kuyoginām \|\|*

**Oh! In this body you will not be able to see me again. Lax practitioners who still have some contamination cannot see me. SB 1.6.21**

Though he had some contamination in his previous birth, it is described that Nārada had prema:

*premātibhara-nirbhinna-pulakāṅgo ’tinirvṛtaḥ \|*  
*ānanda-samplave līno nāpaśyam ubhayaṁ mune \|\|*

**My limbs covered in distinct goose bumps out of excessive prema, filled with delight, I fainted out of bliss, and could not see myself or the Lord. SB 1.6.17**

Bharata is also an example. He had a deep contamination of sattva, supported by his prārabdha-karmas, in the form of desire to protect living entities. He is also described as having prema. Though all three types of siddhas have prema, the first type with spiritual body has the best prema. The siddha with all contamination cleared is second best, and then the type with slight contamination is third.

If one sees a person in his material body with transformations of prema (symptoms) which are very great, by the amount of symptoms one can infer the amount of prema. There is difference in the essential nature of prema by difference in the object of worship-- whether it is an expansion of the Lord or the aṁśī, Kṛṣṇa, and by difference in the relationship such as dāsya, sakhya etc.

There is difference in the quantity of prema by the development of prema as a sprout (bhāva) or full prema. This will be shown in Prīti Sandarbha.

Though the goal is directly meeting the Lord, in that meeting there is excellence according to the realization of the Lord’s quality of affection. Without realization of the quality of affection in the shelter of causeless affection, there is no meeting with the Lord, even if one sees him. The afflicted tongue does not really taste the sweetness of sugar candy though it is on the tongue. Thus Ṛṣabha says:

*evaṁ manaḥ karma-vaśaṁ prayuṅkte*  
*avidyayātmany upadhīyamāne*  
*prītir na yāvan mayi vāsudeve*  
*na mucyate deha-yogena tāvat*

**Thus,karmas make the mind fixed upon further action. Since the jīva is engaged in action by ignorance, having accepted a subtle body, as long as he does not have devotion for me, Vāsudeva, he will not be liberated from the body. SB 5.5.6**

Thus the greatness of the devotee is according to the amount of prema. That is expressed in *mayīśe kṛta-sauhṛdārthāḥ*: the devotees’ goal is prema for me, the Lord. (SB 5.5.3)The greatest devotee is he who has great prema, has a meeting with the Lord and no contamination. When any of these three items are deficient, the devotee is of lower status. Thus SB 5.5.3 quoted above speaks of devotees who have not attained spiritual bodies and thus possibly have some deep impressions of material objects, though they practice renunciation of material life. This will be dealt with in another section.

*atha bhāgavataṁ brūta yad-dharmo yādṛśo nṛṇām*  
*yathācarati yad brute yair liṅgair bhagavat-priyaḥ*

**Now please tell me about the devotees of the Supreme Lord--their natures, the different types, the conduct, and speech by which one can know who is dear to the Lord. SB 11.2.44**

After this, speak of the devotee *(bhāgavatam*), in order that I can understand. Describe his nature (*dharmaḥ*). Speak of his conduct, how he speaks (*yad brute*). He asks about the devotee’s mind, body and words.

## Anuccheda 188

In previous verses those qualities were described by Kavi. (SB 11.2.39) That is true. But again describe the devotee, considering which among his characteristics determine different levels of uttama, madhyama and kaniṣṭha. In answer Havi says:

*sarva-bhūteṣu yaḥ paśyed bhagavad-bhāvam ātmanaḥ*  
*bhūtāni bhagavaty ātmany eṣa bhāgavatottamaḥ*

**The most advanced devotee sees the Supreme Lord, one’s object of worship, within everything, and sees everything in the Supreme Lord’ heart. SB 11.2.45**

Here the mahā-bhāgavata is described in terms of mental qualities which are understood by external symptoms. These symptoms, produced by anurāga, were described by Kavi:

*evaṁ-vrataḥ sva-priya-nāma-kīrtyā*  
*jātānurāgo druta-citta uccaiḥ*  
*hasaty atho roditi rauti gāyaty*  
*unmāda-van nṛtyati loka-bāhyaḥ*

**Regulated with such devotion, chanting the name of his dear Kṛṣṇa, he develops prema. His heart melts and he laughs loudly, weeps, wails and sings. He dances like a madman without regard for the public. SB 11.2.40**

He experiences (*bhāvaṁ paśyati*) only his cherished Lord in all conscious and unconscious matter as described in the following:

*khaṁ vāyum agniṁ salilaṁ mahīṁ ca*  
*jyotīṁṣi sattvāni diśo drumādīn*  
*sarit-samudrāṁś ca hareḥ śarīraṁ*  
*yat kiṁ ca bhūtaṁ praṇamed ananyaḥ*

**A person devoted only to the Lord should bow down to ether, fire, air, water, earth, the sun and other luminaries, all living beings, the directions, trees and other plants, the rivers and oceans, seeing them as the body of Kṛṣṇa. SB 11.2.40**

He experiences all entities in the Lord since the Lord is their shelter. All beings appear in the Lord’s heart. This is the uttama devotee. The gopīs are described in this way:

*vana-latās tarava ātmani viṣṇuṁ*  
*vyañjayantya iva puṣpa-phalāḍhyāḥ*  
*praṇata-bhāra-viṭapā madhu-dhārāḥ*  
*prema-hṛṣṭa-tanavo vavṛṣuḥ sma*

**Kṛṣṇa moves about the forest in the company of his friends, who vividly chant the glories of his magnificent deeds. He thus appears as the most outstanding person, exhibiting his inexhaustible sweetness. When the cows wander onto the mountainsides and Kṛṣṇa calls out to them with the sound of his flute, the trees and creepers in the forest respond by becoming so luxuriant with fruits and flowers that they seem to be manifesting Kṛṣṇa, spread everywhere, within their hearts. As their branches bend low with the weight, the filaments on their trunks and vines stand erect out of prema, and both the trees and the creepers shower sweet sap. SB 10.35.9**

Or the uttama devotee sees his prema for the Lord in all entities conscious and unconscious. The rest is the same. Therefore he offers respect to all entities with devotion because he thinks that they are all devotees as explained in SB 11.2.40. Thus it is said:

*nadyas tadā tad upadhārya mukunda-gītam*  
*āvarta-lakṣita-manobhava-bhagna-vegāḥ*  
*āliṅgana-sthagitam ūrmi-bhujair murārer*  
*gṛhṇanti pāda-yugalaṁ kamalopahārāḥ*

**When the rivers hear the flute-song of Kṛṣṇa, the flow of their currents is broken and their waters are agitated out of desire, moving around in whirlpools. Presenting offerings of lotus flowers with the arms of their waves, the rivers grasp Murāri’s lotus feet, covering them in their embrace. SB 10.21.15**

The queens also say:

*kurari vilapasi tvaṁ vīta-nidrā na śeṣe*  
*svapiti jagati rātryām īśvaro gupta-bodhaḥ*  
*vayam iva sakhi kaccid gāḍha-nirviddha-cetā*  
*nalina-nayana-hāsodāra-līlekṣitena*

**O kurarī bird, you are lamenting. Now it is night, and somewhere in this world our husband is asleep in a hidden place. But you are wide awake, O friend, unable to fall asleep. Is it that, like us, you have had your heart pierced to the core by the lotus-eyed Lord’s munificent, playful smiling glances? SB 10.90.15**

They are not described as brahma-jñānīs (though it is said they see the Lord everywhere) since that jñāna and the results of that jñāna are despised by the devotees and it is contrary to being a devotee because then there would be no difference between the jīva and the Lord. This would contradict the position of the uttama expressed in the following description of aikāntika-bhakti:

*lakṣaṇaṁ bhakti-yogasya nirguṇasya hy udāhṛtam*  
*ahaituky avyavahitā yā bhaktiḥ puruṣottame*

**That quality of bhakti beyond the guṇas is thatbhakti which is devoid of results other than bhaktiand is unobstructed by other processes. SB 3.29.12**

Nor does the definition represent realization of a Lord without form as this would contradict the highest position summarized in the conclusion of the section:

*visṛjati hṛdayaṁ na yasya sākṣād*  
*dharir avaśābhihito 'py aghaugha-nāśaḥ*  
*praṇaya-rasanayā dhṛtāṅghri-padmaḥ*  
*sa bhavati bhāgavata-pradhāna uktaḥ*

**He who binds the lotus feet of the Lord by ropes of love, and whose heart the Lord, destroyer of heaps of sin, does not leave, even if beckoned accidentally, is called the best of devotees. SB 11.2.55**

## Anuccheda 189

The madhyama-bhāgavata is characterized by special mental qualities:

*īsvare tad-adhīneṣu bāliśeṣu dviṣatsu ca*  
*prema-maitrī-kṛpopekṣā yaḥ karoti sa madhyamaḥ*

**An intermediate or second-class devotee, called madhyama-adhikārī*****,*** **offers his love to the Supreme Lord, is a sincere friend to all the devotees of the Lord, shows mercy to ignorant people who are innocent, and disregards those who are envious of the Lord. SB 11.2.46**

He has prema for the Lord. This means he has bhakti for the Lord (since he has not developed prema). He is friendly towards devotees *(tad-adhineṣu*). He is merciful to those who do not know about bhakti or who are indifferent. Prahlāda says:

*śoce tato vimukha-cetasa indriyārtha-*  
*māyā-sukhāya bharam udvahato vimūḍhān*

**I lament for the fools, averse to you, who carry the burden of material responsibilities for gaining material happiness from sense objects. SB 7.9.43**

He ignores those who are inimical to himself, not being disturbed by their enmity to him since he has somecompassion for them as ignorant souls. Prahlāda had this attitude towards his father. Though he is disturbed when persons are inimical to the Lord and other devotees, he is not overcome by that. He manifests mercy to the innocent and indifference to those who hate the Lord. Unlike the uttama, he does not see everything in terms of prema. Therefore he is in the category of madhyama.

The uttama devotee however does show great bliss on seeing the devotees, taking them as a manifestation of the Lord. Great friendliness to the devotee is not forbidden for the uttama, but such attitude must also be there for all beings. Śiva expresses his friendliness to devotees:

*kṣaṇārdhenāpi tulayena svargaṁ nāpunar-bhavam*  
*bhagavat-saṅgi-saṅgasyamartyānāṁ kim utāśiṣaḥ*

**What to speak of any blessings on this earth, or on Svarga, even the blessing of liberation cannot compare with even half a moment’s association with the devotee of the Lord. SB 4.24.27**

*atha bhāgavatā yūyaṁ priyāḥ stha bhagavān yathā*  
*na mad bhāgavatānāṁ ca preyān anyo 'sti karhicit*

**You devotees are dear to me just as the Supreme Lord is dear to me. The devotees hold me dear, just as they hold the Lord dear. SB 4.24.30**

Sūta says:

*harer guṇākṣipta-matir bhagavān bādarāyaṇiḥ \|*  
*adhyagān mahad ākhyānaṁ nityaṁ viṣṇu-jana-priyaḥ \|\|*

**Śukadeva, the son of Vyāsa, whose mind became distracted from trance by the qualities of Kṛṣṇa, and who became attracted to the Vaiṣṇavas, studied this great Bhāgavatam. SB 1.7.11**

Śukadeva (uttama) shows hatred for Kaṁsa. But this hatreddoes not manifest constantly as in the case of madhyamas. In the uttamas the sphūrti of the Lord(seeing him everywhere) is not obstructed by this hatred since they see the Lordin the demons as the punishers of the demons. Uddhava offers respect to Duryodhana by seeing the Lordin this way.

Śiva says (concerning the Lord appearing within):

*sattvaṁ viśuddhaṁ vasudeva-śabditaṁ*  
*yad īyate tatra pumān apāvṛtaḥ*  
*sattve ca tasmin bhagavān vāsudevo*  
*hy adhokṣajo me namasā vidhīyate*

**The pure antaḥ-karaṇa is called Vasudeva, because there the Supreme Lord appears without coverings. In that antaḥ-karaṇa, I worshipped the Supreme Lord Vāsudeva, beyond the material senses, by offering respects. SB 4.3.23**

In the kidnapping of Lakṣmaṇā, Uddhava offered respects to Duryodhana.

*so ’bhivandyāmbikā-putraṁ bhīṣmaṁ droṇaṁ ca bāhlikam*  
*duryodhanaṁ ca vidhi-vad rāmam āgataṁ abravīt*

**After he had offered proper respects to Dhṛtarāṣṭra and to Bhīṣma, Droṇa, Bāhlika and Duryodhana, Uddhava informed them that Balarāma had arrived. SB 10.68.17**

The uttamas, who see everything in terms of their prema, see the demons in the same way. This is not unsuitable for they are agitated on seeing the bad behavior of the demons who are particles of pollen at the lotus feet of the Lord worshipped by millions of their life airs. According to their bhāva they then think, “What kind of person is this who does not have love for our dear one, the Supreme Lord, with sweet pastimes benefiting all beings, who is ornamented with gems of good qualities and is compassionate to all people, who is the shelter of the highest, unconditional prema, who is the culmination of all bliss? The cause of their hatred is beyond my comprehension.

All beings from Brahmā down to immovable beings, good or evil, are attracted to the Lord.”

Śukadeva says:

*govinda-bhuja-guptāyāṁ dvāravatyāṁ kurūdvaha*  
*avātsīn nārado 'bhīkṣṇaṁ kṛṣṇopāsana-lālasaḥ*

**O best of the Kurus! Eager to engage in the worship of Kṛṣṇa, Nārada Muni stayed constantly in Dvārakā, which was always protected by the arms of Govinda.**

*ko nu rājann indriyavān mukunda-caraṇāmbujam*  
*na bhajet sarvato-mṛtyur upāsyam amarottamaiḥ*

**O King! Who among the conditioned souls with senses, confronted by death at every step of life, would not render service to the lotus feet of Mukunda, who is worshipable even for the greatestdevatās? SB 11.2.1-2**

## Anuccheda 190

The kaniṣṭḥa devotee is described in terms of body and somewhat the mind in relation to conduct related to the Lord.

*arcāyām eva haraye pūjāṁ yaḥ śraddhayehate*  
*na tad-bhakteṣu cānyeṣu sa bhaktaḥ prākṛtaḥ smṛtaḥ*

**A devotee who faithfully engages in the worship of the deity in the temple but does not behave properly toward devotees or people in general is called an unrefined devotee. SB 11.2.47**

He has faith in the deity (*arcāyām*) but not in the devotees or others since he does not have prema for the Lord, lacks knowledge of the glories of the devotees and has not manifested the devotee’s quality of respecting all beings. This devotee is a person who has begun bhakti (*prākṛtaḥ).* His faith has not arisen from understanding the meaning of scriptures.

*yasyātma-buddhiḥ kuṇape tri-dhātuke*  
*sva-dhīḥ kalatrādiṣu bhauma ijya-dhīḥ*  
*yat-tīrtha-buddhiḥ salile na karhicij*  
*janeṣv abhijñeṣu sa eva go-kharaḥ*

**One who identifies his self as the inert body composed of mucus, bile and air, who assumes his wife and family are permanently his own, who thinks an earthen image or the land of his birth is worshipable, or who sees a place of pilgrimage as merely the water there, but who never identifies himself with, feels kinship with, worships or even visits those who are wise in spiritual truth—such a person is no better than a cow or an ass. SB 10.84.13**

This condition arises from not understanding scriptural statements like the verse above. This faith is based on tradition only. Thus the category of kaniṣṭha sādhaka has faith not based on scripture and has not developed prema.

## Anuccheda 191

The commentary says, “Then in eight verses, the qualities of the uttama are again described because of great respect for him.”

*gṛhītvāpīndriyair arthān yo na dveṣṭi na hṛṣyati*  
*viṣṇor māyām idaṁ paśyan sa vai bhāgavatottamaḥ*

**Even while engaging his senses in contact with their objects, one who sees this whole world as the energy of Viṣṇu and is neither repelled nor elated is the greatest among devotees. SB 11.2.48**

As was previously explained his mind is not overwhelmed. Though accepting objects with his senses, he does not hate or rejoice. He sees the world *(idam*) as disgusting since it is the play of the external energy (*māyām*). This is a mixture of bodily and mental qualities.

## Anuccheda 192

Qualities of the mind only are described for the rest of the section:

*dehendriya-prāṇa-mano-dhiyāṁ yo*  
*janmāpyaya-kṣud-bhaya-tarṣa-kṛcchraiḥ*  
*saṁsāra-dharmair avimuhyamānaḥ*  
*smṛtyā harer bhāgavata-pradhānaḥ*

**A person who, by remembering the Lord, is not bewildered by the qualities of saṁsāra, such as birth and death of the body, hunger of the prāṇas, fear in the mind, longing in the intelligence and fatigue in the senses, is the best among the devotees. SB 11.2.49**

He who is not bewildered by the qualities of saṁsāra, by birth, death, hunger, fear, longing and fatigue of the body, senses, prāṇa, mind and intelligence is the best devotee. Gītā says:

*yeṣāṁ tv anta-gataṁ pāpaṁ janānāṁ puṇya-karmaṇām \|*  
*te dvandva-moha-nirmuktā bhajante māṁ dṛḍha-vratāḥ \|\|*

**But those whose sins have been destroyed by actions pleasing to the great souls, who are free of this ignorance arising from duality, worship me with determination. BG 7.28**

## Anuccheda 193

Then it is said:

*na kāma-karma-bījānāṁ yasya cetasi sambhavaḥ*  
*vāsudevaika-nilayaḥ sa vai bhāgavatottamaḥ*

**The person completely surrendered to the Lord, in whose mind no impressions, desires or actions arise, is considered to be the best of devotees. SB 11.2.50**

*Bījāni*means impressions. Vāsudeva is his shelter.

## Anuccheda 194

*na yasya janma-karmabhyāṁ na varṇāśrama-jātibhiḥ*  
*sajjate 'sminn ahaṁ-bhāvo dehe vai sa hareḥ priyaḥ*

**The devotee whose body does not give rise to false identity with good family, pious actions, or caste considerations is the best devotee. SB 11.2.51**

That person in whose body false identity does not adhere by good family *(janma*), pious acts like austerity, by anuloma, mixed caste etc. (*jātibhiḥ*), but who is attached to the body useful for serving the Lord is dear to the Lord. He is the best devotee. *Bhagavatottamaḥ* is understood from the previous verse since the subjects is the uttama devotee. *Hareḥ priyaḥ* only indicates that he is a devotee.

## Anuccheda 195

*na yasya svaḥ para iti vitteṣv ātmani vā bhidā*  
*sarva-bhūta-samaḥ śāntaḥ sa vai bhāgavatottamaḥ*

**He who does not think that wealth is his, who does not think in terms of self and other, who is equal to all beings and peaceful, is the best devotee. SB 11.2.52**

Wealth, the object of possessiveness, does not belong to himself or others. He does not think of himself in terms of self and others. He does not distinguish difference between self and others, siding with himself.

*para-duḥkhenātma-duḥkhaṁ manyante ye nṛpottama \|*  
*bhagavad-dharma-niratās te narā vaiṣṇavottamāḥ \|\|*

**O king! Those who think of others suffering as their own are the best of devotees, constantly engaged in serving the Lord. Skanda Purāṇa.**

## Anuccheda 196

Then it is said:

*tri-bhuvana-vibhava-hetave 'py akuṇṭha-*  
*smṛtir ajitātma-surādibhir vimṛgyāt*  
*na calati bhagavat-padāravindāl*  
*lava-nimiṣārdham api yaḥ sa vaiṣṇavāgryaḥ*

**He, whose remembrance is not lured by dominion over the three worlds, and who does not move for half a minute or half a second from the Lord’s lotus feet which are sought by the devatās who also meditate on the Lord, is the best of devotees. SB 11.2.53**

The cause of his steadiness is explained in this verse. He is not lured by the wealth of the three world because he does not move from the lotus feet of the Lord rare for the devatās like Brahmā and Śiva, whose soul is the Lord (*ajita).*

## Anuccheda 197

If one burns with desire one should deviate and search for material enjoyment. This is not possible if one is blissful in service to the Lord:

*bhagavata uru-vikramāṅghri-śākhā-*  
*nakha-maṇi-candrikayā nirasta-tāpe*  
*hṛdi katham upasīdatāṁ punaḥ sa*  
*prabhavati candra ivodite 'rka-tāpaḥ*

**How can the heat of material existence again arise in the hearts of those who worship the Lord, since the heat of lust is destroyed by the Lord’s heroic lotus feet, his toes, his toenails, his toe jewels and the soothing light from his feet? When the moon rises, the heat of the sun is dissipated. SB 11.2.54**

The Lord’s feet are heroic (*uru-vikrama*). Heat is extinguishedby thelight from the nails of the toes of his heroic feet. The light destroys the heat of lust.

## Anuccheda 198

*visṛjati hṛdayaṁ na yasya sākṣād*  
*dharir avaśābhihito 'py aghaugha-nāśaḥ*  
*praṇaya-rasanayā dhṛtāṅghri-padmaḥ*  
*sa bhavati bhāgavata-pradhāna uktaḥ*

**He who binds the lotus feet of the Lord by ropes of love, and whose heart the Lord, destroyer of heaps of sin, does not leave, even if beckoned accidentally, is called the best of devotees. SB 11.2.55**

The commentary says, “The essence of all the qualities previously mentioned is now stated. The Lord does not leave his heart. Being addressed even accidently the Lord destroys sin. Why does he not leave? His lotus feet are bound firmly to the devotee’s heart by ropes of love. This is the uttama bhakti.”

Material desire is not possible in this devotee because the Lord will personally (*sākṣāt*) become present later on. Being glorified accidently the Lord destroys sin. How much more then, being glorified by the devotee with full absorption at all times, the Lord will destroy sins! In either case, the impressions of sin cannot remain.

*etan nirvidyamānānām icchatām akuto-bhayam \|*  
*yogināṁ nṛpa nirṇītaṁ harer nāmānukīrtanam \|\|*

**O King! Continuous chanting of the name of the Lord has been prescribed for devotees, for those with desires for liberation and material elevation, and for those satisfied with the self. SB 2.1.11**

It was asked “What does the devotee speak?” (SB 11.2.42) This verse answers by saying the devotee glorifies the Lord.

The above verses are understood to represent individual qualities (appearing in some uttamas) and common qualities (appearing in all uttamas) since they are included in qualities of the uttama devotee who brings the Lord under his control. Sometimes two or three qualities will manifest. The common quality is that one sees the Lord everywhere, with the individual qualities later mentioned in the respective verses. All these qualities are summarized in the verse presently under discussion, which is the cause of these qualities. Remembrance has been mentioned in verse 49 as the cause of the Lord’s presence. Thus the present verse may be considered the concluding statement. The word bhāgavatottama should be understood for each verse. Though relationship with the Lord is not mentioned in particular verses, the connection with the Lord is understood since the section is dealing with the devotee and these verses mention the word *bhāgavata.*Or the verses may be connected with verse 49. Or the verses may be considered to depend on SB 11.2.45 defining the uttama devotee.

There is a sequence of increasingly superior qualities. Some verses deal with saṁskāras (impressions): 47 (worshipping the Lord), 51,52, 49, and 48. But the saṁskāras are faint (mūrcchita-kaśāya) and the person willnot be bewildered by them. The person has a new sprout of prema (bhāva). Verses 50 and 53 describe a devotee who is fixed in the state called dhruvānusmrti or meditation. The sprout of prema (bhāva) has become unobstructed. Otherwise the person could not continually remember the Lord. That person is at the stage of having destroyed all contamination (nirdhūta-kaṣāya). The sprout has grown up. There is friendship with devotees and prema for the Lord,which are causes of bhakti (verse 46). There is no contamination at this stage. Above that, prema manifests. At the stage of prema, the contamination is absent and there is mahāprema. That is indicated in verse 45 and described in 55.

*tāpādi-pañca-saṁskāro navejyā-karma-kārakaḥ \|*  
*artha-pañcaka-vid vipro mahā-bhāgavataḥ smṛtaḥ \|\|*

**The mahā-bhāgavata is a person who has undergone the five saṁskāras such as branding, performing the rituals like navejyā and knows the artha-pañcaka. Padma Purāṇa 6.253.27**

This mahā-bhāgavata in terms of arcana, since he has not perfected prema. The five saṁskāras refers to branding, accepting a name, tilaka, mantra and deity worship. Navejyā-karma is described in the following:

*arcanaṁ mantra-paṭhanaṁ yogo yāgo hi vandanam \|*  
*nāma-saṅkīrtanaṁ sevā tac-cihner aṅkanaṁ tathā \|\|*  
*tadīyārādhanaṁ cejyā navadhā bhidyate śubhe \|*  
*nava-karma-vidhānejyā viprāṇāṁ satataṁ smṛtā \|\|*

**The nine parts of worship of the Lord are arcana, reciting mantras, meditation, sacrifice, offering respects, chanting the name, service, wearing the marks of a Vaiṣṇava, and respect for devotees. These are known as the navejyā karmas of the brāhmaṇa.**

One should know the artha-pañcaka: the Lord, his abode, objects in his abode, the Lord’s mantra and the jīva. This is briefly described in Hayaśīrṣa-pañcarātra:

*eka eveśvaraḥ kṛṣṇaḥ sac-cid-ānanda-vigrahaḥ \|*  
*puṇḍarīka-viśālākṣaḥ kṛṣṇa-cchurita-mūrdhajaḥ \|\|*  
*vaikuṇṭhādhipatir devyā līlayā cit-svarūpayā \|*  
*svarṇa-kāntyā viśālākhyā svabhāvād gāḍham āśritaḥ \|\|*  
*nityaḥ sarvagataḥ pūrṇo vyāpakaḥ sarva-kāraṇam \|*  
*veda-guhyo gabhīrātmā nānā-śaktyodayo naraḥ \|\|*

**The one Lord is Kṛṣṇa, full of eternity, knowledge and bliss. He has lotus petal eyes, and black hair. The Lord of Vaikuṇṭha is embraced deeply by a woman golden in color, with spiritual form and playful pastimes because of hereminent nature.He is eternal, all-pervading, complete, omnipresent, the cause of all causes, hidden in the Vedas, inscrutable, possessing many śaktis.**

*sthāna-tattvam ato vakṣye prakṛteḥ param avyayam \|*  
*śuddha-sattva-mayaṁ sūrya-candra-koṭi-sama-prabham \|\|*  
*cintāmaṇimayaṁ sākṣāt sac-cid-ānanda-lakṣaṇam \|*  
*ādhāraṁ sarva-bhūtānāṁ sarva-pralaya-varjitam \|\|*

**I will explain the nature of the place: it is beyond matter, indestructible, made of śuddha-sattva, effulgent as ten million suns and moons. It is made of cintāmaṇi, is full of eternity, knowledge and bliss. It is the support for all beings and is devoid of any destruction.**

*dravya-tattvaṁ śṛṇu brahman pravakṣyāmi samāsataḥ \|*  
*sarva-bhoga-pradā yatra pādapāḥ kalpa-pādapāḥ \|\|*  
*bhavanti tādṛśā vallyas tad-bhavaṁ cāpi tādṛśam \|*  
*gandha-rūpaṁ svādu-rūpaṁ dravyaṁ puṣpādikaṁ ca yat \|\|*  
*heyāṁśānām abhāvāc ca rasa-rūpaṁ bhaved dhi tat \|*  
*tvag-bījaṁ caiva heyāṁśaṁ kaṭhināṁśaṁ ca yad bhavet \|\|*  
*sarva tad bhautikaṁ viddhi na hy abhūtamayaṁ ca tat \|*  
*rasasya yogato brahman bhautikaṁ svāduvad bhavet \|\|*  
*tasmāt sādhyo raso brahman rasaḥ syād vyāpakaḥ paraḥ \|*  
*rasavad bhautikaṁ dravyam atra syād rasa-rūpakam \|\|*

**Please hear about the nature of the objects there. I will explain it in brief. The trees are desire trees bestowing all enjoyment. The creepers are similar. There are many flowers and fruits, the essence of fragrance, taste and rasa since they have no inferior parts such as skin, seeds or hard sections.**

Know that all this is not material. It is most astonishing. Because of having rasa Brahman appears full of taste like material objects. Brahman is ultimately rasa. That supreme rasa spreads everywhere. Because of spiritual rasa, material objects have rasa and are celebrated as tasty.

*vācyatvaṁ vācakatvaṁ ca deva-tan-mantrayor iha \|*  
*abhedenocyate brahmaṁs tattvavidbhir vicāritaḥ \|\|*

**The Lord and his mantra as what is expressed and the means of expression are non-different. In this way the wise understand Brahman.**

*marut-sāgara-saṁyoge taraṅgāt kaṇikā yathā \|*  
*jāyante tat-svarūpāś ca tad-upādhi-samāvṛtāḥ \|\|*  
*āśleṣād ubhayos tadvad ātmanaś ca sahasraśaḥ \|*  
*sañjātāḥ sarvato brahman mūrtāmūrta-svarūpataḥ \|\|*

**Just as water drops arise from waves by combination of wind with the ocean, thesvarūpas of thousands of ātmās combined with their upādhis arise when the Lord and his consort. Brahman spreads everywhere with form and without form.**

One should understand some differences in the descriptions of the Lord and his manifestations according to the scriptures used in one’s worship.

Jīva with his upādhis is described as follows:

*na ghaṭata udbhavaḥ prakṛti-pūruṣayor ajayor*  
*ubhaya-yujā bhavanty asu-bhṛto jala-budbuda-vat*  
*tvayi ta ime tato vividha-nāma-guṇaiḥ parame*  
*sarita ivārṇave madhuni lilyur aśeṣa-rasāḥ*

**Neither material nature nor the soul who tries to enjoy her are ever born, yet living bodies come into being when these two combine, pervaded by you, just as bubbles form where water and air join with ākāśa. And just as rivers merge into the ocean or the nectar from many different flowers blends into honey, so all these conditioned beings eventually merge back into you, the Supreme, along with their various names and qualities. SB 10.87.31**

Without upādhis the jīva is described as follows:

*viṣṇu-śaktiḥ parā proktā kṣetrajñākhyā tathāparā \|*  
*avidyā-karma-saṁjñānyā tṛtīyā śaktir iṣyate \|\|*

**The śakis of Viṣṇu are the superior śakti, the knower of the field (jīva) and the third śakti called ignorance and karma (acit-śakti). Viṣṇu Purāṇa 6.7.61**

Gītā described the jīva thus:

*jīva-bhūtāṁ mahā-bāho yayedaṁ dhāryate jagat \|\|*

**This is my inferior energy. But understand my superior energy which is different from this inferior energy. It is the jīvas, who employ the inferior energy for their enjoyment. BG 7.5**

*mamaivāṁśo jīva-loke jīva-bhūtaḥ sanātanaḥ \|*  
*manaḥ-ṣaṣṭhānīndriyāṇi prakṛti-sthāni karṣati \|\|*

**This eternal jīva, one of my parts, drags with it the senses and the sixth sense called the mind, which are the products of ahaṅkāra in prakṛti. BG 15.7**

*yat taṭasthaṁ tu cid-rūpaṁ sva-saṁvedyād vinirgatam \|*  
*rañjitaṁ guṇa-rāgeṇa sa jīva iti kathyate \|\|*

**The jīva is the marginal energy, conscious, ignorant of the Lord, and stained by the color of the guṇas. Nārada-pañcarātra**

The Lord speaks to Nimi.

## Anuccheda 199

This is what has been taught: among the devotees, different types of great devotees such as mūcchita-kaśāya and different general categories of devotees (uttama, madhyama and kaniṣṭha) in the general categories, ranking of devotees according to degrees of manifestation of respective.

The difference in the devotee according to the difference in sādhana is now explained in five verses of which three verses describe inferior, mixed bhakti:

*kṛpālur akṛta-drohas titikṣuḥ sarva-dehinām*  
*satya-sāro ’navadyātmā samaḥ sarvopakārakaḥ*  
*kāmair ahata-dhīr dānto mṛduḥ śucir akiñcanaḥ*  
*anīho mita-bhuk śāntaḥ sthiro mac-charaṇo muniḥ*  
*apramatto gabhīrātmā dhṛtimāñ jita-ṣaḍ-guṇaḥ*  
*amānī māna-daḥ kalyo maitraḥ kāruṇikaḥ kaviḥ*

**O Uddhava, a saintly person is merciful and never injures others. He is tolerant toward all living entities. He is most truthful. He is free from all envy and jealousy, and his mind is equal in material happiness and distress. He dedicates his time to work for the welfare of all others. His intelligence is never bewildered by material desires and he has controlled his senses. His behavior is always pleasing and exemplary and he is free from possessiveness. He never endeavors in worldly activities and he controls his eating. He has śānti-rati and performs his duties until completion. A saintly person is contemplative and accepts me as his only shelter. Such a person is alert and inscrutable and not subject to changes. He has conquered over the six material qualities—namely hunger, thirst, lamentation, illusion, old age and death. He is free from all desire for prestige and offers honor to others. He is expert in understanding others and is free from cheating. He is compassionate and understands bondage and liberation. SB 11.11.29-31**

The commentary says, “*Kṛpāluḥ*means compassionate, not tolerating other’s suffering. The devotee does not commit violence against any living entity. He is tolerant. He is strong in truth (*satya-sāram*). He is devoid of envy *(anavadyātmā*). He is calm in happiness and distress (*samaḥ*). To the best of his ability he helps others. His heart is not disturbed by desires. He controls the external senses (*dāntaḥ*). His heart is soft (*mṛduḥ).* He is non possessive (*akiñcanaḥ*). He does not perform sinful actions *(anīhaḥ*). He eats moderately (*mitabhuk*). He has controlled mind *(śāntaḥ*). He is steady in his dharma *(sthiraḥ*). He takes shelter only of me. He meditates (*muniḥ*). He is attentive (*apramattaḥ*). He is without change *(gabhīrātmā*). He is not hard hearted in dangerous situations *(dhṛtimān*). He has conquered the six urges of lamentation, illusion, old age, death, hunger and thirst. He does not desire honor *(amānī*). He gives respect to others. He is expert in understanding others (*kalyaḥ*). He does not cheat others (*maitraḥ*). He acts out of compassion not for personal greed (*kāruṇikaḥ*). He is knowledgeable (*kaviḥ*).”

The main item is “taking shelter of me.” In the next verse it says*māṁ bhajeta sa ca sattamaḥ*: the person who worships me is the best. The word *ca* indicates that also what was previously stated: the person who takes shelter of me, is the best.

## Anuccheda 200

The next higher or madhyama stage, unmixed direct bhakti is described:

*ājñāyaivaṁ guṇān doṣān mayādiṣṭān api svakān*  
*dharmān santyajya yaḥ sarvān māṁ bhajeta sa ca sattamaḥ*

**A person who, understanding good and bad aspects of dharma as taught by me gives up all his duties and simply worships me is the best of all. SB 11.11.32**

The commentary says, “Giving up one’s sva-dharma as taught by me, who am the form of the Vedas, he who worships me, who was mentioned before, is the best. Does a person give up sva-dharma out of ignorance or atheism? No. Knowing the good qualities and the faults (*ājñāya guṇān doṣān*) from not performing dharma, convinced that by performing bhakti all results will appear, he gives up dharma. Or, he gives up sva-dharma because of strength of his bhakti, which makes him qualified for renouncing it.”

Giving up dharmas is expressed in the following:

*ye tyakta-loka-dharmārthā viṣṇu-bhakti-vaśaṁ gatāḥ \|*  
*dhyāyanti paramātmānaṁ tebhyo’pīha namo namaḥ \|\|*

**I offer repeated respects to those who having given up goals of material dharma, and dedicating themselves to Viṣṇu,meditate on Paramātmā. Hāyaśīrṣa-pañcarātra**

The verse may be explained as follows also. If one lacks all the qualities mentioned above, but understands good qualities like compassion and their opposites (*doṣān*) as well, determining good and badamong those qualities, and if one gives up all dharma prescribed by varṇāśrama (*svākān dharmān)*, including jñāna, with all one’s nitya and naimittika duties, which were instructed by me, because they are obstacle to bhakti, that person who worships me is the best. The word *ca* indicates that he who lacks the qualities mentioned in the previous verses, but takes shelter of me is also the best,as if he had all the qualities mentioned. And the person whoattains those qualities, but worships only mewithout dharma and jñāna, he is even better than the devotee just mentioned. One should also see the list of qualities of the devotee mentioned in Bhagavad-gītā, chapter twelve (BG 12.12).

Thus here the lesser devotee (mixed devotee described in verses 29-31), the better (sattara who rejects mixture of karma and jñāna) and very best (sattama who rejects karma and jñāna, but is endowed with the favorable qualities mentioned in verses 29-31) are described.

This devotee is called sattama because he behaves properly. Though he may perform sinful acts he is still considered proper (sādhu has the samemeaning as sattva) because he accepts only the Lord as the object of worship. This is explained in BG 9.30 with *api cet sudrācāraḥ.. sādhur eva sa mantavyaḥ.* However such a devotee with bad conduct is not mentioned when speaking of association of devotees (even though he is sādhu) because by that association bhakti does not develop. Thus Prahlāda says:

*guru-śuśrūṣayā bhaktyā sarva-labdhārpaṇena ca*  
*saṅgena sādhu-bhaktānām īśvarārādhanena ca*  
*śraddhayā tat-kathāyāṁ ca kīrtanair guṇa-karmaṇām*  
*tat-pādāmburuha-dhyānāt tal-liṅgekṣārhaṇādibhiḥ*

**One performs bhakti-yoga by service to guru, offering him all that one possesses, by association with devotees of good conduct, by worship of the Lord, by faith in topics of the Lord, by chanting about the Lord’s qualities and activities, by meditation on the Lord’s lotus feet, and by seeing the deity and making offerings to the deity. SB 7.7.30-31**

In this verse the word *sādhu*means proper behavior.

In this way the two grades of devotees (mixed and pure) on the path of vaidhī bhakti, with awareness of the powerful Lord, have been described. That latter type is superior because of being ananya or unmixed.

On the path of arcana there are three types of devotees according to Padma Purāṇa. The best devotee has the five saṁskāras, navejyā karma and artha-pañcaka. The madhyama is described as follows:

*tāpaṁ puṇḍraṁ tathā nāma mantro yāgaś ca pañcamaḥ \|*  
*amī pañcaiva saṁskārāḥ paramaikānti-hetavaḥ \|\| ity atra \|*

**The five saṁskāras of branding, tilaka, accepting a name, mantra and worship are the causes of being the best devotee.**

The kaniṣṭha is described as follows:

*śaṅkha-cakrādy-ūrdhva-puṇḍra-dhāraṇādy-ātma-lakṣaṇam \|*  
*tan-namaskaraṇaṁ caiva vaiṣṇavatvam ihocyate \|\|*

**One should offer respects to a person with branding of conch and cakra and tilaka as a Vaiṣṇava.**

## Anuccheda 201

The ananya-bhakti with pure dāsya, sakhya etc. is the best among all:

*jñātvājñātvātha ye vai māṁ yāvān yaś cāsmi yādṛśaḥ*  
*bhajanty ananya-bhāvena te me bhaktatamā matāḥ*

**Those who may or may not know the extent of my powers, my forms and my nature, but who worship me with great possessiveness, I consider to be the best of devotees. SB 11.11.33**

Those who worship me, the soul of all beings (*yaḥ*), a form of eternity, knowledge and bliss, (*yādṛśaḥ)* at all times and places *(yāvāt*),knowing or not knowing me, with ananya-bhāva, one bhāva among dāsya, sakhya, vātsalya and mādhurya, for the son of Nanda-- they are considered the best devotees by me. The great yogīs say:

*preyān na te 'nyo 'sty amutas tvayi prabho*  
*viśvātmanīkṣen na pṛthag ya ātmanaḥ*  
*athāpi bhaktyeśa tayopadhāvatām*  
*ananya-vṛttyānugṛhāṇa vatsala*

**O Lord! There is no one dearer to you than those who see the jīvas as non-different from you, the soul of the universe. However, O Lord, most affectionate to the devotees, please bestow pure bhaktion those serving you! SB 4.7.38**

*jñānaṁ te ’haṁ sa-vijñānam idaṁ vakṣyāmy aśeṣataḥ \|*  
*yaj jñātvā neha bhūyo ’nyaj jñātavyam avaśiṣyate \|\|*

**I will explain to you knowledge of my powers (jñāna) along with knowledge of my sweetness (vijñāna), knowing which nothing else will remain to be known. BG 7.2**

*mattaḥ parataraṁ nānyat kiñcid asti dhanañjaya \|*  
*mayi sarvam idaṁ protaṁ sūtre maṇi-gaṇā iva \|\|*

**There is nothing superior to me, O conqueror of wealth. Everything is in me as pearls are strung on a thread. BG 7.4-7**

By the śaktis called pradhāna and jīva, the Lord creates the world. He is non-different from the world since he is the possessor of the śaktis. He is superior to the two śaktis. He is their shelter. After explaining this, Kṛṣṇa teaches about himself and related to that, knowledge of the jīva’s svarūpa. The jñānī, understanding my svarūpa and powers, surpassing the jñānī-bhaktas and artha-bhatas, becomes dear to me. That is related at the end:

*catur-vidhā bhajante māṁ janāḥ sukṛtino ’rjuna \|*  
*ārto jijñāsur arthārthī jñānī ca bharatarṣabha \|\|*

**Four types of religious persons worship me, O Arjuna: the sufferer, the inquirer, the enjoyer and the jñānī.**

*teṣāṁ jñānī nitya-yukta eka-bhaktir viśiṣyate \|*  
*priyo hi jñānino ’tyartham ahaṁ sa ca mama priyaḥ \|\|*

**Ofthese four types, the jñānī, who is constantly engaged in thinking of me, who is practicing pure bhakti, is the best. This jñānī loves onlyme, and I love only him.**

*udārāḥ sarva evaite jñānī tv ātmaiva me matam \|*  
*āsthitaḥ sa hi yuktātmā mām evānuttamāṁ gatim \|\|*

**They are all exalted, but the jñānī is my soul, because he remains firmly fixed in devotion to me, taking me as the highest goal. That is my opinion. BG 7.16-18**

The meaning of SB 4.7.38 is this. There is no one dearer than (*amutaḥ*) he who sees the jīvas (*ātmanaḥ*) in you, the soul of the universe (*viśvātmani*), since the jīva is your śakti, non-different from you. This person does not see the jīva as an independent entity (*pṛthak*). O Lord dear to the servant (*vatsala)!* Though that is so, be merciful to those who worship you as the Lord of the servants with pure bhakti (*ananya-vṛttyā*). They see themselves as jñānī-bhaktas.

In the verse under discussion, knowing or not knowing the Lord should not be thought to be good or bad, for this devotee is the best (*bhaktatamaḥ)*. This devotee is superior to the devotee called *sattama.* Use of the word *bhakta* indicates the svarūpa of bhakti is greater in this devotee.

The Lord says, “This is my opinion. I agree with this.” He has not said this previously about any other devotee. Though the rest of the section referred to the devotee in the singular, in this verse the devotees are mentioned in the plural out of respect. How much more respect will be given to devotees with bhāva and prema! The mode of worship in bhāva will be known later in discussing rāgānuga-bhakti. The Lord speaks the verses.

## Anuccheda 202

These devotees were designated as mahat or sat. The grades of sat devotees, though not discussed, will be considered later in defining the types of bhakti. Others are called Vaiṣṇavas, according to the values of other groups. Among those doing karmas some are called Vaiṣṇavas:

*dharmārthaṁ jīvitaṁ yeṣāṁ santānārthaṁ ca maithunam \|*  
*pacanaṁ vipramukhyārthaṁ jñeyās te vaiṣṇavā narāḥ \|\|*

**The person who lives his life for dharma, performs sex for producing children, and cooks for the great brāhmaṇas is known as a Vaiṣṇava. Skanda Purāṇa**

The word *vaiṣṇava* indicates a person who performs his actions with the thought that they are the order of Viṣṇu.

*na calati nija-varṇa-dharmato ya.*  
*sama-matir ātma-suhṛd-vipakṣa-pakṣe \|*  
*na harati na hanti kiñcid uccai.*  
*sthita-manasaṁ tam avehi viṣṇu-bhaktam \|\|*

**Know the person who does not deviate from the dharma of his varṇa, who sees equally his friends and enemies, who does not steal or harm anything, who has steady mind, to be a devotee of Viṣṇu. ViṣṇuPurāṇa 3.7.20**

One who offers to Viṣṇu is called a vaiṣṇava:

*jīvitaṁ yasya dharmārthaṁ dharmo hary-artham eva ca \|*  
*aho-rātrāṇi puṇyārthaṁ taṁ manye vaiṣṇavaṁ bhuvi \|\|*

**I consider the person to be a Vaiṣṇava on earth who lives for dharma, whose dharma is for the Lord, and day and night lives for gaining piety. PadmaPurāṇa 5.94.8**

There are Vaiṣṇavas among the Śaivites as well:

*śive ca parameśāne viṣṇau ca paramātmani \|*  
*samabuddhyā pravarttante te vai bhāgavatottamāḥ \|\|*

**Those who think that Śiva and Viṣṇu are the same are the greatest devotees. Nārada Purāṇa 1.5.72**

Among the followers of Śiva such devotees are well known as the greatest. However such persons are criticized in Vaiṣṇava-tantra:

*yas tu nārāyaṇaṁ devaṁ brahma-rudrādi-daivataiḥ \|*  
*samatvenaiva vīkṣeta sa pāṣaṇḍī bhaved dhruvam \|\|*

**He who considers Viṣṇu to be on the level of Brahmā, Śiva or other devatās is a pāṣaṇḍī for certain.**

Among these many types of devotees, bhakti arises in them with different strength, with different mercy, with different impressions of bhakti and with different svarūpas and speeds of progress according to association. From association with a jñānī, jñāna arises. Akiñcana-bhaktiis the abhidheya and its cause is devotee association. The devotee is known by his qualities. To examine this, there will be some repetition.

First, from devotee association arises faith and a taste for devotional topics. By that one becomes favorable to the Lord. Along with this, a taste for a particular path of worship and a taste for a particular form of the Lord to be worshipped arises. A desire to know develops and one hears, after taking shelter of one or many gurus. One understands the meaning of scripture with initial and concluding statements (*upakramopasamhāra*) and one contemplates the meaning while distinguishing what arecontrary and impossible views, one develops faith that the Lord always exists everywhere in all his forms. That faith increases with the selection of a particular form of the Lordwhich is able to fulfill one’s expectations completely, with the first appearance of taste. Though it is possible to concentrate exclusively on one form,that does not take place in all cases. Thus some persons develop faith which does not develop such exclusive perception, because of not appreciating the special qualities of the Lord.

The particularmethod of worship should also be explained. To gain knowledge to perfect jñāna, a type of worship with repeated meditation (*nididhyāsana*) is undertaken. This is the path for person predominated with deliberation (*vicāra*).For persons with prominence taste (*ruci*), this dependence of deliberation does not arise. This path involves repeated hearing and other actions which increase faith and a taste for hearing recitation of pastimes and devotee association. This was previously shown:

*śuśrūṣoḥ śraddadhānasya vāsudeva-kathā-ruciḥ \|*  
*syān mahat-sevayā viprāḥ puṇya-tīrtha-niṣevaṇāt \|\|*

**O brāhmaṇas! Attraction for topics concerning Kṛṣṇa will arise by service to the great devotees, followed by faith, by surrender to the feet of the pure guru, and by the desire to hear. SB 1.2.16**

*satāṁ prasaṅgān mama vīrya-saṁvido*  
*bhavanti hṛt-karṇa-rasāyanāḥ kathāḥ*  
*taj-joṣaṇād āśv apavarga-vartmani*  
*śraddhā ratir bhaktir anukramiṣyati*

**From association with the best devotees, topics of my glorious pastimes become directly realized, bringing the devotee to niṣṭhā. Then the topics become an elixir for the heart and ears at the stage of ruci. By taste for these topics, āsakti, bhāva and then prema for the Lord who is the destroyer of material life, quickly develop in sequence. SB 3.25.25**

For those desiring bhakti with prīti, the path predominated by ruci is best. For those who have not developed ruci, the path of vicāra is available. Prahlāda says:

*naite guṇā na guṇino mahad-ādayo ye*  
*sarve manaḥ prabhṛtayaḥ sahadeva-martyāḥ*  
*ādy-antavanta urugāya vidanti hi tvām*  
*evaṁ vimṛśya sudhiyo viramanti śabdāt*

**Neither the three guṇas, nor their predominating deities, nor the five gross elements, nor the mind, nor the devatās, nor the human being, all having a beginning and end, can understand you. Considering this, the wise have given up such study.**

*tat te 'rhattama namaḥ stuti-karma-pūjāḥ*  
*karma smṛtiś caraṇayoḥ śravaṇaṁ kathāyām*  
*saṁsevayā tvayi vineti ṣaḍ-aṅgayā kiṁ*  
*bhaktiṁ janaḥ paramahaṁsa-gatau labheta*

**O Lord worthy of worship! The six parts ofbhakti are offering respects, praising you, deity worship, menial service, remembering your lotus feet and hearing about you. How can a person attain prema without these six types of bhakti to you, who also award liberation to the jñānīs. 7.9.49-50**

*Karma* means deity service. *Karma-smṛti*means remembering the pastimes of the Lord. The word *caraṇayoḥ* should be connected with all items to indicate bhakti.

For both paths one accepts a guru who teaches (*śikṣā*) the method of worship, who was the previous śravana-guru, since one can obtain teaching on worship from a person who previously gave attractive teachings. If one had many śravana-gurus, among them one may accept one as śikṣā guru to whom one has great attraction. Selecting a person to whom one is attracted, a person takes oneas mantra guru, since many mantra gurus are forbidden.

There are scriptural proofs for this. For instance developing taste for a particular form of the Lord is illustrated in the following words of Āvirhotra:

*labdhvānugraha ācāryāt tena sandarśitāgamaḥ*  
*mahā-puruṣam abhyarcen mūrtyābhimatayātmanaḥ*

**Having obtained the mercy of hisguru, who reveals to the disciple the injunctions of Vedic scriptures, the devotee should worship the Supreme Lord in the particular personal form of the Lord the devotee finds most attractive. SB 11.3.48**

Taste for a particular type of worship is illustrated in the Lord’s words:

*vaidikas tāntriko miśra iti me tri-vidho makhaḥ*  
*trayāṇām īpsitenaiva vidhinā māṁ samarcaret*

**One should carefully worship me by selecting one of the three methods of deity worship: Vedic, tantric or mixed. SB 11.27.7**

The śravaṇa-guru is described:

*tasmād guruṁ prapadyeta jijñāsuḥ śreya uttamam*  
*pare ca niṣṇātaṁ brahmaṇy upaśamāśrayam*

**Therefore one who is inquisitive about the highest truth should surrender to a guru who is skilful in the scriptures and fixed in the forms of the Lord by that knowledge, while free of anger and greed. SB 11.3.22**

The guru should be fixed in the Vedas (*śābde brahmaṇi*) by dedication to deliberation and should be fixed in the forms of the Lord (pare brahmaṇi) by experience of the non-material Lord.

Nārada says in summarizing the story of Purañjana:

*sa vai priyatamaś cātmāyato na bhayam aṇv api*  
*iti veda sa vai vidvānyo vidvān sa gurur hariḥ*

**The Lord is most beloved, though the jīva is also dear, because from worshipping the Lord there is not the least fear or suffering. He who knows the two ātmās is learned. Such a learned person should be considered your guru and should be worshipped like the Lord. SB 4.29.51**

Prabuddha speaks to Nimi.

## Anuccheda 203

*vaktā sa-rāgo nīrāgo dvividhaḥ parikīrtitaḥ \|*  
*sa-rāgo lolupaḥ kāmī tad uktaṁ hṛn na saṁspṛśet \|\|*  
*upadeśaṁ karoty eva na parīkṣāṁ karoti ca \|*  
*aparīkṣyopadiṣṭaṁ yal loka-nāśāya tad bhavet \|\|*

**There are two types of speakers: with attachment and without attachment. The speaker with attachment is full of material desire and greed. What he says does not touch the heart. He teaches in this way, without examining the subject matter carefully. What is taught in this way is harmful to the population. Brahma-vaivarta Purāṇa**

*kulaṁ śīlam athācāram avicārya paraṁ gurum \|*  
*bhajeta śravaṇādy-arthī sarasaṁ sāra-sāgaram \|\|*

**The person desiring to hear should worship the supreme guru who is potent with bhakti, who is the ocean of wealth of knowledge, without regard for the guru’s family lineage, character or conduct.**

The guru’s potency of bhakti is described (*sarasam*). Elsewhere it is said:

*kāma-krodhādi-yukto’pi kṛpaṇo’pi viṣādavān \|*  
*śrutvā vikāśam āyāti sa vaktā paramo guruḥ \|\|*

**The speaker is the best guru if he enlightens people filled with lust and anger, misers and depressed personswhen they hear him.**

If such a guru is not available some people take shelter of many persons with a desire to know varieties of means.

*na hy ekasmād guror jñāna su-sthiraṁ syāt su-puṣkalam*  
*brahmaitad advitīyaṁ vai gīyate bahudharṣibhiḥ*

**Although the Lord is one without a second, the sages have described him in many different ways. Therefore one may not be able to acquire firm and complete knowledge from one guru. SB 11.9.31**

The meaning is clear. Dattātreya speaks to Yadu.

## Anuccheda 204

Hearing (śravaṇam) for the person with prominent ruci is described:

*tatrānvahaṁ kṛṣṇa-kathāḥ pragāyatām*  
*anugraheṇāśṛṇavaṁ manoharāḥ \|*  
*tāḥ śraddhayā me ’nupadaṁ viśṛṇvataḥ*  
*priyaśravasy aṅga mamābhavad ruciḥ \|\|*

**By the mercy of the sages who were chanting about the attractive pastimes of Kṛṣṇa daily, I heard about Kṛṣṇa in that place with attachment. Hearing constantly, I then developed ruci for Kṛṣṇa. SB 1.5.26**

An example śravaṇam with prominent vicāra is the catuḥślokī. An example of contemplation (mananam) is the following:

*bhagavān brahma kārtsnyena trir anvīkṣya manīṣayā \|*  
*tad adhyavasyat kūṭa-stho ratir ātman yato bhavet \|\|*

**Brahmā with fixed mind reviewed (manana) three times the Vedas completely with his intelligence, and determined that process which produces prema in the self. SB 2.2.34**

Faith in the Lord from hearing is as follows:

*asti yajña-patir nāma keṣāñcid arha-sattamāḥ*  
*ihāmutra ca lakṣyante jyotsnāvatyaḥ kvacid bhuvaḥ*

**According to some authorities, the Supreme Lord, the master of sacrifice, exists. This can be observed by the presence of beautiful bodies in this life and the next in some place.**

*manor uttānapādasya dhruvasyāpi mahīpateḥ*  
*priyavratasya rājarṣer aṅgasyāsmat-pituḥ pituḥ*  
*īdṛśānām athānyeṣām ajasya ca bhavasya ca*  
*prahlādasya baleś cāpi kṛtyam asti gadābhṛt.*  
*dauhitrādīn ṛte mṛtyoḥ śocyān dharma-vimohitān*  
*varga-svargāpavargāṇāṁ prāyeṇaikātmya-hetunā*

**This is also the conclusion of Manu, Uttānapāda, King Dhruva, the saintly king Priyavrata, my grandfather Aṅga, many other great persons, Brahmā, Śiva, Prahlāda and Bali—but not those lamentable persons straying from dharma like Vena. The achievements of artha, dharma, kāma, Svarga and mokṣa are generally given by the independent Supreme Lord, holder of the club. SB 4.21.27-31**

O great devotees! The Supreme Lord, described in the śruti as the giver of all results of karma (*yajña-patiḥ*) according persons who know the real meaning of śruti (*keśāṁcit*), exists. “That cannot be true because of contrary evidence.” Confirming proof is given by the existence of variety in the universe. For instance, by perception in this life and by scripture which testifies about next life, there is sometimes, not always, beauty bodies meant for enjoyment on earth (*bhuvaḥ*). By this one can infer the existence of the Lord. Insentient karma cannot give results. *phalam ata upapatteḥ*: the Lord alone gives the results because that is reasonable. (Brahmā-sūtra 3.2.39) The devatās are not independent according to antrayāmī śruti. And people do not get the same results from the same actions. People do to the same actions and get different results. (Thus a sentient being is in control.) Thus the Lord is independent.

The wise have realized this also. This is proof. Three verses state this. The grandfatheris Aṅga. Prahlāda and Balī are mentioned (though they appeared after the speaker Pṛthu) since they were understood from scriptures. The achievements are produced by the Lord (*gadābhṛtā*). Appearing internally in the heart and externally, the Lord performs necessary actions for them. They also do activities with the Lord and with no one else. Others are criticized. Veṇa and others were bewildered about dharma. The word *gadābhṛti* indicates Viṣṇu and no one else as the Supreme Lord. That Lord is described. He, who is one being in all entities *(ekātmyena*), bestows the results of artha, dharma and kāma (*varga*), Svarga (the result of dharma) and mokṣa for preaching *(prāyeṇa).* It is said in Skanda Purāṇa:

*bandhako bhava-pāśena bhava-pāśāc ca mocakaḥ \|*  
*kaivalyadaḥ paraṁ brahma viṣṇur eva sanātanaḥ \|\|*

**Eternal Viṣṇu, the supreme Brahman, binds with the ropes of the material world, frees a person from the bondage of world and awards liberation.**

## Anuccheda 205

Faith in the process of worship is described:

*yat-pāda-sevābhirucis tapasvinām*  
*aśeṣa-janmopacitaṁ malaṁ dhiyaḥ*  
*sadyaḥ kṣiṇoty anvaham edhatī satī*  
*yathā padāṅguṣṭha-viniḥsṛtā sarit*

**Like the water flowing from the big toe of the Lord, a taste of service to the Lord’s feet immediately destroys the contamination in the intelligence of those suffering in saṁsāra, which has been acquired through unlimited births. Day by day that spiritual taste increases.**

*vinirdhutāśeṣa-mano-malaḥ pumān*  
*asaṅga-vijñāna-viśeṣa-vīryavān*  
*yad-aṅghri-mūle kṛta-ketanaḥ punar*  
*na saṁsṛtiṁ kleśa-vahāṁ prapadyate*

**One who takes shelter of the Lord’s feet, with mind cleansed of unlimited dirty, and who is strong in detachment from enjoyment and realization of the beauty of the Lord’s form, does not surrender again to material life full of suffering. SB 4.21.31-32**

*Tapasvinām*means those suffering in saṁsāra. The power of taste for serving the Lord is compared to the power of the water related to the Lord’s foot. He has the strength (*vīryavān*) of directly meeting *(vijñāna*) the Lord in one the Lord’s particular (*viṣeṣa*) forms from being unattached to anything else (*asaṅga*). He has taken shelter (*kṛta-ketanaḥ*) at the base of the Lord’s feet. Pṛthu speaks to the assembly.

## Anuccheda 206

The śravaṇa-guru (from who one gets taste) and the bhajana-śikṣā-guru (from whom one learns worship) are generally the same person.

*tatra bhāgavatān dharmān śikṣed gurv-ātma-daivataḥ*  
*amāyayānuvṛttyā yais tuṣyed ātmātma-do hariḥ*

**He who holds the guru dear and worthy of worship should learn bhaktifrom the guru, by which the Lord, who gives himself in the deity form, is satisfied. SB 11.3.22**

The śravaṇa-guru was described in tasmād guruṁ prapadyeta (SB 11.3.21). Accepting the guru as his deity of worship (*gurv-ātma-daivataḥ),* he should learn by obedience without pride (*amāyayānuvṛttyā).* By those dharmas (bhakti), Paramātmā (ātmā) who gives himself to the devotees like Bali (*ātma-daḥ*) is pleased. As previously explained the śikṣā-gurus can be many. Prabuddha speaks to Nimi.

## Anuccheda 207

The mantra guru is only one:

*labdhvānugraha ācāryāt tena sandarśitāgamaḥ*  
*mahā-puruṣam abhyarcen mūrtyābhimatayātmanaḥ*

**Having obtained the mercy in the form of receiving mantrafromhis guru, who reveals to the disciple the rules of the mantra, the devotee should worship the Lord in a form of his liking. SB 11.3.48**

*Labdhānugrahaḥ* means having received mercy in the form of mantra-dīkṣā. From guru he learns the scriptures which describe the rules for the mantra (*sandarśitāgamaḥ*). Because *ācāryāt* is in the singular, it indicates one takes only one dīkṣa-guru.

Giving up guru (dīkṣā) is forbidden:

*bodhaḥ kaluṣitas tena daurātmyaṁ prakaṭīkṛtam.*  
*gurur yena parityaktas tena tyaktaḥ purā hariḥ \|\|*

**Having intelligence polluted and showing evil nature, if a person gives up guru, the Lord has already given up that person. Brahma-vaivarta Purāṇa**

Unsatisfied with one guru, one takes another guru. By taking more than one(dīkṣā) guru (when one should have only one dīkṣā guru), one accomplishes giving up the previous guru. But there is an exception in which one can give up a dīkṣā guru:

*avaiṣṇavopadiṣṭena mantreṇa nirayaṁ vrajet \|*  
*punaś ca vidhinā samyag grāhayed vaiṣṇavād guroḥ \|\|*

**One goes to hell by accepting a mantra from a non-devotee. One should again take the mantra according to rules from a Vaiṣṇava guru. Padma Purāṇa**

Āvihotra speaks to Nimi.

## Anuccheda 208

By association with the śravaṇa-guru there must be arousal of knowledge of scripture:

*ācāryo ’raṇir ādyaḥ syād ante-vāsy uttarāraṇiḥ*  
*tat-sandhānaṁ pravacanaṁ vidyā-sandhiḥ sukhāvahaḥ*

**The guru is the wood base, the student is the wood on top, the teachings are the kindling stick, and knowledge, which brings bliss, is the fire. SB 11.10.12**

The guru is the base. The teachings (*pravacanam*) are the kindling stick between (*sandhānam*) the bottom (guru) and top (student). *Vidyā*means knowledge arising from scriptures. This is like fire arising by the combination of the three pieces of wood. Śruti says *tad-vijñānārthaṁ sa gurum evābhigacched*: to attain that knowledge one must approach a guru. (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad 1.1.12) *ācāryavān puruṣo veda*: accepting a guru one can attain knowledge. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 6.14.2) *naiṣā tarkeṇa matir apaneyā proktānyenaiva sujñānāya preṣṭhā*: this knowledge cannot be achieved by logic. O dear one! It should be taught by another person in order to be understood properly. (Kaṭha Upaniṣad 1.2.9.

The Lord speaks.

## Anuccheda 209

Śikṣā-guru is necessary:

*vijita-hṛṣīka-vāyubhir adānta-manas tura-gaṁ*  
*ya iha yatanti yantum ati-lolam upāya-khidaḥ*  
*vyasana-śatānvitāḥ samavahāya guroś caraṇaṁ*  
*vaṇija ivāja santy akṛta-karṇa-dharā jaladhau*

**The mind is like an impetuous horse that even persons who have regulated their senses and breath cannot control. People in this world who try to tame the uncontrolled mind but who abandon the feet of their spiritual master encounter hundreds of obstacles in their cultivation of various distressful practices. O unborn Lord, they are like merchants on a boat in the ocean who have failed to employ a helmsman. SB 10.87.33**

Those who rejects the feet of the guru and try to control the uncontrolled mind, full of desire (*atilolam*) like a horse by controlling the senses and prāṇas become exhausted by trying many methods (*upāya-khidaḥ*). They encounter hundreds of obstacles: they remain in saṁsāra. O Lord*(aja)!* They are like merchants in the ocean with no captain. When one develops knowledge about the process to reach the Lord by the method of worship taught by the guru, and by his mercy one is not overcome by the obstacles, the mind quickly becomes steady.

*guru-bhaktyā sa milati smaraṇāt sevyate budhaiḥ \|*  
*milito’pi na labhyeta jīvair ahamikā-paraiḥ \|\|*

**By devotion to guru one attains the Lord. The intelligent serve the Lord by remembrance. Those subject to pride do not attain the Lord, even though they may encounter him. Brahma-vaivarta Purāṇa**

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā guruḥ \|*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ \|\|*

**The meaning of scripture is revealed to the person who has intense devotion for guru as much as he has devotion to the Lord. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 6.23**

The śrutis speak.

## Anuccheda 210

Thus mantra-guru is necessary still more.One must take shelter of a genuine spiritual (paramārtha) guru giving up customary (family) gurus:

*gurur na sa syāt sva-jano na sa syāt*  
*pitā na sa syāj jananī na sā syāt*  
*daivaṁ na tat syān na patiś ca sa syān*  
*na mocayed yaḥ samupeta-mṛtyum*

**He who cannot deliver a person in saṁsāra is not a guru, a relative, a father, mother, devatāor husband. SB 5.5.18**

*Samupetya-mṛtyum*means “a person who has attained saṁsāra.” Nārada says:

*jugupsitaṁ dharma-kṛte ’nuśāsataḥ*  
*svabhāva-raktasya mahān vyatikramaḥ \|*  
*yad-vākyato dharma itītaraḥ sthito*  
*na manyate tasya nivāraṇaṁ janaḥ \|\|*

**You have created a great disturbance by teaching a s condemned subject to people attached to material enjoyment in order to make them accept dharma. Thinking that what you have taught is real dharma, they do not consider giving it up. SB 1.5.15**

Unless one takes shelter of a guru who can deliver one from death,one will accepta person who is not a real guru, father, mother etc.Ṛṣabha speaks to his sons.

## Anuccheda 211

Those practicing karma (varṇāśrama) should see the guru as the Lord:

*ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyān nāvanmanyeta karhicit*  
*na martya-buddhyāsūyeta sarva-deva-mayo guruḥ*

**One should know the ācārya as myself and never disrespect him in any way. One should not envy him, thinking him an ordinary man, for he is the representative of all the devatās. SB 11.17.27**

This verse is from the section concerning brahmacārī dharma. (This is an injunction for the brahmacārī in varṇāśrama.) The Lord speaks.

## Anuccheda 212

More so those interested in spiritual advancement (rather than those engaged in karma) should see the guru as the Lord:

*yasya sākṣād bhagavati jñāna-dīpa-prade gurau*  
*martyāsad-dhīḥ śrutaṁ tasya sarvaṁ kuñjara-śaucavat*

**For a person having faulty intelligence who thinks that the guru, who gives the lamp of knowledge and is directly the Lord, is an ordinary mortal, all that he has heard from guru becomes as useless as cleaning an elephant that merely becomes dirty again.**

*eṣa vai bhagavān sākṣāt pradhāna-puruṣeśvaraḥ*  
*yogeśvarair vimṛgyāṅghrir loko yaṁ manyate naram*

**He whose feet are sought by great yogīs,is directly the Supreme Lord, though common persons think of him as human. SB 7.15.26-27**

Guru is Kṛṣṇa. Thus material vision is not the authority for receiving the truth about the Lord. Nārada speaks to Yudhiṣṭhira.

## Anuccheda 213

The pure devotees think of guru and Śiva as non-different from the Lord because he is dearest to the Lord.

*vayaṁ tu sākṣād bhagavan bhavasya*  
*priyasya sakhyuḥ kṣaṇa-saṅgamena*  
*suduścikitsyasya bhavasya mṛtyor*  
*bhiṣaktamaṁ tvādya gatiṁ gatāḥ sma*

**O Lord! By association with your dear friend Śiva, we have today attained you, the best remedy for birth and death, which are difficult to cure. SB 4.30.38**

The commentary says, “*Bhavasya priyasya sakhyuḥ*means ‘of Śiva who is your dear friend.’ We have attained you, the correct medicine (*bhiṣaktamam*) for birth and death (*bhavasya mṛtyoḥ*) which is difficult to cure.”

Śiva is the guru of the speakers. The Pracetas speak to eight armed form of the Lord.

## Anuccheda 214

After taking shelter of guru by having taste etc. the preliminary parts of worship, of many varieties, with different types of processes for approachingthe Lord (sāmmukhyam), is shown. The characteristic of direct worship is shown with many varieties, according to different vision. There are two types of sāmmukhyam: without qualities and with qualities. Without qualities the method is jñāna. With qualities there are two types: self worship and bhakti. *Jñānaṁ caikātmya-darśanam*: jñānam means worship with non-difference. (SB 11.19.27) The Lord speaks.

Jñāna Sādhana

## Anuccheda 215

The sādhana for jñāna is of many types. That will be explained. The sādhana is called jñāna. In that there is śravaṇam. This is seen in the conversation between Pṛthu and Sanatkumāra.

According to that discussion there is also mananam (contemplation). First the listeners should develop discrimination so that they distinguish a substance of mere consciousness beyond matter.

They are not able to distinguish the qualities or the form of Bhagavān arranged by his svarūpa-śakti that exists in that conscious substance.

This is similar to the sun. Though the light which dispels darkness is only light, the material eye cannot distinguish the variety in within and exterior to the sun, such as the celestial chariot of the sun god, the different rays and the light particles. If one develops celestial vision by special great mercy, then one can see those details. If not, then they merge in the realization of the impersonal Brahman of consciousness alone.

Their meditation is described:

*sthiraṁ sukhaṁ cāsanam āsthito yatir*  
*yadā jihāsur imam aṅga lokam \|*  
*kāle ca deśe ca mano na sajjayet*  
*prāṇān niyacchen manasā jitāsuḥ \|\|*

**O King! When the renounced yogī, seated comfortably on his seat, desires to give up his body, he does not attach the mind to time or place. Conquering life, he controls the senses by the mind.**

*manaḥ sva-buddhyāmalayā niyamya*  
*kṣetra-jña etāṁ ninayet tam ātmani \|*  
*ātmānam ātmany avarudhya dhīro*  
*labdhopaśāntir virameta kṛtyāt \|\|*
