**He merges the mind into the pure intelligence, and merges the intelligence into the witnessing jīva. He merges the witnessing jīva into the pure jīva, and merges the pure jīva into the Brahman. Having done so, the yogī, attaining cessation of material life, withdraws from all action. SB 2.2.15-16**

He merges the intelligence into the witness of the intelligence, the jīva *(kṣetrajñe*). He merges the witness into the pure jīva (*ātmani*), devoid of witnessing, by intelligence arising from his svarūpa.

He merges the pure jīva into Brahman *(ātmani),* thinking of jīva as one with Brahman. Having attained liberation (*labdhopaśāntiḥ),* he withdraws from action since there is nothing beyond this to attain. Śukadeva speaks.

## Anuccheda 216

That liberation from jñāna is described in Bhagavad-gītā. *Svābhāvo ‘bhyātmam ucyate*: the conception of the pure jīva is called adhyātmā. (BG 8.3) The conception (*bhāva*) of the pure jīva *(sva*) is called adhyātmā since it exists in relation to (*adhi)* the jīva *(ātmā*).

**Self worship**

Self worship (*ahaṁgrahopāsanam*) means thinking oneself to be the Lord endowed with his śakti. The result of this is the manifestation of the Lord’s śakti in the jīva. It is related in Viṣṇu Purāṇa that when Prahlāda was tied by the snake ropes he meditated on himself as the Lord and escaped the ropes. The final result is attainment of sārūpya, sārṣti etc. just as an insect trapped by a wasp, by meditating on the wasp,becomes similar to a wasp.

**Bhakti sādhana**

Now bhakti will be discussed. Its taṭastha and svarūpa lakṣaṇas are described in Garuḍa Purāṇa:

*viṣṇu-bhaktiṁ pravakṣyāmi yayā sarvam avāpyate \|*  
*yathā bhaktyā haris tuṣyeta tathā nānyena kenacit \|\|*

**I will speak about bhakti to Viṣṇu by which everything is attained. The Lord is not pleased with anything as he is pleased by bhakti.**

*bhaja ity eṣa vai dhātuḥ sevāyāṁ parikīrtitaḥ \|*  
*tasmāt sevā budhaiḥ proktā bhaktiḥ sādhana-bhūyasī \|\|*

**The word** ***bhakti*** **comes from the root** ***bhaj*** **which means service. Thus the wise say bhakti, service to the Lord is the best sādhana. Garuḍa Purāṇa**

“That by which everything is attained” is the taṭastha-lakṣaṇa. This is proved by *akāmaḥ sarva-kamo vā* (SB 2.3.10). And nothing more can be attained by any other process is expressed in the second line of the verse. Since this is stated by the wise (second verse), it is definitely true. Service (sevā) is the svarūpa-lakṣaṇa. That service has three types: bodily, vocal and mental. This service to the Lord excludes self worship as well as enmity and fear as modes of worship. It is the best sādhana *(sādhana-bhūyasī).*

These two characteristics are expressed in another way:

*ye vai bhagavatā proktā upāyā hy ātma-labdhaye*  
*añjaḥ puṁsām aviduṣāṁ viddhi bhāgavatān hi tān*

**Even ignorant living entities can very easily come to know the Supreme Lord if they adopt those means prescribed by the Supreme Lord for their benefit. The process recommended by the Lord is known as bhāgavata-dharma. SB 11.2.34**

This process is even for ignorant people (*aviduṣām puṁsām*). This is glorification of bhakti. The sādhanas (*upāyāḥ*) were spoken by the Lord for easily attaining manifestation of Brahman, Paramātmā and Bhagavān, which arise from his own nature (*ātma)*.

*kālena naṣṭā pralaye vāṇīyaṁ veda-saṁjñitā*  
*mayādau brahmaṇe proktā dharmo yasyāṁ mad-ātmakaḥ*

**By the influence of time, the Vedic knowledge was lost at the time of annihilation. Therefore, when the subsequent creation took place, I spoke to Brahmā the Vedic knowledge in which bhakti is the essence. SB 11.14.3**

The above verse confirms that the Lord himself spoke about bhakti. Know the methods (*upāyān*) which are *bhāgavatān dharmān*, bhakti to the Lord. The word *hi* indicate that this is well known. Bhakti is called bhāgavata-dharma as in the following:

*etāvān eva loke 'smin puṁsāṁ dharmaḥ paraḥ smṛtaḥ*  
*bhakti-yogo bhagavati tan-nāma-grahaṇādibhiḥ*

**Pure bhaktito the Supreme Lord, beginning with the chanting of the holy name of the Lord, is the real dharma (*****dharmaḥ paraḥ*****), the supreme object for the human living in this world. SB 6.3.22**

In the verse *ātma-labdhaye*(for their benefit) represents the taṭastha-lakṣaṇa, for if one has not attainedbenefits one still continues the process. *Upāyāḥ* for attaining the Lord (another meaning of *ātmā-labdhaye)*indicates the svarūpa-lakṣaṇa. Attaining this method, one follows the Lordfavorably. Kavi speaks to Nimi.

## Anuccheda 217

This bhakti is of three types: āropa-siddha, saṅga-siddha and svarūpa-siddha.

Āropa-siddha bhakti (bhakti by superimposition), taking the form of karma (prescribed duties), though devoid of bhakti by its nature, brings about bhakti by offering those karmas to the Lord. Saṅga-siddha-bhakti (bhakti by association with bhakti), taking the form of jñāna and karma, by acting as aṅgas of bhakti, though devoid of bhakti by their nature, is included in bhakti because these elements are established as bhakti’sassistants.

*sarvato manaso 'saṅgam ādau saṅgaṁ ca sādhuṣu*  
*dayāṁ maitrīṁ praśrayaṁ ca bhūteṣv addhā yathocitam*

**The disciple should in the beginning learn detachment of the mind from all things of this world, while showing mercy to the inferior, friendship to equals and respect to superiors. SB 11.3.22-3**

(In these verses, bhakti is the process and detachment is an element of jñāna which assists. Though not bhakti in itself, it becomes (siddha) bhakti by association (saṅga) with bhakti..

Svarūpa-siddha-bhakti, not appearing by jñāna or karma, takes the form of hearing and chanting about the Lord, is directly favorable to the Lord, and is fixed in its devotion.Since hearing and chanting are the main elements of the processes, they are not superimposed (āropa) on the process. Rather these elements constitute the essence of it (svarūpa-siddha). Thus, some ignorant people who imitate this bhakti attain results by relation with it. For instance in his previous life Prahlāda fasted on Nṛsiṁha-caturdaśī by accident. And the bird escaping from the mouth of a dog circled the temple and attained results. Of if someone offers respects to the Lord accidently while seeing someone, they achieve results.

These three types may be divided into non-cheating and cheating forms. Āropa siddha and saṅga siddha bhakti are without cheating if,gaining fitness to attain bhakti by relationship with bhakti, they expect only bhakti as a result. If the two are used to attain results for one self or someone else, it is cheating.

Svarūpa-siddha-bhakti is without cheating if,established in its essential greatness to produce bhakti by a relationship with the Lord,its assisting elements also are used only for producing bhakti. It is cheating if it uses karma and jñāna as assistants for some other goal. The bhaktidevoid of cheating was previously called akiñcana-bhakti. The two types (cheating and non-cheating) are stated in *dharma projjhita-kaitvo ‘tra paramaḥ* (SB 1.1.2)

*nālaṁ dvijatvaṁ devatvam ṛṣitvaṁ vāsurātmajāḥ*  
*prīṇanāya mukundasya na vṛttaṁ na bahu-jñatā*  
*na dānaṁ na tapo nejyā na śaucaṁ na vratāni ca*  
*prīyate 'malayā bhaktyā harir anyad viḍambanam*

**O sons of demons! Being a brāhmaṇa, devatā or sage, good conduct, learning, charity, austerity, worship, cleanliness and vows cannot please the Lord. The Lord is pleased by pure bhakti. Other than bhakti, everything else is a cause of mockery. SB 7.7.51-52**

**Aropa-siddha-bhakti: offering karmas**

Now āropa-siddha-bhakti will be discussed. Both forms of karma, sakāma (enjoyment) and niṣkāma (liberation),are condemned since both types are averse to the Lord.

*naiṣkarmyam apy acyuta-bhāva-varjitaṁ*  
*na śobhate jñānam alaṁ nirañjanam \|*  
*kutaḥ punaḥ śaśvad abhadram īśvare*  
*na cārpitaṁ karma yad apy akāraṇam \|\|*

**Even the stage of jñāna without the bondage of karma is not glorious if it is devoid of bhakti to the Supreme Lord. What is the use of having destroyed ignorance? What then to speak of sakāma-karma which causes suffering, both during practice and at the stage perfection, and niṣkāma-karma, when not offered to the Lord? SB 1.5.12**

If the daily (material) activities are by chance offered to the Lord it becomes bhagavad-dharma. What then to speak of Vedic activities offered to the Lord. To express this, āropa-siddha bhaktias bhāgavata-dharma by offering daily activities is expressed in the following:

*kāyena vācā manasendriyair vā*  
*buddhyātmanā vānusṛta-svabhāvāt*  
*karoti yad yat sakalaṁ parasmai*  
*nārāyaṇāyeti samarpayet tat*

**In accordance with one’s particular nature, whatever one does with body, words, mind, senses, intelligence or purified consciousness one should offer to the Lord, thinking, "This is for the pleasure of Nārāyaṇa." SB 11.2.36**

In the previous verse (SB 11.2.31) the question was asked “Please speak about bhāgavad-dharma.” (Thus the above verse involving material activities is part of bhāgavata-dharma.) SB 11.2.34then explains the direct method to attain the Lord. The characteristics of hearing and chanting are presented:

*śṛṇvan su-bhadrāṇi rathāṅga-pāṇer*  
*janmāni karmāṇi ca yāni loke*  
*gītāni nāmāni tad-arthakāni*  
*gāyan vilajjo vicared asaṅgaḥ*

**Hearing the most auspicious birth and activities of Kṛṣṇa which are sung by all people and singing his names which describe those activities while having no attachment to anything else, a person should without shame wander in the world. SB 11.2.39**

The next chapter also included other elements within bhāgavata-dharma. The introduction starts SB 11.3.22 and the conclusion comes in SB 11.3.33:

*iti bhāgavatān dharmān śikṣan bhaktyā tad-utthayā*  
*nārāyaṇa-paro māyām añjas tarati dustarām*

**Having learned bhaktiand being dedicated to Nārāyaṇa, one will easily cross insurmountable māyā by prema-bhakti.**

Because the previously bhāgavata-dharma was being discussed, it included giving up association with material objects stated in the following (though it is not direct bhakti) which occurs before the conclusion in SB 11.3.33.

*sarvato manaso 'saṅgam ādau saṅgaṁ ca sādhuṣu*  
*dayāṁ maitrīṁ praśrayaṁ ca bhūteṣv addhā yathocitam*

**The disciple should in the beginning learn detachment of the mind from all things of this world, while showing mercy to the inferior, friendship to equals and respect to superiors. SB 11.3.23**

Thus offering of material activities to the Lordturns those actions into a type of bhakti (āropa-siddha bhakti).

That is the meaning expressed in the commentary. “All that one does with body, words, mind, senses intelligence or citta or ahaṅkāra *(ātmanā*), arising from one’s nature (*anusṛta-svabhāvāt*), should be offered to the Lord. The meaning is this. It is not a rule that only actions prescribed by scriptural rules can be offered. According to one’s nature, one can offer ordinary material activities. Gītā also says:

*yat karoṣi yad aśnāsi yaj juhoṣi dadāsi yat \|*  
*yat tapasyasi kaunteya tat kuruṣva mad-arpaṇam \|\|*

**Whatever you do, whatever you eat, whatever you sacrifice, whatever you give, and whatever austerity you perform, offer it to me, O son of Kuntī. BG 9.27**

This is also expressed in the mantra recited during deity worship *itaḥ pūrvam prāṇa-buddhi-dharmādhikārataḥ.*

In offering actions according to one’s nature, there are two processes for dealing with unwholesome actions. Those desiring jñāna do not make distinctions between the unwholesome and good actions. Those desiring bhakti offer the unwholesome actions thinking, ‘May the Lord who is full of compassion be merciful by seeing the suffering I undergo due to my wicked desires.’

Or he may think, ‘May all the attraction that I have for good and bad actions be directed to the Lord completely.’That is illustrated in the following:

*yā prītir avivekānāṁ viṣayeṣv anapāyinī \|*  
*tvām anusmarataḥ sā me hṛdayān nāpasarpatu \|\|*

**May the constant attraction that a materialist has for material objects (becoming spiritual attraction) not leave my heart, by remembering you. Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.20.19**

*yuvatīnāṁ yathā yūni yūnāṁ ca yuvatau yathā \|*  
*mano’bhiramate tadvan mano’bhiramatāṁ tvayi \|\|*

**Just as the minds of young girls are attracted to boys and young boys’ minds are attracted girls, my mind should be attracted to you. PadmaPurāṇa 6.128.258**

This is how low actions or desires are offered to the Lord. This is a prescription for persons who are very materially attached. Not all people will offer sinful actions in this way. The following indicates that only actions prescribed by the scriptures should be offered:

*vedoktam eva kurvāṇo niḥsaṅgo 'rpitam īśvare*  
*naiṣkarmyaṁ labhate siddhiṁ rocanārthā phala-śrutiḥ*

**A person who performs the karmas mentioned in the Vedas becomes detached from the results, offers the results to the Lord and attains destruction of all karmas. Only for attracting the people are material results mentioned in the Vedas. SB 11.3.46**

Kavi speaks to Nimi.

## Anuccheda 218

Offering scripturally prescribed actsis praised:

*kleśa-bhūry-alpa-sārāṇi karmāṇi viphalāni vā*  
*dehināṁ viṣayārtānāṁ na tathaivārpitaṁ tvayi*

**Activities performed for you are not like the activities of persons desiring material enjoyment--which require great effort and which yield little or no result. SB 8.5.47**

The activities of materialists some give great pain and yield little result or no result, like farming. But offering actions to you is not like that: it is without suffering and if offering with desire, the desire will be definitely fulfilled. That attainment of result is most excellent. Not only does one attain the result expected, but one even attains the result of destroying saṁsāra. That is the meaning. It is said:

*yān āsthāya naro rājan na pramādyeta karhicit*  
*dhāvan nimīlya vā netre na skhalen na pated iha*

**O King! One who accepts this process of devotional service will never be affected by pride. Even while running with eyes closed, he will never trip or fall. SB 11.2.35**

*satyaṁ diśaty arthitam arthito nṛṇāṁ*  
*naivārthado yat punar arthitā yataḥ*  
*svayaṁ vidhatte bhajatām anicchatām*  
*icchāpidhānaṁ nija-pāda-pallavam*

**The Lord certainly gives desired objects to devotees who request them, but he does not give those objects in such a way that the devotee will ask again after finishing his enjoyment. In other words, he gives his lotus feet, which include all desirables, to those worshippers who do not even desire them. SB 5.19.27**

As a result of his actions King Nabhi received Ṛṣabhadeva, the Supreme Lord, as his son. And Gītā says:

*nehābhikrama-nāśo ’sti pratyavāyo na vidyate \|*  
*sv-alpam apy asya dharmasya trāyate mahato bhayāt \|\|*

**In this process, there is no loss by incomplete performance, and no sin incurred by imperfection in performance. One is protected from birth and death even by practicing a little of this process. BG 2.40**

The devatās speak to Ajita.

## Anuccheda 219

Three verses propound offering karmas.

*etat saṁsūcitaṁ brahmaṁs tāpa-traya-cikitsitam \|*  
*yad īśvare bhagavati karma brahmaṇi bhāvitam \|\|*

**O brāhmaṇa! They indirectly explained the destruction of the three types of miseries, in which actions are offered to Paramātmā, Bhagavān and the impersonal Brahman. SB 1.5.32**

O Veda-vyāsa (*brāhmaṇa*)! The paramahaṁsas, staying during caturmāsya, indicated the cure for the three miseries. What is that? It is offering actions to Bhagavān. Offering actions is the solution. Who is that Lord? He is Bhagavān, the source of all, since he is endowed fully with all qualities in his svarūpa. He is Paramātmā (*īśvare)*, Bhagavān’s aṁśa who controls the jīvas and prakṛti. He is Brahman with consciousness alone, devoid of qualities of his svarūpa.

## Anuccheda 220

“How can action which is the cause of saṁsāra, destroy the three sufferings since actions arise because of desire for enjoying saṁsāra?” Action can destroy suffering because the ingredients are different.

*āmayo yaś ca bhūtānāṁ jāyate yena suvrata \|*  
*tad eva hy āmayaṁ dravyaṁ na punāti cikitsitam \|\|*

**The substance by which a living being’s disease arises and which does not cure the disease, when combined with other substances in a medicine, destroys the disease. SB 1.5.33**

Disease which arises because of excessive ghee, the cause of the disease, will not remove the disease. But becoming medicine by adding other substances, ghee can cure the disease.

## Anuccheda 221

*evaṁ nṛṇāṁ kriyā-yogāḥ sarve saṁsṛti-hetavaḥ \|*  
*ta evātma-vināśāya kalpante kalpitāḥ pare \|\|*

**In the same way, all types of karma-yoga performed by men, which are normally causes of continued rebirth in this world, are able to destroy karma when offered to the Supreme Lord. SB 1.5.34**

Offered (*kalpitaḥ*) to the Lord (*pare*) even with material desire, activities will destroy karma (*ātma-vināśāya*) even destroying saṁsāra. Nārada speaks to Vyāsa.

## Anuccheda 222

Moreover, the results of karmas actually take shelter of the Lord (they are caused by him). But the foolish person performing actions for himself achieves only meager results and bondage in saṁsāra. The intelligent person, performing actions for the Lord, attains great results and release from saṁsāra. This is stated in two prose sections:

*sampracaratsu nānā-yāgeṣu viracitāṅga-kriyeṣv apūrvaṁ yat tat kriyā-phalaṁ dharmākhyaṁ pare brahmaṇi yajña-puruṣe sarva-devatā-liṅgānāṁ mantrāṇām artha-niyāmakatayā sākṣāt-kartari para-devatāyāṁ bhagavati vāsudeva eva bhāvayamāna ātma-naipuṇya-mṛdita-kaṣāyo haviḥṣv adhvaryubhir gṛhyamāṇeṣu sa yajamāno yajña-bhājo devāṁs tān puruṣāvayaveṣv abhyadhyāyat.*

**In performance of various sacrifices with execution of all parts, Bharata thought of the apūrva result of the sacrifices—dharma-- as an offering to the supreme Brahman, the supreme object of worship, Vāsudeva, the ultimate performer of all actions, the recipient of all sacrifices, since Vāsudeva alone is the controller of all the devatās who are the subject of the mantras which have power to reveal those devatās. Having destroyed all impressions of karmaby his skilful actions when having theadhvaryu priests perform rites, Bharata, the sponsor of the sacrifices, meditated on the devatās, the recipients of the sacrifices, as limbs of the Lord. SB 5.7.6**

The commentary says, “In the actions of the parts undertaken in performing sacrifices Bharata thought of the apūrva results as an offering Vāsudeva alone. The sponsor (*yajamānaḥ)*meditated on the devatās, recipients of the sacrifice (*yajña-bhājaḥ*) as limbs (for instance the eye) of Vāsudeva. He did not think of them as separate from the Lord.

The Mīmāṁsakas have two ideas about the results of sacrifice (apūrva). Some say that the result appears immediately in subtle form. Others say that the śakti of the sacrifice produces results later on. It is said:

*yāgād eva phalaṁ tad dhi śakti-dvāreṇa sidhyati \|*  
*sūkṣma-śakty-ātmakaṁ vāpi phalam evopajāyate \|\|*

**The result of sacrifice appears by śakti (later on). Or the result appears as a subtle śakti itself.**

This is expressed by the words *kriyā-phalaṁ dharmākhyam* in the prose.

If one considers the purpose is to worship devatās, the result should reside in the performer of sacrifice. It is said:

*karmabhyaḥ prāg ayogyasya karmaṇaḥ puruṣasya vā \|*  
*yogyatā śāstra-gamyā yā parā sāpūrvam iṣyate \|\|*

**The ability of the agent and the actions of sacrifice which follow scriptures to manifest results, even previous to the actions,is called apūrva.**

If the main actions is to worship devatās, the result comes from the devatās by their mercy. “Previous to the actions” means that rice and other elements are the shelter of results such as consecration (not yet performed). Why then is it said that Vāsudeva is the shelter.

If the cause of results lies in the performer, since Vāsudeva as antaryāmī is the chief agent by inspiring the actions, he is the shelter of the results, not the performer of the sacrifice, for it is said the results mentioned in scripture lie with the agent. Otherwise, taking things a step further, the results could be said to lie with the other priests involved in the sacrifice. Thus the prose passage says that Vāsudeva is the chief agent (*sākṣāt kartari).* If the results lie in the devatās, is should be said that they take shelter of Vāsudeva. Thus he is called *para-devatāyām*. As the supreme devatā he gives results as the controller of the devatās such as Indra whose mantras reveal the devatās. Thus it is proper to say Vāsudeva is the shelter of the results. Bharata had destroyed all contamination such as material attachment by his own skill. The plural *adhvaryubhiḥ* (priests) indicates that many sacrifices were performed..

It follows from the above statement that to worship Viṣṇu as a secondary element of the sacrifice is a fault.

*uddiśya devatā eva juhoti ca dadāti ca \|*  
*sa pāṣaṇḍīti vijñeyaḥ svatantro vāpi karmasu \|\|*

**If a person performs sacrifices or makes offerings to devatās or regards them as independent while performing is duties, he is known as a pāṣaṇḍa. Padma Purāṇa 6.235.8**

*Pāṣaṇḍa* means a person who falls from the path of the Vaiṣṇavas. Gītā says:

*ye ’py anya-devatā-bhaktā yajante śraddhayānvitāḥ \|*  
*te ’pi mām eva kaunteya yajanty avidhi-pūrvakam \|\|*

**Those who are devoted to other gods and with faith worship them, worship me by the wrong method, O son of Kuntī.**

*ahaṁ hi sarva-yajñānāṁ bhoktā ca prabhur eva ca \|*  
*na tu mām abhijānanti tattvenātaś cyavanti te \|\|*

**I am the enjoyer and master of all sacrifices. Those who do not know me as such continue to take birth. BG 9.23-24**

In substance, all the paths of the Vedas lead to Bhagavān. This is indicated by Akrūra:

*sarva eva yajanti tvāṁ sarva-deva-mayeśvaram*  
*ye ’py anya-devatā-bhaktā yady apy anya-dhiyaḥ prabho*

**But all these people, my Lord, even those who have turned their attention away from you and are worshiping other deities, are actually worshiping you alone, the embodiment of all the devatās.**

*yathādri-prabhavā nadyaḥ parjanyāpūritāḥ prabho*  
*viśanti sarvataḥ sindhuṁ tadvat tvāṁ gatayo ’ntataḥ*

**As rivers born from the mountains and filled by the rain flow from all sides into the sea, so do all deliberations from the Vedas (paths) with commentaries by sages in the end reach you, O master. SB 10.40.9-10**

*Gataḥ*means paths. As a result of deliberation (*antataḥ)* all paths end in you.

The second prose section is this:

*evaṁ karma-viśuddhyā viśuddha-sattvasyāntar-hṛdayākāśa-śarīre brahmaṇi bhagavati vāsudeve mahā-puruṣa-rūpopalakṣaṇe śrīvatsa-kaustubha-vana-mālāri-dara-gadādibhir upalakṣite nija-puruṣa-hṛl-likhitenātmani puruṣa-rūpeṇa virocamāna uccaistarāṁ bhaktir anudinam edhamāna-rayājāyata.*

**Performing karmas purified by his intentions, the bhaktiof Bharata, filled with śuddha-sattva, daily increased to higher levels of intensity. He directed his bhaktito the Lord situated in the heart, to the Brahman, and to Bhagavān, Vāsudeva, who is also seen the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha, endowed Śrīvatsa, Kaustubha, garland, cakra, conch and club, shining in the hearts of his devotees with his human form. SB 5.7.7**

In the manner described previously, by purification through karmas, the bhakti of Bharata, filled with śuddha-sattva, composed of faith, hearing, chanting etc., manifested. To whom? It manifested in bhakti for Bhagavān, with complete svarūpa and qualities, for Vāsudeva, dwelling in all beings, for the antaryāmī or Paramātmā manifesting in his special abode (*śarire)*, the space of heart who is also called Brahman (not the impersonal feature since that could not manifest), for the mahāpuruṣa described in scriptures, for the form decorated with śrīvatsa and other marks. That bhakti had ever increasing excellence (*edhamāna-rayāḥ*). Śukadeva speaks.

## Anuccheda 224

There are two types of offering karmas: offering to please the Lord and simply offering to him as renunciation for the self. It is said:

*prīṇātu bhagavān īśaḥ karmaṇānena śāśvataḥ \|*  
*karoti satataṁ buddhyā brahmārpaṇam idaṁ param \|\|*  
*yad vā phalānāṁ sannyāsaṁ prakuryāt parameśvare \|*  
*karmaṇām etad apy āhur brahmārpaṇam anuttamam \|\|*

**May the Lord be pleased always by this action. In this way one offers actions always. This offering the Lord is best. Or, one may reject all the results of actions for the self and offer them to the Lord. This is said to be incomparable. Kūrma Purāṇa**

There are three reasons for offering karmas: to fulfillmaterial desires, to gain freedom from karma and to obtain bhakti. Pure action without desire is not possible, for it is said *yad yad dhi kurute jantus tat tat kāmasya ceṣṭitam:* whatever a person does is an act of desire.(Manu-smṛti) Offering actions to the Lord with material desire or desiring freedom from karma generally takes the form of renunciation of the results for the self. Pleasing the Lord in these two cases is an ābhāsa since one has self interest. If one offers karmas to increase one’s bhakti, that is only for pleasing to the Lord, since pleasing the Lord is the life of bhakti.

Offering results to fulfill one’s own desires is expressed in *kleśa-bhūry-alpa-phalāni*: it gives much suffering in execution and little result. (SB 8.5.47)Offering karmas to become free from karma is illustrated in the following:

*vedoktam eva kurvāṇo niḥsaṅgo 'rpitam īśvare*  
*naiṣkarmyaṁ labhate siddhiṁ rocanārthā phala-śrutiḥ*

**A person who performs the karmas mentioned in the Vedas becomes detached from the results, offers the results to the Lord and attains destruction of all karmas*****.*** **Only for attracting the people are material results mentioned in the Vedas. SB 11.3.46**

Offering results for bhakti is illustrated in Bharata’s statement in SB 5.7.7 and the following:

*yad atra kriyate karma bhagavat-paritoṣaṇam \|*  
*jñānaṁ yat tad adhīnaṁ hi bhakti-yoga-samanvitam \|\|*

**That knowledge of the Lord which arises from karmawhich is pleasing to the Lord because of being offered to him is endowed with bhakti*****.*** **SB 1.5.35**

Jñānameans knowledge of the Lord since that accompanies bhakti. The greatest devotees pray to please the Lord.

*yan naḥ svadhītaṁ guravaḥ prasāditā*  
*viprāś ca vṛddhāś ca sad-ānuvṛttyā*  
*āryā natāḥ suhṛdo bhrātaraś ca*  
*sarvāṇi bhūtāny anasūyayaiva*  
*yan naḥ sutaptaṁ tapa etad īśa*  
*nirandhasāṁ kālam adabhram apsu*  
*sarvaṁ tad etat puruṣasya bhūmno*  
*vṛṇīmahe te paritoṣaṇāya*

**We have studied and satisfied the teachers, brāhmaṇas and seniors in knowledge by our proper conduct. We have respected the senior devotees, friends, and our brothers. We have been kind to all beings, without envy. We have performed severe penance—not taking food for a long time while remaining in the water. We select all this for pleasing you, the great Lord. SB 4.30.39-40**

We pray “Let this be for your pleasure.” The Pracetas speak to the eight armed Lord.

Saṅga-siddha-bhakti

## Anuccheda 225

Āropa-siddha-bhakti was shown. Now saṅga-siddha bhakti will be shown. An example is mixed bhakti. Bhakti of other types through the association of svarūpa-siddha bhakti was shown already. For instance, in the section where Prabuddha speaks bhāgavatān dharmān, bhāgavata-dharma was defined with mixed friendliness and compassion.

*sarvato manaso 'saṅgam ādau saṅgaṁ ca sādhuṣu*  
*dayāṁ maitrīṁ praśrayaṁ ca bhūteṣv addhā yathocitam*

**The disciple should in the beginning learn detachment of the mind from all things of this world, while showing mercy to the inferior, friendship to equals and respect to superiors. SB 11.3.23**

There are three types of karma-miśra-bhakti: desire for material gains, desire for liberation and desire for bhakti. Desires of course are condemned:

*yā vai sādhana-sampattiḥ purṣārtha-catuṣṭaye \|*  
*tayā vinā tad āpnoti naro nārāyaṇāśrayaḥ \|\|*

**One who takes shelter of Nārāyaṇa attains the goal without having to perform a great amount of sādhana for the four human goals.**

Though it is possible to attain the goals by pure bhakti, because of various desires, people develop taste for various things. To attain those things people practice mixed bhakti. Bhakti mixed with material desires is generally known as karma-miśra bhakti. The word karma means dharma. The qualities of dharma were mentioned in general by the Yama-dūtas:

*veda-praṇihito dharmo hy adharmas tad-viparyayaḥ*  
*vedo nārāyaṇaḥ sākṣāt svayambhūr iti śuśruma*

**Acts which are prescribed in the Vedas constitute dharma, and acts which are not prescribed by the Vedas are adharma. The Vedas are directly Nārāyaṇa and are born from him. This we have heard from Yamarāja. SB 6.1.40**

The Vedas deal with the three guṇas: *traiguṇya-viṣayā vedāḥ*. (BG 2.45) Karma becomes accomplished by consciously following the Vedas, unlike bhakti that may be performed unknowingly. Karma is also defined in the Gītā elsewhere. *Bhūta-bhāvodbhava-karo visargaḥ karma-saṁjñitaḥ:* actions directly towards the devatās which create desires of the living entities are called karma. (BG 8.3) *Visarga* means giving up objects as an offering to the devatās. That is representative of all dharma. This creates (*udbhava-karaḥ*) desires (*bhāva*) for the living beings (*bhūta*). This definition excludes bhakti.

Dharma becomes excellent by association with bhakti. The Lord says:

*dharmo mad-bhakti-kṛt prokto jñānaṁ caikātmya-darśanam*  
*guṇesv asaṅgo vairāgyam aiśvaryaṁ cāṇimādayaḥ*

**Dharmameans what produces bhakti. Real jñāna is seeing Paramātmā everywhere. Real vairāgya is complete disinterest in sense objects. Real aiśvarya is acquisition of the siddhis*****.*** **SB 11.19.27**

Dharma is called bhakti by offering results to the Lord and using it as an assistant to bhakti. By mixing such actions with bhakti, sakāma-bhakti results:

*prajāḥ sṛjeti bhagavān kardamo brahmaṇoditaḥ*  
*sarasvatyāṁ tapas tepe sahasrāṇāṁ samā daśa*

**O dear Vidura! When Brahmā said to Kardama “Produce progeny.” Kardama went to the bank of the Sarasvatī River and performed penance for ten thousand years.**

*tataḥ samādhi-yuktena kriyā-yogena kardamaḥ*  
*samprapede hariṁ bhaktyā prapanna-varadāśuṣam*

**Then, through his austerities, using methods of worship with concentrated mind, Kardama with devotion worshipped the Supreme Lord, giver of benedictions to the surrendered. SB 3.21.6-7**

When he saw the Lord, he developed niṣkāma-bhakti, indicated by his tears. He had desire in his bhakti because of the respect for the order of Brahmā. Maitreya speaks the verses.

## Anuccheda 226

The desire for liberation is sometimes karma-jñāna-miśra-bhakti and sometimes jñāna-miśra-bhakti.*Jñānaṁ caikātmya-darśanam*: jñānam means seeing oneness. (SB 11.16.27) Since vairāgya, yoga and sāṅkhya as well has hearing about jñāna are its aṅgas, they are included in jñāna.

Now karma-jñāna-miśra-bhakti is illustrated:

*animitta-nimittena sva-dharmeṇāmalātmanā*  
*tīvrayā mayi bhaktyā ca śruta-sambhṛtayā ciram*  
*jñānena dṛṣṭa-tattvena vairāgyeṇa balīyasā*  
*tapo-yuktena yogena tīvreṇātma-samādhinā*  
*prakṛtiḥ puruṣasyeha dahyamānā tv ahar-niśam*  
*tiro-bhavitrī śanakair agner yonir ivāraṇiḥ*

**By energetic bhakti to me for a long time, without desire for results, which purifies the heart and is performed according to one’s occupational duties, by knowledge with realization of the elements, by strong renunciation, by yoga accompanied by austerities, by intense concentration on the Lord, the subtle body of the jīva burns continually and gradually disappears, like the kindling stick is consumed by fire. SB 3.27.21-2.**

*Animitta-nimittena*means niṣkāma. (This is the karma element.) *Amalātmanā* means with pure mind. *Jñānena* means with knowledge gained from scriptures. *Yoga* means meditation on jīva and Paramātmā. (These are jñāna elements.) Nānārtha-varga says that yoga means dhyāna. When the meditation reaches the point of no distinction between the meditator and the object of meditation it is called samādhi.

*svargāpavargayoḥ puṁsāṁ rasāyāṁ bhuvi sampadām*  
*sarvāsām api siddhīnāṁ mūlaṁ tac-caraṇārcanam*

**Devotional service to his lotus feet is the root cause of all the perfections a person can find in heaven, in liberation, in the subterranean regions and on earth. SB 10.81.19**

Thus though bhakti is the aṅgī, the root of all methods, in the above verse it is treated as an aṅga, as one among various sādhanas from the point of view of followers of other processes. The result of these sādhanas is just liberation. Kapila speaks.

## Anuccheda 227

Jñāna-miśra-bhakti is described:

*vivikta-kṣema-śaraṇo mad-bhāva-vimalāśayaḥ*  
*ātmānaṁ cintayed ekam abhedena mayā muniḥ*

**Dwelling in a safe and solitary place, his mind purified by constant thoughts of me, the sage should contemplate that the jīva is non-different from me. SB 11.18.21**

His heart is pure (*vimala*) because of thinking (*bhāva)* of me*(mat*). The Lord speaks.

## Anuccheda 228

Thus with the desire for liberation, jñāna-miśra-bhakti has been described. Now karma-miśra-bhakti with a desire only for bhakti is described.

*śraddhāmṛta-kathāyāṁ me śaśvan mad-anukīrtanam*  
*pariniṣṭhā ca pūjāyāṁ stutibhiḥ stavanaṁ mama*  
*mad-arthe ’rtha-parityāgo bhogasya ca sukhasya ca*  
*iṣṭaṁ dattaṁ hutaṁ japtaṁ mad-arthaṁ yad vrataṁ tapaḥ*  
*evaṁ dharmair manuṣyāṇām uddhavātma-nivedinām*  
*mayi sañjāyate bhaktiḥ ko ’nyo ’rtho ’syāvaśiṣyate*

**Firm faith in the narration of my sweet pastimes, constant chanting of my glories, unwavering attachment to my worship, praising me through verses, giving up wealth for my sake, renouncing material sense gratification and happiness, performing acts of charity, offering homa, and chanting japa, performing vows like Ekādaśī as austerity with the purpose of achieving me—by these those human beings who have surrendered themselves to me develop bhaktifor me. What other result could remain for my devotee? SB 11.19.20, 23-24**

One gives up material wealth in order to worship me, since it is contrary to bhakti. One gives up enjoyment (*bhogasya)* such as applying sandalwood which gives enjoyment. One gives up happiness in the form of caressing sons etc. One performs Vedic rites for me. Thus they are a cause of bhakti. By these bhāgavata-dharmas *(dharmaiḥ*), acts performed by body, words and mind, only for me, surrendered souls develop bhakti to me. What other results in sādhana and in sādhya, remain for the person who desires only bhakti? Though all other results are not given regard, they take shelter of bhakti.

*yasyāsti bhaktir bhagavaty akiñcanā*  
*sarvair guṇais tatra samāsate surāḥ*  
*harāv abhaktasya kuto mahad-guṇā*  
*manorathenāsati dhāvato bahiḥ*

**The devatās constantly dwell with all good qualities in that person who has pure bhakti for the Lord. There are no good qualities in the non-devotee who chases after temporary material objects with desire for material pleasure. SB 5.18.12**

The Lord speaks.

## Anuccheda 229

Now an example of karma-jñāna-miśra-bhakti with a desire only for bhakti is given:

*niṣevitenānimittena sva-dharmeṇa mahīyasā*  
*kriyā-yogena śastena nātihiṁsreṇa nityaśaḥ*

**The mind should be purified by following varṇāśrama duties approved by previous devotees, without desire for results, and which are favorable for bhakti, and by always performing powerful Pañcarātrika deity worship with best choice of items, and without unnecessary violence.**

*mad-dhiṣṇya-darśana-sparśa-pūjā-stuty-abhivandanaiḥ*  
*bhūteṣu mad-bhāvanayā sattvenāsaṅgamena ca*

**The mind should become purified by offering obeisances to, praying to, worshipping, touching and seeing my deity forms, by thinking of my presence in all beings, and by giving up bad association with determination.**

*mahatāṁ bahu-mānena dīnānām anukampayā*  
*maitryā caivātma-tulyeṣu yamena niyamena ca*

**The mind should become purified by greatly respecting the great devotees, showing compassion to the unfortunate, being friendly to one’s equals, and by following yama and niyama of aṣṭāṅga-yoga.**

*ādhyātmikānuśravaṇān nāma-saṅkīrtanāc ca me*  
*ārjavenārya-saṅgena nirahaṅkriyayā tathā*

**The mind should become purified by continually hearing about the good and bad qualities of the antaḥkaraṇa, by chanting my names, by sincere association with great devotees without pride.**

*mad-dharmaṇo guṇair etaiḥ parisaṁśuddha āśayaḥ*  
*puruṣasyāñjasābhyeti śruta-mātra-guṇaṁ hi mām*

**Being purified by the above methods, the mind of a person engaged in serving me quickly attains the perfect form of bhakti, consisting of actions such as hearing my qualities, which is non-different from attaining me. SB 3.19.15-19**

One becomes purified by sva-dharma perfectly performed (*niṣevitena*) without desire *(anitmittena*), and with faith (*mahīyasā*), by Pañcarātrika activities (*kriya-yogena*), performed without desire at the best time and place *(śastena*), and without excessive violence. This indicates that one should take leaves or fruits, avoiding pain by taking of life as much as possible. *Mad-dhiṣṇya*means “my deity form.” One should think of me (*mad-bhāvanayā)* in all beings as the antaryāmī. By determination (*sattvena)* and vairāgya (*asaṅgamena*) one becomes purified. The yamas are non-violence, not stealing, celibacy, and non-possessiveness. The niyamas are cleanliness, satisfaction, austerity, study and worship of the Lord. One should listen to scriptures that explain understanding the ātmā. One should be without pride (*nirahaṅkriyayā*). The heart of the person who performs these dharmas for me (*mad-dharmanaḥ*) easily attains dhruvānusmṛti, continuous remembrance of me, by hearing about my qualities. This is described in the following:

*mad-guṇa-śruti-mātreṇa mayi sarva-guhāśaye*  
*mano-gatir avicchinnā yathā gaṅgāmbhaso 'mbudhau*  
*lakṣaṇaṁ bhakti-yogasya nirguṇasya hy udāhṛtam*  
*ahaituky avyavahitā yā bhaktiḥ puruṣottame*

**Because the mind, by hearing about my qualities, flows continuously to me, the Supreme Lord residing in the hearts of all people, just as the Gaṅgā flows to the ocean, it is said that the quality of bhakti beyond the guṇas is absence of results other than bhaktiand lack of obstructions from other processes. SB 3.29.11-12**

There is a mixture of jñāna in this process because of hearing scriptures about ātmā. Kapila speaks.

## Anuccheda 230

Now jñāna-miśra-bhakti with bhakti as the only aim is explained.

*dṛṣṭa-śrutābhir mātrābhir nirmuktaḥ svena tejasā*  
*jñāna-vijñāna-santṛpto mad-bhaktaḥ puruṣo bhavet*

**Freeing oneself from all sense objects seen and heard by strength of discrimination, and being satisfied with scriptural knowledge and realization*****,*** **one should become my devotee. SB 6.16.62**

One should free oneself from objects seen in this world (*dṛṣṭa)* by the strength of discrimination (*svena tejasā*). (This is the jñāna element.) Saṅkarṣaṇa speaks to Citraketu.

## Anuccheda 231

Now pure svarūpa-siddha bhakti(no karma or jñāna methods mixed) is explained. This is figurative in one sense because some worshippers still harbor desires for material enjoyment and liberation. Those who have material desire are of two types: tamasic and rajasic desire. An example of tamasic desire in svarūpa-siddha bhakti is as follows:

*abhisandhāya yo hiṁsāṁ dambhaṁ mātsaryam eva vā*  
*saṁrambhī bhinna-dṛg bhāvaṁ mayi kuryāt sa tāmasaḥ*

**The angry person, devoid of compassion, who worships me with intentions of violence, pride and hatred, is tamasic in his bhakti. SB 2.29.8**

Having a decision (*abhisandhāya*) for violence etc., being angry (*saṁrambhī),* he does not see the suffering and happiness of others as his own, having limited vision (*bhinna-dṛk*). This means he has no compassion.

## Anuccheda 232

The rajasic person practicing svarūpa-siddha bhakti is described:

*viṣayān abhisandhāya yaśa aiśvaryam eva vā*  
*arcādāv arcayed yo māṁ pṛthag-bhāvaḥ sa rājasaḥ*

**He who worships me with desire for other objects, with goals of material gain, fame or power has rajasic bhakti. SB 3.29.9**

He has desires (*bhāvaḥ*) for objects other than me (*pṛthak*). This is the reason he is classed as rajasic.

## Anuccheda 233

Those who practice svarūpa-siddha-bhakti with desire for liberation are persons in sattva.

*karma-nirhāram uddiśya parasmin vā tad-arpaṇam*  
*yajed yaṣṭavyam iti vā pṛthag-bhāvaḥ sa sāttvikaḥ*

**He who worships me with a desire to destroy karma, making his works an offering the Supreme Lord, or executing the worship as a duty, having a desire for liberation, has sattvic bhakti. SB 3.29.10**

He who desiring liberation (*karma-nirhāram*) offers his work to the Supreme Lord (*parasmin*) or worships the Lord thinking it is a duty (*yaṣṭavyam)* as a rule for all people, rather than because of knowing the truth about the Lord, whose vision is divided as with the rajasic devotee-- who thinks that liberation is goal separate from bhakti, is called a devotee in sattva-guṇa. Even if he worships the Lord out of duty, his final result is only destruction of karma.

*Sāttvikaḥ kārako ’saṅgī:*a performer of action who is free of attachment is in sattva.(SB 11.25.26) *Kaivalyaṁ sāttvikaṁ jñāna*: knowledge concerning liberation insattva. (SB 11.25.24) *Sāttvikaṁ sukham ātmottham:* happiness arising from knowledge of ātmā is in sattva.(SB 11.25.29) Such statements show that the sādhana and sādhya (attainment) of such bhakti are in the guṇas. The second half of SB 3:29:10 particularly illustrates the three quotations given above.

Svarūpa-siddha-bhakti or kevala-bhakti

## Anuccheda 234

The different types of bhakti were delineated in order to have good knowledge. The root of them is svarūpa-siddha bhakti. This svarūpa-siddha bhakti which desiresonlybhakti,which is called niṣkāma, nirguṇa and kevala, will now be described.

*mad-guṇa-śruti-mātreṇa mayi sarva-guhāśaye*  
*mano-gatir avicchinnā yathā gaṅgāmbhaso 'mbudhau*  
*lakṣaṇaṁ bhakti-yogasya nirguṇasya hy udāhṛtam*  
*ahaituky avyavahitā yā bhaktiḥ puruṣottame*

**Because the mind, by hearing about my qualities, flows continuously to me, the Supreme Lord residing in the hearts of all people, just as the Gaṅgā flows to the ocean, it is said that the svarūpa ofbhakti beyond the guṇas is absence of results other than bhaktiand lack of obstructions from other processes.**

*sālokya-sārṣṭi-sāmīpya- sārūpyaikatvam apy uta*  
*dīyamānaṁ na gṛhṇanti vinā mat-sevanaṁ janāḥ*

**The devotees do not accept living on my planet, having similar powers, staying close to me, or having a similar form, what to speak of merging into me, when these things are offered, unless it involves service to me.**

*sa eva bhakti-yogākhyaātyantika udāhṛtaḥ*  
*yenātivrajya tri-guṇaṁmad-bhāvāyopapadyate*

**The highest state of existence is called bhakti, by which one surpasses the three guṇas and attains prema for me. SB 3.29.11-14**

Hearing about my qualities (*mad-guṇa-śruti-mātreṇa*) means there is no other goal than hearing. The mind, not distracted by other objects (*avicchinnā*), goes to me who resides in a place invisible to all the material senses (*guhā*), secretly, without moving. An example of continuous thinking of the Lord is given: it is like the flow of the Gaṅgā. The word *gati (*flow*)* in *mano-gatiḥ* should be supplied in the example of the Gaṅgā. The abbreviation is for metrical reasons. The svarūpa lakṣaṇaof bhakti beyond the guṇas is said to be that bhakti which is *ahaitukī* and*avyavahitā.*

“How can you say define bhakti in terms of having no other goal than hearing and the mind flowing to the topics continuously? That is impossible.” The answer is given: that bhakti is without desire for results and is unobstructed, ie. the direct form of bhakti, being svarūpa-siddha. Āropa-siddha bhakti has obstacles in the form of karma-yoga for instance. Such bhakti is exclusively service to the Lord through hearing and other actions. That is its svarūpa. By using the word *mātreṇa* it is indicated that the hearing is also continuous (with no obstacles). Furthermore the flow of the mind is not only continuous but without desire for material result (*ahaitukī*). Both adjectives should be applied to hearing and the flow of the mind.

The devotee’s actions are beyond the guṇas. *Nirguṇo mad-apāśrayaḥ*: a performer of action who has taken shelter of me alone is beyond the guṇas. (SB 11.25.26) His qualities are beyond the guṇas:

*māṁ bhajanti guṇāḥ sarve nirguṇaṁ nirapekṣakam*  
*suhṛdaṁ priyam ātmānaṁ sāmyāsaṅgādayo ’guṇāḥ*

**All superior qualities, which are eternal, such as equality to all beings and attachment to my devotee, reside in me. I am beyond the material guṇas, not dependent on material qualities, and am the devotees’ friend, giving love to them. SB 11.13.40**

Thus it is established that bhakti is without material motivation (since the devotee’s actions and qualities are beyond the guṇas).

My devotees (*janāḥ*) do not accept sālokya etc. even if offered, if it is without service to me. If they accept, it is for serving me, not for themselves. *Sārṣṭi*means having similar powers as the Lord. *Ekatvam* means merging in the Lord or merging in Brahman. Since both involve merging the self, there is no possibility of serving the Lord. Thus the devotee never accepts merging.

Therefore this method alone gives the highest result (*ātyantika*). This is apavarga,meaning ātyantika-pralaya or final destruction of the body. *Nātyantikaṁ vigaṇayanty api te prasādam*: persons who have realized you do not regard attainment of impersonal liberation as your mercy. (SB 3.15.48)

“How can surpassing the three guṇas and attaining the Lordbe apavarga?” Bhakti’s nature of doing so is self-evident. The verse elaborates by saying “By this bhakti, never giving it up, one canattain (*upapadyate)* my presence (*mad-bhāvāyan*), a direct meeting with me..

It is said *yathā-varṇa-vidhānam apavargaś cāpi bhavati*: one attains apavarga according to the prescribed actions. The next verse explains what apavarga means:

*yo 'sau bhagavati sarva-bhūtātmany anātmye 'nirukte 'nilayane paramātmani vāsudeve 'nanya-nimitta-bhakti-yoga-lakṣaṇo nānā-gati-nimittāvidyā-granthi-randhana-dvāreṇa yadā hi mahā-puruṣa-puruṣa-prasaṅgaḥ.*

**That liberation, whose essential nature is unmotivated bhakti-yogato the Lord full of qualities, who attracts the minds of all beings, who is not the object of merging, who is not described by material words, who remains beyond destruction of the universe, who is the most excellent ātmā, who is the son of Vasudeva, takes place by destruction of the knot of ignorance which causes various material goals, when there is association with devotees of the Lord. SB 5.19.20**

Nirguṇa-bhakti is of various types. At the beginning of the section it is said:

*bhakti-yogo bahu-vidho mārgair bhāvini bhāvyate*  
*svabhāva-guṇa-mārgeṇa puṁsāṁ bhāvo vibhidyate*

**Bhaktiappears in many forms by particular actions in a person according to his intentions. The intentions of men are classed by the actions of the guṇasarising from one’s nature. SB 3.29.7**

*Margaiḥ* means “by particular methods.” The intentions of men are classed by the actions (*margeṇa)* in one’s bhakti (*sva*) according to differences in material guṇas like tamas and differences in one’s spiritual identity (*bhāva*).

The commentary Muktā-phala says, “This method, called bhakti-yoga, is *ātyantika* since there is no method beyond it. This refers to the pure bhakti according to the context. There is attraction to a result in other types of bhakti, but no attraction to Viṣṇu, since on attaining the result they give up bhakti..

Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad says:

*bhaktir asya bhajanam \| tad ihāmutropādhi-nairāsyenāmuṣmi.*  
*manaḥ-kalpanam \| etad eva ca naiṣkarmyam \|\|*

**Bhajanam or service means devotional service to Kṛṣṇa. It is concentration of the mind on Kṛṣṇa without desires for enjoyment in this life or the next. It destroys all karma. Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad 1.14**

*sa hovāca yājñavalkyas tat pumān ātma-hitāya premṇā hariṁ bhajet*

**Yājñavalkya said, “Man should worship the Lord with prema which is for his own benefit.” Śatapatha-śruti**

By desire for prīti alone (prema), which is for his benefit, man should worship. Kapila speaks.

## Anuccheda 235

This bhakti called akiñcana or ātyantikī, performed in many ways, has two types: vaidhī and rāgānugā. Vaidhi is performed according to the rules of scriptures. The rules are of two types: those inspiring performance and those giving knowledge of what to do and what not to do in performance.

Rules to inspire performance are as follows:

*tasmād ekena manasā bhagavān sātvatāṁ patiḥ \|*  
*śrotavyaḥ kīrtitavyaś ca dhyeyaḥ pūjyaś ca nityad.*

**Therefore, with mind dedicated only to bhakti, devoid of karma and jñāna, one should constantly hear about, glorify and meditate upon the Supreme Lord — the master of the devotees. SB 2.1.1.**

The second type of rule is used in deity worship and vows:

*mām eva nairapekṣyeṇa bhakti-yogena vindati*  
*bhakti-yogaṁ sa labhata evaṁ yaḥ pūjayeta mām*

**But one who simply engages in devotional service with no motives at all attains me. Hewho worships meattains bhakti-yoga. SB 11.27.53**

The bhakti should be without motives (*nairapekṣyeṇa)*. How does one attain unmotivated bhakti-yoga? He who worships me attains bhakti-yoga. Bhakti-yoga was previously described according to rules:

*yadā sva-nigamenoktaṁ dvijatvaṁ prāpya pūruṣaḥ*  
*yathā yajeta māṁ bhaktyā śraddhayā tan nibodha me*

**Now please listen faithfully as I explain exactly how a person who has achieved twice-born status through the relevant Vedic prescriptions should worship me with devotion. SB 11.27.8**

*arcāyāṁ sthaṇḍile ’gnau vā sūrye vāpsu hṛdi dvijaḥ*  
*dravyeṇa bhakti-yukto ’rcet sva-guruṁ mām amāyayā*

**A twice-born person should worship me, his worshipable Lord, without duplicity, offering appropriate paraphernalia in loving devotion to me in the deity form, in the ground, in fire, in the sun, in water or within the worshiper’s own heart. SB 11.27.9**

The Lord speaks.

## Anuccheda 236

There are similarly rules for Janmāṣṭamī, Ekādaśī and other days. There are different types of vaidhībhakti such as surrender, serving guru and other devotees, hearingand chanting . These can be performed singly or two or three can be performed together as this is mentioned in scriptures. First surrender or śaraṇāpatti will be discussed.

Afflicted by fear of saṁsāra by the enemies in the form of the six vargas (lust, greed, anger, delusion, pride and envy) one surrenders to the Lord, being without any other shelter. Though a person desires bhakti, he is afflicted by aversion to the Lord caused by the six vargas.Thus the person surrenders.

There are two methods of taking shelter of the Lord: by announcing that one has no other shelter and by giving up other means of shelter by using good intelligence. The first type is illustrated:

*martyo mṛtyu-vyāla-bhītaḥ palāyan*  
*lokān sarvān nirbhayaṁ nādhyagacchat*  
*tvat pādābjaṁ prāpya yadṛcchayādya*  
*susthaḥ śete mṛtyur asmād apaiti*

**No one in this material world, fearful of the snake of time, has become free from fear, even by fleeing to various planets. But now that you have appeared, death is fleeing in fear of you, and the living entities, having obtained shelter at your lotus feet by your mercy, are fearless and tranquil. SB 10.3.27**

The second type is illustrated:

*tasmāt tvam uddhavotsṛjya codanāṁ praticodanām*  
*pravṛttiṁ ca nivṛttiṁ ca śrotavyaṁ śrutam eva ca*  
*mām ekam eva śaraṇam ātmānaṁ sarva-dehinām*  
*yāhi sarvātma-bhāvena mayā syā hy akuto-bhayaḥ*

**O Uddhava! Give up śruti and smṛti, the path of enjoyment and the path of renunciation, desire to hear more about dharma, and what you have already heard about dharma. Surrender to me alone, the soul of all beings, using all methods, with bhāva in your mind. You will be fearless by my mercy. SB 11.12.14-15**

*Codanām*means śruti and *praticodanām*means smṛti according to Śrīdhara Svāmī.

Also Gītā says *sarva-dharmān parityajya*: give up all dharmas. (BG 18.66) Śaraṇa-patti is described in Vaiṣṇava-tantra.

*ānukūlyasya saṅkalpaḥ pratikūlya-vivarjanam \|*  
*rakṣiṣyatīti viśvāso goptṛtve varaṇaṁ tathā \|*  
*ātma-nikṣepa-kārpaṇye ṣaḍ-vidhā śaraṇāgatiḥ \|\|*

**The six divisions of surrender are the acceptance of those things favorable to devotional service, the rejection of unfavorable things, the conviction that Kṛṣṇa will give protection, the acceptance of the Lord as one’s guardian or master, full self-surrender, and humility.**

There are six items (five are aṅgas and one is aṅgī). Accepting the Lord as one’s master*(goptṛtve varaṇam*) is the aṅgī (main element) since it means the same as śaraṇāgati. The other five items are aṅgas, since they assist that surrender. The devotee accepts what is favorableand rejects what is unfavorable for his particular case or for the mood of surrender. Faith that he will be protected (rakṣisyatīti viśvāsaḥ) is illustrated:

*viśvasya yaḥ sthiti-layodbhava-hetur ādyo*  
*yogeśvarair api duratyaya-yogamāyaḥ*  
*kṣemaṁ vidhāsyati sa no bhagavāṁs tryadhīśas*  
*tatrāsmadīya-vimṛśena kiyān ihārthaḥ*

**The Lord of the three worlds who is the cause of creation, maintenance and destruction, the original person, whose power ofyoga-māyā cannot be understood by even the greatest yogīs, will give security. What is the use of us deliberating on this? SB 3.16.37**

*Ātma-niṣkepa*(renouncing the self, renouncing ego) is illustrated in *kenāpi devena hṛdi sthitena yathā niyukto’smi tathā karomi:* I do as I am enjoined by the Lord situated in my heart.(Gautamīya-tantra). It is said in discussing the meaning of *namaḥ* in the eight syllable mantra:

*ahaṅkṛtir ma-kāraḥ syān na-kāras tan-niṣedhakaḥ \|*  
*tasmāt tu namasā cātra svātantryaṁ pratiṣidhyate \|\|*  
*bhagavat-para-tantro'sau tadāyattātma-jīvanaḥ \|*  
*tasmāt sva-sāmarthya-vidhiṁ tyajet sarvam aśeṣataḥ \|\|*  
*īśvarasya tu sāmarthyān nālabhyaṁ tasya vidyate \|*  
*tasmin nyasta-bharaḥ śete tat-karmaiva samācaret \|\|*

***Ma*** **represents ahaṅkāra and** ***na*** **represents negation of ahaṅkāra. Thus by uttering** ***namaḥ*** **one negates one’s independent attitude. The jīva is dependent on the Lord. He lives with dependence. Therefore one should completely reject the idea of accomplishing by one’s own capacity. Nothing is left to be accomplished by the ability of the Lord. Consigning one’s body to the Lord, one should serve him. Padma Purāṇa 6.226.41-46**

*ahaṅkāra-nivṛttānāṁ keśavo na hi dūragaḥ \|*  
*ahaṅkāra-yutānāṁ hi madhye parvata-rāśayaḥ \|\|*

**The Lord is easily accessible for those who renounce ahaṅkāra (sense of I). For those with ahaṅkāra there is a mountain range between them and the Lord. Brahma-vaivarta Purāṇa**

The person who thinks he is independent (proud) is bound in saṁsāra:

*yāvat pṛthaktvam idam ātmana indriyārtha-*  
*māyā-balaṁ bhagavato jana īśa paśyet*  
*tāvan na saṁsṛtir asau pratisaṅkrameta*  
*vyarthāpi duḥkha-nivahaṁ vahatī kriyārthā*

**As long as people see the only the condition of the jīva’s body, possessing the strength of the Lord’s māyā in the form of sense objects, the jīva’s material existence, though insubstantial, will not be destroyed. It produces great suffering and giving results for all actions. SB 3.9.9**

Humility (*kārpaṇyam*) is expressed by thinking, “There is no one more merciful than the Lord. There is no one more lamentable than me.”

Accepting the Lord as one’s master (gopṭrtve varaṇam, the aṅgī) is illustrated:

*tvāṁ prapanno’smi śaraṇaṁ deva-devaṁ janārdanam \|*  
*iti yaḥ śaraṇaṁ prāptas taṁ kleśād uddharāmy aham \|\|*

**“I surrender to you, Janardana, Lord of Lords.” I deliver the person from suffering who surrenders in this manner. Nṛsiṁha Purāṇ.**

Surrender is of three types: with body, voice and mind.

*karmaṇā manasā vācā ye’cyutaṁ śaraṇaṁ gatāḥ \|*  
*na samartho yamas teṣāṁ te mukti-phala-bhāginaḥ \|\|*

**Those who surrenders with actions, mind and words cannot be touched by Yamarāja and enjoy the results of liberation. Brahma Purāṇa**

This is explained in Hari-bhakti-vilāsa:

*tavāsmīti vadan vācā tathaiva manasā vidan .*  
*tat-sthānam āśritas tanvā modate śaraṇāgataḥ \|\|*

**Saying “I am yours,” knowing this in the mind, and taking shelter of the Lord’s places (with the body), the surrendered soul experiences joy. Hari-bhakti-vilāsa 11.677**

Those who surrender with all the six aṅgas quickly achieve full results. Others, according to the amount of surrender, attain proportionate results.

Surrender is glorified:

*tāpa-trayeṇābhihatasya ghore*  
*santapyamānasya bhavādhvanīśa*  
*paśyāmi nānyac charaṇaṁ tavāṅghri-*  
*dvandvātapatrād amṛtābhivarṣāt*

**O Lord! For one who is being tormented on the terrible path of birth and death and is constantly overwhelmed by the threefold miseries, I do not see any shelter other than your two lotus feet, which are like a refreshing umbrella that pours showers of nectar. SB 11.19.9**

For those who surrender and remove all suffering, there is a complete shower of the Lord’s sweetness. Uddhava speaks to the Lord.

Service to guru

## Anuccheda 237

Surrender has been described. It has been described first since without it, one cannot become a servant of the Lord. By surrender, everything is accomplished.

*śaraṇaṁ taṁ prapannā ye dhyāna-yoga-vivarjitāḥ \|*  
*te vai mṛtyum atikramya yānti tad vaiṣṇavaṁ padam \|\|*

**Those who surrender to you without meditating overcome death and go the Vaikuṇṭha. Garuḍa Purāṇa**

If possible, those who desire excellence in bhaktiand surrender should particularly and constantly serve those who teach the scriptures about the Lord and those who teach mantras about the Lord or serve the lotus feet of guru or gurus. His or their mercy is the root of destruction of anarthas difficult to give up by various methods and the root of receiving the greatest mercy of the Lord. Nārada says:

*asaṅkalpāj jayet kāmaṁ krodhaṁ kāma-vivarjanāt*  
*arthānarthekṣayā lobhaṁ bhayaṁ tattvāvamarśanāt*

**One conquers lust by negative determination. One conquers anger by giving up the desire for violence. One gives up greed by seeing the uselessness of objects of greed. One gives up fear by considering that all suffering is caused by karma.**

*ānvīkṣikyā śoka-mohau dambhaṁ mahad-upāsayā*  
*yogāntarāyān maunena hiṁsāṁ kāmādy-anīhayā*

**By considering what is ātmā and non-ātmā one can conquer lamentation and illusion. By serving a great devotee one can become prideless. By keeping silent one can avoid obstacles on the path of yoga. By giving up desire for sense gratification one can conquer violence.**

*kṛpayā bhūtajaṁ duḥkhaṁ daivaṁ jahyāt samādhinā*  
*ātmajaṁ yoga-vīryeṇa nidrāṁ sattva-niṣevayā*

**One conquers violence to other living entities by doing beneficial acts for them. One conquers anxiety from seeing omens by concentration on the Lord. One conquers physical suffering by practice of yoga. One conquers sleep by taking small quantities of sattvika food.**

*rajas tamaś ca sattvena sattvaṁ copaśamena ca*  
*etat sarvaṁ gurau bhaktyā puruṣo hy añjasā jayet*

**One conquers rajas and tamas by a predominance of sattva. One conquers sattva by being indifferent to the results of actions. One conquers all of these obstacles quickly and easily by devotion to guru. SB 7.15.25**

Brahmā says:

*yo mantraḥ sa guruḥ sākṣād yo guruḥ sa hariḥ svayam \|*  
*gurur yasya bhavet tuṣṭas tasya tuṣṭo hariḥ svayam \|\| iti \|*

**The mantra is guru and guru is the Lord himself. If one’s guru is satisfied, the Lord is satisfied. Vāmana-kalpa**

Elsewhere it is said:

*harau ruṣṭe gurus trātā gurau ruṣṭe na kaścana \|*  
*tasmāt sarva-prayatnena gurum eva prasādayet \|\|*

**If the Lord is angry, the guru can save one. If guru is angry, no one can save one. Therefore with all effort one should please the guru. Quoted in Hari-bhakti-vilāsa 4.36.**

Therefore service to guru should be constant. The Lord says elsewhere:

*prathamaṁ tu guruḥ pūjyaḥ tataś caiva mamārcanam \|*  
*kurvan siddhim avāpnoti hy anyathā niṣphalaṁ bhavet \|\|*

**First one should worship guru and then worship me. Doing that, one attains all results. If one does not do so, one attains no results. Quoted in Hari-bhakti-vilāsa 4.344**

*vaiṣṇavaṁ jñāna-vaktāraṁ yo vidyād viṣṇuvad gurum \|*  
*pūjayed vāṅ-manaḥ-kāyaiḥ sa śāstrajñaḥ sa vaiṣṇavaḥ \|\|*  
*śloka-pādasya vaktāpi yaḥ pūjyaḥ sa sadaiva hi \|*  
*kiṁ punar bhagavad-viṣṇoḥ svarūpaṁ vitanoti yaḥ \|\|*

**He who regards guru, the speaker of knowledge,a devotee, as similar to Viṣṇu, who worships him with body, mind and words, is a Vaiṣṇava and knows scripture. He who explains one line of a verse is always worthy of worship, what to speak of the person who reveals the form of Viṣṇu.**

Nārada-pañcarātra

Devadyuti says:

*bhaktir yathā harau me’sti tadvan niṣṭhā gurau yadi \|*  
*mamāsti tena satyena svaṁ darśayatu hariḥ \|\|*

**If I have devotion to guru as I have devotion to the Lord, by that strength may the Lord reveal himself to me. Padma Purāṇa**

One does not need any other type of worship of the Lord if one serves guru. In discussing the results of atonement it is said:

*yathā siddha-rasa-sparśāt tāmraṁ bhavati kāñcanam \|*  
*sannidhānād guror evaṁ śiṣyo viṣṇumayo bhavet \|\|*

**Just as from the touch of quicksilver copper becomes gold, by association with guru the disciple becomes filled with Viṣṇu.**

Therefore it is said:

*nāham ijyā-prajātibhyāṁ tapasopaśamena vā*  
*tuṣyeyaṁ sarva-bhūtātmā guru-śuśrūṣayā yathā*

**I, the Soul of all beings, am not as satisfied by ritual worship, brahminical initiation, penances or self-discipline as I am by faithful service rendered to one’s guru. SB 10.80.34**

The commentary says, “It was already stated there is no one more worthy of service than the guru who gives knowledge. This verse states that there is no greater dharma than worshipping him. I, the Supreme Lord, am not as satisfied with household dharma (*ijyā*), with brahmacārī dharma (*prajāti,* taking second birth), with vanaprastha dharma (*tapasā*), or sannyāsa *(upaśamena*), as much as I am satisfied with a person who serves guru, though I am the soul of all beings.”

There are two types of knowledge: about Brahman and about Bhagavān. In Śrīdhara Svāmī’s commentary above, the knowledge is concerning Brahman. (Thus his explanation refers to āśramas.) If knowledge is that ofBhagavān, then *ijyā*means worship, *prajāti* means vaiṣṇava-dīkṣā, tapasāmeans “by samādhi” and *upaśamena*means “by being fixed in the Lord”. The Lord speaks to Śrīdāma brāhmaṇa.

## Anuccheda 238

It is best to serve other devotees on the order of guru, which does not contradict service to him. Otherwise there is fault. Nārada says:

*gurau sannihite yas tu pūjayed anyam agrata.*  
*sa durgatim avāpnoti pūjanaṁ tasya niṣphalam*

**A person who worships another person in front of guru attains a bad destination and his worship yields no result. Quoted in Hari-bhakti-vilāsa 4.345**

He who from the beginning did not take shelter of a guru with qualities described in SB 11.3.21and doesnot get permission from the guru to respect mahābhāgavatas because of the guru’s envy, since in the beginning he did not follow scripture in selecting guru, is not the subject considered in talking about respect to guru. He falls into difficulty both by respecting devotees and thus disobeying guru or obeying guru and thus disrespecting devotees.

With this intention it is said:

*yo vakti nyāya-rahitam anyāyena śṛṇoti yaḥ \|*  
*tāv ubhau narakaṁ ghoraṁ vrajataḥ kālam akṣayam \|\|*

**He who speaks incorrectly or he who hears what is incorrect goes to fearful hell for unlimited time. Nārada-pañcarātra**

Therefore one should worship such a guru from a distance. If he hates Vaiṣṇavas, he should be rejected for it is said:

*guror apy avaliptasya kāryākāryam ajānataḥ \|*  
*utpathapratipannasya parityago vidhīyate \|\|*

**The guru who proud, does not know what to do and what not to do, who has strayed from the correct path, should be given up. Mahābhārata 5.178.24**

He should be rejected since he is not a Vaiṣṇava because he does not have a Vaiṣṇava attitude, being the subject of the following verse.

*avaiṣṇavopadiṣṭena mantreṇa nirayaṁ vrajet \|*  
*punaś ca vidhinā samyag grāhayed vaiṣṇavād guroḥ \|\|*

**One goes to hell by accepting a mantra from a non-devotee. One should again take the mantra according to rules from a Vaiṣṇava guru. Padma Purāṇa**

In the absence of a venerable guru having the said scriptural characteristics, the best path is regular service to a mahābhāgavata.Amahābhāgavatawho has similar mentality, as would be the case in choosinga venerable guru, and who shows mercy to that devotee, should be accepted.

*yasya yat-saṅgatiḥ puṁso maṇivat syāt sa tad-guṇaḥ \|*  
*sva-kularddhyai tato dhīmān sva-yūthāny eva saṁśrayet \|\|*

**A man will attain the qualities of the person with whom he associates, just as a crystal takes up the color of the object next to it. Therefore, the wise man will take shelter of those who have qualities like his own for the prosperity of his family.Hari-bhakti-sudhodaya**

In accordance with this scriptural statement, without that great devotee’s mercy and without attraction for him how can one succeed? As appropriate to the case, one should serve all those who carry the marks of a devotee.

There are two types of service rendered to the mahābhāgavata: prasaṅga-rūpa (associating, hearing), paricaryā-rūpa (rending physical service). Prasaṅga-rūpa service is as follows:

*na rodhayati māṁ yogo na sāṅkhyaṁ dharma eva ca*  
*na svādhyāyas tapas tyāgo neṣṭā-pūrtaṁ na dakṣiṇā*  
*vratāni yajñaś chandāṁsi tīrthāni niyamā yamāḥ*  
*yathāvarundhe sat-saṅgaḥ sarva-saṅgāpaho hi mām*

**O Uddhava! Only by associating with my pure devotees one can destroy material attachment and attain me. One cannot attain me by aṣṭāṅga-yoga, distinction of ātmā from body, practice of nonviolence, study of the Vedas, austerity, sannyāsa, sacrifices, charitable projects, donations, vows, worship ofdevatās, secret mantras, holy places, or observing prohibitions and rules. SB 11.12.1-2**

*iṣṭā-pūrtena mām evaṁ yo yajeta samāhitaḥ*  
*labhate mayi sad-bhaktiṁ mat-smṛtiḥ sādhu-sevayā*

**He who worships me with attention by sacrifices, constructing gardens and other things mentioned above,rememberingme, by service to devotees, attains pure bhakti. SB 11.11.47**

Though steadiness in bhakti arises from serving devotees, the above verse seems to give deference to other sādhanas such performing sacrifices and constructing gardens. *Iṣṭa* refers to sacrifices like agni-hotra, darśa-paruṇamāsa, cāturmāsya, paśu-yāga, vaiśvadeva-bali-haraṇa etc. *Pūrta* refers to making temples, gardens, wells, ponds, lakes and places for drinking water. This is according to SB 7.15.49-49.However sacrifices are also described in *haviṣāgnau yajeta mām*: one worships me in fire by offering oblations. (SB 11.11.43) This agni-hotra represents other sacrifices as well. As well there is the following description of *pūrta*:

*mamārcā-sthāpane śraddhā svataḥ saṁhatya codyamaḥ*  
*udyānopavanākrīḍa- pura-mandira-karmaṇi*

**One should have faith in establishing deities. One should engage in special projects, such as gardens, groves, playgrounds, houses and temples, constructing them personally, or engaging others. SB 11.11.38**

Thus the meaning should be “He who worships meby sacrifices and gardens, remembering me, attains steadiness in the essence of bhakti by devotee association.”Sacrifices are included in bhakti since one makes offerings to fire as the residence of the Lord in the form of antaryāmī. Gardens and wells are part of bhakti because they are made for serving the Lord. Thus what is stated is that everything else depends on association of devotees. Moreover in the same chapter it is said that association with devotees can, on its own, yield all desired results.

In order to explain the great power of devotee association on which all else depends, the Lord teaches the greatest secret:

*athaitat paramaṁ guhyaṁ śṛṇvato yadu-nandana*  
*su-gopyam api vakṣyāmi tvaṁ me bhṛtyaḥ suhṛt sakhā*

**O beloved of the Yadu dynasty! Because you are my servant, well-wisher and friend, I shall now speak to you who listen well the supreme secret. SB 11.11.49**

Because no other process has such power, devotee association is called the greatest secret. This is then explained in SB 11.12.1-2 (quoted above).

*na rodhayati māṁ yogo na sāṅkhyaṁ dharma eva ca*  
*na svādhyāyas tapas tyāgo neṣṭā-pūrtaṁ na dakṣiṇā*  
*vratāni yajñaś chandāṁsi tīrthāni niyamā yamāḥ*  
*yathāvarundhe sat-saṅgaḥ sarva-saṅgāpaho hi mām*

**O Uddhava! Only by associating with my pure devotees one can destroy material attachment and attain me. One cannot attain me by aṣṭāṅga-yoga, distinction of ātmā from body, practice of nonviolence, study of the Vedas, austerity, sannyāsa, sacrifices, charitable projects, donations, vows, worship of devatās, secret mantras, holy places, or observing prohibitions and rules. SB 11.12.1-2**

*Tyāgaḥ*means sannyāsa. *Dakṣināḥ*means charity work. *Yajñaḥ*means deity worship. *Chandāṁsi* means secret mantras. Yoga and other processes do not bring me under control as much as association with devotees brings me under control. This indicates that the elements in the list control the Lord to some extent. That means that these elements are connected with the Lord, and are not the ordinary yoga, sannyāsa, or charity. Thus *vratāni*means Ekādaśī and other devotional fasts according to the commentator.

Because association is so pleasing to the Lord, this does not mean that one does not have to perform the regular Vaiṣṇava vratas, since by glorifying the power of one action of bhakti to give results it does not negate the obligation to perform other actions regularly.

*na hy agni-mukhato ’yaṁ vai bhagavān sarva-yajña-bhuk*  
*ijyeta haviṣā rājan yathā vipra-mukhe hutaiḥ*

**O King! The Supreme Lord, enjoyer of all sacrifices, is not worshipped by offerings to the fires as much as he is worshiped by offerings to the mouths of the brāhmaṇas. SB 7.14.17**

Hearing this, the followers of karma cannot give up fire sacrifices. Similarly the devotees hearing that one should worship the devotee profusely (*mad-bhakta-pūjādhyadhikā* SB 11.19.21) should not give up worshipping the Lord which is prescribed as regular practice after taking dīkṣā.

*ṣaḍbhir māsopavāsais tu yat phalaṁ parikīrtitam \|*  
*viṣṇor naivedya-sikthena tat phalaṁ bhuñjatāṁ kalau \|\|*

**The glorified results from doing six months fasting are attained in Kali-yuga by taking a mouthful of food offered to Viṣṇu. Skanda Purāṇa**

Such a statement does not negate the rule to observe fasting on Ekādaśī which are also parts of bhakti, and which also give great results. Therefore one must observe such Vaiṣṇava vratas, even just to maintain their regular performance. Regular Vaiṣṇava vratas will later be discussed in relation to arcana. Thus Śridhara Svāmī, commenting on giving up all dharmas and worshipping the Lord in SB 11.11.32 says “One gives up dharmas contrary to bhakti such as offering śrāddha food which is not Viṣṇu prasāda, observing viddha ekādaśī or not observing ekādaśī during the waning phase of the moon.” Performance of bhagavad-dharma is mentioned by Bhīṣma in the first Canto. The commentary says, “This means following rules for Dvādaśī etc. for the pleasure of the Lord.” In the commentary on *vratāni cere hari-toṣaṇāni*:he performed austerities for the satisfaction of the Lord. SB 3.1.19) he says, “Vratas means Ekādaśī etc..

The necessity of observing many aṅgas of bhakti (not just engaging in devotee association) is confirmed by conduct of Ambarīṣa, crest jewel among devotees, who vowed to eat only food offered to the Lord.

Now we return to the subject. Controlling the Lord (mentioned in SB 11.12.1-2) has two types: principle and secondary. By the principle control one attains prema.

*rājan patir gurur alaṁ bhavatāṁ yadūnāṁ*  
*daivaṁ priyaḥ kula-patiḥ kva ca kiṅkaro vaḥ*  
*astv evam aṅga bhagavān bhajatāṁ mukundo*  
*muktiṁ dadāti karhicit sma na bhakti-yogam*

**O King Parīkṣit! Kṛṣṇa was the protector, instructor, object of worship, giver of affection, and the protector of both the Yadus and Pāṇḍavas. But he was sometimes your servant. The Lord gives liberation but not even bhāva-bhakti to those who worship him. SB 5.6.18**

By secondary control over the Lord one attains results other than prema. An example of primary control is the gopīs. An example of secondary control is Bāṇa. Secondary control over the Lordmakes the Lordinclined to give benedictions. Here is an example:

*sat-saṅgena hi daiteyā yātudhānā mṛgāḥ khagāḥ*  
*gandharvāpsaraso nāgāḥ siddhāś cāraṇa-guhyakāḥ*  
*vidyādharā manuṣyeṣu vaiśyāḥ śūdrāḥ striyo ’ntya-jāḥ*  
*rajas-tamaḥ-prakṛtayas tasmiṁs tasmin yuge yuge*  
*bahavo mat-padaṁ prāptās tvāṣṭra-kāyādhavādayaḥ*  
*vṛṣaparvā balir bāṇo mayaś cātha vibhīṣaṇaḥ*  
*sugrīvo hanumān ṛkṣo gajo gṛdhro vaṇikpathaḥ*  
*vyādhaḥ kubjā vraje gopyo yajña-patnyas tathāpare*

**In every yuga many living entities entangled in the modes of passion and ignorance gained the association of my devotees. Thus, such living entities as the Daityas, Rākṣasas, birds, beasts, Gandharvas, Apsarās, Nāgas, Siddhas, Cāraṇas, Guhyakas and Vidyādharas, as well as such lower-class human beings as the vaiśyas, śūdras, women and others, were able to achieve my supreme abode. Vṛtrāsura, Prahlāda Mahārāja and others like them also achieved my abode by association with my devotees, as did personalities such as Vṛṣaparvā, Bali Mahārāja, Bāṇāsura, Maya, Vibhīṣaṇa, Sugrīva, Hanumān, Jāmbavān, Gajendra, Jaṭāyu, Tulādhāra, Dharma-vyādha, Kubjā, the** ***gopīs*** **in Vṛndāvana and the wives of thebrāhmaṇaswho were performing sacrifice. SB 11.12.3-6**

*Daiteyā* also mans Dānavas and other demons. *Yātudhānā*means Rākṣasas. Some examples are given. Tvāṣṭrā was the demon Vṛtra. In his previous life he had association with Nārada, Aṅgirā and Saṅkarṣaṇa. Kāyādhavaḥ means Pralāda, the son of Kayādhu. In the womb he had Nārada’s association. The word *ādayaḥ* indicates that examples of persons mentioned in the previous list are given in order. Vṛṣa-parvā was a Dānava. As soon as he was born he was given up by his mother and raised by a sage. He became a devotee of Viṣṇu. This is stated in another Purāṇa.

Bali had association of Prahlāda and Vāmana. Only after that association did his bhakti manifest. Bāṇa had association of Bali, Śiva and the Lord. After his arms were severed he recognized the greatness of Viṣṇu andhe attained the great devotee Śiva. Attaining the devotee is included in *mat-padaṁ prāptāḥ:* they attained me. Maya was a Dānava. He attained association of the Pāṇḍavas and the Lord when he built the assembly hall. Later he attained the Lord. Vibhīṣaṇa was a Yātudhāna. He had association of Hanumān and the Lord.

Sugrīva, Hanumān, Ṛkṣa and Gajendra are animals. Ṛkṣa means Jāmbavān. He had the Lord’s association. Gajendra in his previouslife had devotee association. In the nest life he attained the Lord’s association. Gṛdhdra refers to Jaṭāyu who was a bird. He had the association of Garuḍa and Daśaratha. He saw Sītā and Rāma. Examples of Gandharvas and others are not given since they are not too well known. Among humans the vaiśya is illustrated with Tulādhāra, a merchant. In Mahābhārata he is inferred to have had devotee association and is glorified in relation to the story of Jājāli and a Gandharva. Vyādha means Dharma-vyāda who was a śūdra or antyaja (outcaste).

The story is found in Ādivarāha Purāṇa. In a previous kali-yuga, in a previous life, a brāhmaṇa was killed bya king named Vasu, who was a devotee, mistaking him for a deer. The brāhmaṇa later became a brahma-rākṣasa. When the king attained Vaikuṇṭha in the material world, that rākṣasa entered his body. When the King returned to earth after his enjoyment was finished and attained a king’ṣ body again, the rākṣasa was released from his body by the power of the king’s recitation of a prayer called Brahma-pāra. The king named him Dharma-vyādha. He was non-violent and at the end of his life seeing Jagannatha hepraised him. Embraced by the Lord he merged with the Lord.

Kubjā attained the Lord’s association and in previous life Nārada’s association. This is known from Māthura-hari-vaṁśa. Thegopīs mentioned here are ordinary gopīs (not the nityasiddhas). Through marriage they came to Vraja. There they attained association of the nitya-siddha gopīs, andattained Kṛṣṇa’s association in the form of seeing him etc. The wives of the brāhmaṇas had association of reciters who narrated the qualities of Kṛṣṇa and later attained his association. The word *apare* at the end of the verse indicates the sons of Diti and others.

## Anuccheda 240

Without any other sādhana except association, these persons attained the Lord:

*te nādhīta-śruti-gaṇā nopāsita-mahattamāḥ*  
*avratātapta-tapasaḥ mat-saṅgān mām upāgatāḥ*

**The persons I have mentioned did not undergo serious studies of the Vedic literature, nor did they worship great saintly persons, nor did they execute severe vows or austerities. Simply by association with my devotees, they achieved me. SB 11.12.7**

They did not study the śrutis (*nādhītāḥ).* Consequently they did not worship great saints. They did not do any vratas or austerities. These acts were for pleasing the Lord. But they did not perform them. Sādhana performed in a previous life in the case of Vṛtra and others was also a result of previous association of devotees. Dharma-vyāda and others however attained perfection only by association.

*Mat-saṅgāt*means from association with me or with my devotees. In either case there is a relationship with the Lord*. Sat-saṅga* also means association with the Lord since the Lord is also *sat* or saintly. Previously it was said that only by association of devotees can one attain the Lord’s mercy. This means that the person becomes inclined to the Lord. In the above verses it is stated that the association of devotees is a special sādhana. This is not a fault. Though sometimes the Lord’s association is the cause of developing a favorable attitude to the Lord, it should be explained as follows. When the Lord sometimes appears as avatāra, gives his association and spreads mercy everywhere directly, that takes place in relation to devotees. (He appears mainly to please the devotees.) That is the meaning of *sat* (devotee). Thus there is no contradiction.

## Anuccheda 241

An example of primary control of the Lord is the gopīs who had devotee association alone, without any other sādhana.

*kevalena hi bhāvena gopyo gāvo nagā mṛgāḥ*  
*ye ’nye mūḍha-dhiyo nāgāḥ siddhā mām īyur añjasā*

**The inhabitants of Vṛndāvana, including the gopīs, cows, mountains, animals, living entities with stunted consciousness such as bushes and thickets, and snakes such as Kāliya, all achieved the perfection of life by unalloyed love for me and thus very easily achieved me. SB 11.12.8**

*Bhāvena*means “by prīti which arose only by association with me (according to the subject under discussion).” Bhāva is a symptom of primary control.

*mayi nirbaddha-hṛdayāḥ sādhavaḥ sama-darśanāḥ*  
*vaśe kurvanti māṁ bhaktyā sat-striyaḥ sat-patiṁ yathā*

**As chaste women bring their gentle husbands under control by service, the pure devotees, who see others’ suffering as their own and are completely attached to me in the core of the heart, bring me under their full control. SB 9.4.66**

*bhaktyāham ekayā grāhyaḥ śraddhayātmā priyaḥ satām*  
*bhaktiḥ punāti man-niṣṭhā śva-pākān api sambhavāt*
