**Only by bhaktiwith full faith can the devotees obtain me, Paramātmā, the object of love. Bhakti fixed in me purifies even a dog eater of his low birth. SB 11.14.20**

The cows, like these gopīs, were new comers to Vraja. Trees were Yamalārjuna trees. Animals were previously mentioned. Snakes were Kāliya and others. The attainment of Yamalārjuna trees and Kāliya was momentary at the time of Kṛṣṇa’s earthly pastimes, but it should be understood that later they attained the Lord permanently.

They attained perfection *(siddhāḥ*) by either association of the Lord or his devotees. They could not attain bhāva by yoga or other means. *Yathāvarundhe sat-saṅgaḥ* (other processes cannot attain me as much as association of devotees does.)*Yathā* indicates that association leads to the highest attainment, bhāva.

## Anuccheda 242

This is explained:

*yaṁ na yogena sāṅkhyena dāna-vrata-tapo-’dhvaraiḥ*  
*vyākhyā-svādhyāya-sannyāsaiḥ prāpnuyād yatnavān api*

**But I cannot be attained by intense efforts of yoga, Sāṅkhya, charity, vows, austerity, sacrifices, explaining the Vedas, study of the Vedas, or sannyāsa. SB 11.12.9**

One does not attain that bhāva (*yam*) by yoga etc.even if they are linked to the Lord, even if those methods are executed with effort. From this effort it is understood that these methods are employed to attain the Lord. Among those who attained the Lord, the gopīs, are the most exalted. This can be understood from other sections of the work. That they attained the highest position is indicated as the greatest secret:

*athaitat paramaṁ guhyaṁ śṛṇvato yadu-nandana*  
*su-gopyam api vakṣyāmi tvaṁ me bhṛtyaḥ suhṛt sakhā*

**O beloved of the Yadu dynasty! Because you are my servant, well-wisher and friend, I shall now speak to you who listen well the supreme secret. SB 11.11.49**

The highest attainment should be understood starting in the next verse:

*rāmeṇa sārdhaṁ mathurāṁ praṇīte*  
*śvāphalkinā mayy anurakta-cittāḥ*  
*vigāḍha-bhāvena na me viyoga-*  
*tīvrādhayo ’nyaṁ dadṛśuḥ sukhāya*

**The gopīs were always completely attached to me with the deepest love. Therefore, when my uncle Akrūra brought my brother Balarāma and me to the city of Mathurā, suffering extrememental distress because of separation from me, they could not find any other source of happiness. SB 11.12.10**

The Lord speaks.

## Anuccheda 243

Association with devotees even without knowing is valuable.

*saṅgo yaḥ saṁsṛter hetur asatsu vihito 'dhiyā*  
*sa eva sādhuṣu kṛto niḥsaṅgatvāya kalpate*

**Association with materialists done in ignorance is a cause of bondage and association with devotees done while unaware leads to liberation. SB 3.23.5.**

*Adhiyā* means “without knowing.” Previously it was said that regarding Nārada or other devotees to be ordinary sages is offensive. That was in reference to people who were not affectionate and had developed wrong concepts because of a little knowledge. Devahūti speaks.

## Anuccheda 244

The results of association with mahābhāgavatas is described. The result of serving them is as follows:

*yat-sevayā bhagavataḥ kūṭa-sthasya madhu-dviṣaḥ*  
*rati-rāso bhavet tīvraḥ pādayor vyasanārdanaḥ*

**By service to the devotees, intense rasa of bhāva-bhakti to the Lord who is fixed in one form and destroys obstacles for the devotee appears, and destruction of material suffering then takes place. SB 3.7.19**

By serving great devotees a festival of prema (*rati-rasaḥ*) for the feet of the eternal *(kūṭasthasya*) Lord develops. The word *tīvraḥ* (intense) indicates the result of serving is superior to just association. The accompanying result is described: saṁsāra *(vyasana* )is destroyed. It was said *mad-bhakta-pūjābhyadhikā:* worshipping my devotee pleases me*more (abhyadhikāḥ)* than worshipping me. (SB 11.19.19)

*ārādhanānāṁ sarveṣāṁ viṣṇor ārādhanaṁ param \|*  
*tasmāt parataraṁ devi tadīyānāṁ samarcanam \|\|*

**O Devī! Worshipping Viṣṇu is superior to worshipping all others. Superior to that is worshipping my devotees. Padma Purāṇa 6.253.176**

Vidura speaks to Maitreya.

## Anuccheda 245

This is expressed in the opposite way:

*yasyātma-buddhiḥ kuṇape tri-dhātuke*  
*sva-dhīḥ kalatrādiṣu bhauma ijya-dhīḥ*  
*yat-tīrtha-buddhiḥ salile na karhicij*  
*janeṣv abhijñeṣu sa eva go-kharaḥ*

**One who identifies his self as the inert body composed of mucus, bile and air, who assumes his wife and family are permanently his own, who thinks an earthen image is worshipable, or who sees a place of pilgrimage as merely the water there, but who never identifies himself with, feels kinship with, worships or even visits those who are wise in spiritual truth—such a person is no better than a cow or an ass.**

The body is like a corpse (*kuṇape*), like earth, since it is unconscious. Even combined with consciousness it is still contaminated by the vāta, pitta and kapha (*tridhātuke).Bhaume*means images of devatās. If his *ātmā buddhi, sva-dhīḥ, ijya-dhīḥ* and *tīrtha-buddhiḥ* are not directed to the wise (*abhijñeṣu*), he is lower than a cow (*gokharaḥ).Ātma-buddhiḥ*means one’s awareness of the object of greatest affection*. Gokhara* also refers to a species wild donkey well known in Sindhu-sauvīra province. It also refers to a class of mlecchas. He is nothing else other than that. Though he thinks himself intelligent, because he is not intelligent, he is lower than a donkey.

He thinks that earthen forms of devatās are worthy of worship since that worship was mentioned in previous texts. It does not refer to deities of Viṣṇu since it is said *arcāyāṁ eva haraye*: the kaniṣṭha devotee worships the Lordin the deity. (SB 11.2.45) As well the following verse should be remembered:

*yathā taror mūla-niṣecanena*  
*tṛpyanti tat-skandha-bhujopaśākhāḥ*  
*prāṇopahārāc ca yathendriyāṇāṁ*  
*tathaiva sarvārhaṇam acyutejyā*

**Just as the branches and twigs are satisfied by watering the root, and just as the senses are satisfied by nourishing the life air, by worshipping the Supreme Lord all are worship is accomplished. SB 4.31.14**

The Lord speaks to the sages.

## Anuccheda 246

Perfection through serving mahā-bhāgavatas is described:

*te na smaranty atitarāṁ priyam īśa martyaṁ*  
*ye cānv adaḥ suta-suhṛd-gṛha-vitta-dārāḥ*  
*ye tv abja-nābha bhavadīya-padāravinda-*  
*saugandhya-lubdha-hṛdayeṣu kṛta-prasaṅgāḥ*

**O Lord with lotus navel! Those who have taken association with those whose hearts have smelled the fragrance of your lotus feet do not care for this very dear material body or the things accompanying it, such as sons, friends, house, wealth and wife. SB 4.9.12**

They do not care for this body *(martyam*) though it is very dear to most people. They do not remember things related to the body (*anu adaḥ)* such as sons, friends, house, wealth or wife. Who are they? They are those who have associated with devotees. Dhruva speaks to the Lord.

## Anuccheda 247

One should worship suitably any Vaiṣṇava:

*tasmād viṣṇu-prasādāya vaiṣṇavān paritoṣayet \|*  
*prasāda-sumukho viṣṇus tenaiva syān na saṁśayaḥ \|\|*

**Therefore one should please the Vaiṣṇavas to please Viṣṇu. Viṣṇu will be pleased by this. There is no doubt. Itihāsa-samuccaya**

*sarvatrāskhalitādeśaḥ sapta-dvīpaika-daṇḍa-dhṛk*  
*anyatra brāhmaṇa-kulād anyatrācyuta-gotrataḥ*

**His order went unchallenged everywhere. He had authority over all except the brāhmaṇas and Vaiṣṇavas. SB 4.21.12**

From the story of Pṛthu it is understood that the Vaiṣṇava is in the highest position even if his caste is low (since he is designated as acyuta-gotra, family of the Lord). Nārada states this principle (qualities over birth) by an example (caste designation):

*yasya yal lakṣaṇaṁ proktaṁ puṁso varṇābhivyañjakam*  
*yad anyatrāpi dṛśyeta tat tenaiva vinirdiśet*

**If anyone shows the symptoms of being a brāhmaṇa, kṣatriya, vaiśya or śūdra, as described above, even if he has born in a different class, he should be designated according to those symptoms. SB 7.11.35**

*śvapākam iva nekṣeta loke vipram avaiṣṇavam \|*  
*vaiṣṇavo varṇa-bāhyo’pi punāti bhuvana-trayam \|\|*  
*na śūdrā bhagavad-bhaktās te tu bhāgavatā narāḥ \|*  
*sarva-varṇeṣu te śūdrā ye na bhaktā janārdane \|\|*

**One should not consider abrāhmaṇa who is not a devotee to be even at the level of a dog eater. A Vaiṣṇava, even outside of the caste system, purifies the three worlds. The devotees of the Lord are not śūdras but men of the Lord. O Janārdana! Those who are not devotees in all castes are śūdras. Padma Purāṇa**

*smṛtaḥ sambhāṣito vāpi pūjito vā dvijottama \|*  
*punāti bhagavad-bhaktaś cāṇḍālo’pi yadṛcchayā \|\|*

**O best of the brāhmaṇas! The devotees of the Lord, even a Cāṇḍala by birth, whether remembered, spoken to, or worshipped, cause purification spontaneously. Itihāsa-samucchaya**

To see otherwise is a fault:

*śūdraṁ vā bhagavad-bhaktaṁ niṣādaṁ śvapacaṁ tathā \|*  
*vīkṣate jāti-sāmānyāt sa yāti narakaṁ dhruvam \|\|*

**He who considers a devotee to be a śūdra, niśāda, or dog eater because of low caste goes to hell for certain.**

Because of bhakti, a person is special:

*mad-bhakta-jana-vātsalyaṁ pūjāyāṁ cānumodanam \|*  
*mat-kathā-śravaṇe prītiḥ svara-netrādi-vikriyā \|\|*  
*viṣṇoś ca kāraṇaṁ nṛtyaṁ tad-arthe dambha-varjanam \|*  
*svayam abhyarcanaṁ caiva yo viṣṇuṁ nopajīvati \|\|*  
*bhaktir aṣṭa-vidhā hy eṣā yasmin mlecche’pi vartate \|*  
*sa viprendro muni-śreṣṭhaḥ sa jñānī sa ca paṇḍitaḥ \|*  
*tasmai deyaṁ tato grāhyaṁ sa ca pūjyo yathā hariḥ \|\|*

**Bhakti has eight symptoms: affection for other devotees, joy when the Lord is worshipped, fondness for hearing about me, symptoms like choking of the voice and tears, dancing for the Lord, giving up deceit for the Lord, worshipping the Lord, not using the Lord to make a living. If these qualities are found in a mleccah, he is the best brāhmaṇa, the best sage, a jñānī and paṇḍita. One should give to him and receive from him. He is to be worshipped like the Lord. Garuḍa Purāṇa**

*na me bhaktaś catur-vedī mad-bhaktaḥ śvapacaḥ priyaḥ \|*  
*tasmai deyaṁ tato grāhyaṁ sa ca pūjyo yathā hy aham \|\|*

**If someone merely knows the four Vedas he is not my devotee. A dog eater who is my devotee is dear to me. One should give to him and receive from him. He is to be worshipped like me.**

Being endowed with the greatness of bhakti, Durvāsa grasped Ambarīṣa’s feet. Ambarīṣa however was not pleased. This is explained in the text. Since Kṛṣṇa and Uddhava also offered respects to anyone who was a brāhmaṇa, Vaiṣṇavas who are not brāhmaṇas never consider receiving respect from brāhmaṇas. Doing so would disobey the order of the Lord:

*vipraṁ kṛtāgasam api naiva druhyata māmakāḥ*  
*ghnantaṁ bahu śapantaṁ vā namas-kuruta nityaśaḥ*

**My dear followers, never treat a learned brāhmaṇa harshly, even if he has sinned. Even if he attacks you physically or repeatedly curses you, always continue to offer him obeisances. SB 10.64.41**

The above statement that a brāhmaṇa who is not a Vaiṣṇava is not even a dog eater means that one should not be attached to seeing such a person. One should see the respectfulbehavior of Yudhiṣṭhiraand Draupadī towards Aśvatthāmā.

But those who worship Vaiṣṇavas should not consider the conduct of the Vaiṣṇava, for it is said *api cet sudurācāraḥ*: even if the devotee has sinned he is still my devotee. (BG 9.30)

*viṣṇu-bhakti-samāyukto mithyācāro’py anāśramī \|*  
*punāti sakalān lokān sahasrāṁśur ivoditaḥ \|\|*

**A person endowed with bhakti, even though having bad conduct or no āśrama, purifies all the worlds like the sun rising. Garuḍa Purāṇa**

Here is an example:

*aho bata śva-paco 'to garīyān*  
*yaj-jihvāgre vartate nāma tubhyam*  
*tepus tapas te juhuvuḥ sasnur āryā*  
*brahmānūcur nāma gṛṇanti ye te*

**How astonishing! The outcaste on the tip of whose tongue your name appears becomes the guru! All those who chant your name, becoming most respectable, have completed all austerities, all sacrifices, all bathing and all study of the Vedas. SB 3.33.7**

The word *śvapacaḥ* is given the etymological meaning: one who eats dogs. The devotee should not be disrespected even if he comes from low caste or had bad conduct (like eating dogs).

One should not disrespect a person who does not offer respects to you.

*rukṣākṣaraṁ tu śṛṇvan vai tathā bhāgavateritam \|*  
*praṇāma-parvaṁ taṁ kṣāntyā yo vaded vaiṣṇavo hi saḥ \|\|*

**He is a Vaiṣṇava who after hearing harsh words spoken by another devotee offers respects to him and speaks to pacify him. Garuḍa Purāṇa**

Service to mahats has been shown. This takes place before hearing and other activities.

*mahat-sevāṁ dvāram āhur vimuktes*  
*tamo-dvāraṁ yoṣitāṁ saṅgi-saṅgam*  
*mahāntas te sama-cittāḥ praśāntā*  
*vimanyavaḥ suhṛdaḥ sādhavo ye*

**Service to saintly persons is the cause of two types of liberation, whereas association with men who associate with women is the cause of bondage. The impersonalist sees no differences and is peaceful whereas the devotee has a sincere heart and his intelligence is fixed in me. Both types of saintly persons are devoid of anger, are friendly to all and do not see faults in others. SB 5.5.2**

From these great devotees indescribable auspiciousness arises.

*teṣu nityaṁ mahā-bhāga mahā-bhāgeṣu mat-kathāḥ*  
*sambhavanti hi tā nṝṇāṁ juṣatāṁ prapunanty agham*

**O greatly fortunate Uddhava! In the association of such saintly devotees there arises constant discussion of me, and those topics destroy the sins of the speaker and listener.**

*tā ye śṛṇvanti gāyanti hy anumodanti cādṛtāḥ*  
*mat-parāḥ śraddadhānāś ca bhaktiṁ vindanti te mayi*

**Whoever attentively hears, chants and relishes these topics about me becomes faithfully dedicated to me and achievesbhakti to me.**

*bhaktiṁ labdhavataḥ sādhoḥ kim anyad avaśiṣyate*  
*mayy ananta-guṇe brahmaṇy ānandānubhavātmani*

**What more remains to be accomplished for the perfect devotee after achieving prema for me, with unlimitedqualities in prema, accompanied by the bliss of liberation in Brahman?**

*yathopaśrayamāṇasya bhagavantaṁ vibhāvasum*  
*śītaṁ bhayaṁ tamo ’pyeti sādhūn saṁsevatas tathā*

**Just as cold, fear and darkness are eradicated for one who has approached a fire, so apathy, fear and ignorance are destroyed for one engaged in serving the devotees of the Lord. SB 11.26.28-31**

The qualities of the such devotees are as follows:

*santo ’napekṣā mac-cittāḥ praśāntāḥ sama-darśinaḥ*  
*nirmamā nirahaṅkārā nirdvandvā niṣparigrahāḥ*

**My devotees fix their minds on me and do not depend upon anything material. They are always peaceful, endowed with equal vision, and free from possessiveness, false ego, duality and attachment. SB 11.26.27**

The word bhakti in verse 30 means prema.

*kṣaṇārdhenāpi tulaye na svargaṁ nāpunar-bhavam*  
*bhagavat-saṅgi-saṅgasya martyānāṁ kim utāśiṣaḥ*

**What to speak of any blessings on this earth, or on Svarga, even the blessing of liberation cannot compare with even half a moment’s association with the devotee of the Lord. SB 4.24.57**

*tulayāma lavenāpi na svargaṁ nāpunar-bhavam \|*  
*bhagavat-saṅgi-saṅgasya martyānāṁ kim utāśiṣaḥ \|\|*

**Let us not compare even a particle of devotee association to Svarga or liberation, what to speak of any blessings in this world. SB 1.18.13**

Accompanying results are also stated in verse 31. *Vibhāvasum* means fire. A person approaching (*upaśrayamānasya*), thinking it worthy of worship, lights a fire for performing sacrifice. But it also removes cold, fear of other wicked living entities, and darkness. Serving devotees destroys being trapped in the inertness of karma (like cold), fear of bad future in saṁsāra, and ignorance the root of saṁsāra (like darkness). The Lord speaks.

Sravaṇam

## Anuccheda 248

The next aṅga of bhakti is hearing. This refers to touching the ear with words denoting the name, form, qualities and pastimes of the Lord. Hearing the name is as follows:

*na hi bhagavann aghaṭitam idaṁ*  
*tvad-darśanān nṛṇām akhila-pāpa-kṣayaḥ*  
*yan-nāma sakṛc chravaṇāt*  
*pukkaśo 'pi vimucyate saṁsārāt*

**O Lord! Nothing is impossible for you. Just from seeing you, all my sins have been destroyed. By hearing one of your names once, even the outcaste is delivered from the material world. SB 6.16.44**

The name is such that when an outcaste hears it once, he attains liberation. An advanced devotee on hearing attains the highest bhakti. Citraketu speaks to Saṅkarṣaṇa.

## Anuccheda 249

Hearing about the form of the Lord is illustrated:

*ye tu tvadīya-caraṇāmbuja-kośa-gandhaṁ*  
*jighranti karṇa-vivaraiḥ śruti-vāta-nītam*  
*bhaktyā gṛhīta-caraṇaḥ parayā ca teṣāṁ*  
*nāpaiṣi nātha hṛdayāmburuhāt sva-puṁsām*

**O master! You do not give up the lotus hearts of your devotees who smell through their ears the fragrance of your lotus bud feet brought by the wind of the Vedas, and take your lotus feet as the greatest treasure through prema-bhakti. SB 3.9.5**

The previous verse criticized those who do not revere the form of the Lord. *Anuvidhema tubhyaṁ*

*yo ’nādṛto naraka-bhāgbhir asat-prasaṅgaiḥ: ’nuvidhema tubhyaṁ yo ’nādṛto naraka-bhāgbhir asat-prasaṅgaiḥ:* let me only serve you, who are not respected by those who will go to hell, proclaiming that your personal form is false. (SB 3.9.4) The word *tu* in the present verse indicates a contrast to the previous persons. Thus those who oppose hearing are the asat persons mentioned in the previous verse. Taking the lotus feet indicates the intensity of their bhakti. Just as one relishes fragrance through the nostrils, they relish through the ears the sweetness of the syllables (*gandham),* attained on the wind of the Veda-- meaning other words following the Vedas. With your feet bound by the highest bhakti, prema, you cannot leave their hearts.

Brahmā speaks to Garbhodaśāyī.

## Anuccheda 250

Hearing about the qualities is illustrated:

*kathā imās te kathitā mahīyasāṁ*  
*vitāya lokeṣu yaśaḥ pareyuṣām*  
*vijñāna-vairāgya-vivakṣayā vibho*  
*vaco-vibhūtīr na tu pāramārthyam*

**O mighty Parīkṣit! I have related to you the narrations of all these great person who perished, who spread their fame throughout the world, with a desire to speak about their renunciation and their realization of the Lord. This was just a play of words, not the highest spiritual knowledge.**

*yas tūttamaḥ-śloka-guṇānuvādaḥ*  
*saṅgīyate 'bhīkṣṇam amaṅgala-ghnaḥ*  
*tam eva nityaṁ śṛṇuyād abhīkṣṇaṁ*  
*kṛṣṇe 'malāṁ bhaktim abhīpsamānaḥ*

**The person who desires pure bhaktito Kṛṣṇa should daily hear the narrations of the Lord’s glorious qualities, the constant chanting of which destroys everything inauspicious. SB12.3.14-15**

The commentary says, “The goal of narrating the dynasties of kings is explained. *Vijñānam*means knowledge about the uselessness of material things. These stories were recited with a desire to speak of knowledge of the uselessness of material things and of renunciation. These stories of dead men (*pareyuṣām*) are amere play of words. They were not related to the spiritual goal. What is that spiritual goal which is best for men? The next verse answers. One should daily hear the narrations and at all times during the day.”

Though Rāma and Lakṣmaṇa are included among the kings for showing renunciation, they are included only in the manner of the crowd of umbrella bearers (some of whom are not carrying umbrellas), since their stories illustrate more than that. Though everything in the Bhāgavatam should relate to rasa, since it is the highest sweetest fruit on the desire tree of the Vedas (SB 1.1.3), sometimes there is direct bhakti with various rasas starting with śānta and sometimes there is assistants for those rasas. (Thus stories of kings are there to encourage distaste for material rasa.) There are also degrees in the bhakti-rasas in the Bhāgavatam (not just the highest rasa).

*Guṇa* refers to the Lord’s qualities like compassion. The nature of singing about his qualities is to produce joy:

*sthāne hṛṣīkeśa tava prakīrtyā*  
*jagat prahṛṣyaty anurajyate ca \|*

**O controller of the senses! Rightly, the world rejoices and becomes attracted to you by this celebrated form. BG 11.36**

One should hear about the qualities of the great devotees just as one hears about the Lord. Śaunaka says:

*tat kathyatāṁ mahā-bhāga yadi kṛṣṇa-kathāśrayam \|\|*  
*athavāsya padāmbhoja- makaranda-lihāṁ satām \|*

**O great soul! Please tell us if it concerns Kṛṣṇa, or concerns his devotees who lick the honey from his lotus feet. SB 1.16.6**

Though by the word *guṇa* (quality) incude the beauty of the Lord’s form and pastimes, the narration of the qualities is particularly singled out to indicate verses where his qualities are most prominent. This should be kept in mind in later discussions also. One who desires pure prema (*bhaktim)*devoid of desire for liberation etc. (*amalām*) should hear constantly, daily. Śukadeva speaks.

## Anuccheda 251

Moreoverit is said:

*yatrottamaśloka-guṇānuvādaḥ*  
*prastūyate grāmya-kathā-vighātaḥ*  
*niṣevyamāṇo 'nudinaṁ mumukṣor*  
*matiṁ satīṁ yacchati vāsudeve*

**Where there are great devotees, talks concerning the qualities of the Lord, which destroy material topics, are sung. Heard constantly even by those desiring liberation, those topics produce pure thoughts of Vāsudeva. SB 5.12.13**

If persons desiring liberation will develop pure thoughts of Vāsudeva, how much more will persons desiring bhakti develop thoughts of the Lord. *Satīm* means the thoughts are devoid of other desires like liberation. Other desires are distracting. The brāhmaṇa Jaḍa-bharata speaks to Rahūgaṇa.

## Anuccheda 252

*nivṛtta-tarṣair upagīyamānād*  
*bhavauṣadhāc chrotra-mano-’bhirāmāt*  
*ka uttamaśloka-guṇānuvādāt*  
*pumān virajyeta vinā paśughnāt*

**Except for a killer, what person would ever cease hearing glorification of the qualities of the famous Lord, sung profusely by liberated souls, by persons desiring relief from material suffering, and by persons desiring pleasure for the ear and mind? SB 10.1.4**

Three types of people, the liberated, those desiring liberation and those desiring material enjoyment sing about the Lord. Only a killer of animals or hunter (*paśu-ghnaḥ*) would not hear. The killer is described as follows:

*rāja-putra ciraṁ jīva mā jīva muni-putraka \|*  
*jīva vā mara vā sādho vyādho mā jīva mā mara \|\|*

**May the prince live forever. May the son of the sage not live. May the devotee live or die. The killer should not live and should not die.**

Thus there is point in enjoying in the material world. The killer of animals has no understanding of the Lord, particularly no understanding of rasa of the Lord’s topics. Because he is most foolish, he has no capacity for it at all.

Or, *paśu-ghnaḥ* may refer to a person of demonic nature, who simply criticizes others. Because of his violent nature he is called a killer of animals. Taking the meaning to be a hunter, a hunter does not appreciating the beauty of the animals and simply kills them. Since such a person cannot appreciate rasa, it is appropriate that the verse says “Except for the hunter who would not hear the topics of the Lord?” In either case the phrase is a criticism of persons averse to the Lord.

*ko nāma loke puruṣārtha-sāravit*  
*purā-kathānāṁ bhagavat-kathā-sudhām*  
*āpīya karṇāñjalibhir bhavāpahām*  
*aho virajyeta vinā naretaram*

**Using his ears as hands to take the nectar, knowing the essence of all human goals to be bhakti, having drunk the sweet stories of the Lord among all the past narrations, which destroy material existence, what person would then reject them? Only an animal would reject them! SB 3.13.50**

The king speaks to Śukadeva.

## Anuccheda 253

Now hearing about the pastimes will be described:

*jñānaṁ yad āpratinivṛtta-guṇormi-cakram*  
*āt ma-prasāda uta yatra guṇeṣv asaṅgaḥ \|*  
*kaivalya-sammata-pathas tv atha bhakti-yogaḥ*  
*ko nirvṛto hari-kathāsu ratiṁ na kuryāt \|\|*

**Knowledge of the Lord’s pastimes, creates indifference to the multitude of material guṇas, arises and is followed by satisfaction of the self, which creates complete detachment from the guṇas. By the path of kaivalya approved for attaining the Lord, prema should arise. Experiencing bliss in the topics*****,*** **how can that person not develop raga for the Lord? SB 2.3.12**

Knowledge about the Lord’s pastimes arises. What does the knowledge do? From that knowledge, all material attractions *(guṇormi-cakram)* cease.Because in those topics, because of those topics one feels satisfaction of the ātmā, and from that satisfaction, one becomes detached from material enjoyment. The result of that detachment is kaivalya. This is stated in Gītā:

*brahma-bhūtaḥ prasannātmā na śocati na kāṅkṣati*  
*samaḥ sarveṣu bhūteṣu mad-bhaktiṁ labhate parām \|\|*

**Having attained realization of ātmā, being a pure soul, he does not lament for or desire anything except me, and looks upon all other beings as equally inferior. He attains bhakti in the form of realizing me. BG 18.54**

Prema (bhakti) has this kaivalya approved as path towards prema (*kaivalya-sammata-patha*). Being joyful with the happiness of hearing the pastimes of the Lord, not experiencing joy elsewhere, without even expecting such results, who would not develop raga? Śukadeva speaks.

## Anuccheda 254

What more can be said? For this purpose the Bhāgavatam appeared.

*bhavatānudita-prāyaṁ yaśo bhagavato ’malam \|*  
*yenaivāsau na tuṣyeta manye tad darśanaṁ khilam \|\|*

**You have not sufficiently described the glories of the spotless Lord Kṛṣṇa. Because your mind could never be satisfied with Vedānta, I think that writing the Vedānta-sūtras is insufficient. SB 1.5.8**

*atho mahā-bhāga bhavān amogha-dṛk*  
*śuci-śravāḥ satya-rato dhṛta-vrataḥ \|*  
*urukramasyākhila-bandha-muktaye*  
*samādhinānusmara tad-viceṣṭitam \|\|*

**Fortunate one! Therefore, since you have useful knowledge, spotless character, dedication to truth and determined vows, remember with attention the pastimes of the Lord which bring about liberation from all bondage. SB 1.5.13**

There are two types of pastimes: those describing actions like creation of the universe and those describing the activities of the līlāvatāras. The second type are superior:

*prādhānyato yān ṛṣa āmananti*  
*līlāvatārān puruṣasya bhūmnaḥ \|*  
*āpīyatāṁ karṇa-kaṣāya-śoṣān*  
*anukramiṣye ta imān supeśān \|\|*

**I will now speak in order about the beautiful līlāvatāras of the Supreme Lord, whose topics dry up the desire to hear anything else and which the sages glorify for their valuable content. This should be drunk. SB 2.6.46**

Though I have spoken about the puruṣa (SB 2.6.40), his śaktis like time, his effects like the mind, the guṇāvatāras, and vibhūtis like Dakṣa, all constituent the pastimes of creation, I will speak in sequence about the līlāvatāras of the Lord whom the sages principally glorify and who destroy attraction to hearing about anything else (*karṇa-kaśāya-śoṣān*—drying up the contamination of the ear) by entering the heart, and who are most attractive (*supeśām*). They should be drunk completely (*āpītayām*). Brahmā speaks to Nārada.

## Anuccheda 255

One should also see the praise for pastimes given by the personified Vedas:

*duravagamātma-tattva-nigamāya tavātta-tano.*  
*carita-mahāmṛtābdhi-parivarta-pariśramaṇāḥ*  
*na parilaṣanti kecid apavargam apīśvara t.*  
*caraṇa-saroja-haṁsa-kula-saṅga-visṛṣṭa-gṛhāḥ*

**My Lord, some fortunate souls have gotten relief from the fatigue of material life by diving into the vast nectar ocean of your pastimes, which you enact when you manifest your personal forms to propagate your form, qualities and pastimes. These rare souls, indifferent even to liberation, renounce the happiness of home and family because of their association with devotees who are like flocks of swans enjoying the lotus of your feet. SB 10.87.21**

Details are given:

*bhāvayaty eṣa sattvena lokān vai loka-bhāvanaḥ \|*  
*līlāvatārānurato deva-tiryaṅ-narādiṣu \|\|*

**The Lord, creator of the universe, absorbing himself in the forms of various līlāvatāras as devatās, animals and humans, protects the worlds through his role as the controller of sattva-guṇa. SB 1.2.34**

Bhagavad-gītā says:

*janma karma ca me divyam evaṁ yo vetti tattvataḥ \|*  
*tyaktvā dehaṁ punar janma naiti mām eti so ’rjuna \|\|*

**He who understands in truth my spiritual birth and activities attains me on giving up his body, and does not take birth again. BG 4.9**

The pastimes arrange for a person with material body to conquer death, by having the person take on the mood of an associate.

*sādhu vīra tvayā pṛṣṭam avatāra-kathāṁ hareḥ*  
*yat tvaṁ pṛcchasi martyānāṁ mṛtyu-pāśa-viśātanīm*

**O warrior! You have inquired well about the story of the appearance of the Lord. What you ask is the cause of freedom from the rope of death for all human beings.**

*yayottānapadaḥ putro muninā gītayārbhakaḥ*  
*mṛtyoḥ kṛtvaiva mūrdhny aṅghrim āruroha hareḥ padam*

**By hearing Nārada recite this story of the Lord when he was young, Dhruva, the son of Uttānapada, placing his foot upon death’s head, ascended to the abode of the Lord. SB 3.14.5-6**

Nārada (*muninā*) spoke to Dhruva. It is understood that Nārada made Dhruva hear stories about the Lord’s avatāras. In his same body he conquered death and attained the position of an associate:

*parītyābhyarcya dhiṣṇyāgryaṁ pārṣadāv abhivandya ca*  
*iyeṣa tad adhiṣṭhātuṁ bibhrad rūpaṁ hiraṇmayam*

**Circumambulating and worshipping the plane, offering respects to the two associates, assuming a golden form, he desired to board the plane. SB 4.12.29**

Maitreya speaks.

## Anuccheda 256

Hearing the names, qualities and pastimes of the Lord has been described. Hearing about the Lord’s associates should also be included.

*śrutasya puṁsāṁ sucira-śramasya*  
*nanv añjasā sūribhir īḍito ’rthaḥ*  
*tat-tad-guṇānuśravaṇaṁ mukunda-*  
*pādāravindaṁ hṛdayeṣu yeṣām*

**The goal of a person engaged in hearing scripture from the guru for a long time, praised by the great devotees, is hearing the glories of devotees in whose hearts Mukunda resides. SB 3.13.4**

Though one can attain perfection by hearing the name, forms, qualities and pastimes in any order, one should first hear the name in order to purify the antaḥkaraṇa. When the antaḥkaraṇa is pure one develops qualification for hearing about the form. When the form has been manifested fully, the qualities then manifest. When the name, form, qualities and associates manifest completely the pastimes manifest nicely. This is the sequence of sādhana that has been recorded. The same sequence applies to chanting and remembering.

If hearing takes place from the mouths of great devotees, there is great power manifested and the highest bliss for those who have developed taste. Hearing from the mouths of great devotes has two types: hearing something composed by a great devotee and hearing some scripture from the mouth of a great devotee. Here is an example of the first type, using Bhāgavatam:

*idaṁ bhāgavataṁ nāma purāṇaṁ brahma-sammitam \|*  
*uttama-śloka-caritaṁ cakāra bhagavān ṛṣiḥ \|*  
*niḥśreyasāya lokasya dhanyaṁ svasty-ayanaṁ mahat \|\|*

**The Supreme Lord Vyāsa wrote the greatest, most auspicious Purāṇa called Bhāgavatam, which bestows all human goals, is non-different from Kṛṣṇa, and describes the activities and qualities of the Lord, for the highest benefit of the world. SB 1.3.40**

In order to signify the greatness of the author, the name of the author of Bhāgavatam is mentioned. Sūta speaks.

## Anuccheda 257

Another example of a work coming from a great devotee is as follows:

*nigama-kalpa-taror galitaṁ phalaṁ*  
*śuka-mukhād amṛta-drava-saṁyutam*  
*pibata bhāgavataṁ rasam ālayaṁ*  
*muhur aho rasikā bhuvi bhāvukāḥ*

**O knowers of rasa! O fortunate souls! Constantly drink from the mouth of Śukadeva the Bhāgavatam, the fruit of the tree of the Vedas, which has dropped from the tree to this earth, which is immortal, liquid, which is the essence of sweetness and which includes all types of liberation. SB 1.1.3**

The Bhāgavatam gives the greatest happiness because of having liquid nectar from the mouth of Śukadeva. Bhāgavatam is representing other sweet works as well such as Kṛṣṇa-karṇāmṛta by Līlā-śuka, which also give the greatest bliss.

Hearing works spoken by great devotees is illustrated:

*sa uttamaśloka mahan-mukha-cyuto*  
*bhavat-padāmbhoja-sudhā kaṇānilaḥ*  
*smṛtiṁ punar vismṛta-tattva-vartmanāṁ*  
*kuyogināṁ no vitaraty alaṁ varaiḥ*

**O most praised Lord! The topics falling from the mouths of the great devotees, or even the wind touching a drop of nectar from your lotus feet, brings remembrance even for the bad yogīs who have forgotten the truth you have just taught. Away with all these benedictions including liberation! SB 4.20.25**

The previous verse is:

*na kāmaye nātha tad apy ahaṁ kvacin*  
*na yatra yuṣmac-caraṇāmbujāsavaḥ*  
*mahattamāntar-hṛdayān mukha-cyuto*  
*vidhatsva karṇāyutam eṣa me varaḥ*

**I do not desire anything, and especially I do not want liberation, in which there is no nectar of your lotus feet, coming from the hearts of great devotees and falling from their mouths. Give me millions of ears. That is my request.SB 4.20.24**

Because of great happiness the topics flowing from the mouths of great devotees deride the happiness of liberation. The wind carrying the sound of a particle of sweetness of your lotus feet flowing from the mouth of great devotees makes us, who have forgotten you who are the highest truth (*vismṛta-tattva-vartmanām*), remember you. What is the use of other benedictions, since hearing in this way is the best method and gives the highest attainment. Pṛthu speaks to Viṣṇu.

## Anuccheda 258-259

Hearing from great devotees is powerful and bestows great bliss. Both facts are stated in two verses.

*tasmin mahan-mukharitā madhubhic-caritra-*  
*pīyūṣa-śeṣa-saritaḥ paritaḥ sravanti*  
*tā ye pibanty avitṛṣo nṛpa gāḍha-karṇais*  
*tān na spṛśanty aśana-tṛḍ-bhaya-śoka-mohāḥ*

**In devotee association, profuse streams of nectar of the pastimes of the Lord emanating from the mouths of the saintly devotees, the mahāprasāda of the devotees, flow everywhere. Those who drink that nectar with firm ears, with constant thirst, O King, will not be touched by hunger, thirst, fear, lamentation and illusion. SB 4.29.41**

*Tasmin*means “in association with devotees.” In their association, rivers of the essence of nectar of the Lord’s pastimes spoken by the great devotes (*mahan-mukharitā*) flow. Those who drink this nectar with attentive (*gāḍha*) ears, thinking they will never be fully satisfied *(avitṛṣaḥ),* are not touched by hunger (*aśana*), thirst, fear, lamentation or illusion.

*etair upadruto nityaṁjīva-lokaḥ svabhāvajaiḥ*  
*na karoti harer nūnaṁkathāmṛta-nidhau ratim*

**Those who are constantly disturbed by habitual hunger, thirst, fear, lamentation and illusion arising from one’s nature do nothave attraction for the ocean of nectar of topics concerning the Lord. SB 4.29.42**

Disturbed by hunger etc. (*etaiḥ*) a person cannot develop attraction for the ocean of nectar of the Lord’s topics. The glories of the Lord sung by the great devotees remove these disturbances by their power and make people experience the bliss of those topics. That is the meaning of the two verses together. Nārada speaks to Prācīnabarhi.

## Anuccheda 260

In hearing about the Lord, hearing Bhāgavatam is the best since its words are most powerful and it is most tasty. The power of the words is illustrated:

*śrīmad-bhāgavate mahā-muni-kṛte kiṁ vā parair īśvaraḥ*  
*sadyo hṛdy avarudhyate ’tra kṛtibhiḥ śuśrūṣubhis tat-kṣaṇāt \|\|*

**The Supreme Lord becomes immediately captured in the heart of the accomplished devotees by hearing Bhāgavatam and even of those who have suddenly developed a desire to hear it. This does not happened with other works, only in the Bhāgavatam, created by the Lord himself.What is the need of other works? SB 1.1.2**

*Mahā-muni*means the Lord, whose lotus feet are revered by the great devotees. Expressions like “what is the need of other works” indicate the natural power of the words. Vyāsa speaks.

## Anuccheda 261

The great taste or rasa is illustrated:

*sarva-vedānta-sāraṁ hi śrī-bhāgavatam iṣyate*  
*tad-rasāmṛta-tṛptasya nānyatra syād ratiḥ kvacit*

**Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam is declared to be the essence of all Vedānta philosophy. One who has felt satisfaction from its sweet rasa will never be attracted to any other literature. SB 12.13.15**

That rasa is sweet *(amṛta*). One who is satisfied with this sweet rasa will not be attracted elsewhere. Sūta speaks.

## Anuccheda 262

The following should be considered concerning hearing. Hearing the names, forms, qualities and pastimes of the Lord is best. The names, forms, qualities and pastimes are contained in works written by great devotees. These are recited by great devotees. Bhāgavatam is the best among the works. It should be recited by great devotees. From that work one should hear the name, form and pastimes of deity to whom one is most attracted:

*labdhvānugraha ācāryāt tena sandarśitāgamaḥ*  
*mahā-puruṣam abhyarcen mūrtyābhimatayātmanaḥ*

**Having obtained the mercy of his guru, who reveals to the disciple the injunctions of Vedic scriptures, the devotee should worship the Supreme Lord in the particular personal form of the Lord the devotee finds most attractive. SB 11.3.48**

It is most auspicious to hear about Kṛṣṇa, among all the forms of the Lord, from the mouth of a great devotee with similar inclinations, since Kṛṣṇa is the complete manifestation of Bhagavān. The same should be considered of chanting and other types of bhakti. Whatever is being chanted by a person at the present time should be chanted following after what great devotees like Śukadeva have chanted.

Hearing has been shown. It has been explained before chanting and other processes, because without hearing one will not know the other processes. If one does not have the good fortune of hearing from a great devotee, then one should chant oneself (and consequently hear), since hearing is the principal process.

*tad-vāg-visargo janatāgha-viplavo*  
*yasmin prati-ślokam abaddhavaty api \|*  
*nāmāny anantasya yaśo ’ṅkitāni yac*  
*chṛṇvanti gāyanti gṛṇanti sādhavaḥ \|\|*

**The use of words in which, though not perfectly composed, there are the names of the Lord and descriptions of his glories which devotees hear, sing, and again receive, destroys the sins of all people. SB 1.5.11**

The commentary says, “If a speaker is present they hear the names from him, if a hearer is present, they chant the names for his benefit, and if neither is present, they sing for themselves.”

**Kīrtana**

Now chanting will be discussed. As previously, there is chanting of the names, forms, qualities and pastimes of the Lord. Chanting the name is illustrated:

*sarveṣām apy aghavatām idam eva suniṣkṛtam*  
*nāma-vyāharaṇaṁ viṣṇor yatas tad-viṣayā matiḥ*

**The chanting of the holy name of Lord Viṣṇu is the best process of atonement for all other sinful men. Simply by chanting the holy name of Lord Viṣṇu, such sinful persons attract the attention of the Supreme Lord. SB 6.2.10**

The commentary says, “Chanting is the best atonement *(suniṣkrṭam*). The reason is that by chanting the name Viṣṇu thinks, ‘This person who is chanting is mine and I must protect him in all ways.’’’

In the same way, great devotees are pleased even with hearing even part of the Lord’s name since the name arises from his svarūpa and thus causes natural absorption in him. Śiva explains in relation to the hundred and eight names of Rāma:

*rakārādīni nāmāni śṛṇvato devi jāyate \|*  
*prītir me manaso nityaṁ rāma-nāma-viśaṅkayā \|\|*

**When I hear the names starting with the letter** ***r,*** **I develop affection in my mind, thinking it is Rāma’s name.**

That being the case, thatthe name destroys sin is not so significant. The servants of Viṣṇu speak to Yama’s servants.

## Anuccheda 263

The result of chanting the name is described:

*evaṁ-vrataḥ sva-priya-nāma-kīrtyā*  
*jātānurāgo druta-citta uccaiḥ*  
*hasaty atho roditi rauti gāyaty*  
*unmāda-van nṛtyati loka-bāhyaḥ*

**Regulated with such devotion, chanting the name of his dear Kṛṣṇa, he develops prema. His heart melts and he laughs loudly, weeps, wails and sings. He dances like a madman without regard for the public. SB 11.2.40**

The vrata mentioned in *evaṁ vrataḥ*is explained in the previous verse:

*śṛṇvan su-bhadrāṇi rathāṅga-pāṇer*  
*janmāni karmāṇi ca yāni loke*  
*gītāni nāmāni tad-arthakāni*  
*gāyan vilajjo vicared asaṅgaḥ*

**Hearing the most auspicious birth and activities of Kṛṣṇa which are sung by all people and singing his names which describe those activities while having no attachment to anything else, a person should without shame wander in the world. SB 11.2.39**

The person who acts in this way develops anurāga by chanting the names of his beloved deity. And then his heart melts. Thus he laughs and exhibits other symptoms because of various bhāvas. The instrumental case *(kīrtyā*) indicates that chanting the name is the sādhana to produce rāga. Here also the word*api* should be supplied to the phrase*evaṁ vrataḥ* so that the meaning is “If one chants as mentioned above, by chanting one will develop rāga.” The commentary on the next verse says, “This is a rare attainment even for the accomplished yogī after many births. How can this happen in one life by mere chanting of the name?This is explained with an example in the next verse.”

The next verse is this:

*bhaktiḥ pareśānubhavo viraktir*  
*anyatra caiṣa trika eka-kālaḥ*  
*prapadyamānasya yathāśnataḥ syus*  
*tuṣṭiḥ puṣṭiḥ kṣud-apāyo 'nu-ghāsam*

**Devotion, direct experience of the Supreme Lord, and detachment from other things—these three occur simultaneously for one who has taken shelter of Kṛṣṇa, in the same way that pleasure, fullness of the stomach and relief from hunger are experienced simultaneously, with each bite, for a person engaged in eating. SB 11.2.42**

Thus it is said in the commentary on the thousand names in Bhagavan-nāma-kaumudī, quoting from another Purāṇa:

*naktaṁ divā ca gata-bhīr jita-nidra eko*  
*nirviṇṇa īkṣita-patho mita-bhuk praśāntaḥ \|*  
*yady acyute bhagavati sa mano na sajjen*  
*nāmāni tad-rati-karāṇi paṭhed vilajjaḥ \|\|*

**If day and night one is without fear, if one has conquered sleep, and dwells in a solitary place, detached from the world, focusing the eyes only on the path on which one is walking, with controlling eating and peaceful mind, ifthe mind is still not attracted to the Lord, then without shame one should recite the names of the Lord, which cause attraction to him.**

Qualities like having no fear are produced by chanting the name but are not parts of the cultivation of the name. Bhaktiitself does not depend on other elements, and so much more so for the name. Therefore in the story of a fallen kṣatriya who had committed the worst sins, a brāhmaṇa says:

*yady etad akhilaṁ kartuṁ na śaknoṣi bravīmi te \|*  
*svalpam anyan mayoktaṁ bho kariṣyati bhavān yadi \|\|*

**If you cannot do all of this, I will tell you a something else which is easy, if you will do what I say.**

*aśakyam uktaṁ bhavatā cañcalatvād dhi cetasaḥ \|*  
*vāk-śarīra-viniṣpādyaṁ yac chakyaṁ tad udīraya \|\|*

**The fallen kṣatriya said, “I cannot do what you said, since my mind is fickle. Tell me what can be done using the body and words.”**

*uttiṣṭhatā prasvapatā prasthitena gamiṣyatā \|*  
*govindeti sadā vācyaṁ kṣut-tṛṭ-praskhalitādiṣu \|\|*

**The brāhmaṇa said, “Say the name of Govinda while in hunger, thirst or falling down, while standing up, lying down, departing or coming..**

Kavi speaks to Videha.

## Anuccheda 264

Elsewhere it is said:

*na niṣkṛtair uditair brahma-vādibhis*  
*tathā viśuddhyaty aghavān vratādibhiḥ*  
*yathā harer nāma-padair udāhṛtais*  
*tad uttamaśloka-guṇopalambhakam*

**A sinful man is not purified by abundant atonement, or by vows mentioned by Manu and others, as much as he is purified by chanting even some portions of the names of the Lord, which produce realization of his qualities. SB 6.2.11**

## Anuccheda 265

In response to Parīkṣit’s desire to know the highest benefit at the end of the First Canto, at the beginning of the Second Canto in order to give the highest answer the following is said:

*idaṁ bhāgavataṁ nāma purāṇaṁ brahma-sammitam \|*  
*adhītavān dvāparādau pitur dvaipāyanād aham \|\|*

**This scripture concerning the Supreme Lord is a Purāṇa equal to the Lord himself. I learned this scripture from my father Vyāsa at the beginning of the last part of Dvāpara-yuga.**

*pariniṣṭhito ’pi nairguṇya uttama-śloka-līlayā \|*  
*gṛhīta-cetā rājarṣe ākhyānaṁ yad adhītavān \|\|*  
*tad ahaṁ te ’bhidhāsyāmi mahā-pauruṣiko bhavān \|*  
*yasya śraddadhatām āśu syān mukunde matiḥ sate \|\|*

**Though I was fixed in Brahman and beyond the guṇas, my mind became attracted to the pastimes of the Lord. I thus learned about his pastimes. I will tell them to you. You are a great personality, who, among the faithful, will quickly fix your pure mind on Kṛṣṇa. SB 2.1.8-10**

After stating the great glory of Bhāgavatam, starting the Bhāgavatam, Śukadeva teaches chanting the name with favorable attitude to the Lord, among all the different aṅgas of bhakti. He instructs that chanting the name is the highest sādhana among all methods and yields the highest result:

*etan nirvidyamānānām icchatām akuto-bhayam \|*  
*yogināṁ nṛpa nirṇītaṁ harer nāmānukīrtanam \|\|*

**O King! Continuous chanting of the name of the Lord has been prescribed for those with desires for liberation and material elevation, and for jñānīs. SB 2.1.11**

The commentary says, “This verse explains that there is no higher benefit for sādhakas and siddhas. For those with material desires (*icchatām)*, chanting fulfils those desires. For persons desiring liberation *(nirvidyamānānām*) chanting gives liberation. For jñānīs(*yoginām)* it gives liberation. This has been concluded (*nirṇītam*). Thus one does not need to give any other proof.”

Chanting the name loudly is best:

*tad-vāg-visargo janatāgha-viplavo*  
*yasmin prati-ślokam abaddhavaty api \|*  
*nāmāny anantasya yaśo ’ṅkitāni yac*  
*chṛṇvanti gāyanti gṛṇanti sādhavaḥ \|\|*

**The use of words in which, though not perfectly composed, there are the names of the Lord and descriptions of his glories which devotees hear, sing, and again receive, destroys the sins of all people. SB 1.5.11**

One should avoid the ten offenses as stated in Padma Purāṇa. Sanat-kumāra says:

*sarvāparādha-kṛd api mucyate hari-saṁśrayāt \|*  
*harer apy aparādhān yaḥ kuryād dvipada-pāṁsanaḥ \|\|*  
*nāmāśrayaḥ kadācit syāt taraty eva sa nāmataḥ \|*  
*nāmno’pi sarva-suhṛdo hy aparādhāt pataty adhaḥ \|\|*

**A person who commits all offenses is freed by taking shelter of the Lord. If a person, personified evil, commits offenses to the Lord, by taking shelter of the name, is delivered by the name. By offending the name even a person who is friendly to all beings falls to hell.**

*satāṁ nindā nāmnaḥ paramam aparādhaṁ vitanute*  
*yataḥ khyātiṁ yātaṁ katham u sahate tad vigarhām \|*

**One who criticizes devotees commits the greatest offense because how can the name tolerate criticism of those who have spread the glories of the name?**

*śivasya śrī viṣṇor ya iha guṇa nāmādi sakalaṁ*  
*dhiyābhinnaṁ paśyet sa khalu hari nāmāhita karaḥ \|\|*

**He who sees the names and qualities of Śiva and Viṣṇu to be the same injures the Lord’s name.**

*guror avajñā śruti śāstra nindana.*  
*tathārtha vādo hari nāmni kalpanam \|*  
*nāmno balād yasya hi pāpa buddhir*  
*na vidyate tasya yamair hi śuddhiḥ \|\|*

**Disrespecting the guru and criticizing the scriptures are offenses. It is an offense to think that the glories of the name are exaggerated or to give imaginary meanings to the name. One who commits sin on the strength of the name cannot be purified by following rules.**

*dharma vrata tyāga hutādi sarv.*  
*śubha kriyā sāmyam api pramādaḥ \|*  
*aśraddadhāne vimukhe’py aśṛṇvati*  
*yaś copadeśaḥ śiva nāmāparādhaḥ \|\|*

**Thinking that the chanting of the name is no greater than following dharma, practicing vratas and renunciation, or performing sacrifices or other pious acts is madness.**

*śrutvāpi nāma māhātmye yaḥ prīti rahito’dhamaḥ \|*  
*ahaṁ mamādi paramo nāmni so’py aparādha kṛt \|\|*

**The low person who after hearing the glories of the name is still without devotion and is greatly entrenched in concepts of me and mine commits offense.**

Concerning obtaining relief from all offenses by taking shelter of the Lord it is said in Viṣṇu-yamala:

*mama nāmāni loke’smin śraddhayā yas tu kīrtayet \|*  
*tasyāparādha-koṭīs tu kṣamāmy eva na saṁśayaḥ \|\|*

**He who sings my names with faith in this world without doubt is forgiven by me for ten million offenses. Viṣṇu-yamala**

Since criticizing devotees is serious, the implication is that physical violence against devotees cannot even be described. In the conversation between Mārkaṇḍeya and Bhagīratha in Skanda Purāṇa it is said:

*nindāṁ kurvanti ye mūḍhā vaiṣṇavānāṁ mahātmanām \|*  
*patanti pitṛbhiḥ sārdhaṁ mahāraurava-saṁjñite \|\|*  
*hanti nindanti vai dveṣṭi vaiṣṇavān nābhinandati \|*  
*krudhyate yāti no harṣaṁ darśane patanāni ṣaṭ \|\|*

**Fools who criticize great Vaiṣṇavas fall to the hell known as Mahāraurava with their ancestors.**

There are six ways of falling: killing or harming Vaiṣṇavas, criticizing them, hating them, not welcoming them, being angry with them and not showing joy on seeing them.

It is said that hearing criticism of Vaiṣṇavas is also a fault.

*nindāṁ bhagavataḥ śṛṇvaṁs tat-parasya janasya vā*  
*tato nāpaiti yaḥ so ’pi yāty adhaḥ sukṛtāc cyutaḥ*

**Anyone who fails to immediately leave the place where he hears criticism of the Supreme Lord or his faithful devotee will certainly fall down, bereft of his pious credit. SB 10.74.40**

An incapable person should leave the place. If capable he should cut off the tongue of the criticizer. If he cannot do that, he should give up his life. Devī says:

*karṇau pidhāya nirayād yad akalpa īśe*  
*dharmāvitary asṛṇibhir nṛbhir asyamāne*  
*chindyāt prasahya ruśatīm asatīṁ prabhuś cej*  
*jihvām asūn api tato visṛjet sa dharmaḥ*

**When the protector of religion, a master, is insulted by uncontrollable people, one should leave that place, blocking one’s ears, if he cannot do anything else. If possible he should forcibly cut the tongue of the offenders who speak badly and then give up his own life. That is the correct procedure. SB 4.4.17**

Concerning considering the names of Śiva and Viṣṇu to be the same it is said:

*yad yad vibhūtimat sattvaṁ śrīmad ūrjitam eva vā \|*  
*tat tad evāvagaccha tvaṁ mama tejo-’ṁśa-sambhavam \|\|*

**Whatever objects in past, present or future display majesty, beauty, or strength; know that it has arisen from just a fragment of my power. BG 10.41**

*brahmā bhavo ’ham api yasya kalāḥ kalāyāḥ*

**Great devatās like Brahmā and Śiva, and even the goddess of fortune and I, are simply parts of his spiritual identity. SB 10.68.37**

*yac-chauca-niḥsṛta-sarit-pravarodakena*  
*tīrthena mūrdhny adhikṛtena śivaḥ śivo 'bhūt*  
*dhyātur manaḥ-śamala-śaila-nisṛṣṭa-vajraṁ*  
*dhyāyec ciraṁ bhagavataś caraṇāravindam*

**One should meditate continually upon the Lord’s lotus feet from which the Gaṅgā flows and gives extra blessings to Lord Śiva on his head, and whose thunderbolt is released upon mountains of sin in the meditator’s mind. SB 3.28.22**

*sṛjāmi tan-niyukto ’haṁ haro harati tad-vaśaḥ \|*  
*viśvaṁ puruṣa-rūpeṇa paripāti tri-śakti-dhṛk \|\|*

**I create under his order, and Śiva destroys under his order. Holding his three energies, he protects the universe as the Paramātmā. SB 2.6.32**

*rujaṁ drāvayate yasmād rudras tasmāj janārdanaḥ \|*  
*īśanād eva ceśāno mahā-devo mahattvataḥ \|\|*  
*pibanti ye narā nākaṁ muktāḥ saṁsāra-sāgarāt \|*  
*tad-ādhāro yato viṣṇuḥ pinākīti tataḥ smṛtaḥ \|\|*  
*śivaḥ sukhātmakatvena sarva-sarodhanād dharaḥ \|*  
*kṛtyātmakam imaṁ dehaṁ yato vaste pravartayan \|\|*  
*kṛttivāsās tato devo viriñciś ca virecanāt \|*  
*bṛṁhaṇād brahma-nāmāsau aiśvaryād indra ucyate \|\|*  
*evaṁ nānā-vidhaiḥ śabdair eka eva trivikramaḥ \|*  
*vedeṣu ca purāṇeṣu gīyate puruṣottamaḥ \|\|*

**Because he drives away pain Viṣṇu is called Rudra. Because he is the controller he is called Īśāna. Because he is the greatest he is called Mahādeva. He is called Pinākī (holder of a trident) because he is the support of persons who, liberated from the ocean of saṁsāra, enjoy (pi) the spiritual sky (nāka). He is called Śiva because he is the embodiment of happiness. He is called Hara because he destroys everything. He is called Kṛttivāsa because he lives in and operates the body which produces action (*****kṛtya).*** **He is called Viriñci because he extends beyond. He is called Brahmā because he is expands. He is called Indra because he has power. Thus the one Supreme Lord is glorified by various types of names in the Vedas and Purāṇas.**

In Vāmana Purāṇa it is said:

*na tu nārāyaṇādīnāṁ nāmnām anyatra saṁśayaḥ \|*  
*anya-nāmnāṁ gatir viṣṇur eka eva prakīrtitaḥ \|\|*

**There is no doubt that the names of Nārāyaṇa are used for others. Viṣṇu alone is glorified as the shelter of other names.**

In Skanda Purāṇa it is said:

*ṛte nārāyaṇādīni nāmāni puruṣottamaḥ \|*  
*adād anyatra bhagavān rājevarte svakaṁ puram \|\|*

**Except for names like Nārāyaṇa the Lord gave his other names to others, just as a king, apart from his city, gives other places to his minister.**

Brahmā Purāṇa says:

*caturmukhaḥ śatānando brahmaṇaḥ padmabhūr iti\|*  
*ugro bhasmadharo nagnaḥ kapālīti śivasya ca \|*  
*viśeṣa-nāmāni dadau svakīyāny api keśavaḥ \|\|*

**The Lord gives some of his particular names to others. To Brahmā he gives the names Caturmukha, Śatānanda and Padmabhū. To Śiva he gives the names Ugra, Bhasmadhara, Nagna and Kapālī.**

Thus the meaning is “He who sees by his intelligence that Śiva’s qualities and names are different from Viṣṇu’s (*viṣṇoḥ*), arising from a separate śakti, is an offender, since it is well known that Viṣṇu is ultimately everything.” Absolute identity would have been expressed by adding the word *ca* after Viṣṇu (*śivasya viṣnoś ca*). The superiority of Viṣṇu is indicated by the word *śrī* to his name. The word *śiva* in *śiva nāmāparādhaḥ*(ninth offense) refers to Śiva as Viṣṇu’s name. One of Viṣṇu’s thousand names is Śiva.

Now criticizing scriptures is discussed. This refers to pāṣaṇḍas, such as worshippers of Dattātreya and Ṛṣabhadeva. They become pāṣaṇḍas by not accepting the Vedic conclusions.

*Artha-vāda*means to think that the statements concerning the powers of the name are merely exaggerated praise*. Kalpanam*means to give other meanings to the name in order minimize the name’s glories. Vyāsa says in Kūrma Purāṇa:

*deva-drohād guru-drohaḥ koṭi-koṭi-guṇādhikaḥ \|*  
*jñānāpavādo nāstikyaṁ tasmāt koṭi-guṇādhikam \|\|*

**Offending guru is billions of times worse than offending the Lord. Denying scriptural knowledge and not accepting the authority of the Vedas is ten million times worse than offending the guru.**

After hearing the glories of the name, Ajāmila said the following to show his previous state of depravity (rather than doubt the power of the name):

*so 'haṁ vyaktaṁ patiṣyāmi narake bhṛśa-dāruṇe*  
*dharma-ghnāḥ kāmino yatra vindanti yama-yātanāḥ*

**I will certainly fall to a most terrifying hell where those who destroy dharma and are full of lust undergo torture by Yamarāja. SB 6.2.29**

Later he says (expressing the greatness of the name):

*athāpi me durbhagasya vibudhottama-darśane*  
*bhavitavyaṁ maṅgalena yenātmā me prasīdati*

**Though most sinful, I must have done some auspicious acts to see those best of devatās, by which I have become joyful. SB 6.2.32**

*anyathā mriyamāṇasya nāśucer vṛṣalī-pateḥ*  
*vaikuṇṭha-nāma-grahaṇaṁ jihvā vaktum ihārhati*

**Otherwise, it would not be possible for the tongue of a sinful, dying man, the keeper of a prostitute, to chant the name of the Lord. SB 6.2.33**

Now committing sin on the strength of the name will be discussed. Though the results of sins are destroyed by the strength of the name, even if the person uses the name for this purpose, it is most wicked to utilize the power of name to fulfill sinful despicable desires, though one’s original intention was that power of the name bring about attachment to the lotus feet of the Lord composed of condensed eternity, knowledge and bliss, the highest attainment. If one utilizes the name in this way, the offense is ten millions times greater than the sin itself. Thus it is said that there is no purification of that person punished by unlimited punishers (*yama*) or by following many rules (*yama*) to regain his status by atonements. As will be stated later, the only atonement is continuous chanting of the name. It was also said *nāmno’pi sarva-suhṛdo hy aparādhāt pataty adhaḥ:* the committer of all offenses, even if he is a devotee, falls down.

Indra wanted to kill Vṛtra (actually a devotee) by the strength of worshipping the Lord through a horse sacrifice. However this desire was supported by the sages who desired to terminate Vṛtra’s demon body and stop havoc in the world. Thus it was not an offense on Indra’s part.

It is madness (*pramadaḥ*) to think that chanting the name is equivalent to dharma and vratas. This means it is an offense to think in this way. It is thus said:

*vedākṣarāṇi yāvanti paṭhitāni dvijātibhiḥ \|*  
*tāvanti hari-nāmāni kīrtitāni na saṁśayaḥ \|\|*

**There is no doubt that whatever is achieved by brāhmaṇas reciting the syllables of the Vedas is achieved by singing the names the Lord.**

The comparison made here is to show the powers of the name alone (rather than to equate the two items). It is said (showing superiority over Vedas):

*madhura-madhuram etan maṅgalaṁ maṅgalānāṁ*  
*sakala-nigama-vallī-sat-phalaṁ cit-svarūpam*

**The name of Kṛṣṇa is sweeter than the sweetest, the most auspicious of all things auspicious, the highest fruit in the tree of all the Vedas, and is composed entirely of pure consciousness. Padma Purāṇa**

*ṛg-vedo hi yajur-vedaḥ sāma-vedo’py atharvaṇaḥ \|*  
*adhītās tena yenoktaṁ harir ity akṣara-dvayam \|\|*

**He who recites the two syllables “Hari” has studied the Ṛg, Yajur, Sāma and Atharva Vedas. Viṣṇu-dharm.**

Pārvatī says:

*mā ṛco mā yajus tāta mā sāma paṭha kiñcana \|*  
*govindeti harer nāma geyaṁ gāyasva nityaśaḥ \|\|*

**Do not chant the Ṛg,Yajur or Sāma Veda. The Lord’s name Govinda should be chanted. Chant it always. Skanda Purāṇa**

In glorifying the hundred and eight names of Rāma it is said:

*viṣṇor ekaika-nāmaiva sarva-vedādhikaṁ matam*

**Each name of Viṣṇu is greater than all the Vedas. Padma Purāṇa 6.254.27**

Giving the name to the faithless illustrates an offense committed by an instructor of the name (rather than simply chanting). Then an offense committed by the person instructed is given. The meaning is “He who has no respect for the name after hearing its glories, because he completely identifies with I and mine, is an offender.”

Previously the following was quoted:

*nāmaikaṁ yasya vāci smaraṇa-patha-gataṁ śrotra-mūlaṁ gataṁ v.*  
*śuddhaṁ vāśuddha-varṇaṁ vyavahita-rahitaṁ tārayaty eva satyam \|*  
*tac ced deha-draviṇa-janatā-lobha-pāṣaṇḍa-madhye*  
*nikṣiptaṁ syān na phala-janakaṁ śīghram evātra vipra \|\|*

**Anyone who chants, remembers or hears even one name of the Lord truly surpasses pure or impure caste without obstacles. But if the name is given to offenders among the pāṣaṇdas greedy for body, objects and following, it does not give its results quickly.**

The above quotation shows that the pāṣāṇda nature caused by greed for body, objects and following indicates commission of the ten offenses. All the offenses are permeated with this pāṣāṇḍa attitude.

Another offense of this type is described in Padma Purāṇa, in the glorification of Vaiśakha month:

*avamanya ca ye yānti bhagavat-kīrtanaṁ narāḥ \|*  
*te yānti narakaṁ ghoraṁ tena pāpena karmaṇā \|\|*

**Persons who, disrespecting the chanting of the Lord’s name, leave that place where the name is chanted, go to a terrible hell because of that sinful act.**

The atonement for these offenses is stated:

*nāmāparādha-yuktānāṁ nāmāny eva haranty agham \|*  
*aviśrānta-prayuktāni tāny evārtha-karāṇi ca \|\|*

**For those who have committed offenses to the name, chanting the name incessantly will be effective in destroying that sin. Padma Purāṇa**

If one offends a devotee, it is advised to continually chant the name in order to satisfy the devotee. It is seen in the story of Ambarīṣa that the offense to Ambarīṣa was mitigated only by Durvāsa approaching Ambarīṣa as directed by the Lord. It is said in Nāma-kaumudī *mahad-aparādhasya bhoga eva nivartakaḥ tad-anugraho vā*: one can eradicate a great offense by experiencing its results or by obtaining mercy of the devotee. If there is no alternative it is correct said:

*etan nirvidyamānānām icchatām akuto-bhayam \|*  
*yogināṁ nṛpa nirṇītaṁ harer nāmānukīrtanam \|\|*

**O King! Continuous chanting of the name of the Lord has been prescribed for devotees, for those with desires for liberation and material elevation, and for those satisfied with the self. SB 2.1.11**

Śukadeva speaks.

## Anuccheda 266

Nārada said:

*mahimnām api yan-nāmnaḥ pāraṁ gantum anīśvaraḥ \|*  
*manavo’pi munīndrāś ca kathaṁ taṁ kṣuṇṇa-dhīr bhaje \|\|*

**If Manus and great sages cannot understand the glories of his name, how can I do so, being of low intelligence? Nāradīya Purāṇa**

Now glorifying the form of the Lord will be discussed.

*pratyākraṣṭuṁ nayanam abalā yatra lagnaṁ na śekuḥ*  
*karṇāviṣṭaṁ na sarati tato yat satām ātma-lagnam*  
*yac-chrīr vācāṁ janayati ratiṁ kiṁ nu mānaṁ kavīnāṁ*  
*dṛṣṭvā jiṣṇor yudhi ratha-gataṁ yac ca tat-sāmyam īyuḥ*

**Once their eyes were fixed upon his transcendental form, women were unable to withdraw them, and once that form had entered the ears of the sages and became fixed in their hearts, it would never depart. What to speak of acquiring fame, the great poets who described the beauty of the Lord’s form would develop attraction for that glorification. And by seeing that form on Arjuna’s chariot, all the warriors on the battlefield of Kurukṣetra attained the liberation. SB 11.30.3**

The great beauty of Kṛṣṇa when glorified by great poets produces attraction in them for that glorification. The Kumāras say:

*kāmaṁ bhavaḥ sva-vṛjinair nirayeṣu naḥ stāc*  
*ceto ’livad yadi nu te padayo rameta*  
*vācaś ca nas tulasivad yadi te ’ṅghri-śobhāḥ*  
*pūryeta te guṇa-gaṇair yadi karṇa-randhraḥ*

**Let us remain in hellish existence because of sinful actions, if our minds can achieve prema at your lotus feet like bees which are not injured by the thorns as they taste the honey, if our words can remain glorious at your lotus feet like tulasī leaves, and if our ear holes can remain filled with hearing your qualities. SB 3.15.49**

Parīkṣit speaks to Śukadeva.

## Anuccheda 267

Glorifying the Lord’s qualities is discussed:

*idaṁ hi puṁsas tapasaḥ śrutasya vā*  
*sviṣṭasya sūktasya ca buddhi-dattayoḥ \|*  
*avicyuto ’rthaḥ kavibhir nirūpito*  
*yad-uttamaśloka-guṇānuvarṇanam \|\|*

**The unfailing cause of perfection from men’s austerity, study of the Vedas, performance of sacrifices, chanting of mantras, practicing jñāna and giving charity is defined by the wise as glorifying the qualities of the Lord. SB 1.5.22**

The eternal result (*avicyutaḥ arthaḥ*) of men’s austerity, studying the Vedas (*śrutasya*), performing sacrifices (*sviṣṭasya*), chantingmantras (*sūktasya*), understanding according to scriptures (*buddhi*) and charity work (*dattayoḥ*), when offered to the Lord, is defined by the wise as glorifying the qualities of the Lord. When the glorification of qualities becomes perfected, with rati for the Lord, the highest goal for humanity, the glorification will be constant *(avicyutaḥ*) because of continual bliss. And thus the result of that would be continual rati for the Lord. Nārada speaks to Vyāsa.

## Anuccheda 268

Glorifying the Lord’s pastimes is discussed:

*śṛṇvataḥ śraddhayā nityaṁ gṛṇataś ca sva-ceṣṭitam \|*  
*kālena nātidīrgheṇa bhagavān viśate hṛdi \|\|*

**The Lord quickly enters the heart of the devotee who with faith continually hears and chants about the Lord’s activities. SB 2.8.4**

In a short time the Lordappears (*viśate*) in the heart. Parīkṣit speaks.

## Anuccheda 269

*mṛṣā giras tā hy asatīr asat-kathā*  
*na kathyate yad bhagavān adhokṣajaḥ*  
*tad eva satyaṁ tad u haiva maṅgalaṁ*  
*tad eva puṇyaṁ bhagavad-guṇodayam*

**Words that do not describe the transcendental Lord but instead deal with temporary matters are simply illusory anduntrue. Only those words that manifest the qualities of the Supreme Lord are actually truthful, auspicious and pure. SB 12.12.49**

*tad eva ramyaṁ ruciraṁ navaṁ navaṁ*  
*tad eva śaśvan manaso mahotsavam*  
*tad eva śokārṇava-śoṣaṇaṁ nṛṇāṁ*  
*yad uttamaḥśloka-yaśo 'nugīyate*

**Those words describing the glories of the all-famous Lord are pleasing, relishable and ever fresh. They bring intense joy to the mind and they dry up the ocean of misery. SB 12.12.50**

*Asatīḥ*means not true. *Asat-kathā*means speaking about things other than the Lord and his devotees. Words are illusory and untrue whose topics deal with things other than the Lord, in which the Lord is not described. Words concerning his pastimes (*anugīyate*), in which the fame of the Lord is sung,are true and auspicious. Why? They manifest the qualities of the Lord in the heart of the singer. This means the words produce rati for the Lord. It is said:

*yatra yatra mahīpāla vaiṣṇavī vartate kathā \|*  
*tatra tatra harir yāti gaur yathā suta-vatsalā \|\|*

**O king! The Lord goes wherever there are topics glorifying him just as a cow cows goes to the calf out of affection. Skanda Purāṇa**

*mat-kathā-vācakaṁ nityaṁ mat-kathā-śravaṇe ratam \|*  
*mat-kathā-prīti-manasaṁ nāhaṁ tyakṣyāmi taṁnaram \|\|*

**I do not give up that person who recites topics about me constantly, is attracted to hearing my topics and whose mind has attraction to my topics. Viṣṇu-dharma**

*Anugīyate* implies that if one has a good voice it is best to sing the pastimes of the Lord. The same is true of the name, form and qualities of the Lord. It is said:

*śṛṇvan su-bhadrāṇi rathāṅga-pāṇer*  
*janmāni karmāṇi ca yāni loke*  
*gītāni nāmāni tad-arthakāni*  
*gāyan vilajjo vicared asaṅgaḥ*

**Hearing the most auspicious birth and activities of Kṛṣṇa which are sung by all people and singing his names which describe those activities while having no attachment to anything else, a person should without shame wander in the world. SB 11.2.39**

*yānīha viśva-vilayodbhava-vṛtti-hetuḥ*  
*karmāṇy ananya-viṣayāṇi harīś cakāra*  
*yas tv aṅga gāyati śṛṇoty anumodate vā*  
*bhaktir bhaved bhagavati hy apavarga-mārge*

**Kṛṣṇa is the ultimate cause of universal creation, maintenance and destruction but in this** ***avatāra,*** **he performed activities which are non-different from himself. My dear King, anyone who sings about, hears about or simply appreciates the extraordinary activities will surely develop devotion for the Supreme Lord, the bestower of liberation. SB 10.69.45**

If one is unable to sing one can find someone who is better at singing and listen to him. If one cannot listen at least one can be sympathetic (*anumodate*). Viṣṇu says:

*rāgeṇākṛṣyate ceto gāndharvābhimukhaṁ yadi \|*  
*mayi buddhiṁ samāsthāya gāyethā mama sat-kathāḥ \|\|*

**If the mind is musically inclined and attracted to ragas, one should sing my pastimes with intelligence fixed on me. Viṣṇu-dharma**

*nāhaṁ vasāmi vaikuṇṭhe yogināṁ hṛdaye na ca \|*  
*mad-bhaktā yatra gāyanti tatra tiṣṭhāmi nārada \|\|*  
*teṣāṁ pūjādikaṁ gandha-dhūpādyaiḥ kriyate naraiḥ \|*  
*tena prītiṁ parāṁ yāmi na tathā mama pūjanāt \|\|*

**I do not reside in Vaikuṇṭha or in the heart of yogīs. O Nārada! I am situated wherever my devotees sing. Men should worship the devotees with perfumes and incense. I am more pleased this worship than worship of myself. Padma Purāṇa**

Those who sing are the deliverers of all beings what to speak of delivering themselves. Prahlāda says:

*te santaḥ sarva-bhūtānāṁ nirupādhika-bāndhavāḥ \|*  
*ye nṛsiṁha bhavan-nāma gāyanty uccair mudānvitāḥ \|\|*

**O Nṛsiṁha! Those devotees who sing your name loudly in joy are the unmotivated friends of all living beings. Narasiṁha Purāṇa**

When many people gather and sing kīrtana is called *saṅkīrtana.* Since it produces most excellent astonishment it is considered better than kīrtana.

**Nāma-saṅkīrtana**

The kali-yuga avatāra, Caitanya Mahāprabhu, has instructed about nāma-saṅkīrtana:

*tṛṇād api sunīcena taror api sahiṣṇunā \|*  
*amāninā mānadena kīrtanīyaḥ sadā hariḥ \|\|*

**Being lower than grass and more tolerant than a tree, by being prideless and offering respects to others, one should sing the name of the Lord constantly. Padyāvalī 32**

Sūta speaks.

## Anuccheda 270

This type of bhakti, called kīrtana, is unlimitedly merciful to those who are lacking in material objects, caste, qualities and actions. That is the conclusion of śruti and Purāṇas.

The miserable condition in Kali-yuga is described:

*ataḥ kalau tapo-yoga-vidyā-yājñādikāḥ kriyāḥ \|*  
*sāṅgāḥ bhavanti na kṛtāḥ kuśalair api dehibhiḥ \|\|*

**In Kali-yuga austerity, yoga, knowledge, sacrifices, duties, and association are not undertaken even by skilful persons. Brahma-vaivarta Purāṇa**

Appearing in Kali-yuga for people most unfortunate by nature, saṅkīrtana very easily gives all results among the great processes for the respective yugas and makes those processes successful. Thus in Kali-yuga the Lord is especially pleased by saṅkīrtana.

*tathā caivottamaṁ loke tapaḥ śrī-hari-kīrtanam \|*  
*kalau yuge viśeṣeṇa viṣṇu-prītyai samācaret \|\|*

**Chanting the Lord’s name, the best austerity for the people should particularly be performed in Kali-yuga to please the Lord. Skanda Purāṇa**

*kṛte yad dhyāyato viṣṇuṁ tretāyāṁ yajato makhaiḥ*  
*dvāpare paricaryāyāṁ kalau tad dhari-kīrtanāt*

**Whatever result was obtained in Satya-yuga by meditating on Viṣṇu, in Tretā-yuga by performing sacrifices, and in Dvāpara-yuga by deity worship, can be obtained in Kali-yuga simply by chanting about the Lord. SB 12.3.52**

Whatever results were obtained in Satya and other yugas by the sādhana of that age is all available by chanting the Lord’s name. Elsewhere it is said:

*dhyāyan kṛte yajan yajñais tretāyāṁ dvāpare’rcayan \|*  
*yad āpnoti tad āpnoti kalau saṅkīrtya keśavam \|\|*

**What was obtained by meditation in Sayta-yuga, by sacrifice in Tretā-yuga and by deity worship in Dvāpara-yuga is obtained in Kali-yuga by saṅkīrtana glorifying the Lord. Viṣṇu Purāṇa**

Śukadeva speaks.

## Anuccheda 271

Thus it is said:

*kaliṁ sabhājayanty āryā guṇa jñāḥ sāra-bhāginaḥ*  
*yatra saṅkīrtanenaiva sarva-svārtho ’bhilabhyate*

**Those who know the good quality, who have accepted the best part, praise Kali-yuga because in this fallen age all perfection of life can easily be achieved by the performance of saṅkīrtana. SB 11.5.36**

Those who know the quality (*guṇa-jñāḥ*), preaching through kīrtana, and who receive the best part (*sāra-bhāginaḥ*) because of not accepting the faults of Kali but only the good portion, praise Kali-yuga. They see the good quality in which one can attain the results of thousands of sādhanas used in other yugas (*sarva-svārthaḥ)* by the recommended saṅkīrtana without reliance on any other method (*saṅkīrtanena eva*)..

## Anuccheda 272

The greatness of kīrtana is described:

*na hy ataḥ paramo lābho dehināṁ bhrāmyatām iha*  
*yato vindeta paramāṁ śāntiṁ naśyati saṁsṛtiḥ*

**For the conditioned souls wandering in this world, there is no greater attainment than saṅkīrtana, from which one obtains bhakti,with mind fixed on the Lord, and by which saṁsāra is destroyed. SB 11.5.37**

There is no greater attainment than kīrtana (*ataḥ)* from which (*yataḥ*) one attains steadiness (*paramam śāntim*) in the most attractive Lord, not attained by meditation or other methods. And as an accompanying result saṁsāra is destroyed. *Śāntim* or *śama*means having the intelligence fixed in the Lord according SB 11.19.36.The people who attained steadiness in mediation and other processes in other ages did not attain steadiness in Bhagavān. *Mahā-bhāgavatā nityaṁ kalau kurvanti kīrtanam:* great devotees perform kīrtana constantly in Kali-yuga. (Skanda Purāṇa) Thus since the cause of that steadiness and the glory of kīrtana were not revealed by the Lord, who is most merciful to the most fallen, when people had greater abilities in other yugas, the people being capable of other sādhanas would think that kīrtana was not significant as a sādhana, being mere moving the tongue and lips, and would not have faith in it.

## Anuccheda 273

Thus hearing that the people of Kali-yuga could attain excellent steadiness in Bhagavān, the peopleliving during those yugas pray for birth in Kali-yuga to attain that steadiness.

*kṛtādiṣu prajā rājan kalāv icchanti sambhavam*  
*kalau khalu bhaviṣyanti nārāyaṇa-parāyaṇāḥ*

**O King! The inhabitants of Satya-yuga and other ages eagerly desire to take birth in this age of Kali, since in this age there will be many devotees of the Supreme Lord, Nārāyaṇa. SB 11.5.38**

*Nārāyaṇa-parāyaṇāḥ* means devotees with intense prema for the Lord. That is revealed by the effect *paramāṁ śāntim* (mind fixed on the Lord SB 11.5.37). That is also indicated in the following:

*muktānām api siddhānāṁ nārāyaṇa-parāyaṇaḥ*  
*su-durlabhaḥ praśāntātmā koṭiṣv api mahā-mune*

**O great sage! Greater than those who are jīvanmukta and greater than those who attain liberation is the devotee of Lord Nārāyaṇa. Such a devotee, who is fixed on the Lord, even among ten million people. SB 6.14.5**

One should not say that kīrtana becomes glorious because of association with Kali-yuga since there is no restriction of time and place for bhakti.

*cakrāyudhasya nāmāni sadā sarvatra kīrtayet*

**One should cant the name of the Lord at all times and all places. Viṣṇu-dharma**

*na deśa-kālāvasthātma-śuddhy-ādikam apekṣate \|*  
*kintu svatantram evaidaṁ tan nāma kāmita-kāmadam \|\|*

**The name of the Lord, independent and fulfilling all desires, does not depend on time, place, condition or purity of body or mind. Skanda Purāṇa**

*kalau kṛta-yugaṁ tasya kalis tasya kṛte yuge \|*  
*yasya cetasi govindo hṛdaye yasya nācyuta \|\|*

**For one how has Govinda in his heart, Kali-yuga becomes Satya-yuga. For one who does not have the Lord in his heart, Satya-yuga becomes Kali-yuga. Viṣṇu-dharma**

One should not think that even a little effort in kīrtana gives great result because people cannot do any other sādhana in Kali-yuga. The great result arises because of power of kīrtana itself.

*yasmin nyasta-matir na yāti narakaṁ svargo’pi yac-cintan.*  
*vighno yatra niveśitātma-manasāṁ brāhmo’pi loko’lpakaḥ \|*  
*muktiṁ cetasi yaḥ sthito’mala-dhiyāṁ puṁsāṁ dadāty avyaya.*  
*kiṁ citraṁ yad aghaṁ prayāti vilayaṁ tatrācyute kīrtite \|\|*

**Thinking of him one does not go to hell. Thinking of him one can go to Svarga. Concentrating on the Lordsituated in the heart, the imperishable Lord gives liberation to people of impure mind. Is it astonishing that by singing the name of the Lord sin is destroyed? Viṣṇu Purāṇa 6.8.5.**

The Viṣṇu Purāṇa shows that kīrtana is greater than remembering the Lord even at its stage of perfection, samādhi.

*etan nirvidyamānānām icchatām akuto-bhayam \|*  
*yogināṁ nṛpa nirṇītaṁ harer nāmānukīrtanam \|\|*

**O King! Continuous chanting of the name of the Lord has been prescribed for devotees, for those with desires for liberation and material elevation, and for those satisfied with the self. SB 2.1.11**

It is also said:

*aghacchit-smaraṇaṁ viṣṇor bahv-āyāsena sādhyate \|*  
*oṣṭha-spandana-mātreṇa kīrtanaṁ tu tato varam \|\|*

**Remembering Viṣṇu to destroy sin is accomplished with great Endeavour. Kīrtana, by vibrating the lips, is better than that. Vaiṣṇava-cintāmaṇi**

Elsewhere it is said:

*yena janma-śataiḥ pūrvaṁ vāsudevaḥ samarcitaḥ \|*  
*tan-mukhe hari-nāmāni sadā tiṣṭhanti bhārata \|\|*

**O Bhārata! The person who has worshipped Vāsudeva for a hundred lives will have the name of the Lord on his lips.**

*sarvāparādha-kṛd api mucyate hari-saṁśrayāt \|*  
*harer apy aparādhān yaḥ kuryād dvipada-pāṁsanaḥ \|\|*  
*nāmāśrayaḥ kadācit syāt taraty eva sa nāmataḥ \|*  
*nāmno’pi sarva-suhṛdo hy aparādhāt pataty adhaḥ \|\|*

**A person who commits all offenses is freed by taking shelter of the Lord. If a person, personified evil, commits offenses to the Lord, by taking shelter of the name, is delivered by the name. By offending the name even a person who is friendly to all beings falls to hell.**

Thus in all yugas, kīrtana has the same potency. In Kali-yuga, by the mercy of the Lord, this method is to be accepted. Thus it is praised for Kali-yuga.

Though other processes of bhakti are to be performed, they are done along with kīrtana:

*kṛṣṇa-varṇaṁ tviṣākṛṣṇaṁ sāṅgopāṅgāstra-pārṣadam*  
*yajñaiḥ saṅkīrtana-prāyair yajanti hi su-medhasaḥ*

**The intelligent people worship the Lord, black in color, but shining with brilliance, who is accompanied by his associates, weapons, major and minor limbs, chiefly through chanting. SB 11.5.32**

Nāma-kīrtana being independent is the best of all:

*harer nāma harer nāma harer nāmaiva kevalam \|*  
*kalau nāsty eva nāsty eva nāsty eva gatir anyathā \|\|*

**The name of the Lord, the name of the Lord, the name of the Lord alone is the shelter. There is no other shelter in Kali-yuga. Nāradīya Purāṇa**

Therefore it was rightly said:

*kaliṁ sabhājayanty āryā guṇa jñāḥ sāra-bhāginaḥ*  
*yatra saṅkīrtanenaiva sarva-svārtho ’bhilabhyate*

**Those who are actually advanced in knowledge, who know quality, who have accepted the best, praise Kali-yuga because in this fallen age all perfection of life can easily be achieved by the performance of** ***saṅkīrtana. SB 11.5.36***

*na hy ataḥ paramo lābho dehināṁ bhrāmyatām iha*  
*yato vindeta paramāṁ śāntiṁ naśyati saṁsṛtiḥ*

**For the conditioned souls wandering in this world, there is no greater attainment than saṅkīrtana, from which one obtains bhakti, full of peace, and by which saṁsārais destroyed. SB 11.5.37**

Karabhājana speaks to Nimi.

## Anuccheda 274

It has been shown that by the power of spreading nāma-kīrtana in Kali-yuga surrender to the Lord is accomplished. Stating this in the opposite way those who offend nāma-kīrtana, by becoming pāśaṇḍas, will become averse to the Lord. That is confirmed in the following:

*kalau na rājan jagatāṁ paraṁ guruṁ*  
*tri-loka-nāthānata-pāda-paṅkajam*  
*prāyeṇa martyā bhagavantam acyutaṁ*  
*yakṣyanti pāṣaṇḍa-vibhinna-cetasaḥ*

**O King! In the age of Kali, people whose intelligence will be diverted by atheism will almost never offer sacrifice to the Supreme Lord, supremeguru of the universe, at whose lotus feet the devatās bow.**

*yan-nāmadheyaṁ mriyamāṇa āturaḥ*  
*patan skhalan vā vivaśo gṛṇan pumān*  
*vimukta-karmārgala uttamāṁ gatiṁ*  
*prāpnoti yakṣyanti na taṁ kalau janāḥ*

**Terrified, about to die, collapsing, with faltering voice, if a person utters the holy name of the Supreme Lord, he can be freed from the chains of karma and achieve the supreme destination. But still people in the age of Kali will not worship the Supreme Lord. SB 12.3.43-44**

The meaning is clear. Śukadeva speaks.

**Smaraṇam**

## Anuccheda 275
