Kīrtana has been explained. Kīrtana includes recitation of verses expressing one’s low condition and one’s desire for the Lord. Kīrtana ofthe name, form and qualities from the Bhāgavatam is superior to kīrtana verses from other sources. That is advised in Kali-yuga.

*kṛṣṇe sva-dhāmopagate dharma-jñānādibhiḥ saha \|*  
*kalau naṣṭa-dṛśām eṣa purāṇārko ’dhunoditaḥ \|\|*

**Though Kṛṣṇa left Dvārakā, arrived at Prabhāsa, and then disappeared along with his six great qualities, this Purāṇa, another form of the sun, has now risen in Kali-yuga for those who have lost their knowledge. SB 1.3.43**

If the antaḥkaraṇa has been purified by surrender, one should perform smaraṇam without giving up nāma-kīrtana as advised in SB 2.1.11. Smaraṇam is reflecting with the mind. There are many varieties using the name, form etc. General smaraṇam is described as follows:

*etāvān yoga ādiṣṭo mac-chiṣyaiḥ sanakādibhiḥ*  
*sarvato mana ākṛṣya mayy addhāveśyate yathā*

**This yogasystem was taught by my devotees, headed by Sanaka. Having withdrawn the mind from all other objects, one should directly and appropriately absorb it in me. SB 11.13.14**

Whatever yoga was taught is fixing the mind in me. In the Skanda Purāṇa Brahmā says.

*āloḍya sarva-śāstrāṇi vicārya ca punaḥ punaḥ \|*  
*idam eva suniṣpannaṁ dhyeyo nārāyaṇaḥ sadā \|\|*

**Reviewing all scriptures again and again, it is definitely concluded that Nārāyaṇa should be the object of meditation. Skanda Purāṇa**

The Lord speaks.

## Anuccheda 276

Nāma-smaraṇam will be discussed.

*harer nāma paraṁ japyaṁ dhyeyaṁ geyaṁ nirantaram \|*  
*kīrtanīyaṁ ca bahudhā nirvṛtīr bahudhecchatā \|\|*

**One who strongly desires great bliss should do japa of the name, meditate using the name, sing the name continuously and do kīrtana using the name. Jābāli-saṁhita**

Nāma-smaraṇam depends on a pure antaḥkaraṇa. It is thus inferior to kīrtana. That is not clearly indicated in the above example.

Rūpa-smaraṇam is discussed:

*avismṛtiḥ kṛṣṇa-padāravindayoḥ*  
*kṣiṇoty abhadrāṇi ca śaṁ tanoti*  
*sattvasya śuddhiṁ paramātma-bhaktiṁ*  
*jñānaṁ ca vijñāna-virāga-yuktam*

**Remembrance of Kṛṣṇa's lotus feet destroys everything inauspicious and allows one to fix the intelligence in the Lord. It purifies the heart and bestows devotion for Lord, along with knowledge enriched with realization and renunciation. SB 12.12.55**

It bestows bhakti in the form of prema for Kṛṣṇa (paramātma-bhaktim). This is the main result. Other results are secondary. Sūta speaks.

## Anuccheda 277

Moreover it is said:

*smarataḥ pāda-kamalam ātmānam api yacchati*  
*kiṁ nv artha-kāmān bhajato nāty-abhīṣṭān jagad-guruḥ*

**Since he gives even his own self to anyone who simply remembers his lotus feet, what doubt is there that he, the spiritual master of the universe, will bestow upon his sincere worshiper prosperity and material enjoyment, which are not even very desirable? SB 10.80.11**

He gives himself to one who remembers (*smarataḥ*) his lotus feet. He appears directly before the person, falling under the control of the person who remembers him. *Artha-kamān* in the plural indicates that liberation is included according to the rule that a word of the same gender can also be inferred. Because of its power, smaraṇam has been expressed in this way in Garuḍa Purāṇa:

*ekasminn apy atikrānte muhūrte dhyāna-varjite \|*  
*dasyubhir muṣiteneva yuktam ākrandituṁ bhṛśam \|\|*

**If one moment passes without meditating on the Lord one should cry out loudly as if something was stolen by a thieves.**

Śrīdāma’s wife speaks to him.

## Anuccheda 278

As previously there is a sequence in the smaraṇam to make it easy: remembering (after the name and form) the qualities, the associates, service and pastimes. These are the five types of remembrance. Thinking of the Lord somehow or other is smaraṇam. Withdrawing the mind completely from other objects and in a general way concentrating the mind on the Lord is called dhāraṇā. Contemplating the form, qualities etc. particularly is called dhyānam. When this becomes continuous it is called dhruvānusmṛtiḥ. When the Lord appears through meditation it is called samādhi.

Smaraṇam is illustrated in the following:

*yena kenāpy upāyena smṛto nārāyaṇo'vyayaḥ \|*  
*api pātaka-yuktasya prasannaḥ syān na saṁśayaḥ \|\|*

**By somemeans one should remember indestructible Nārāyaṇa. Without doubt the Lord will be pleased with even a sinful person. Bṛhan-nārādīya Purāṇa**

An example of dhāraṇā is the following:

*viṣayān dhyāyataś citta viṣayeṣu viṣajjate*  
*mām anusmarataś citta mayy eva pravilīyate*

**The mind of a person meditating upon the sense objects becomes attached to those objects. The mind of a person who constantly remembers me becomes absorbed in me.SB 11.14.27**

An example of dhyānam is the following:

*bhagavac-caraṇa-dvandva-dhyānaṁ nirdvandvam īritam \|*  
*pāpino’pi prasaṅgena vihitaṁ suhitaṁ param \|\|*

**Meditation on the two feet of the Lord is said to be beyond duality in the scriptures. It is most beneficial even for a sinful person who casually meditates. Narasiṁha Purāṇa**

That meditation makes one indifferent to the suffering caused by hot and cold etc. This is stated in scripture *(īritam*). Even a sinful person, who casually meditates, obtains excellent (*param*) benefit *(suhitam*).

Dhruvānusmṛti is illustrated in the following:

*mad-guṇa-śruti-mātreṇa mayi sarva-guhāśaye*  
*mano-gatir avicchinnā yathā gaṅgāmbhaso 'mbudhau*

**The mind, by hearing about my qualities, flows continuously to me, the Supreme Lord residing in the hearts of all people, just as the Gaṅgā flows to the ocean. SB 3.29.11**

*tri-bhuvana-vibhava-hetave 'py akuṇṭha-*  
*smṛtir ajitātma-surādibhir vimṛgyāt*  
*na calati bhagavat-padāravindāl*  
*lava-nimiṣārdham api yaḥ sa vaiṣṇavāgryaḥ*

**He, whose remembrance is not lured by dominion over the three worlds, and who does not move for half a minute or half a second from the Lord’s lotus feet which are sought by the devatāswho also meditate on the Lord, is the best of devotees. SB 11.2.53**

This process is shown by Rāmānuja in his commentary on the first sutra of Brahma-sūtras.

An example of samādhi is as follows:

*tayor āgamanaṁ sākṣād īśayor jagad-ātmanoḥ*  
*na veda ruddha-dhī-vṛttir ātmānaṁ viśvam eva ca*

**Because Mārkaṇḍeya's material mind had stopped functioning, the sage failed to notice that Lord Śiva and his wife, the controllers of the universe, had personally come to see him. Mārkaṇḍeya was unaware of either himself or the external world. SB 12.10.9**

He was not aware that Śiva and his wife *(tayoḥ*), called controllers and originators of the universe because they are śaktis of the Lord’s aṁśa, had come, because his mind was absorbed in the Lord (*ruddha-dhī-vṛttiḥ*). Previously it was stated that he had achieved the highest bhakti for the Lord. (SB 12.10.6) This is different from the asamprajñāta-samādhi of the impersonalists. Sūta speaks.

## Anuccheda 279

Sometimes there is samādhi with an exclusive appearance of the Lord engaged in pastimes.

*atho mahā-bhāga bhavān amogha-dṛk*  
*śuci-śravāḥ satya-rato dhṛta-vrataḥ \|*  
*urukramasyākhila-bandha-muktaye*  
*samādhinānusmara tad-viceṣṭitam \|\|*

**Fortunate one! Therefore, since you have useful knowledge, spotless character, dedication to truth and determined vows, remember in samādhi the pastimes of the Lord which bring about liberation from all bondage. SB 1.5.13**

The meaning is clear. This type of form is experienced by devotees with dāsya and other rasas. The previous Anuccheda described the form (with no pastimes) generally experienced by śānta devotees. This is understood in the following:

*sva-sukha-nibhṛta-cetās tad-vyudastānya-bhāvo*  
*'py ajita-rucira-līlākṛṣṭa-sāras tadīyam*  
*vyatanuta kṛpayā yas tattva-dīpaṁ purāṇaṁ*  
*tam akhila-vṛjina-ghnaṁ vyāsa-sūnuṁ nato 'smi*

**Let me offer my respectful obeisances unto the son of Vyāsadeva, Śukadeva Gosvāmī, who destroys of all sin, and who, though fixed in his own bliss with no distractions (śānta), became attracted to the pastimes of Kṛṣṇa and mercifully spread the Bhāgavatam which describes Kṛṣṇa and reveals the rasa of his pastimes. SB 12.12.69**

Nārada speaks to Vyāsa.

**Pāda-sevanam**

## Anuccheda 280

Then if one has taste and ability one should engage in serving the feet without giving up smaraṇam etc. This service is sometimes done in order to perfect smaraṇam. The Lord says:

*na me dhyāna-ratāḥ samyag yoginaḥ parituṣṭaye \|*  
*tathā bhavanti devarṣe kriyā-yoga-ratā yathā \|*  
*kriyākrameṇa yogo’pi dhyāninaḥ sampravartate \|\|*

**O Nārada! Yogīs engaged in meditation do not please me as much persons engaged in service. By service,samādhi can be accomplished by the meditator.**

*Yogaḥ* means samādhi in the verse. The word *pāda* in pāda-sevanam means “with bhakti.” Thus the service should be performed with respect and devotion. Sevā consists of service according to time and place. For instance it is said:

*yat-pāda-sevābhirucis tapasvinām*  
*aśeṣa-janmopacitaṁ malaṁ dhiyaḥ*  
*sadyaḥ kṣiṇoty anvaham edhatī satī*  
*yathā padāṅguṣṭha-viniḥsṛtā sarit*

**Like the water flowing from the big toe of the Lord, a taste of service to the Lord’s feet immediately destroys the desires in the intelligence of persons suffering in saṁsāra, which has been acquired through unlimited births. Day by day that spiritual taste increases. SB 4.21.31**

*Tapasvinām*means people suffering in saṁsāra and *malam*means desires. This is the power of the Lord’s feet. An example is given. Pṛthu speaks to Viṣṇu.

## Anuccheda 281

*na kāmaye ’nyaṁ tava pāda-sevanād*  
*akiñcana-prārthyatamād varaṁ vibho*  
*ārādhya kas tvāṁ hy apavarga-daṁ hare*  
*vṛṇīta āryo varam ātma-bandhanam*

**O all-powerful one, I desire no boon other than service to your lotus feet, the boon most eagerly sought by those who reject liberation as insignificant. O Hari, what enlightened person who worships you, the giver of bhakti, would choose a boon that causes his own bondage? SB 10.51.55**

Your feet are desired by persons devoid of even the desire for liberation *(akiñcana).* Because of that, what person who worships you should take shelter *(vṛṇīta*) of you in such a way as to give liberation? Though somewhat desirable (*varam*), anything else is bondage of the self.

The next verse says:

*tasmād visṛjyāśiṣa īśa sarvato*  
*rajas-tamaḥ-sattva-guṇānubandhanāḥ*  
*nirañjanaṁ nirguṇam advayaṁ paraṁ*  
*tvāṁ jñāpti-mātraṁ puruṣaṁ vrajāmy aham*

**Therefore, O Lord, having put aside all objects of material desire, which are bound to the modes of passion, ignorance and goodness, I am approaching you, in human form, for shelter. You are not covered by mundane designations; you are beyond the guṇas, non-dual, and pure consciousness. SB 10.51.56**

What is indicated is that the Lord is full of eternity, knowledge and bliss and this is achieved by serving his lotus feet. Mucukunda speaks to the Lord.

## Anuccheda 283

Seeing, touching, circling and following after the deity of the Lord, and bathing at the tīrthas of the Lord’s temples, or at the Gaṅgā, Puri, Dvārakā and Mathurā are included in serving the feet of the Lord since they accompany service. Living one’s whole life at a temple or tīrtha is included in śaranāpatti. Living entities residing at these holy places are the highest devotees. Thus serving these places is great service to the devotees. Thus these places are causes of bhakti.

*śuśrūṣoḥ śraddadhānasya vāsudeva-kathā-ruciḥ \|*  
*syān mahat-sevayā viprāḥ puṇya-tīrtha-niṣevaṇāt \|\|*

**Obrāhmaṇas! Attraction for topics concerning Kṛṣṇa will arise by service to the great devotees, followed by faith, with service to the pure tīrthas, and by the desire to hear. SB 1.2.16**

In this verse puṇya-tīrtha means that Gaṅgā and other holy places are separate causes of bhakti.

*yac-chauca-niḥsṛta-sarit-pravarodakena*  
*tīrthena mūrdhny adhikṛtena śivaḥ śivo 'bhūt*  
*dhyātur manaḥ-śamala-śaila-nisṛṣṭa-vajraṁ*  
*dhyāyec ciraṁ bhagavataś caraṇāravindam*

**One should meditate continually upon the Lord’s lotus feet from which the Gaṅgā flows and gives extra blessings to Lord Śiva on his head, and whose thunderbolt is released upon mountains of sin in the meditator’s mind. SB 3.28.22**

*Śiva śivo ‘bhūt* means “Śiva attained the highest happiness” according to Śrīdhara Svāmī’s commentary. That happiness is attained by bhakti since there is no higher happiness.

Speaking of Puri it is said:

*aho kṣetrasya māhātmyaṁ samantād daśa-yojanam \|*  
*diviṣṭhā yatra paśyanti sarvān eva caturbhujān \|\|*

**The glory of Puri with its surrounding area of 80 miles is inconceivable. The devatās see everyone residing there as having four arms. Brahmā Purāṇa**

*saṁvatsaraṁ vā ṣaṇ-māsān māsaṁ māsārdham eva vā \|*  
*dvārakā-vāsinaḥ sarva-narā nāryaś caturbhujāḥ \|\|*

**Anyone who lives in Dvārakā for one year, six months, one month or even half a month, whether man or woman, attains a four-handed form in the spiritual sky. Skanda Purāṇa**

*aho madhupurī dhanyā vaikuṇṭhāc ca garīyasī \|*  
*dinam ekaṁ nivāsena harau bhaktiḥ prajāyate*

**Oh, Mathurā is most auspicious, and greater than Vaikuṇṭha (Goloka)! By staying only one day in Mathurā, bhaktito the Lord appears. Padma Purāṇa**

Ādi-varāha Purāṇa says concerning Mathurā janma*-bhūmiḥ priyā mama*: my birth place is dear to me. Those places where one’s chosen deity is worshipped are to be more particularly served. Kṛṣṇa’s place will thus yield the perfect result of all places since he is the full form of Bhagavān. Thus it is said:

*mathurāṁ ca parityajya yo’nyatra kurute ratim \|*  
*mūḍho bhramati saṁsāre mohito mama māyayā \|\|*

**The fool who gives up Mathurā and develops attraction for some other place wanders in the material world birth after birth, bewildered by my māyā. Ādi-varāha Purāṇa**

Similarly service to tulasī is also included in service to devotees since she is dearest to the Lord.

*viṣṇos trailokya-nāthasya rāmasya janakātmajā \|*  
*priyā tathaiva tulasī sarva-lokaika-pāvanī \|\|*

**As Sītā is dear to Rāma, controller of the three worlds, Viṣṇu, so tulasī, purifier of all the worlds, is dear to him. Garuḍa-saṁhitā, Agastya-saṁhitā**

*ratiṁ badhnāti nānyatra tulasī-kānanaṁ vinā \|*  
*deva-devo jagat-svāmī kali-kāle viśeṣataḥ \|\|*  
*nirīkṣitā narair yais tu tulasī-vana-vāṭikā \|*  
*ropitā yais tu vidhinā samprāptaṁ paramaṁ padam \|\|*

**The Lord of the universe, Lord of lords is not attracted to any place devoid of tulasī groves, particularly in Kali-yuga. Anyone who sees a garden of tulasī or plants tulasī according to the rules attains Vaikuṇṭha. Skanda Purāṇa**

*tulasī-nāma-mātreṇa prīṇāty asura-darpa-hā*

**The Lord is pleased just by the name of tulasī.**

In this way serving the feet has been explained. Related to that, service to Gaṅgā and other places has been explained.

**Arcana**

Now arcana or deity worship will be discussed. There are sequential procedures starting with avāhana stated in the scriptures. If one has faith in this path of arcanaone should ask the details from the mantra guru of whom one has taken shelter.

*labdhvānugraha ācāryāt tena sandarśitāgamaḥ*  
*mahā-puruṣam abhyarcen mūrtyābhimatayātmanaḥ*

**Having obtained the mercy of his guru, who reveals to the disciple the method of worship, the devotee should worship the Supreme Lord in the particular personal form of the Lord the devotee finds most attractive. SB 11.3.48**

The path of arcana as described in Pañcarātra scriptures is not necessary according to Bhāgavatam, since even without it one can attain perfection of the puruṣārtha, even by any one among the methods of bhakti like śaraṇa-patti. However, if the person desires a specific relationship with the Lord,which is accomplished through the mercy of guru by the process of dīkṣā, following the path of Nārada and other sages, he should necessarily perform arcana after taking dīkṣā,.

*divyaṁ jñānaṁ yato dadyāt kuryāt pāpasya saṅkṣayam \|*  
*tasmād dīkṣeti sā proktā deśikais tattva kovidaiḥ \|\|*  
*ato guruṁ praṇamyaivaṁ sarvasvaṁ vinivedya ca \|*  
*gṛhṇīyād vaiṣṇavaṁ mantraṁ dīkṣā pūrvaṁ vidhānataḥ \|\|*

**The knowers of truth define dīkṣā as such because from it one receives divya-jñānam** ***(di*****) and sin is destroyed (*****kṣa*****). Therefore after offering respects to guru and offering everything to him one should accept a Vaiṣṇava mantra, with the aforesaid dīkṣā, according to the rules. Hari-bhakti-vilāsa 2.9-10, quoting from āgama**

Divya-jñānam means knowledge of the Lord’s svarūpa conveyed in the sacred mantra and knowledge of a particular relationship with the Lord (mentioned in the mantra). This is explained in Padma Purāṇa, Uttara-khaṇḍa in discussing the eight syllable mantra. Arcana is mainly for wealthy householders. The sages say to Vāsudeva:

*ayaṁ svasty-ayanaḥ panthā dvi-jāter gṛha-medhinaḥ*  
*yac chraddhayāpta-vittena śuklenejyeta pūruṣaḥ*

**This is the most auspicious path for a religious householder of the twice-born orders—to selflessly worship the Lord with wealth honestly obtained. SB 10.84.37**

If the wealthy householder does not do deity worship and instead become fixed in remembering or other processes like a renunciate, there will be the fault of cheating (not using his wealth for the Lord). If he arranges for others to do the worship, it means he is more inclined to material affairs or he is lazy. He is misfortunate, because of his lack of faith. He fails to follow this instruction:

*sa veda dhātuḥ padavīṁ parasya*  
*duranta-vīryasya rathāṅga-pāṇeḥ \|*  
*yo ’māyayā santatayānuvṛttyā*  
*bhajeta tat-pāda-saroja-gandham \|\|*

**He who worships the scent of the lotus feet of the Lord, sincerely, continuously, and with favorable attitude, knows the greatness of the holder of the cakra, the possessor ofinfinite prowess. SB 1.3.38**

Though there is no difference between paricārya-marga (serving the deity, not direct service) and arcana (directly serving the Lord) in terms of serving through various materials, arcana is the main method for householders because makes them follow many rules. For householders devatā worship is like watering the branches of a tree and worshipping the Lord through arcana is like watering the root. Not performing arcana is a great fault. Prahlāda says:

*keśavārcā gṛhe yasya na tiṣṭhati mahīpate \|*  
*tasyānnaṁ naiva bhoktavyam abhakṣyeṇa samaṁ smṛtam \|\|*

**O king! If a person does not have a deity in his house his food should not be eaten. It should be considered equal to uneatable food. Skanda Purāṇa**

Person who have received dīkṣā but do not worship the deity are said to fall to hell.

*eka-kālaṁ dvi-kālaṁ vā tri-kālaṁ pūjayed dharim \|*  
*apūjya-bhojanaṁ kurvan narakāṇi vrajen naraḥ \|\|*

**One should worship the Lord once, twice or three times a day. If one eats one’s food without doing deity worship one goes to hell. Viṣṇu-dharmottara**

If one cannot do deity worship or is unqualified, it is said:

*pūjitaṁ pūjyamānaṁ vā yaḥ paśyed bhaktimato harim \|*  
*śraddhayā modayed yas tu so’pi yoga-phalaṁ labhet \|\|*

**If a person one sees with devotion the Lord being worshipped or after it has been worshipped, who permits the worship with faith, he attains the results of performing the worship. Agni Purāṇa**

The word *yoga* in the verses means kriya-yoga explained in Pañcarātra scriptures. Sometimes mānasa-puja is prescribed if one is incapable or unqualified of doing external worship. It is said:

*sādhāraṇaṁ hi sarveṣāṁ mānasejyā nṝṇāṁ priyā*

**Mānasa-pūja is suitable for all ordinary men. Padma Purāṇa**

Moreover on the path of arcana one must follow the rules. Therefore before doing deity worship one must take dīkṣā. (This is one rule for arcana.) Then the rules concerning worship should be learnedfrom scripture. Concerning dīkṣā it is said:

*dvijānām anupetānāṁ sva-karmādhyayanādiṣu \|*  
*yathādhikāro nāstīha syāc copanayanād anu \|\|*  
*tathātrādīkṣitānāṁ tu mantra devārcanādiṣu \|*  
*nādhikāro’sty ataḥ kuryād ātmānaṁ śiva saṁstutam \|\|*

**Just as brāhmaṇas who have not taken the thread are not qualified for studying the Vedas or other duties and gain qualification from receiving the thread, those who have not taken dīkṣā are not qualified for uttering the mantra or worshipping the deity. Therefore one should undergo dīkṣā and be praised by Śiva. Hari-bhakti-vilāsa 2.3-4**

Worship should follow the scriptures rules:

*avijñāya vidhānoktaṁ hari-pūjā-vidhi-kriyām \|*  
*kurvan bhaktyā samāpnoti śata-bhāgaṁ vidhānataḥ \|\|*

**If one worships with devotion but does not know the method of worshipping the Lord according to scriptural rules, one attains only one percent of the result. Viṣṇu-rahasya**

Because of the great respect in the worship (*bhaktyā*) one receives one percent results. If there is not that much devotion there is less result. The authority for the rules is in accordance with a Vaiṣṇava sampradāya.

*arcayanti sadā viṣṇuṁ mano-vāk-kāya-karmabhiḥ \|*  
*teṣāṁ hi vacanaṁ grāhyaṁ te hi viṣṇu-samā matāḥ \|\|*

**One should accept the words of those who worship Viṣṇu at all times with mind, words, body and actions. They are considered to be equal to Viṣṇu. Viṣṇu-rahasya**

*saṁpṛṣṭvā vaiṣṇavān viprān viṣṇu-śāstra-viśāradān \|*  
*cīrṇa-vratān sadācārān tad uktaṁ yatnataś caret \|\|*

**Having inquired from Vaiṣṇavas who are brāhmaṇas, expert in Vaiṣṇava scriptures and long practicing with good conduct, one should follow their instructions with care. Kūrma Purāṇa**

*yeṣāṁ gurau ca japye ca viṣṇau ca paramātmani \|*  
*nāsti bhaktiḥ sadā teṣāṁ vacanaṁ parivarjayet \|\|*

**One should reject the instructions of those who do not have bhakti for guru, the mantra (given by guru), and Viṣṇu, the Paramātmā. Vaiṣnava-tantra**

Concerning Ambarīṣa it is said:

*evaṁ sadā karma-kalāpam ātmanaḥ*  
*pare 'dhiyajñe bhagavaty adhokṣaje*  
*sarvātma-bhāvaṁ vidadhan mahīm imāṁ*  
*tan-niṣṭha-viprābhihitaḥ śaśāsa ha*

**Performing devotional acts daily with rati by his mind and senses to the Supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa, whose worship is superior to other processes, he ruled the world, taking advice from devotee brāhmaṇas. SB 9.4.21**

Śukadeva speaks.

## Anuccheda 284

“The mantra’s essential element is the name of the Lord. That is additionally ornamented by words like *namaḥ.* Endowed with special śakti by the Lord and the sages (who reveal the mantra), it produces a particular relationship between the self and the Lord. If the names of the Lord are independent and have the ability by themselves of bestowing all results up to the highest human goal (prema), since the mantras have more power than the name, how can the mantras be dependent on dīkṣā?.

Even though by its svarūpa the mantra is not dependent on dīkṣā, generally for persons of low nature and distracted minds because of relationship with their bodies etc.arising from their nature, the sages have at some time established some rules on the path of arcana for curtailing that low nature. If one breaks those rules one must perform atonement according to scripture.

There is thus no contradiction between the mantra and the name. The mantra and the name do not depend on dīkṣā. Concerning Rāmacandra it is said:

*vaiṣṇaveṣv api mantreṣu rāma-mantrāḥ phalādhikāḥ \|*  
*gāṇapaty-ādi-mantrebhyaḥ koṭi-koṭi-guṇādhikāḥ \|\|*  
*vinaiva dīkṣāṁ viprendra puraścaryāṁ vinaiva hi \|*  
*vinaiva nyāsa-vidhinā japa-mātreṇa siddhidāḥ \|\|*

**The mantras of Rāma bestow more results than other Vaiṣṇava mantras. They have qualities billions of times more than Ganeśa mantras. O best of the brāhmaṇas! They give results just by japa, without dīkṣā, without purification rites, without performance of nyāsa. Rāmārcana-candrikā**

Thus there is no need to test the mantra according to procedures of sādhya, siddha etc. in the puraścarya rites.It is said:

*saura-mantrāś ca ye’pi syur vaiṣṇavā nārasiṁhakāḥ \|*  
*sādhya-siddha-susiddhāri-vicāra-parivarjitāḥ \|\|*

**Mantras of the sun god, Viṣṇu and Narasiṁha do not require sādhya, siddha, susiddha and ari considerations. Mantra-deva-parīkṣā**

*nṛsiṁhārka-varāhāṇāṁ prasāda-pravaṇasya ca \|*  
*vaidikasya ca mantrasya siddhādīn naiva śodhayet \|\|*

**One should not purify the merciful mantras of the sun, Nṛsiṁha, or Varāha, or Vedic mantras, according to the methods of siddha etc. quoted in Hari-bhakti-vilāsa 1.210**

*sādhyaḥ siddhaḥ susiddhaś ca ariś caiva ca nārada \|*  
*gopāleṣu na boddhavyaḥ sva-prakāśo yataḥ smṛtaḥ \|\|*

**O Nārada! Sādhya, siddha, susiddha and ari are not applied to the mantras of Kṛṣṇa since Kṛṣṇa mantras are considered self-manifesting. Sanat-kumāra-saṁhitā**

*sarveṣu varṇeṣu tathāśrameṣ.*  
*nārīṣu nānāhvaya-janmabheṣu \|*  
*dātā phalānām abhivāñchitānā.*  
*prāg eva gopālaka-mantra eṣaḥ \|\|*

**The mantra of Gopāla directly bestows results desired to men of all varṇas and āśramas, to women, and to people born under various constellations. Nārada-pañcarātra**

Respect for the rules is explained:

*śruti-smṛti-purāṇādi-pañcarātra-vidhiṁ vinā*  
*aikāntikī harer bhaktir utpātāyaiva kalpate \|\|*

**Without the rules of śruti, smṛti, Purāṇas, and Pañcarātra pure bhakti to the Lord is subject to calamity. Brahma-yāmala**

Thus the earth says:

*asmiḹ loke 'thavāmuṣmin munibhis tattva-darśibhiḥ*  
*dṛṣṭā yogāḥ prayuktāś ca puṁsāṁ śreyaḥ-prasiddhaye*

**The sages who have seen the truth have acknowledged methods for attaining human benefit in this life and the next, and put them into practice.**

*tān ātiṣṭhati yaḥ samyag upāyān pūrva-darśitān*  
*avaraḥ śraddhayopeta upeyān vindate 'ñjasā*

**The inexperienced person, having faith, who applies these methods previously shown, easily attains the desire results.**

*tān anādṛtya yo 'vidvān arthān ārabhate svayam*  
*tasya vyabhicaranty arthā ārabdhāś ca punaḥ punaḥ*

**The goals of a fool who, ignoring the authorized methods, acts independently, and attempts repeatedly, are not fulfilled. SB 4.18.3-5**

Therefore in the discussion between Nārāyaṇa and Nārada it is said.

*mad-bhakto yo mad-arcāṁ ca karoti vidhivad ṛṣe \|*  
*tasyāntarāyāḥ svapne’pi na bhavanty abhayo hi saḥ \|\|*

**O sage! The devotee who performs arcana to me according to the rules become fearless and has no disturbances in dreams. Padma Purāṇa**

There are two types of arcana: pure and mixed with karma. Pure arcana is practiced by persons with faith, with no dependence at all on anything. Āvirhotra explains pure arcana:

*ya āśu hṛdaya-granthiṁ nirjihīṛṣuḥ parātmanaḥ*  
*vidhinopacared devaṁ tantroktena ca keśavam*

**One who desires to cut the knot of false ego, which binds the spirit soul, should worship the Supreme Lord, Keśava, by the regulations found in literatures such as the Pañcarātra and the Vedas. SB 11.3.47**

Nārada also says:

*yadā yasyānugṛhṇāti bhagavān ātma-bhāvitaḥ*  
*sa jahāti matiṁ loke vede ca pariniṣṭhitām*

**When the Lord shows mercy to the devotee, the devotee gives up thinking of karma-kāṇḍaand material affairs. SB 4.29.46**

*yathā vidhi-niṣedhau ca muktaṁ naivopasarpataḥ \|*  
*tathā na spṛśato rāmopāsakaṁ vidhi-pūrvakam \|\|*

**Just rules and prohibitions (of karma) are not required for the liberated person, they are not necessary for the worshipper of Rāma who follows (pañcarātra) rules. Agastya-saṁhit.**

Mixed worship is performed by persons with faith based on haphazard performance of bhakti with strong inclination to traditional customs. There are also performers of mixed arcana who are householders with fixed faith in scriptural procedures, unlike the first type, who feel obligated to teach ordinary people. They think that one should not disregard performance of ordinary Vedi.

karmas and thus they put on a show for persons who cannot understand the path of bhakti. Here is an example of arcana mixed with karma:

*na hy anto ’nanta-pārasya karma-kāṇḍasya coddhava*  
*saṅkṣiptaṁ varṇayiṣyāmi yathāvad anupūrvaśaḥ*

**O Uddhava! There is no end to the details of deity worship; so I shall explain this topic to you briefly, one step at a time. SB 11.27.6**

*sandhyopāstyādi-karmāṇi vedenācoditāni me*  
*pūjāṁ taiḥ kalpayet samyak- saṅkalpaḥ karma-pāvanīm*

**Completely satisfied, the person should perform my worship, which destroys karma, along with performing his various prescribed duties, such as chanting the Gāyatrī mantra at the three junctures of the day mentioned in the Vedas. SB 11.29.11**

The meaning is clear. The Lord speaks.

## Anuccheda 285

In discussing faith, Nārāyaṇa says:

*nācared yas tu siddho’pi laukikaṁ dharmam agrataḥ \|*  
*upaplavāc ca dharmasya glānir bhavati nāradaḥ \|\|*  
*vivekajñair ataḥ sarvair lokācāro yathā-sthitaḥ \|*  
*ādeha-pātād yatnena rakṣaṇīyaḥ prayatnataḥ \|\|*

**O Nārada! He who does not perform, before anything else, material dharma, using the excuse of calamity, even if he is perfected, strays from dharma. All intelligent persons should carefully protect common conduct with effort until death. Nārada-pañcarātra**

These two types of worship with karma elements should be performed while thinking of antaryāmī Viṣṇu..

The following is also prescribed:

*viṣṇu-pādodakenaiva pitṝṇāṁ tarpaṇa-kriyā \|*  
*viṣṇor niveditānnena yaṣṭavyaṁ devatāntaram \|\|*

**Tarpana to the pitas should be done using the foot water of Viṣṇu. Devatās should be offered the remnants of Viṣṇu’s offering. Viṣṇu-yamala**

The devatās such as Durgā and Ganeśa in the worship of the āvaraṇa during worship are seen as the Lord’s eternal servitors in Vaikuṇṭha such as Viṣvaksena. They are not the Ganeśa and Durgā composed of māyā-śakti.

*pravartate yatra rajas tamas tayoḥ*  
*sattvaṁ ca miśraṁ na ca kāla-vikramaḥ \|*  
*na yatra māyā kim utāpare harer*  
*anuvratā yatra surāsurārcitāḥ \|\|*

**In Vaikuṇṭha there areno rajas or tamas, and no sattva mixed with rajas and tamas. There is no influence of time. There is no influence of māyā at all, what to speak of its products such as material elements. In Vaikuṇṭha the inhabitants are fully dedicated to the Lord and are worshipable by the devas, asuras and devotees. SB 2.9.10**

Rather these people are filled with the Lord’s svarūpa-śakti. The controlling position of the special function of the śakti emanating from Kṛṣṇa’s svarūpa is described in the śruti and tantras.

In the discussion of Śruti-vidyā it is said:

*bhaktir bhajana-sampattir bhajate prakṛtiḥ priyam \|*  
*jñāyate’tyanta-duḥkhena seyaṁ prakṛtir ātmanaḥ \|\|*  
*durgeti gīyate sadbhir akhaṇḍa-rasa-vallabhā \|\|*

**Bhakti, who is known as prakṛti, endowed with a wealth of worship, worships her dear Lord. She, the prakṛti of the Lord, is known with great difficulty. Thus the devotees glorify her, dear to the Lord of rasa, as Durgā (difficult to approach).**

Her non-difference from the Lord is stated in Gautamīya-kalpa.

*yaḥ kṛṣṇaḥ saiva durgā syād yā durgā kṛṣṇa eva saḥ*

**He who is Kṛṣṇa is Durgā. She who is Durgā is Kṛṣṇa.**

*tvam eva parameśāni asyādhiṣṭhātṛ-devatā*

**You are the supreme goddess, the controlling deity.**

This hints at worshipping the Lord and Durgā as non-different, just asworship of the universal form and Mahāviṣṇuare treatedas non-different.

Durgā is a portion of māyā, engaged to serveby protecting the mantra in this world,which is dependent on the Lord. She is the servant of the spiritual Durgā and is not the controller of bhaktiservice. Padma Purāṇa described the āvaraṇa devatās beyond māyā in Vaikuṇṭha:

*satyācyutānanta-durgā-viṣvaksena-gajānanāḥ \|*  
*śaṅkha-padma-nidhī lokāś caturthāvaraṇaṁ śubham \|\|*  
*aindra-pāvaka-yāmyāni nairṛtaṁ vāruṇaṁ tathā \|*  
*vāyavyaṁ saumyam aiśānaṁ saptamaṁ munibhiḥ smṛtam \|\|*  
*sādhyā marud-gaṇāś caiva viśvedevās tathaiva ca \|*  
*nityāḥ sarve pare dhāmni ye cānye ca divaukasaḥ \|\|*  
*te vai prākṛta-loke’sminn anityās tridaśeśvarāḥ \|*  
*te ha nākaṁ mahimānaḥ sacanta iti vai śrutiḥ \|\|*

**Durgā, Viṣvaksena and Ganeśa made of eternity, knowledge and bliss, Śaṅkha-nidhi and Padma-nidhi are in the fourth glorious circle. Indra, Agni, Yama, Nairṛta, Varuṇa, Vāyu, Saumya and Śiva are in the seventh circle. The Sādhyas, Maruts, and Viśvadevas are also there. All the others in the spiritual world are also eternal. Those in the material heavenly planets are temporary devatās.**

Śruti says “The devatās associate with the Lord in the spiritual world.” Padma Purāṇa 6.228.60, 64-66

They are aṁśas of the Lord. In describing the different devatās of the eighteen syllable mantra it is said:

*sarvatra deva-devo’sau gopa-veśa-dharo hariḥ \|*  
*kevalaṁ rūpa-bhedena nāma-bhedaḥ prakīrtitaḥ \|\|*

The Lord, wearing the dress of a cowherd, the Lord of Lords, having various names, is everywhere glorified in different forms.

The ananya-bhaktas should not worry that the spiritual devatās share the same name with their material counterparts. Viṣvaksena and others should be honored since they are engaged in eternal service to the Lord in Vaikuṇṭha. Nor worshipping them is considered a fault. It is said “He who never identifies himself with, feels kinship with, worships or even visits those who are wise in spiritual truth (devotees)—such a person is no better than a cow or an ass.” (SB 10.84.13)

*arcayitvā tu govindaṁ tadīyān nārcayet tu yaḥ*  
*na sa bhāgavato jñeyaḥ kevalam dāmbhikaḥ smṛtaḥ*

**He who after worshipping Govinda does not worship his followersis not known as devotee. He is simply a pretender. Padma Purāṇa 6.253.177**

Thus it is said:

*durgāṁ vināyakaṁ vyāsaṁ viṣvakṣenaṁ gurūn surān*  
*sve sve sthāne tv abhimukhān pūjayet prokṣaṇādibhiḥ*

**With prokṣaṇa and other items, one should worship Durgā, Ganeśa, Vyāsa, Viṣvaksena, the gurus and the various devatās. All these personalities should be in their proper places facing the deity of the Lord. SB 11.27.29**

*tasmād avaidikānāṁ ca devānām arcanaṁ tyajet \|*  
*svatantra-pūjanaṁ yatra vaidikānām api tyajet \|\|*  
*arcayitvā jagad-vandyaṁ devaṁ nārāyaṇaṁ harim \|*  
*tad-āvaraṇa-saṁsthānaṁ devasya parito'rcayet \|\|*  
*harer bhuktāvaśeṣeṇa baliṁ tebhyo viniḥkṣipet\|*  
*homaṁ caiva prakurvīta tac-cheṣeṇaiva vaiṣṇavaḥ \|\|*

**One should reject worship of devatās and forms not authorized by the Vedas. Even if the forms are authorized, if the worship is concocted, it should be rejected. After worshipping Nārāyaṇa, worshipped by the universe, one should worship the āvaraṇa devatās. PadmaPurāṇa 6.253.103-6**

The Lord speaks.

## Anuccheda 286

Though worship of Bhūtasis also prescribed as an aṅga of worship it is not to be done since they are not part of the devatā āvaraṇa. It is forbidden:

*yakṣāṇāṁ ca piśācānāṁ madya-māṁsa-bhujāṁ tathā \|*  
*divaukasāṁ tu bhajanaṁ surāpāna-samaṁ smṛtam \|\|*

**Worship of Yakṣas, Piśācas or celestial beings who drink liquor and eat meat is equal to drinking liquor. Padma Purāṇa 6.253.95**

Therefore worship of others who should be worshipped, by offering wine, even though they accept it, is forbidden. An example is Balarāma.

In worshipping the pīṭha ,though adharma and the three guṇas are present there (and to be worshipped) they are clearly not mentioned in the Padma Purāṇa or in Svāyambhuva Āgama. Therefore they are not to be worshipped.Some explain them in another way according to Nārada-pañcarātra. There it is said adharmādy-ādya-catuṣkaṁ tu aśreyasi niyojanam: adharma, ajñāna, avairāgya and anaiśvarya are used in inauspicious events. The meaning is that these items are mentioned with the meaning that the Lord is the antaryāmī śakti in these items.

In the pītha-pūja, worship of the guru’s shoes is prescribed in the Lord’s abode. Just as the Lord exists in the form of guru in an individual form (vyasti-rūpa) as a devotee avatāra, the Lord exists in the pīṭha in the Lord’s abode in this form of guru’s shoes as the total form (samaṣṭi-rūpa) as a direct avatāra of the Lord, to the deity’s left side.

The āvaraṇa deities like Mainda and Dvivida in worship of Rāma are nitya-siddhas dwelling in the Lord’s eternal abode. When Akrūra performed agha-marṣaṇa in the Yamunā he saw Prahlāda and others surrounding Viṣṇu. Prahlāda also became the calf when Pṛthu milked the earth. This is the eternal Prahlāda since at that time Prahlāda had not been born on earth during Svāyambhuva Manvantara. He was born during Cākṣuṣa Manvantara at the time of Hiraṇyakaśipu.

There are others however, jīvas, who are endowed with some of the śakti of eternal associates like Mainda and Dvivida (and are called by the same name) to help Rāma or other avatāras when they appear on earth, though Rāma and others reside eternally in their spiritual abodes as well. These jīvas became corrupted. Mainda associated with Bāli who hated the devotee Sugrīva. Later, Dvivida associated with Narakāsura who hated the Lord. This occurs because the Lord’s appearance with his devoteesis mixed with elements of the material world (jīvas invested with nitya-siddhas’ powers).

In worship of Kṛṣṇa in Gokula there is a prescription for worshipping āvaraṇas including Rukmiṇī and others. These are just particular śaktis representing them, similar to Vimalā and others, and are invisible in Gokula. They do not appear in the pastimes of Gokula. Therefore they are not mentioned in meditating on Gokula. Some consider that Rukmiṇī and others are just the names of Rādhā and other gopīs. Similarly, some accept wearing the symbols of conch, cakra and club (symbols of Viṣṇu) to be the marks on Kṛṣṇa’s feet (rather than Viṣṇu’s weapons). Though it is prescribed to worship Gaṅgā and Yamunā at the door of the temple, for Kṛṣṇa worshippers Gaṅgā is considered to be the famous Mānasa-gaṅgā at Govardhana. Similarly Viṣvaksena (of Vaikuṇṭha) is identified as Bhadrasena in Gokula. In worshipping Kṛṣṇa’s pīṭha there is worship of the milk ocean of Śvetadvīpa (where Viṣṇu resides). But in Kṛṣṇa worship Śvetadvīpa is considered to be another name of Goloka(area surrounding Gokula) and there millions of kāmadhenus produce abundant milk, making it like the milk ocean.

*sa yatra kṣīrābdhiḥ sravati surabhībhyaś ca su-mahān*  
*nimeṣārdhākhyo vā vrajati na hi yatrāpi samayaḥ \|*  
*bhaje śvetadvīpaṁ tam aham iha golokam iti yaṁ*  
*vidantas te santaḥ kṣiti-virala-cārāḥ katipaye \|\|*

**I worship Goloka, pure and uncontaminated, where extensive oceans of milk flow from the cows, where not even a moment of time passes, and which only a few rare devotees wandering on this earth have realized. Brahma-saṁhit.**

Other discrepencies should be understood in the same way. For instance there are sun, moon and fire worshipped in the pīṭha. These are spiritual items since the spiritual world is devoid of qualities of intense heat or cold. Objects named in the spiritual world have all auspicious qualities since material qualities are forbidden there.

*tad vā etat paramaṁ dhāma mantra-rājādhyāpakasya yatra na duḥkhādi yatra na sūryas tapati yatra na vāyur vāti yatra na candramā bhāti yatra na nakṣatrāṇi bhānti yatra nāgnir dahati yatra na mṛtyuḥ praviśati yatra na doṣaḥ \|*

**That is the supreme abode of the controller of the king of mantras, where there is no suffering, no sun shining, no wind blowing, no moon shining, no contellations shining, no fire burning, no death entering, no fault at all. Nṛsiṁha-tāpanī Upaniṣad 5.9**

In relation to discussing the elemination elements of karma from deity worship, the associates of the Lord have been discussed.

(Now removal of karma elements continues.)

Those dedicated to serving the Lord can perform bhūta-suddhi (purification of elements in the body) by meditating on possessing a body of an associate of the Lord suitable for service to one’s cherished form of the Lord(for instance a cowherd boy or gopī for a Kṛṣṇa worshipper) since it is favorable for one’s devotional development.

Wherever there is prescribed meditation on identiyfing oneself with the form of the Lord, one should accept the identity of an associate since the śuddha bhaktas detest ahaṅgrahopāsana. The oneness is general since the associates are portions of viśuddha-sattva, a function of the Lord’s cic-cakti.

When it comes to placing deities on the lower parts of the body in keśavādi-nyāsa,one should meditate on the deity, chant the appropriate mantras for that part of the body, and just touch the specific limb. One should not meditate on placing the form of the Lord represented in the mantra on the lower part of one’s body since that would be inappropriate for a devotee.

The principle meditation of the form of the Lord is the form within the Lord’s abode.Yogīs meditate on a form in the heart. *Smared vṛndāvane ramye*: one should remember the Lord in pleasant Vṛndāvana. (Mṛtuṅjaya-saṁhitā.

During mānasa-pūja one should meditate on the Lord in his abode also.In meditation on kāma-gāyatrī, when the sun is mentioned, one should think of the Lord in his abode only, for it is said *goloka eva nivasati*: he resides *only* in Goloka. (Brahma-saṁhitā) The Lord of Vṛndāvana does not directly reside in the sun. He resides in the sun as a glowing likeness of himself.

In using external objects like the flute in internal worship, there is a prescription for meditating one’s limbs merging with the effulgence from the Lord’s limbs (ahaṁgrahopāsana). Instead of that, one should meditate on placing the flute in the Lords’ mouth, instead of one’s own mouth.

There is a prescription to show various mūdras like flute, garland and śrīvatsa and for instance, placing flute (shown in mūdra) to one’s mouth.One should think of the flute or another objects which is dear to the Lord, being shown to him rather than being places in one’s mouth since that would be ahaṁgrahopāsana.

In mānasa-pūja and at other times, one thinks of the pastimes of the Lord with his associates which took place long ago. One should not think of these pastimes as imaginary, but rather real, since the pastimes and associates that manifested when the Lord was present on earth are eternal and unlimited , though invisible (aprakaṭa), in his abode.

Demons are not actually present in the spiritual abode, but illusory likenesses represent them.

*evaṁ vihāraiḥ kaumāraiḥ kaumāraṁ jahatur vraje*  
*nilāyanaiḥ setu-bandhair markaṭotplavanādibhiḥ*

In this way the boys spent their childhood in the land of Vṛndāvana playing hide-and-go-seek, building play bridges, jumping about like monkeys and engaging in many other such games. SB 10.14.61

Since in the spiritual pastimes there will be imitations of demons for fun, this is how demons’ presence in the spiritual world is explained in Bhagavat Sandarbha.

In glorifying mānasa-pūja the Lord says:

*ayaṁ yo mānaso yogo jarā-vyādhi-bhayāpaha.*  
*yaś caitat parayā bhaktyā sakṛt kuryān mahāmate \|*  
*kramoditena vidhinā tasya tuṣyāmy ahaṁ mune \|\|*

**Mānasa-pūja counteracts fear of old age and illness. O great soul! I am pleased with the person who even once performs mānasa-pūja with intense bhakti according to the rules in order.**

Mānasa-pūja may be done independently of the arcana process (which is largely external) since mental deity is one of the eight forms of the Lord used in deity worship. *Arcādau hṛdaye cāpi yathā-labdhopacārakaiḥ:* the devotee should prepare properly obtained ingredients, the earth, the mind and the deity. (SB 11.3.50)

The places for performing worship are now discussed. They are of various sorts. The śālagrāma should be thought of as the place where the form of the Lord resides. The śalagrāma is different from the Lord’s form for it is said *śālagrāma-śilā yatra tatra sannihito hariḥ*: where there is a śālagrāma there the Lord is present. Thinking of the śālagrāma as the place where one’s cherished form of the Lord manifests produces results easily since without effort the Lord can appear there.

*labdhvānugraha ācāryāt tena sandarśitāgamaḥ*  
*mahā-puruṣam abhyarcen mūrtyābhimatayātmanaḥ*

Having obtained the mercy of his guru*,* who reveals to the disciple the injunctions of Vedic scriptures, the devotee should worship the Supreme Lord in the particular personal form of the Lord the devotee finds most attractive. SB 11.3.48

However, powerful locations of Kṛṣṇa and other forms are places like Mathurā.

*rājadhānī tataḥ sābhūt sarva-yādava-bhūbhujām*  
*mathurā bhagavān yatra nityaṁ sannihito hariḥ*

**Since that time, the city of Mathurā had been the capital of all the kings of the Yadu dynasty. The city and district of Mathurā are very intimately connected with Kṛṣṇa, for Kṛṣṇa lives there eternally. SB 10.1.28**

Thus in Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad, Mathurā and Vṛndāvana are described as having great powers and are the subject of meditation in the deitymantras.The Lord is contemplated in these mantras, with Mathurā and other sacred placesbeing revealed by meditation, even though one is situated elsewhere.

In the deity form one thinks of the Lord’s form since the two are non-different for it is a fault to think that the deity form is merely stone. The Lord himself says:

*calācaleti dvi-vidhā pratiṣṭhā jīva-mandiram*  
*udvāsāvāhane na staḥ sthirāyām uddhavārcane*

**O Uddhava! The deity form of the Lord, nondifferent from me, the shelter of all living entities, can be established in two ways: as a movable or immovable form. But a deity, having been installed, is not subject to calling and sending away. SB 11.27.13**

The deity *(pratiṣṭhā)* is the temple of me, Paramātmā, the life of the jīva (*jīva-mandiram*). The deity is the shelter, non-different from the Lord, of all my limbs. Or the deity established by actions called pratiṣṭhā is my shelter. Thus in Hayaśīrṣa-pañcarātra, in discussing establishing deities,the following is found after the sānnidhya-karaṇa-mantra:

*yac ca te paramaṁ tattvaṁ yac ca jñāna-mayaṁ vapuḥ \|*  
*tat sarvam ekato līnam asmin dehe vibudhyatām \|\|*

**That supreme entity with body full of knowledge should be considered to be merged completely with the deity form.**

Or another meaning is the deity is directly the Lord who is the supreme shelter of all jīvas (*jīva-mandiram*). The great devotees see the deity as directly the Supreme Lord since by seeing the deity and Lord as different, their bhakti will be interrupted. Taking this view, the Lord says:

*vastropavītābharaṇa- patra-srag-gandha-lepanaiḥ*  
*alaṅkurvīta sa-prema mad-bhakto māṁ yathocitam*

**My devotee should then lovingly decorate me with clothing, a sacred thread, various ornaments,** ***tulasī*** **garlands, and he should anoint my body with unguents, all in the prescribed manner.SB 1111.27.32**

The Lord identifies the deity as me*(mām).* The devotee decorates the deity in prema (*sa-prema*).

Viṣṇu says to Ambarīṣa in Viṣṇu-dharma:

*tasyāṁ cittaṁ samāveśya tyaja cānyān vyāpāśrayān \|*  
*pūjitā saiva te bhaktyā dhyātā caivopakāriṇī \|\|*  
*gacchaṁs tiṣṭhan svapan bhuñjaṁs tām evāgre ca pṛṣṭhataḥ \|*  
*upary-adhas tathā pārśve citnayaṁs tām athātmanaḥ \|\|*

**Concentrating the mind on that form, giving up other thoughts, one should worship and meditate on the form, which is the benefactor. While moving, standing, lying down, and eating, one should think of the deity of the Lord to be in front, behind, above, below and on the side of oneself.**

The process of āvāhana etc in deity worship is explained:

*āvāhanaṁ cādareṇa sammukhīkaraṇaṁ prabhoḥ \|*  
*bhaktyā niveśanaṁ tasya saṁsthāpanam udāhṛtam \|\|*  
*tavāsmīti tadīyatva-darśanaṁ sannidhāpanam \|*  
*kriyā-samāpti-paryanta-sthāpanaṁ sannibodhanam \|\|*  
*sakalīkaraṇaṁ proktaṁ tat-sarvāṅga-prakāśanam \|\|*

**Bringing the Lord respectfully is called āvāhanam. Establishing him in that place with devotion is called sammukhī-karaṇam. Showing the Lord “I am yours” is called sannidhāpanam. Making the Lord stay until the worship is finished is called sannirodhanam (or avagunthanam). Requesting the Lord to reveal all his limbs is called sakalī-karaṇam.**

The injunction that śūdras and other cannot worship the deity applies to persons who are not Vaiṣṇavas.

*na śūdrā bhagavad-bhaktās te tu bhāgavatā narāḥ \|*  
*sarva-varṇeṣu te śūdrā ye na bhaktā janārdane \|\|*

**Devotees of the Lord are not śūdras but rather men of the Lord. O Lord! Those who are not devotees in all varṇas are actually śūdras. Padma Purāṇa**

Starting SB 7.14.34 and the next chapter Nārada explains that in considering factors which make place of worship excellent, men in general are superior to the deity forms (SB 7.14.41), and among men, the jñānī, the person who takes shelter of bhakti to attain liberation, is better. Summarizing the subject SB 7.15.2 indicates that the jñānīs are the best object of charity. But elsewhere it is said that the devotee is superior. *Na me bhaktaś caturvedī*: the knower of the four Vedas is not my devotee.

*nāyaṁ sukhāpo bhagavān dehināṁ gopikā-sutaḥ*  
*jñānināṁ cātma-bhūtānāṁ yathā bhaktimatām iha*

**The Lord, son of Yaśodā, is not easily available to materialists or to jñānīs with realization of ātmā, but is easily available to persons with devotion to the son of Yaśodā. SB 10.9.21**

*muktānām api siddhānāṁ nārāyaṇa-parāyaṇaḥ*  
*su-durlabhaḥ praśāntātmā koṭiṣv api mahā-mune*

**O great sage! Greater than those who are jīvanmukta and greater than those who attain liberation is the devotee of Lord Nārāyaṇa. Such a devotee, who is in śānta or other rasas, is very rare, even among ten million people. SB 6.14.5**

What then to speak of the object of worship of the devotee, the deity form?

*nānuvrajati yo mohād vrajantaṁ jagad-īśvaram \|*  
*jñānāgni-dagdha-karmāpi sa bhaved brahma-rākṣasaḥ \|\|*

**He who does not follow the Lord in his chariot when he moves becomes a brahma-rākṣasa even though he has destroyed all karmas with the fire of knowledge.**

In order, the meaning of the verses starting with SB 7.14.34 will be discussed.

*pātraṁ tv atra niruktaṁ vai kavibhiḥ pātra-vittamaiḥ*  
*harir evaika urvīśa yan-mayaṁ vai carācaram*

**O King of the earth! It has been decided by learned scholars that only the Supreme Lord, in whom all that is moving or nonmoving within this universe is resting, is the best person to whom everything should be given.**

*devarṣy-arhatsu vai satsu tatra brahmātmajādiṣu*  
*rājan yad agra-pūjāyāṁ mataḥ pātratayācyutaḥ*

**O King Yudhiṣṭhira! The venerable sages, the devatās and the sons of Lord Brahmā present at your Rājasūya sacrifice decided that Kṛṣṇa was qualified as the first person to be worshiped. SB 7.14.34-35**

*Tatra* means “at the rājasūya sacrifice.”

## Anuccheda 287

*jīva-rāśibhir ākīrṇa aṇḍa-kośāṅghripo mahān*  
*tan-mūlatvād acyutejyā sarva-jīvātma-tarpaṇam*

**The universe is a huge tree filled with billions of jīvas. Worship of Acyuta is the tarpaṇa for all jīvas and the self since the Lord is the root of the tree. SB 8.14.36**

*Sarva-jīvātma-tarpaṇam*means that deity worship is the tarpaṇa for all jīvas and for the self.

## Anuccheda 288

*purāṇy anena sṛṣṭāni nṛ-tiryag-ṛṣi-devatāḥ*  
*śete jīvena rūpeṇa pureṣu puruṣo hy asau*

**The Lord has created bodies for the jīvas in human, animal, sage and devatā forms. The Lord resides as Paramātmā in all their bodies. SB 7.1.37**

The Lord resides as the antaryāmī (*jīvena rūpeṇa*) in all bodies.

*teṣv eva bhagavān rajas tāratamyena vartate*  
*tasmāt pātraṁ hi puruṣo yāvān ātmā yatheyate*

**O King! The Paramātmā resides in all beings proportionately. Therefore man in general is the object of worship according to the degree of their knowledge and renunciation. SB 7.14.38**

According to degrees of the Lord’s presence, man in general (*puruṣaḥ)* is the object of worship. The jñānī is best because the Lord’s presence is stronger there. And in the jñānī according to the amount of jñāna that person is the object of worship.

## Anuccheda 290

Though this is so, over time the worshipper has developed faults. The Lord thus revealed a different, special location of the Lordfollowing the Vedas:

*dṛṣṭvā teṣāṁ mitho nṛṇām avajñānātmatāṁ nṛpa*  
*tretādiṣu harer arcā kriyāyai kavibhiḥ kṛtā*

**My dear King, when great sages saw that people were disrespectful of each other at the beginning of Tretā-yuga, deity worship in the temple was introduced. SB 7.14.39**

Seeing that people thought of themselves while disrespecting others the wise created the deity form for worship (*kriyayai)*. This was revealed in order to show the method of serving the Lord. From this it is understood that the deity form is superior to humans as an object of worship since it is effective for people with the fault of disrespect for humans.*Pratimā svalpa-buddhīnām*means the deity is even for people of less intelligence (and others also). Ambarīṣa and Brahmā worshipped deities according to Nṛsimha Purāṇa.

## Anuccheda 291

*tato ’rcāyāṁ hariṁ kecit saṁśraddhāya saparyayā*  
*upāsata upāstāpi nārthadā puruṣa-dviṣām*

**Because of the power of the deity, somepeople offered all the paraphernalia with faith to the deity, but the deity gave no result for those who offended humans. SB 7.14.41**

By the power of the deity (*tataḥ*), some people, dedicated to the highest sādhana, which was superior to previous sādhana, since the deity was superior dwelling place of the Lord, worshipped the deity. “In that case, this process is also effective for those who hate, since it was meant for thepeople who disrespected each other.” With a desire to refute this suggestion, and with a desire to maintain respect for human as a residence of the Lord previously mentioned, Nārada says that those who disrespect humans obtain no effect from the worship.

## Anuccheda 292

Among people there is excellence according to birth:

*puruṣeṣv api rājendra supātraṁ brāhmaṇaṁ viduḥ*  
*tapasā vidyayā tuṣṭyā dhatte vedaṁ hares tanum*

**O best of kings! Among all persons, a brāhmaṇa must be accepted as the best within this material world because such a brāhmaṇa, by austerity, knowledge and satisfaction, possesses the Vedas, which are the body of the Supreme Lord. SB 7.14.41**

They know that the brāhmaṇa who holds the Vedas is the best receptacle.

## Anuccheda 293

This previously mentioned brāhmaṇa is praised:

*nanv asya brāhmaṇā rājan kṛṣṇasya jagad-ātmanaḥ*  
*punantaḥ pāda-rajasā tri-lokīṁ daivataṁ mahat*

**O King! Thebrāhmaṇas, who purify the three worlds with the dust of their feet, are greatly worshiped by the Supreme Lord. SB 7.14.42**

Kṛṣṇa is the soul of the universe (*jagad-ātmaṇaḥ*) because he acts as the monitor, spreading dharma within the universe for everyone’s benefit. The brāhmaṇa is shown to be worthy of worship *(daivatam*) by Kṛṣṇa. Nārada speaks to Yudhiṣṭhira.

## Anuccheda 294

Two verses then speak of the best among the worshipable brāhmaṇas:

*karma-niṣṭhā dvijāḥ kecit tapo-niṣṭhā nṛpāpare*  
*svādhyāye ’nye pravacane kecana jñāna-yogayoḥ*

**Somebrāhmaṇas are devoted to performing rituals and duties. Some are devoted to austerity. Some are devoted to studying and preaching the Vedas. Some are devoted to jñāna-yoga. SB 7.15.1**

*jñāna-niṣṭhāya deyāni kavyāny ānantyam icchatā*  
*daive ca tad-abhāve syād itarebhyo yathārhataḥ*

**A person desiring liberation for his forefathers or himself should give offerings meant for the Pitṛs or the devatās to a brāhmaṇa who is fixed in jñāna. In the absence of such an advanced brāhmaṇa, charity may be given to other brāhmaṇas. SB 7.15.2**

For those desiring liberation worship of the jñānī is best. If that person is not available, they should worship other men. From this it is understood that those desiring prema-bhakti should worship prema-bhaktas. For the prema-bhaktas, the deity form is more effective that the manifestation of the Lord in their hearts. Though he resides in their hearts as well, theLord’s presence is designated in śālagrāma and other forms, even though he is all-pervading, since those are special places of his manifestation. This is not dependent on seeing the Lord as antaryāmī dwelling in all humans. He is present with his special nature in these deity forms. For instance it is said that even worms become successful by living in places where the deities reside since those places have become mahātīrthas.

*śālagrāma-śilā yatra tat-tīrthaṁ yojana-trayam \|*  
*tatra dānaṁ japo homaḥ sarvaṁ koṭi-guṇaṁ bhavet \|\|*

**Where there is a śālagrāma the area is holy for three yojanas. Charity, japa and sacrifice at that place have ten million times the effect. Skanda Purāṇa**

*śālagrāma-samīpe tu krośa-mātraṁ samantataḥ \|*  
*kīkaṭe’pi mṛto yāti vaikuṇṭha-bhuvanaṁ naraḥ \|\|*

**Even an outcaste who dies within a krośa of a śālagrāma attains Vaikuṇṭha. Padma Purāṇa**

This shows the superiority of the deity over humans as an object of worship. Nārada speaks to Yudhiṣṭhira.

## Anuccheda 295

Other places of worship are given.

*sūryo ’gnir brāhmaṇā gāvo vaiṣṇavaḥ khaṁ maruj jalam*  
*bhūr ātmā sarva-bhūtāni bhadra pūjā-padāni me*

**O saintly Uddhava! Please know that you may worship me in the sun, fire, brāhmaṇas, cows, Vaiṣṇavas, sky, wind, water, earth, the individual soul and all living entities.**

*sūrye tu vidyayā trayyā haviṣāgnau yajeta mām*  
*ātithyena tu viprāgrye goṣv aṅga yavasādinā*

**One worships me in the sun by reciting verses and performing specific Vedic rituals. One worships me in the fire by offering oblations. One worships me in the brāhmaṇaby giving him proper reception. One worships me in the cow by feeding her grass and tickling her.**

*vaiṣṇave bandhu-sat-kṛtyā hṛdi khe dhyāna-niṣṭhayā*  
*vāyau mukhya-dhiyā toye dravyais toya-puraḥsaraiḥ*

**One worships the Lord in the devotees by respecting them as great friends. One worships the Lord in ether by meditating on the space of the heart. One worships the Lord in air by thinking that air is the chief prāṇa. One worships the Lord in water by offering items starting with water.**

*sthaṇḍile mantra-hṛdayair bhogair ātmānam ātmani*  
*kṣetra-jñaṁ sarva-bhūteṣu samatvena yajeta mām*

**One worships me in earth by uttering secret mantras on purified ground. One worships me in the ātmā by offering food to the ātmā in the body. One worships me in all beings by understanding that I am the antaryāmī of all beings.**

*dhiṣṇyeṣv ity eṣu mad-rūpaṁ śaṅkha-cakra-gadāmbujaiḥ*  
*yuktaṁ catur-bhujaṁ śāntaṁ dhyāyann arcet samāhitaḥ*

**Thus, in the previously mentioned places of worship and by the described methods, one should meditate on my peaceful, transcendental form with four arms holding a conch, disc, club and lotus flower, and then worship me with fixed attention. SB 11.11.42-46**

The commentary says, “Eleven places where the Lord can be worshipped are described. O auspicious Uddhava! According to the different location, the worship is different. That is described in three verses. One worships the Lord in the sun by reciting sūktas and sūrya upasthāna(*trayyā vidyayā*). O Uddhava *(aṅga)!* One worship the Lord in air by seeing the prāṇa in it (*mukhya-dhiyā*). One worshipstheLord in water by offering water and other items (*dravyaiḥ*). One worships the Lord in earth (*sthaṇḍile)* by touching it with secret mantras (*mantra-hṛdayaiḥ*). In all the places (*eṣu*) one should meditate on the Lord in this way (*iti*).”

There are two types of worshipping four handed Viṣṇu in the elements listed. One way is to worship the deity within the location by mere service to the location, just as one can clean the temple where the deity resides and thus serve the Lord. For instance, one should worship the Lord by welcoming the devotees or giving grass to the cows. It is appropriate to express friendliness to devotees but it is taught one should see the Lord as the master, for it is said:

*īsvare tad-adhīneṣu bāliśeṣu dviṣatsu ca*  
*prema-maitrī-kṛpopekṣā yaḥ karoti sa madhyamaḥ*

**An intermediate or second-class devotee, called madhyama-adhikārī*****,*** **offers his love to the Supreme Lord, is a sincere friend to all the devotees of the Lord, shows mercy to ignorant people who are innocent, and disregards those who are envious of the Lord. SB 11.2.46**

One gives grass to the cows not to the four handed form, since the Lord does not eat grass. That is according to the following rulementioned in the previous verse:

*yad yad iṣṭatamaṁ loke yac cāti-priyam ātmanaḥ*  
*tat tan nivedayen mahyaṁ tad ānantyāya kalpate*

**If one offers to me what is considered best according to the scriptures and to the people, and also according to one’s own preferences, one becomes qualified for eternal life. SB 11.11.41**

The other method is direct worship of the deity within the location. Thus one should think of the antaryāmīwhile worshipping the Lord in ether by meditating on him in the heart, or in the water by offering water to the antaryāmī there. In other instances such as fire, one should think of the antaryāmī form only. This is not an offering to one’s cherished form of the Lord, the shelter of one’s service in prema. That form should be served with affection while thinking of him as very tender in body (not able to tolerate fire.) The Lord says:

*vastropavītābharaṇa- patra-srag-gandha-lepanaiḥ*  
*alaṅkurvīta sa-prema mad-bhakto māṁ yathocitam*

**My devotee should then lovingly decorate me with clothing, a sacred thread, various ornaments, tulasī garlands, and he should anoint my body with unguents, all in the prescribed manner. SB 11.29.34**

According to the type of bhakti of these devotees, theLord assumes an appropriate bhāva. It is said:

*bhakti-grāhyo hṛṣīkeśo na dhanair dharaṇī-dhara \|*  
*bhaktyā saṁpūjito viṣṇuḥ pradadāti manoratham \|\|*  
*jalenāpi jagannāthaḥ pūjitaḥ kleśahā bhavet \|\|*  
*paritoṣaṁ vrajaty āśu tṛsārtaḥ sujalair yathā*

**The Lord, the support of the whole earth, is attained by bhakti, not by wealth. Worshipped with bhakti the Lord gives what the devotee desires. By worshipping the Lord, dispeller of all suffering, with water, he is satisfied, just as a thirsty person becomes satisfied by clear water. Nāradīya Purāṇa 2.3.3-4**

In the example, a person depends on water. (Similarly the Lord’s bhāva depends on the devotee’s offering.)In opposite conditions however, offering water may be a fault. For instance worship using water is excellent in summer but detested in the rainy season.

*śuci-śukra-gate kāle ye’rcayiṣyanti keśavam \|*  
*jalasthaṁ vividhaiḥ puṣpair mucyante yama-tāḍanāt \|\|*  
*ghanāgame prakurvanti jalasthaṁ vai janārdanam \|*  
*ye janā nṛpati-śreṣṭha teṣāṁ vai narakaṁ dhruvam \|\|*

**When the hot season has arrived those who worship the Lord with various flowers while the Lordis in the water are freed from the punishment of Yama. Those who place the Lord in water in the rainy season go to hell for certain. Garuḍa Purāṇa**

Elsewhere also there are hundreds of methods of pleasing the Lord according to time and place, according to rules of service. The opposite actions(which do not please the Lord) are forbidden. *Viṣṇoḥ sarva-rtu-caryām*: Viṣṇu should be served according to all the seasons. (Viṣṇu-yamala) Therefore it is said:

*yad yad iṣṭatamaṁ loke yac cāti-priyam ātmanaḥ*  
*tat tan nivedayen mahyaṁ tad ānantyāya kalpate*

**If one offers to me what is considered best according to the scriptures and to the people, and also according to one’s own preferences, one becomes qualified for eternal life. SB 11.11.41**

While meditating with the deity mantra it is prescribed to meditate on the place, filled with attractive forms, tastes, fragrances, touch and sound of all seasons. Otherwise one’s efforts will be useless.

Thus in fire and other elements one should meditate on the form of antaryāmī. The Lord speaks.

## Anuccheda 296-297

In Krama-dīpikā it is mentioned that one should use the mantra of Aniruddha when offering food to the deity.The dedicated devotees of Kṛṣṇa however desire to use the mūla-mantra of Kṛṣṇa. Though there it is prescribed to meditate on light emanating from the Lord’s mouth to consume the offering, the devotees think of the Lord directly satisfied with eating at that time sincein his earthy pastimes Kṛṣṇa ate like everybody in this world.

Though there are many meanings of the mantra, during japa one should think of what is favorable for one’s attainment of the goal. Thus though the eight syllable mantra(śrī-kṛṣṇa śaraṇam mama) lacks words in dative case to indicate surrender, one should still meditate on surrender when chanting the mantra.Other rules of worship should be applied in an appropriate way.

In order to attain perfection in pure bhakti there are two types of all actions of bhakti, pure and impure. Worship with desire for material results (impure worship) is described:

*evaṁ kriyā-yoga-pathaiḥ pumān vaidika-tāntrikaiḥ*  
*arcann ubhayataḥ siddhiṁ matto vindaty abhīpsitām*

**By worshiping me through the various methods prescribed in the Vedas and tantras, one will gain from me his desired perfection in both this life and the next. SB 11.27.49**

*Ubhayataḥ* means “in this life and the next*.”*

*mām eva nairapekṣyeṇa bhakti-yogena vindati*  
*bhakti-yogaṁ sa labhata evaṁ yaḥ pūjayeta mām*

**But one who simply engages in devotional service with no motives at all attains prema, and by prema attains me. SB 11.27.53**

One attains me by unmotivated (*nairapekṣyeṇa*) prema (*bhakti-yogena*). That bhakti yoga *(prema*) results from worship. That is expressed in the second line. He who worships me attains bhakti-yoga*(prema*). The Lord speaks.

## Anuccheda 298

The individual glorification of elements of arcana such as wearing Vaiṣṇava markings, accepting prasāda garlands, and drinking caraṇāmṛta will be found in many scriptures.

Now the qualification for arcana will be discussed.

*etad vai sarva-varṇānām āśramāṇāṁ ca sammatam*  
*śreyasām uttamaṁ manye strī-śūdrāṇāṁ ca māna-da*

**This type of worship is approved as the best for all the occupational and spiritual orders of society, even for women and śūdras. SB 11.27.4**

“All varṇas” here means the three upper varṇas (excluding śūdra, mentioned separately).

Padma Purāṇa and Smṛti-artha-sāra say:

*āgamoktena mārgeṇa strī-śūdrair api pūjanam \|*  
*kartavyaṁ śraddhayā viṣṇoś cintayitvā patiṁ hṛdi \|\|*  
*śūdrāṇāṁ caiva bhavati nāmnā vai devatārcanam \|*  
*sarve’py āgama-mārgeṇa kuryur vedānukāriṇā \|\|*  
*strīṇām apy adhikāro'sti viṣṇor ārādhanādiṣu \|*  
*pati-priya-ratānāṁ ca śrutir eṣā sanātanī \|\|*

**Women and śūdras, thinking of the Lord in the heart, should perform worship of Viṣṇu with faith according to the agama rules.Śūdras perform arcana using the name of the Lord. All should follow the āgamas, which follow the Vedas. Women who are devoted to their husband are also qualified for worshipping Viṣṇu. This is the eternal śruti. PadmaPurāṇa 6.84.48, 52-4**

*devatāyāṁ ca mantre ca tathā mantra-prade gurau \|*  
*bhaktir aṣṭa-vidhā yasya tasya kṛṣṇaḥ prasīdati \|\|*  
*tad-bhakta-jana-vātsalyaṁ pūjāyāṁ cānumodanam \|*  
*sumanā arcayen nityaṁ tad-arthe dambha-varjanam \|\|*  
*tat-kathā-śravaṇe rāgas tad-arthe cāṅga-vikriyā \|*  
*tad-anusmaraṇaṁ nityaṁ yas tan-nāmopajīvati \|\|*  
*bhaktir aṣṭa-vidhā hy eṣā yasmin mlecche’pi vartate \|*  
*sa muniḥ satya-vādī ca kīrtimān sa bhaven naraḥ \|\|*

**Kṛṣṇa is pleased with that person who performs eight types of bhakti to the Lord, the mantra, and the guru who gives mantra. These eight types are: affection for devotes, approving worship of the Lord, worship daily the Lord with favorable mind, giving up false pretense for the sake of the Lord, attraction for hearing topics of the Lord, engaging the body in the Lord’s service, constantly remembering the Lord, and full dedication to the Lord’s name. If a person performs these acts, even a mleccah becomes a sage, truthful and glorious. Viṣṇu-dharma**

*yathā kāñcanatāṁ yāti kāṁsyaṁ rasa-vidhānataḥ \|*  
*tathā dīkṣā-vidhānena dvijatvaṁ jāyate nṝṇām \|\|*

**Just as bell metal becomes gold by treatment with mercury, men become second born by the process of dīkṣā. Tattva-sāgara**

In different yugas the different forms of the Lord that appear for worship are described:

*kṛte śuklaś catur-bāhur jaṭilo valkalāmbaraḥ*  
*kṛṣṇājinopavītākṣān bibhrad daṇḍa-kamaṇḍalū*

**In Satya-yuga the Lord is white and four-armed, has matted locks and wears a garment of tree bark. He carries a black deerskin, a sacred thread, prayer beads and a rod and water pot. SB 11.5.21**

These are general indications only because scriptures indicate worship of forms other than these four in the yugas. If worship of the other forms did not take place during the four yugas, there would be no time at which that worship could take place. Worshippers of all forms are described in scriptures in all yugas. Thus all the forms can be worshipped by all people at all times according to their wish. And thus the statement about, that all varṇas are qualified to worship the Lord, is approved by all. Uddhava speaks to the Lord.

## Anuccheda 299

Arcana have been explained. Its secondary elements are understood in the āgamas. Included in arcana are observance of Janmāṣṭamī, Kārtitika-vrata, Ekādaśī and bathing during Māgha month.

Janmāṣṭamī is described in Viṣṇu-rahasya, in a discussion between Brahmā and Nārada:

*tuṣṭy-arthaṁ devakī-sūnor jayantī-sambhavaṁ vratam \|*  
*kartavyaṁ vittā-śāṭhyena bhaktyā bhakta-janair api \|*  
*akurvan yāti nirayaṁ yāvad indrāś caturdaśa \|\|*

**All devotees should with devotion observe the vrata of Kṛṣṇa’s appearance in order to please him, spending according to one’s means. By not observing the vrataa person goes to hell for the lifetimeof fourteen Indras. Viṣṇu-rahasya**

*kṛṣṇa-janmāṣṭamīṁ tyaktvā yo’nyad vratam upāsate \|*  
*nāpnoti sukṛtaṁ kiñcid dṛṣṭaṁ śrutam athāpi vā \|\|*

**The person who gives up observing Kṛṣṇa’s appearance and performs other vratas does not obtain any pious results seen by anyone or mentioned in scripture.**

Concerning cheating with money (not spending properly) it is said:

*dharmāya yaśase 'rthāya kāmāya sva-janāya ca*  
*pañcadhā vibhajan vittam ihāmutra ca modate*

**A person who divides his wealth in five parts—for religion, for reputation, for material objects, for enjoyment and for the maintenance of his family members, is happy in this world and in the next. SB 9.19.37**

Kārttika-vrata is described in Skanda Purāṇa:

*ekataḥ kārttiko vatsa sarvadā keśava-priyaḥ \|*  
*yat kiñcit kriyate puṇyaṁ viṣṇum uddiśya kārttike \|*  
*tad-akṣayaṁ bhavet sarvaṁ satyoktaṁ tava nārada \|\|*

**Kārttika-vrata is always pleasing to the Lord. Whatever pious act is done in this month for Viṣṇu has indestructible results. All that I have said is the truth, O Nārada.**

*avratena kṣiped yas tu māsaṁ dāmodara-priyam \|*  
*tiryag yonim avāpnoti sarva-dharma-bahiṣkṛtaḥ \|\|*

**One who passes the monthdear to Dāmodara without performing vrata becomes devoid of all dharma and becomes an animal. Skanda Purāṇa**

Ekādaśī will now be discussed. It is compulsory even for non-vaiṣṇavas. It is prescribed for everyone in Viṣṇu-dharma:

*vaiṣṇavo vātha sauro vā kuryād ekādaśī-vratam*

**The Vaiṣṇava or even the worshipper of the sun should observe Ekādaśī vrata.**

*vaiṣṇavo vātha śaivo vā sauro’ py etat samācaret*

**Vaiṣṇavas as well as worshippers of the sun and Śiva should observe this vrata. Saura Purāṇa**

In Nārada-pañcarātra it is said, in discussing the obligatory acts after taking dīkṣā:

*ekādaśyāṁ na bhuñjīta pakṣayor ubhayor api \|*  
*jāgaraṁ niśi kurvīta viśeṣāc cārcayed vibhum \|\|*

**One should not eat on the eleventh day of the waxing and waning moons. One should stay awake on that night and worship the Lord with excellence. Nārada-pañcarātra**

Viṣṇu-yamala discusses what is forbidden:

*śuklākṛṣṇāvibhedaś cāsad-vyāpāro vrate tathā \|*  
*śaktau phalādi-bhuktiś ca śrāddhaṁ caikādaśī-dine \|\|*  
*dvādaśyāṁ ca divā-svāpas tulasyāvacayas tathā \|\|*

**One should not distinguish between the waxing and waning Ekādaśī. One should not perform sinful acts on that day. If possible one should not eat even fruit. One should not perform śrāddha rite on that day. One should not sleep and should not pluck tulasī leaves during the Dvādaśī day. Viṣṇu-yamala**

In that scripture it said that bathing the deity is also forbidden on Dvādaśī day.In discussing Vaiṣṇava dharma, Padma Purāṇa says *dvādaśī-vrata-niṣṭhatā*: one should observe the Dvādaśī vrata. In Skanda Puruṣa, Kāśī-khaṇḍa, in the glorification of sauparṇa-dvārakā, Candra-śarma makes a promise to follow bhagavad-dharma:

*adya-prabhṛti kartavyaṁ yan mayā kṛṣṇa tac chṛṇu \|*  
*ekādaśyāṁ na bhoktavyaṁ kartavyo jāgaraḥ sadā \|\|*  
*mahā-bhaktyātra kartavyaṁ pratyahaṁ pūjanaṁ tava \|*  
*palārdhenāpi biddhaṁ tu moktavyaṁ vāsaraṁ tava \|\|*  
*tvat-prītyāṣṭau mayā kāryā dvādaśyāṁ vrata-saṁyutāḥ \|\|*

**O Kṛṣṇa! Please hear what I will do from today. On Ekādaśī I will not eat and will stay awake all night. I will perform your worship with great devotion every day. If the day is contaminated even for a second, I will reject that day for observance. To please you I will observe the eight Mahādvādaśīs with vrata. Skanda Purāṇa**

*ekādaśyāṁ na bhoktavyaṁ tad vrataṁ vaiṣṇavaṁ maha.*

**Not eating on Ekādaśī is a great Vaiṣṇava vrata.**

*vaiṣṇavo yadi bhuñjīta ekādaśyāṁ pramādataḥ \|*  
*viṣṇv-arcanaṁ vṛthā tasya narakaṁ ghoram āpnuyāt \|\|*

**If a devotee eats on Ekādaśī out of negligence his worship of Viṣṇu becomes useless and he goes to a terrible hell. Gautamīya-tantra**

*ekādaśyāṁ nirāhāro yo bhuṅkte dvādaśī-dine \|*  
*śuklā vā yadi vā kṛṣṇā tad vrataṁ vaiṣṇavaṁ mahat \|\|*

**He who does not eat on Ekādaśī, waxing or waning phase, and eats on Dvādaśī day, observes a great Vaiṣṇava vrata. Matsya and Bhaviṣya Purāṇas**

*mātṛhā pitṛhā caiva bhrātṛhā guruhā tathā \|*  
*ekādaśyāṁ tu yo bhuṅkte viṣṇu-loka-cyuto bhavet \|\|*

**He who eats on Ekādaśī is the killer of his mother, father, brother and guru. He cannot attain Vaikuṇṭha. Skanda Purāṇa**

Fasting for the Vaiṣṇavas means not eating even mahāprasādam since the Vaiṣṇavas are forbidden from eating non-prasādam food at all times anyway. This is explained in Nārada-pañcarātra:

*prasādānnaṁ sadā grāhyam ekādaśyāṁ na nārada \|*  
*ramādi-sarva-bhaktānām itareṣāṁ ca kā kathā \|\|*

**O Nārada! On Ekādaśī all devotes including Lakṣmī, what to speak of others, do not accept prasādam.**

*patraṁ puṣpaṁ phalaṁ toyam anna-pānādyam auṣadham \|*  
*anivedyaṁ na bhuñjīta yad āhārāya kalpitam \|\|*  
*anivedyaṁtu bhuñjānaḥ prāyaścittī bhaven naraḥ \|*  
*tasmāt sarvaṁ nivedyaiva viṣṇor bhuñjīta sarvadā \|\|*

**Whatever is taken as food--leaves, flowers, fruit, water, grains, drinks and medicine--should not be eaten without first offering it to Viṣṇu. If he eats unoffered food he must perform atonement. Therefore always one should offer everything to Viṣṇu and then eat. Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa**

Staying awake at night as a rule is described in the conversation between Śiva and Pārvatī:

*samprāpte vāsare viṣṇor ye na kurvanti jāgaram \|*  
*bhraśyate sukṛtaṁ teṣāṁ vaiṣṇavānāṁ ca nindayā \|\|*  
*matir na jāyate yasya dvādaśyāṁ jāgaraṁ prati \|*  
*na hi tasyādhikāro’sti pūjane keśavasya hi \|\|*

**Those devotees who do not stay awake all night on Ekādaśī lose all their pious credits, as if by criticizing others. He who does not stay awake on Dvādaśī vrataloses his qualification for worshipping Keśava. Padma Purāṇa**

Observing the vow pleases Viṣṇu:

*śṛṇu devi pravakṣyāmi dvādaśyāś ca vidhānakam \|*  
*tasyāḥ smaraṇa-mātreṇa santuṣṭaḥ syāj janārdanaḥ \|\|*

**O Devī! Please listen. I will explain the rules for Dvādaśī. Just remembering the Dvādaśī the Lord will be pleased.PadmaPurāṇa 6.234.3**

*ekādaśī mahā-puṇyā sarva-pāpa-vināśinī \|*  
*bhaktes tu dīpanī viṣṇoḥ paramārtha-gati-pradā \|\|*

**Ekādaśī is most auspicious and destroys all sin. It increasesbhakti and makes Viṣṇu award prema. Bhaviṣya Purāṇa**

Bhāgavatam, showing that Ambarīṣa and other great devotees, who ate mahāprasādam, observed this vow, indicates that this vow is an integral part of Vaiṣṇava activities. Padma Purāṇa also shows that the daughter of a brāhmaṇa attained the feet of Satyabhāmā by the power of observing Kārttika and Ekādaśī vratas. What more can be said.

Concerning bathing during Māgha month it is said:

*durlabho māgha-māsas tu vaiṣṇavānām ati-priyaḥ \|*  
*devatānām ṛṣīṇāṁ ca munīnāṁ sura-nāyaka \|*  
*viśeṣeṇa śacīnātha mādhavasyātivallabhaḥ \|\|*

**O leader of the devatās! Master of Śacī! Māgha month is rare and very dear to the Vaiṣṇavas, devatās, sages and hermits. It is extremely dear to Mādhava. Garuḍa Purāṇa**

In a conversation between Brahmā and Nārada it is said:

*sarva-pāpa-vināśāya kṛṣṇa-santoṣaṇāya ca \|*  
*māgha-snānaṁ sadā kāryaṁ varṣe varṣe ca nārada \|\|*

**O Nārada! You should bath during Māgha month every year, to destroy all sins and please Kṛṣṇa. Skanda Purāṇa**

*ekaviṁśa-gaṇaiḥ sārdhaṁ bhogān tyaktvā yathepsitam \|*  
*māgha-māsy uṣasi snātvā viṣṇu-lokaṁ sa gacchati \|\|*

**If one bathes during Māgha month at the dawn, giving up enjoyment as one wishes, one attains Vaikuṇṭha along with twenty-one generations of his family. Bhaviṣyottara Purāṇa**

Similarly observance of Rāma-nāvami, Vaiśākha vrata and other vratas should be understood. All these observances are confirmed by the descriptions of proper conduct in Bhāgavatam.

*gāṁ paryaṭan medhya-vivikta-vṛtti.*  
*sadāpluto ’dhaḥ śayano ’vadhūtaḥ*  
*alakṣitaḥ svair avadhūta-veṣ.*  
*vratāni cere hari-toṣaṇāni*

**Wandering over the earth, maintaining himself solely according to his pure vow, bathing constantly for purity, sleeping on the earth, not caring for his body, wearing clothing of a renunciate, and remaining unrecognized by his relatives, he performed vratas for the satisfaction of the Lord. SB 3.1.19**

*Vratāni* means vows like Ekādaśī. This is taken from the story of Vidura. Śukadeva speaks.

## Anuccheda 300

It should be understood that among such vratas, the worshipper should especially observe those vratas concerning his particular deity.

On the path of arcana and pāda-sevana, there are thirty-two offenses (such as entering the temple in a palanquin or with shoes) mentioned in the āgama scriptures and many others as well (such as eating kingly food). Concerning these offenses it is said:

*mamārcanāparādhā ye kīrtyante vasudhe mayā \|*  
*vaiṣṇavena sadā te tu varjanīyāḥ prayatnataḥ \|\|*

**O earth! The offenses in arcana spoken by me should be avoided by the devotee with great care. Varāha Purāṇa**

That offenses should be avoided is understood in the following statement:

*śraddhayopāhṛtaṁ preṣṭhaṁ bhaktena mama vāry api*  
*bhūry apy abhaktopāhṛtaṁ na me toṣāya kalpate*

**Actually, whatever is offered to me with faith by my devotee—even if only a little water—is most dear to me. Even excellent presentations do not satisfy me if they are offered by nondevotees. SB 11.27.17-18**

The words śraddhā and bhaktimean “having respect.” Offenses are filled with disrespect. The offense may be rejecting the lordship of the Lord or rejecting his order. The cause of offense is disrespect and that disrespect should be avoided. The Lord speaks.

## Anuccheda 301

Disrespecting the great devotees destroys everything:

*na bhajati kumanīṣiṇāṁ sa ijyāṁ*  
*harir adhanātma-dhana-priyo rasa-jñaḥ*  
*śruta-dhana-kula-karmaṇāṁ madair ye*  
*vidadhati pāpam akiñcaneṣu satsu*

**The Lord, relisher of bhakti, who accepts devotees who have no wealth except the wealth of the Lord, does not accept the worship offered by persons with contaminated minds--persons who throw insults at the devotees who have no material possessions--because of their pride in their education, wealth, family and activities. SB 4.31.21**

The Lord is affectionate to persons who have no wealth *(adhana*) but have the wealth of owning the Lord (*ātma*). The Lord relishes bhakti (*rasajñaḥ*). Who are the contaminated persons? They are people proud of the learning, wealth, family and activities. Because of that pride, they offend *(pāpam*) devotees. Nārada speaks to the Pracetas.

## Anuccheda 302

Moreover it is said:
