*na vikriyā viśva-suhṛt-sakhasya*  
*sāmyena vītābhimates tavāpi*  
*mahad-vimānāt sva-kṛtād dhi mādṛṅ*  
*naṅkṣyaty adūrād api śūlapāṇiḥ*

**Because of the great offense I have committed to you who are the friend of the Lord who is the friend of all, to you who are free of false identity and see all beings as your own self, persons like me and even Śiva are quickly destroyed, even if you are not disturbed by the offense. SB 5.10.25**

The meaning is clear. Rahūgaṇa speaks to Bharata.

## Anuccheda 303

If one should offend the Lord by inattention one should perform acts to please the Lord. Vyāsa says:

*ahany ahani yo martyo gītādhyāyaṁ paṭhet tu vai \|*  
*dvātriṁśad-aparādhāṁs tu kṣamate tasya keśavaḥ \|\|*

**Theperson who recites a chapter of the Gītā daily is excused of the thirty-two offenses by the Lord. Skanda Purāṇa, Avantī-khaṇḍa**

*sahasra-nāma-māhātmyaṁ yaḥ paṭhec chṛṇuyād api \|*  
*aparādha-sahasreṇa na sa lipyet kadācana \|\|*

**He who recites or hears the glorification of the thousand names of Viṣṇu cannot be contaminated by a thousand offenses. Skanda Purāṇa, Dvārakā-māhātmya**

*dvādaśyāṁ jāgare viṣṇor yaḥ paṭhet tulasī-stavam \|*  
*dvātriṁśad-aparādhāni kṣamate tasya keśavaḥ \|\|*

**He who stays awake on Dvādaśī and reads the tulasī prayers is pardoned of the thirty-two offenses by the Lord. Skanda Purāṇa Revā-khaṇḍa**

*tulasyā ropaṇaṁ kāryaṁ śrāvaṇeṣu viśeṣataḥ \|*  
*aparādha-sahasrāṇi kṣamate puruṣottamaḥ \|\|*

**One should plant tulasī in Śrāvana month especially. The Lord pardons a thousand offenses. Skanda Purāṇa**

*tulasyā kurute yas tu śālagrāma-śilārcanam \|*  
*dvātriṁśad-aparādhāṁś ca kṣamate tasya keśavaḥ \|\|*

**One who worships śālagrāma with tulasī is forgiven by the Lord for committing the thirty-two offenses. Skanda Purāṇa**

*yaḥ karoti hareḥ pūjāṁ kṛṣṇa-śaṅkāṅkito naraḥ \|*  
*aparādha-sahasrāṇi nityaṁ harati keśavaḥ \|\|*

**The person ornamented with the conch who worships the Lord is relieved of a thousand offenses by the Lord.**

*saṁvatsarasya madhye tu tīrthe śaukarake mama .*  
*kṛtopavāsaḥ snānena gaṅgāyāṁ śuddhim āpnuyāt \|\|*  
*mathurāyāṁ tathāpy evaṁ sāparādhaḥ śuci bhavet \|*  
*anayos tīrthayor ekaṁ yaḥ seveta sukṛtī naraḥ \|\|*  
*sahasra-janma-janitān aparādhān jahāti saḥ \|\|*

**One who fasts at my tīrtha during the year and bathes in the Gaṅgā becomes pure. If one does the same at Mathurā one becomes purified of offences. The pious person who serves one of these two tīrthas frees himself of offenses produced for a thousand births. Ādi-varāha Purāṇa**

*Śaukarake*means at Varāha’s tīrtha.

Offense to a great devotee is absolved by uttering pleasing words to him or obtaining his forgiveness and satisfying him with continuous kīrtana for a long timewith the intention of pleasing him. Without pleasing him the offense will not be absolved. Dakṣa says to Śiva:

*yo 'sau mayāvidita-tattva-dṛśā sabhāyāṁ*  
*kṣipto durukti-viśikhair vigaṇayya tan mām*  
*arvāk patantam arhattama-nindayāpād*  
*dṛṣṭyārdrayā sa bhagavān sva-kṛtena tuṣyet*

**Though I insulted you, with harsh words in the assembly, not knowing the truth about you, you did not consider the insult significant, and you, by your glance of mercy, saved me, who had fallen down by my offense to the most respectable person. You should be satisfied with your show of mercy. SB 4.7.15**

This is understood from later statements in the Bhāgavatam also.

**Vandanam**

Now vandanam will be discussed. Though Vandanam is also a part of arcana, it is dealt with separately since it can be done independent of arcana also, like smaraṇam and kīrtana. This should be understood for other types of bhakti also. Vandanam is prescribed as a separate act for persons who feel too low to engage in serving the Lord’s feet or inquire further about his qualities, after hearing about the unlimited qualities and powers of the Lord. They thus concentrate on offering respects (namskāra). Namaskāra goes beyond the realm of arcana. It is said:

*namaskāraḥ smṛto yajñaḥ sarva-yajñeṣu cottamaḥ \|*  
*namaskāreṇa caikena sāṣṭāṅgena hariṁ vrajet \|\|*

**Offering respects is known as worship. It is the best among all types of worship. One goes to the Lord by offering one namaskāra with eight limbs.Narasiṁha Purāṇa**

Vandanam is described in the following:

*tat te ’nukampāṁ su-samīkṣamāṇo*  
*bhuñjāna evātma-kṛtaṁ vipākam*  
*hṛd-vāg-vapurbhir vidadhan namas te*  
*jīveta yo mukti-pade sa dāya-bhāk*

**One who earnestly waits for you to bestow your mercy upon him, all the while patiently suffering the reactions of his past misdeeds and passes his life by offering you respectful obeisances with his heart, words and body, is the rightful claimant of you, the shelter of liberation. SB 10.14.8**

This verse is connected with the previous verse in the text. Since you have uncountable qualities, thus (*tat)* a person who bows to you *(namaḥ*) is your rightful claimant. You are the shelter *(pade),* the full tenth topic of Bhāgavatam, for mukti, the ninth topic. Or, you are the object (*pade)* of our prema (*mukti*). According to Fifth Canto usage, mukti is sometimes used to indicate prema. You become the devotee’s share just as brothers receive a share of the father’s property.

Mere liberation is achieved by offering one namaskāra. Thus it is said:

*durga-saṁsāra-kāntāram apāram abhidhāvatām \|*  
*ekaḥ kṛṣṇe namaskāro mukti-tīrasya deśikaḥ \|\|*

For persons absorbed in the insurmountable forest of saṁsāra one namaskāra to Kṛṣṇa leads them to the bank of liberation.

Śrīdhara Svāmīsays *susamīkṣamānaḥ*means “one who waits for the Lord’s mercy.” Or, it can mean “one who sees very well the mercy enacted in various places by the Lord with unconditional compassion.” Joyful because of the Lord’s mercy, he thinks of that mercy at all times *(susamīkṣamānaḥ*). He offers respects (*namaḥ*) in his heart, with words or with body. One should avoid offenses in offering respects according to Viṣṇu-smṛti. It is an offense to bow with one hand, with the body covered by cloth, in front of, behind or at the left side of the Lord, too close or in the deity room. Brahmā speaks to the Lord.

**Dāsyam**

## Anuccheda 304

Now dāsyam will be discussed. This means thinking oneself the servant to Viṣṇu. That is shown in the following statement:

*janmāntara-sahasreṣu yasya syān matir īdṛśī \|*  
*dāso’haṁ vāsudevasya sarvān lokān samuddharet \|\|*

**The person who thinks “I am the servant of Vāsudeva for a thousand births” delivers all people. Itihāsa-samucchaya**

“One may put effort in worship. But just by thinking in this way perfection can be achieved.” This is the indication, for after the above verse the following line is found. *kiṁ punas tad-gata-prāṇāḥ puruṣāḥ saṁyatendriyāḥ*: what to speak of person who have given their life to the Lord and control the senses completely?”

The following verse represents dāsya with namaskāra, praises, offering karmas, service, remembering the feet, and hearing as explained by Śrīdhara Svāmī in his commentary.

*tat te 'rhattama namaḥ stuti-karma-pūjāḥ*  
*karma smṛtiś caraṇayoḥ śravaṇaṁ kathāyām*  
*saṁsevayā tvayi vineti ṣaḍ-aṅgayā kiṁ*  
*bhaktiṁ janaḥ paramahaṁsa-gatau labheta*

**O Lord worthy of worship! The six parts of bhakti are offering respects, praising you, deity worship, menial service, remembering your lotus feet and hearing about you. How can a person attain prema without these six types of bhakti to you, who also award liberation to the jñānīs? SB 7.9.50**

Uddhava says:

*tvayopabhukta-srag-gandha- vāso-’laṅkāra-carcitāḥ*  
*ucchiṣṭa-bhojino dāsās tava māyāṁ jayema hi*

**Simply by decorating ourselves with the garlands, fragrant oils, clothes and ornaments that you have already enjoyed, and by eating the remnants of your meals, we, your servants, will indeed conquer your illusory energy. SB 11.6.46**

Here dāsya is indicated through the various acts. An example is the following:

*pādau hareḥ kṣetra-padānusarpaṇe*  
*śiro hṛṣīkeśa-padābhivandane*  
*kāmaṁ ca dāsye na tu kāma-kāmyayā*  
*yathottamaśloka-janāśrayā ratiḥ*

**He engaged his legs in walking to the holy places and temples of the Lord, his head in bowing down before the Lord, and all his determination in servingthe Lord, without desiring material enjoyment, in order to develop rati as possessed by great devotees. SB 9.4.20**

The word *ca* connects the statement about dāsya to the previous items. The determination (*kāmam*) to perform all these acts was caused by dāsya. He performed the acts with the determination “I am the Lord’s servant. I will serve him.” He performed the actions in this way, without desire for enjoyments like Svarga (*kāma-kāmyayā*).

He did everything as a servant (*dāsye*), without desiring material enjoyment. All other desires are excluded. Śukadeva speaks.

## Anuccheda 305

By relationship with such dāsya, all worship becomes greater.

*yan-nāma-śruti-mātreṇa pumān bhavati nirmalaḥ*  
*tasya tīrtha-padaḥ kiṁ vā dāsānām avaśiṣyate*

**What is impossible for the persons who think of themselves as servants of the Lord, whose feet are like holy places? Merely by hearing the name of the Lord the person becomes completely purified. SB 9.5.16**

By hearing theLord’s names one becomes purified, what to speak of directly worshipping him? For those who worship, thinking “I am the Lord’s servant” what remain in sādhana and sādhya? There is nothing more than this. Durvāsa speaks to Ambarīṣa.

**Sakhyam**

## Anuccheda 306

Now sakhyam will be discussed. This means an attitude of friendship with desire to benefit another person. That is the meaning of the word *mitram* in the following:

*aho bhāgyam aho bhāgyaṁ nanda-gopa-vrajaukasām*  
*yan-mitraṁ paramānandaṁ pūrṇaṁ brahma sanātanam*

**How greatly fortunate are Nanda Mahārāja, the cowherd men and all the other inhabitants of Vrajabhūmi! There is no limit to their good fortune, because Kṛṣṇa, their friend, gave themprema, which is the highest bliss, eternal and complete. SB 10.14.32**

*paricaryā-parāḥ kecit prāsādādiṣu śerate \|*  
*manuṣyam iva taṁ draṣṭuṁ vyavahartuṁ ca bandhuvat \|\|*

**A person who is dedicated to serving the Lord, and out of friendship sees and treats him as a human, lies down in the Lord’s temple. Rāmārcana-candrikā**

Sakhyam is listed after dāsyam in SB 7.5.23. Thus sakhyam is considered superior to dāsya because of the attitude of trust and intimacy in the prema (no restraint).That friendship is prescribed even with the Lord is not surprising for there is a scriptural rule concerning attitude. *Na devo devam arcayet*: if one is not identical with the Lord one cannot worship the Lord. But the pure devotees give no regard to the attitude of complete identity since it is contradictory to the service (but they accept equality as a bhāva). However sakhyam is accepted by them as most favorable to service. In the commentary dāsyam and sakhyam as integral parts of worship are shown in the following.

*tasyaiva me sauhṛda-sakhya-maitrī- dāsyaṁ punar janmani janmani syāt*  
*mahānubhāvena guṇālayena viṣajjatas tat-puruṣa-prasaṅgaḥ*

**Life after life may I attain friendship and service to him and may he be my companion and intimate. May I attain association with his devotees, since he is the reservoir of all qualities and powers. SB 10.81.36**

Śrīdāma speaks the verse. Śrīdhara Svāmī says “Seeing Kṛṣṇa’s affection for his devotee, Śrīdāma prays for bhakti to the Lord in this verse. Birth after birth may I have prema (*sauhṛdam*), desire to benefit him (*sakhyam)*, giving assistance (*maitrī*) and service (*dāsyam*). The singular number is viewed in the compound as one item. May I have this relationship, not power..

Prema is not included in the list of nine items of bhakti because it is the goal of bhakti. Maitrī is included in sakhya. Thus only two items, dāsya and sakhya, are accepted as items of sādhana. Dāsya should not be explained as offering karmas and sakhya should not be explained as conviction (though this is the definition according to some) since these are not directly part of bhakti. Bhakti is the result of offering karmas. Conviction is a cause of becoming absorbed in bhakti. This was previously explained. Thus sakhyam as a part of bhaktimeans the desire to give benefit to the Lord. It is not too difficult to execute this special bhakti by specific worship since the Lord eternally desires the benefit of the devotee and the Lord lives eternally with the devotee.

*ko 'ti-prayāso 'sura-bālakā harer*  
*upāsane sve hṛdi chidravat sataḥ*  
*svasyātmanaḥ sakhyur aśeṣa-dehināṁ*  
*sāmānyataḥ kiṁ viṣayopapādanaiḥ*

**O demon boys! What difficulty is there in worshipping the Lord, the friend of the jīva, who remains in the heart like ether? What is the use of obtaining material enjoyment for humans who are like animals in their quest for enjoyment? SB 7.7.38**

The Lord always remains in the heart like ether, uncontaminated. The reason there is no difficulty in worshipping the Lord is given. He is the pure svarūpa (*ātmanaḥ*) of all jīvas (*dehinām*). Everywhere without distinction *(sāmānyataḥ*) he is the friend (*sakhyuḥ*). The Lord is the benefactor because he gives wealth in the form of his prema and material objects for the external and internal senses. What then is the use of acquiring wife and sons, temporary and superimposed? Prahlāda speaks the boys.

## Anuccheda 307

*mayi nirbaddha-hṛdayāḥ sādhavaḥ sama-darśanāḥ*  
*vaśe kurvanti māṁ bhaktyā sat-striyaḥ sat-patiṁ yathā*

**As chaste women bring their gentle husbands under control by service, the pure devotees, who see others’ suffering as their own and are completely attached to me in the core of the heart, bring me under their full control. SB 9.4.66**

The example illustrates bhakti with sakhya to some degree. The Lord speaks to Durvāsa.

## Anuccheda 308

Thus it is said:

*śāntāḥ sama-dṛśaḥ śuddhāḥ sarva-bhūtānurañjanāḥ*  
*yānty añjasācyuta-padam acyuta-priya-bāndhavāḥ*

**The friends of the Lord, those who are peaceful, seeing all things equally, pure, and pleasing to all beings, easily go to the Lord’s abode. SB 4.12.37**

Those who have Acyuta as their friend easily attain the planet with the Lord as the master (*acyuta-padam)*. *Acyuta*means he who does not deviate at all from giving the result. Maitreya speaks.

**Ātma-nivedanam**

## Anuccheda 309

Now ātma-nivedanam will be discussed. This means offering to the Lord alone everything from the body up to the ātmā. The action is devoid of actions for the self, offering to the Lord one’s sādhana and sādhya, doingeverything only for the Lord. This is an offering of the self to the Lord. The seller of a cow does not perform actions for maintaining the cow which is already sold to someone else. The new owner takes care of the cow. The cow works for the new owner, not for the seller. Rukmiṇī expresses offering the self:

*tan me bhavān khalu vṛtaḥ patir aṅga jāyām*  
*ātmārpitaś ca bhavato ’tra vibho vidhehi*  
*mā vīra-bhāgam abhimarśatu caidya ārād*  
*gomāyu-van mṛga-pater balim ambujākṣa*

**Therefore, my dear Lord, I have chosen you as my husband, and I surrender myself to you. Please come swiftly, O almighty one, and make me your wife. My dear lotus-eyed Lord, let Śiśupāla never touch the hero’s portion like a jackal stealing the property of a lion. SB 10.52.39**

Some consider offering the body to be ātmārpaṇam.

*cintāṁ kuryān na rakṣāyai vikrītasya yathā paśoḥ \|*  
*tathārpayan harau dehaṁ viramed asya rakṣaṇāt \|\|*

**Just a purchased animal does not think of its protection a person who offers his body to the Lord gives up protecting himself. Bhakti-viveka**

Some say that it means offering the pure ātmā to the Lord.

*vapur-ādiṣu yo’pi ko’pi vā*  
*guṇato’māni yathā-tathā-vidhaḥ \|*  
*tad ayaṁ tava pāda-padmayor*  
*aham adyaiva mayā samarpitaḥ \|\|*

**Whoever I may be, either a soul inhabiting the body and other material elements, or a deva or a human body made of the guṇas, today I offer that ”I” to Your lotus feet. Stotra-ratnam 49, Yāmunācārya**

Some also offering the right hand or other limbs to the Lord and perform actions only for the Lord with that limb and not for the body. That is seen even today. Ātma-nivedanam involving offering everything along with the effects is seen in the following example:

*sa vai manaḥ kṛṣṇa-padāravindayor*  
*vacāṁsi vaikuṇṭha-guṇānuvarṇane*  
*karau harer mandira-mārjanādiṣu*  
*śrutiṁ cakārācyuta-sat-kathodaye*  
*mukunda-liṅgālaya-darśane dṛśau*  
*tad-bhṛtya-gātra-sparśe 'ṅga-saṅgamam*  
*ghrāṇaṁ ca tat-pāda-saroja-saurabhe*  
*śrīmat-tulasyā rasanāṁ tad-arpite*  
*pādau hareḥ kṣetra-padānusarpaṇe*  
*śiro hṛṣīkeśa-padābhivandane*  
*kāmaṁ ca dāsye na tu kāma-kāmyayā*  
*yathottamaśloka-janāśrayā ratiḥ*

**Mahārāja Ambarīṣa always engaged his mind in meditating upon the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa, his words in describing the glories of the Lord, his hands in cleansing the Lord's temple, and his ears in hearing the words spoken by Kṛṣṇa or about Kṛṣṇa. He engaged his eyes in seeing the deity of Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa's temples and Kṛṣṇa's places like Mathurā and Vṛndāvana, he engaged his sense of touch in touching the bodies of the Lord's devotees, he engaged his sense of smell in smelling the fragrance of tulasī offered to the Lord, and he engaged his tongue in tasting the Lord's prasāda. He engaged his legs in walking to the holy places and temples of the Lord, his head in bowing down before the Lord, and all his desires in serving the Lord, without desiring material enjoyment, in order to developratias possessed by great devotees. SB 9.4.18-20**

The word *cakāra*means “he offered.” The actions listed are representative services (not a complete list). *Liṅga*means the deity. *Ālaya*means the devotee and the temple. He used his eyes to see the devotees, temple and the deity. He engaged his nose in smelling the fragrance of tulasī on the Lord’s lotus feet. He engaged his tongue in tasting what was offered to the Lord (*tad-arpite*), mahāprasādam. He made the decision (k*āmam*) out of the attitude of dāsya, so that somehow he would develop rati for the Lord, which is found in the devotees. He offered everything as a whole to the Lord. As a special case, offering ātmā here means offering even the forms of worship such as remembering. Thus it is said:

*śraddhāmṛta-kathāyāṁ me śaśvan mad-anukīrtanam*  
*pariniṣṭhā ca pūjāyāṁ stutibhiḥ stavanaṁ mama*  
*ādaraḥ paricaryāyāṁ sarvāṅgair abhivandanam*  
*mad-bhakta-pūjābhyadhikā sarva-bhūteṣu man-matiḥ*  
*mad-artheṣv aṅga-ceṣṭā ca vacasā mad-guṇeraṇam*  
*mayy arpaṇaṁ ca manasaḥ sarva-kāma-vivarjanam*  
*mad-arthe ’rtha-parityāgo bhogasya ca sukhasya ca*  
*iṣṭaṁ dattaṁ hutaṁ japtaṁ mad-arthaṁ yad vrataṁ tapaḥ*  
*evaṁ dharmair manuṣyāṇām uddhavātma-nivedinām*  
*mayi sañjāyate bhaktiḥ ko ’nyo ’rtho ’syāvaśiṣyate*

**Firm faith in the narration of my sweet pastimes, constant chanting of my glories, unwavering attachment to my worship, praising me through verses, great respect for serving the deity, offering obeisances with the entire body, performing extensive worship of my devotees, consciousness of me in all living entities, offering of ordinary bodily activities in my service, use of words to describe my qualities, offering the mind to me, rejection of all material desires, giving up wealth for my sake, renouncing material sense gratification and happiness, performing acts of charity, offering homa, and chanting japa, performing vows like Ekādaśī as austerity with the purpose of achieving me—by these those human beings who have surrendered themselves to me develop bhaktifor me. What other result could remain for my devotee? SB 11.19.20-24**

Worship consisting of remembering, chanting and serving the lotus feet becomes arcana by specialization,using the rules of āgama. Thus they are not distinguished in the verse. Bathing and putting on clothing, by being suitable for serving the Lord, become part of ātma-nivedana. They do not interfere with offering the ātmā to the Lord.

Ātmārpaṇam is seen in Bali clearly. It is also illustrated in the following spoken by Prahlāda:

*dharmārtha-kāma iti yo 'bhihitas tri-varga*  
*īkṣā trayī naya-damau vividhā ca vārtā*  
*manye tad etad akhilaṁ nigamasya satyaṁ*  
*svātmārpaṇaṁ sva-suhṛdaḥ paramasya puṁsaḥ*

**I considerdharma, artha and kāma, knowledge of ātmā, knowledge of karma, argumentation, justice and various occupations to be part of the Vedasrecommended for some people. But I consider that surrender to the Supreme Lord, who is one’s friend, is recommended in the Vedas for the saintly people. SB 7.6.26**

The Lord says:

*martyo yadā tyakta-samasta-karmā*  
*niveditātmā vicikīrṣito me*  
*tadāmṛtatvaṁ pratipadyamāno*  
*mayātma-bhūyāya ca kalpate vai*

**When a person who gives up all karmas offers himself entirely and eagerly desires to render service to me alone, he achieves freedom from death and, more important, is qualified to be an associate of mine in prema. SB 11.29.34**

Ātma-nivedanam is seen in two forms: without a specific bhāva and with a specific bhāva. Without bhāva,ātma-nivedanam is seen in SB 11.29.34 quoted above. Ātma-nivedanam with bhāva is seen in the following (dāsya-bhāva):

*mat-kathā-śravaṇe śraddhā mad-anudhyānam uddhava*  
*sarva-lābhopaharaṇaṁ dāsyenātma-nivedanam*

**O Uddhava! One should have faith in hearing topics about me. One should meditate upon me. One should offer valued objects. One should offer oneself, since one is a servant. SB 11.11.35**

Ātma-nivedanam with mādhurya-bhāva is seen in Rukmiṇī’s words:

*tan me bhavān khalu vṛtaḥ patir aṅga jāyām*  
*ātmārpitaś ca bhavato ’tra vibho vidhehi*  
*mā vīra-bhāgam abhimarśatu caidya ārād*  
*gomāyu-van mṛga-pater balim ambujākṣa*

**Therefore, my dear Lord, I have chosen you as my husband, and I surrender myself to you. Please come swiftly, O almighty one, and make me your wife. My dear lotus-eyed Lord, let Śiśupāla never touch the hero’s portion like a jackal stealing the property of a lion. SB 10.52.39**

Śukadeva speaks.

## Anuccheda 310

Vaidhī bhakti has been shown. Of all the aṅgas of bhakti stated or unstated, one or the other may be glorified more than the others. That depends on the manifestation of its particular power by faith in that aṅga. There is thus no controversy among the various limbs (as to which is greater). Similarly according to the patient, medicine acts differently.

Now rāgānuga bhakti will be discussed.

Affection which the experiencer has a natural, intense desire to associate with an object is called rāga, just as the eye may have a strong attraction for a beautiful object. Such natural affection of the devotee for the Lord is called rāga. That rāga has many varieties with different distinguishing features.

*na karhicin mat-parāḥ śānta-rūpe*  
*naṅkṣyanti no me 'nimiṣo leḍhi hetiḥ*  
*yeṣām ahaṁ priya ātmā sutaś ca*  
*sakhā guruḥ suhṛdo daivam iṣṭam*

**The devotees in the spiritual world are never deprived of any enjoyment. My wheel of time does not afflict those devotees, for whom I am a lover, the ātmā*****,*** **son, friend, elder, companion or worshipable deity. SB 3.25.38**

*Priyaḥ* refers to the Lord as seen by gopīs. *Ātmā* refers to the Lord as parabrahman seen by the Kumāras. *Suta* refers to form seen by Nanda and others. *Sakhā* refers to the form seen by Śrīdāma and other cowherd boys. *Guru* refers to the Lord as seen through the affection of Pradyumna and other sons. For one person Kṛṣṇa is a brother, for another he is a cousin and for another he is a husband. In this way, the Lord has a friendly relationship (*suhṛdaḥ)* as a relative in many forms with various devotees. He is the worshipable deity of his servants such as Dāruka.

The bhāva of Śiva for Mohinī is not accepted as rāga since it is not mentioned in this verse and rāga could not develop by Śiva’s bewilderment through māyā.

When there is excellence of natural rāga with qualification of bhāvas with identity as lover, friend, parent etc., thebhakti of such devotees, consisting of hearing, chanting, remembering, serving the lotus feet, vandanam and ātma-nivedanam endowed with that rāga is called rāgātmika-bhakti. Such bhakti is manifested as sādhya, the perfection, since it is a wave in the Gaṅgā river of bhakti in its perfected state, which is characterized by rāga. It is not classed as part of sādhana.

Now rāgānuga bhakti will be discussed. When a person develops a taste for a particular type or rāga mentioned above, but has not actually developed such rāga, and the heart, like a crystal, glows with the rays of the moon of that rāga, he develops a taste for the mode of rāgātmika-bhakti which is described in scriptures. That person performs rāgānuga-bhakti, which follows the taste for that particular rāga. Some call this bhakti*avihitā* or not prescribed, since it does not arise through application of rules but by taste alone. One should not say that bhakti without dependence on rules is impossible.

*prāyeṇa munayo rājan nivṛttā vidhi-ṣedhataḥ \|*  
*nairguṇya-sthā ramante sma guṇānukathane hareḥ \|\|*

**O King! Generally the sages who are beyond rules and prohibitions, who are situated beyond the guṇas, take pleasure in glorifying the qualities of the Lord. SB 2.1.7**

Because vaidhī bhakti is dependent on rules it is weak. Rāgānuga-bhakti however is performed independent of rules. It is thus strong. This point should be understood. The following describes the birth of this taste for nothing except bhakti.

*sā śraddadhānasya vivardhamānā*  
*viraktim anyatra karoti puṁsaḥ*  
*hareḥ padānusmṛti-nirvṛtasya*  
*samasta-duḥkhāpyayam āśu dhatte*

**Taste for topics of the Lord, increasing in the mind of the devotee, creates disgust with everything else. That taste quickly destroys all suffering for the person blissfully engaged in constant remembrance of the feet of the Lord. SB 3.5.13**

The previous verse refers to the topics of the Lord which captured the mind. That is taste or ruci. Because of non-dependence on rules there is difference between the dāsya and sakhya in rāgānuga and the previously discussed dāsya and sakhya in vaidhībhakti as mentioned in the list given by Prahlāda.

*śravaṇaṁ kīrtanaṁ viṣṇoḥ smaraṇaṁ pāda-sevanam*  
*arcanaṁ vandanaṁ dāsyaṁ sakhyam ātma-nivedanam*  
*iti puṁsārpitā viṣṇau bhaktiś cen nava-lakṣaṇā*  
*kriyeta bhagavaty addhā tan manye 'dhītam uttamam*

**If any of the nine processes** ***of*** **bhakti --hearing, chanting, remembering, menial service, deity worship, offering prayers, servitude, friendship, and offering body and soul—is performed for the Lord by any human without prerequisites, after being offered to Viṣṇu, that should be considered the highest learning. SB 7.5.23-24**

Thus the method according to rules is not given much regard in rāgānuga. Instead, the method following the rāgātmika’s is followed. A taste for the rāgātmikas is described in the following:

*suhṛt preṣṭhatamo nātha ātmā cāyaṁ śarīriṇām*  
*taṁ vikrīyātmanaivāhaṁ rame ’nena yathā ramā*

**The Supreme Lord is the dearest for all living beings because he is everyone’s well-wisher and the soul situated in everyone’s heart. Therefore I will now pay the price of complete surrender, and, thus purchasing the Lord, I will enjoy with him just like Lakṣmī. SB 11.8.35**

It is hinted here that after establishing that the Lord is the natural husband by his qualities of natural friendliness, material husbands become illusory. Elsewhere it is said *patyāv ekatvaṁ sā gatā yasmāc caru-mantrāhuti-vratā*: she has become one with her husband, by strict offering of oblations with the caru-mantra. (Chāndogya-pariśiṣṭa) Such procedure establishes an artificial relationship between oneself and the husband. The relationship with Paramātmā is natural. Thus in the above verse the Lord is referred to as ātmā. Though the Lord’s position as husband cannot be taken away, Piṅgalā purchases particularly (*vikrīya)* the Lordwith the self (*ātmanā*) as the price, just as a young girl accepts someone as husband by offering herself through a wedding, just as Lakṣmī (*ramā*), taking shelter of such a bhāva, enjoys with the attractive Lord.

## Anuccheda 311

Thus Piṅgalā’s taste for rāgātmika-bhakti has been indicated. Her behavior in rāgānugais described as follows:

*santuṣṭā śraddadhaty etad yathā-lābhena jīvatī*  
*viharāmy amunaivāham ātmanā ramaṇena vai*

**Satisfied, having faith in the Lord and maintaining myself with whatever comes of its own accord, I shall enjoy life with only the Lord, because he is the real source of love and happiness. SB 11.8.40**

I will enjoy with this husband having such bhāva (*amunā ramaṇena*) using my mind (*ātmanā*). In the path predominated by taste the mind is predominant, since one must relate using the mind in serving the Lord if one has not attained the spiritual form of the Lord’s beloved. To act as the wife (in the present body) in relation to the deity is rejected. This is also so for acting as parent or other identities. Piṅgalā speaks.

## Anuccheda 312-314

The identity as a female lover has been described. This is also seen in Brahma-vaivarta Purāṇa in the activities of a woman called Kāma-kalā. Examples of ruci-bhakti with identity as servant, friend or parent can also be found in the scriptures. Prahlāda says:

*tasmād amūs tanu-bhṛtām aham āśiṣo 'jña*  
*āyuḥ śriyaṁ vibhavam aindriyam āviriñcyāt*  
*necchāmi te vilulitān uruvikrameṇa*  
*kālātmanopanaya māṁ nija-bhṛtya-pārśvam*

**I do not want these objects of enjoyments, life span, wealth, power, or sense enjoyment of living beings up to that of Brahmā, which are all destroyed by your power as time. Take me to the side of your servant. SB 7.9.24**

In Nārada-pañcarātra it is said:

*kadā gambhīrayā vācā śriyā yukto jagat-pate \|*  
*cāmara-vyagra-hastaṁ mām evaṁ kurv iti vakṣyasi \|\|*

**O master of the universe, when will you, accompanied by Lakṣmī, say with a deep voice to me, eager to serve you with cāmara, “Please come here”?**

*aputro’pi sa vai naicchat putraṁ karmānucintayan \|*  
*vāsudevaṁ jagannāthaṁ sarvātmānaṁ sanātanam \|\|*  
*aśeṣopaniṣad-vedyaṁ putrīkṛtya vidhānataḥ \|*  
*abhiṣecayituṁ rājā svarāja upacakrame \|\|*  
*na putram abhyarthitavān sākṣād bhūtāj janārdanāt \|*  
*agre bhagavad-varaś ca ahaṁ te bhavitā putraḥ \|\|*

**Though the king was without a son, he did not desire one, because he thought that was his** ***karma.*** **Thinking of Kṛṣṇa, the eternal Lord of the universe, the soul of all, and the object of Upaniṣadic knowledge as his son, he installed him with abhiṣeka as the king. He did not pray for a son, but the Lord became his son. Skanda Purāṇa**

*pati-putra-suhṛd-bhrātṛ-pitṛvan mātravad dharim \|*  
*ye dhyāyanti sadodyuktās tebhyo’pīha namo namaḥ \|\|*

**I pay my respects repeatedly to those who constantly and eagerly meditate upon the Lord as their husband, son, well-wisher, brother, or the meditator as father or mother. Nārāyaṇa-vyūha-stava**

The husband, son, well-wisher and brother are identities of the Lord and fatherand mother are identities of the meditator. The ending *vat* (like) does not indicate thinking that one is identical to Kṛṣṇa’s mother. One desires a mood similar to hers. The same applies to the other items in the list. This would be a fault, since identifying oneself completely with a great devotee would be similar to identifying oneself with the Lord (ahaṁgrahopāsana). The word *dhyāyanti* (they meditate) indicates that the process is predominantly in the mind. The word *api* (even) means that offering respects to these devotees implies offering even greater respect to those with perfected rāga.

“It is said *codanā-lakṣano ‘rtho dharmaḥ*: dharma should be enacted according to scriptural injunctions. By following the injunctions, apūrva (result) appears according to Pūrva-mīmāṁsa. Also Viṣṇu-yamala says that great calamity in bhakti is produced by not following the rules.

*śruti-smṛtī mamaivājñe yas te ullaṅghya vartate \|*  
*ājñā-cchedī mama dveṣī mad-bhakto’pi na vaiṣṇavaḥ \|\|*

**The person who breaks the rules of śruti and smṛti which are my order is not a Vaiṣṇava, though my devotee, since he disobeys my instructions and thus shows hatred towards me.**

Thus ignoring the necessary actions and prohibitions of the scriptures destroys one’s position as a Vaiṣṇava. How then can rāgānuga bring results by ignoring the rules?.

Bhakti does not depend on following rules as dharma does, since it gives results from the inherent śakti in the Lord’s name and qualities. Thus it is said in many scriptures that bhakti gives results without jñāna or other methods. Injunctions are meant for those who have no natural inclination to follow the rules. The array of rules is for those people. Since those distracted in various ways do not have taste for the Lord, such persons do not understand the nature of rāgātmika bhakti. Thus a system of rules is made in order that one can enter the path to perfection properly with some action and absorb the mind gradually.

Bhakti is similarly encouraged.

*yān āsthāya naro rājan na pramādyeta karhicit*  
*dhāvan nimīlya vā netre na skhalen na pated iha*

**O King! One who accepts this process of devotional service will never be affected by pride. Even while running with eyes closed, he will never trip or fall. SB 11.2.35**

Since the person does not have taste which will produce continuous engagement in bhakti, by not following rules he will be disturbed by mental, physical and circumstantial problems. Rules are not prescribed for those with a natural tendency to follow the rules since the person is absorbed in the methods of Lord’s attractive rāgātmikas out of taste. The Lord himself says:

*jñātvājñātvātha ye vai māṁ yāvān yaś cāsmi yādṛśaḥ*  
*bhajanty ananya-bhāvena te me bhaktatamā matāḥ*

**Those who may or may not know the extent of my powers, my forms and my nature, but who worship me with great possessiveness, I consider to be the best of devotees. SB 11.11.33**

Even with bad intentions towards the rāgātmika bhaktas by mere imitation, a person can attain such a bhāva. An example is Pūtanā who imitated a nurse.

*eṣāṁ ghoṣa-nivāsinām uta bhavān kiṁ deva rāteti naś*  
*ceto viśva-phalāt phalaṁ tvad-aparaṁ kutrāpy ayan muhyati*  
*sad-veṣād iva pūtanāpi sa-kulā tvām eva devāpitā*  
*yad-dhāmārtha-suhṛt-priyātma-tanaya-prāṇāśayās tvat-kṛte*

**O Lord! What can you give to the people of Vraja whose houses, possessions, friends, dear ones, bodies, sons, life airs and hearts are dedicated only to you, since there is nothing superior to you or Vraja (which you have already given)? Thinking of this, my mind becomes bewildered. You awarded yourself even to Pūtanā and her family members because she wore the disguise of a nurse. SB 10.14.35**

If that is so, how much more will be achieved by performing genuine bhakti constantly with taste for the Lord!

*pūtanā loka-bāla-ghnī rākṣasī rudhirāśanā*  
*jighāṁsayāpi haraye stanaṁ dattvāpa sad-gatim*  
*kiṁ punaḥ śraddhayā bhaktyā kṛṣṇāya paramātmane*  
*yacchan priyatamaṁ kiṁ nu raktās tan-mātaro yathā*

**Pūtanā was always hankering for the blood of human children, and with that desire she came to kill Kṛṣṇa; but because she offered her breast to the Lord, she attained Kṛṣṇa. What then is to be said of those who had faith or devotion for Kṛṣṇa and who offer something very dear, as a mother offers something to a child? SB 10.6.35-36**

*na mayy ekānta-bhaktānāṁ guṇa-doṣodbhavā guṇāḥ*  
*sādhūnāṁ sama-cittānāṁ buddheḥ param upeyuṣām*

**The fully dedicated devotees who see equally everywhere and who have achieved the Lord who is superior to prakṛti do not have the guṇas which give rise to qualities of good and bad. SB 11.20.36**

To become ekānta as mentioned above one must be fixed in bhakti. That fixity arises either by taste, or by respect for rules of scripture. Since taste is rare, and if, without following the rules of scriptures, one thinks himself to be an ekānta, it is a sham. Thus the criticism for not following rules of scriptures in *śruti-smṛti-purānādi* is not directed to persons with taste, mentioned in SB 10.6.27 above. Thus it is said:

*svātantryāt kriyate karma na ca vedoditaṁ mahat \|*  
*vinaiva bhagavat-prītyā te vai pāṣaṇḍinaḥ smṛtāḥ \|\|*

**Those who undertake great acts independently, not according to the Vedic rules, and without taste for the Lord are called pāṣaṇḍis. Padma Purāṇa**

*Bhagavat-prītyā*means “with taste for the Lord.” Thus disrespect for scripture is criticized but not unintentional lapses as mentioned in SB 11.2.35. It is also said:

*na japo nārcanaṁ naiva dhyānaṁ nāpi vidhi-kramaḥ \|*  
*kevalaṁ santataṁ kṛṣṇa-caraṇāmbhoja-bhāvinām \|\|*

**For persons alwaysthinking of Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet, there are no rules, no japa, no arcana and no meditation. Gautamīya-tantra**

Rāgānuga, with respect for the great devotees with rāga, but without the development of taste, shouldbe executed with a mixture of vaidhībhakti. Those who have some position in society and have developed tasteshould also execute rāgānuga mixed with vaidhī bhakti for setting an example to the public. In this mixture, vaidhī bhakti should be suitably performed so that it becomes one with rāgānuga. Some people meditate on the eighteen syllable mantra while thinking of all the people being attracted to Kṛṣṇa’s flute when he milks the cows. Others, while meditating on the mantra taught by the guru for attaining the desired spiritual identity, will directly think of their identity as a member of Vraja. They think “I am directly serving the son of Nanda.”

There are two types of not following the necessary injunctions and prohibitions criticized in *śruti-smṛti mamaivājñe*: breaking rules in dharma scriptures and breaking rules of bhakti scriptures. A person does not fall from the position of a Vaiṣṇava if he does not follow an injunction of dharma or performs what is prohibited in dharma, out of faith in bhakti or out of bad character.

*devarṣi-bhūtāpta-nṛṇāṁ pitṝṇāṁ*  
*na kiṅkaro nāyam ṛṇī ca rājan*  
*sarvātmanā yaḥ śaraṇaṁ śaraṇyaṁ*  
*gato mukundaṁ parihṛtya kartam*

**O King! One who has given up all varṇāśrama duties and has taken full shelter of the lotus feet of Mukunda, who offers shelter to all, is not a debtor to or servant of the devatās, great sages, ordinary living beings, relatives or Pitṛs. SB 11.5.41**

*api cet su-durācāro bhajate mām ananya-bhāk \|*  
*sādhur eva sa mantavyaḥ samyag vyavasito hi saḥ \|\|*

**If the most sinful person worships me with no other desire than to please me, I still consider that person to be my devotee, since he has fixed himself completely in me. BG 9.30**

A person with taste has no desire for the bliss of liberation since that is detested because of his taste. What then to speak of desiring happiness from the most sinful acts. By nature he does not have inclination for these. If out of negligence sometimes such inclinations arise, the sin is destroyed instantly.

*sva-pāda-mūlam bhajataḥ priyasya*  
*tyaktānya-bhāvasya hariḥ pareśaḥ*  
*vikarma yac cotpatitaṁ kathañcid*  
*dhunoti sarvaṁ hṛdi sanniviṣṭaḥ*

**One who has thus given up all other engagements and has taken full shelter at the lotus feet of Supreme Lord is very dear to the Lord. If such a surrendered soul accidentally commits some sinful activity, the Supreme Lord, great because of his affection for the devotee, seated within everyone’s heart, immediately takes away the reaction to such sin. SB 11.5.42**

Now rules and prohibitions in the Vaiṣṇava scriptures (and breaking them) will be discussed. The only goal of all these rules and prohibitions is to satisfy Viṣṇu. Though what to do and not to do is written in the scriptures, because of a taste for rāgātmika devotees, the person will *naturally* be inclined to follow the rules and avoid the prohibitions, since the devotee who has developed prīti desires only to satisfy the Lord. Because of following the rāgātmika devotee of his choice, he does not depend on investigating what is to be done or not to be done. Rather what the rāgātmika devotee has done, he is eager to accept.

Sometimes the devotee will depend on rules mentioned in scripture, out of taste for rāga. Such rules are included within rāgānuga bhakti. Those who are following the rāgātmika devotees of Gokula perform ordinary or Vaiṣṇava dharma only following the intentions of those Vraja residents--who have only the desire that there be no obstacle to association with Kṛṣṇa and to his welfare.

Thus the injunction *śruti-smṛtī mamaivājñe* does not apply to the rāgānuga bhaktas since they perform the principles of dharma out of taste. It does not even apply to the vaidhī bhaktas, since it is said *api cet sudurācaraḥ*: even one who commits the most sinful act is still my devotee. (BG 9.30) The injunction applies to persons following the paths of worship instituted by Buddha, Ṛṣabha, Dattātreya and others described in non-Vedic scriptures. Thus it is said:

*veda-dharma-viruddhātmā yadi deva prapūjayet \|*  
*sa yāti narakaṁ ghoraṁ yāvad āhūta-samplavam \|\|*

**If one worships the Lord while one holds ideas contrary to Vedic dharma, he goes to a terrible hell until final dissolution of the universe.**

Thus rāgānuga-bhakti, though not following the rules, is not outside the Vedas. This path is celebrated in the Vedas and pertaining scriptures since those scriptures also have a taste for these devotees. However Vedas describe the Buddhists and others to be outside the Vedas, since their ideas are contrary to the Vedas.

*tataḥ kalau sampravṛtte sammohāya sura-dviṣām \|*  
*buddho nāmnāñjana-sutaḥ kīkaṭeṣu bhaviṣyati \|\|*

**Then when Kali-yuga starts Buddha, the son of Añjanā, will appear in the province of Gayā for bewildering the demons. SB 1.3.24**

Therefore rāgānuga bhakti is proper. Furthermore, it is superior to vaidhī bhakti. It has been shown that rules are used to develop absorption in the Lord. That absorption will be accomplished more by a mental condition with special taste than by inspiration by rules, since taste is absorption’s natural quality. What to speak of favorable states of mind, even with unfavorable states, or forbidden states of mind, absorption will quickly develop if there is taste. And when absorption is accomplished, the unfavorablefeelings will disappear and all anarthas will be destroyed. This demonstrates the strength of the path of bhāva. If there isa favorable state of mind, this is the highest achievement of the ekanta devotees.

Now a section is dedicated to showing the great strength of the path of bhāva in general. Yudhiṣṭhira says:

*aho aty-adbhutaṁ hy etad durlabhaikāntinām ap.*  
*vāsudeve pare tattve prāptiś caidyasya vidviṣa.*

**It is astonishing that the envious Śiśupāla attained Vāsudeva. This attainment is difficult for even unattached persons. SB 7.1.16**

Vāsudeva is difficult for even the greatest jñānīs (*ekāntīnām*). It was not possible for him to attain the Lord because normally such persons go to hell:

*etad veditum icchāmaḥ sarva eva vayaṁ mune*  
*bhagavan-nindayā veno dvijais tamasi pātitaḥ*

**O sage! We all desire to know. King Vena fell to hell through the brāhmaṇasfor criticizing the Lord. SB 7.1.17**

Vena fell to hell (*tamasi*). After much suffering in hell, it is said that, by the influence of Pṛthu’s birth (from his body) he attained a good destination. Śiśupāla is described:

*damaghoṣa-sutaḥ papa ārabhya kala-bhāṣaṇāt*  
*sampraty amarṣī govinde dantavakraś ca durmatiḥ*

**From of his childhood, when he could not even speak properly, up until now, evil Śiśupāla, the son of Damaghoṣa, was envious of Kṛṣṇa. SB 7.1.18**

The meaning is clear. Yudhiṣṭhira speaks to Nārada.

## Anuccheda 315-320

Nārada replies, “Ah! What was your intention in asking that an offender of the Lord should go to hell? Should he go to hell because of harming the Lord or even without that, because it is said that criticism is sinful, like drinking liquor? Foolish people partake of criticism and other acts aimed at persons in the three guṇas. A criticism of persons within the guṇas cannot extend to the Lord whose form is made of spiritual qualities. The Lord does not identify with anything in the material world, unlike the jīva. Therefore he cannot be afflicted. That is stated in three verses:

*nindana-stava-satkāra-nyakkārārthaṁ kalevaram*  
*pradhāna-parayo rājann avivekena kalpitam*

**A body subject to criticism and praise, honor and dishonor is created by lack of discrimination between the self and non-self. SB 7.1.23**

*Nindana* means broadcasting someone’s faults. *Nyakkāra* means contempt. The bodies of the jīvas are made with no ability to discriminate between matter and spirit, for the purpose of perceiving criticism, praise, honor and condemnation.

*hiṁsā tad-abhimānena daṇḍa-pāruṣyayor yathā*  
*vaiṣamyam iha bhūtānāṁ mamāham iti pārthiva*

**O King! Ideas of violence arise by identification with the body. When ideas of punishment and threats arise, there is unequal treatment for all beings in this world. SB 7.1.2.**

Identification with bodies produces violence. For instance, in this world (*iha)* there is the error of me and mine created when living beings think in terms of their bodies. By punishment and criticism arising from that error, violence arises.

*yan-nibaddho 'bhimāno 'yaṁ tad-vadhāt prāṇināṁ vadhaḥ*  
*tathā na yasya kaivalyād abhimāno 'khilātmanaḥ*  
*parasya dama-kartur hi hiṁsā kenāsya kalpyate*

**Because of the bodily conception of life, the conditioned soul thinks that when the body is annihilated the living being is annihilated. Because of having no false identity, the supreme controller, identifying himself as Paramātmā, different from matter and the jīva,though he is a punisher, does not have a concept of being a killer or being killed. SB 7.1.25**

And then when the body in which one is bound and with which one identifies is killed, the jīvas think they are killed. But unlike this, for the Lord (*asya)* with no false identity, from what cause (*kena)* can there be any violence? None at all. He has no false identity because he is pure, with no material body (*kaivalyāt*). Even the devotees are completely pure:

*dehendriyāsu-hīnānāṁ vaikuṇṭha-pura-vāsinām*

**The inhabitants of Vaikuṇṭha are completely spiritual, having no material body, senses or life airs. SB 7.1.35**

He cannot be subject to material criticism because his body is eternity, knowledge and bliss, which is not subject to any criticism. That is also explained in Gītā:

*nāhaṁ prakāśaḥ sarvasya yoga-māyā-samāvṛtaḥ \|*  
*mūḍho ’yaṁ nābhijānāti loko mām ajam avyayam \|\|*

**Being covered by my bewildering power of māyā, I am not visible to all these people. The foolish do not know me, who am without birth and unchanging in qualities. BG 7.25**

The Lord is significantly different from jīvas because he is the ātmā of all beings (*akhilātmanaḥ*). He is the ātmā of all beings because he is devoid of material influence (*parasya*). He is also not the subject of violence because he is the punisher of others (*dama-kartuḥ*), since he possesses astonishing, unlimited powers.

Thus since there was no harm to the Lord when Śiśupāla criticized him, because by absorption in the Lord by anymeans, even from enmity, his sin of criticizing was destroyed. It was thus appropriate that he attained liberation.

*sakṛd yad-aṅga-pratimāntar-āhitā*  
*manomayī bhāgavatīṁ dadau gatim*  
*sa eva nityātma-sukhānubhūty-abhi-*  
*vyudasta-māyo ’ntar-gato hi kiṁ punaḥ*

**If even only once or even by force one brings the form of the Lord into one’s mind, Kṛṣṇa awards himself as the goal. Residing within, he gives thejīvas the experience of bliss by destroying all māyā completely. What more can be said? SB 10.12.39**

That is the intention of the next verse:

*tasmād vairānubandhena nirvaireṇa bhayena vā*  
*snehāt kāmena vā yuñjyāt kathañcin nekṣate pṛthak*

**If by continuous enmity, by favorable relationships*****,*** **or by conjugal feelings arising from strong spiritual affection, out of fear of transgressing morality as an unmarried lover, or even as a married lover, the mind should concentrate on the Lord, one will become absorbed in him. SB 7.1.26**

Since affection and love cannot be ordered, the potential case is used to express possibility (*yuñjāt*). If one can meditate by one of the methods mentioned, one will become absorbed in the Lord (*nekṣate pṛthak*). *Vairānubandhena* means “by continuous enmity.” *Nirvaireṇa*means “by being without enmity, being neutral.” This also means without kāma or vaira, without any bhāva listed in the verse. One can meditate, representing any aṅga of bhakti-yoga, without bhāva, merely with the idea that it is prescribed. Sneha (affection) means a natural type of mutual prema, distinct from kāma.In the sādhaka, it refers to a taste for such a relationship. The result is absorption in the Lord for all of them. Since one can quickly attain absorption by these means filled with bhāva, even with enmity and criticism, are not the same as vaidhībhakti.

*yathā vairānubandhena martyas tan-mayatām iyāt*  
*na tathā bhakti-yogena iti me niścitā matiḥ*

**One cannot achieve such intense absorption in thought of the Supreme Lord by devotional service (bhakti-yoga) as one can through enmity toward him. That is my opinion. SB 71.27**

Fear should be included with continuous enmity in the above verse. The performance of bhakti-yoga while thinking only that it should be done does not bring about absorption in the Lord (*tan-mayatām*) as quickly as enmity and fear. One may say that this takes place because the Lord or an ābhāsa of the Lord(in the case of enmity) appears before them, endowed with such śakti. A material example is given to show the great result in the form of absorption even from enmity.

*kīṭaḥ peśaskṛtā ruddhaḥ kuḍyāyāṁ tam anusmaran*  
*saṁrambha-bhaya-yogena vindate tat-svarūpatām*  
*evaṁ kṛṣṇe bhagavati māyā-manuja īśvare*  
*vaireṇa pūta-pāpmānas tam āpur anucintayā*

**A worm confined in a hole in a wall by a bee, by always thinking of the bee in fear and enmity, later becomes a bee. Similarly, if the conditioned souls out of enmity think of Kṛṣṇa, who is merciful to even the sinful, and become free from their sins, they attain him. SB 7.1.28-29**

By absorption (*yoga)* through hatred or fear one attains one’s own form (*sārūpyam*).*Evam*means “in this way.” Because he is parabrahman in human form, Kṛṣṇa appears to be a material human by māyā (*māya-manuje*). “The insect does not incur sin by hating the bee. However if one hates the Lord there is sin.” But by absorption (*anucintayā*) in hatred one becomes purified of sin, since meditation with absorption has such power.

One should not say that one can only attain perfection by bhagavad-dharma according to scripture and not by kāma and other bhāvas which are not prescribed in scriptures. That is expressed in the next verse:

*kāmād dveṣād bhayāt snehād yathā bhaktyeśvare manaḥ*  
*āveśya tad-aghaṁ hitvā bahavas tad-gatiṁ gatāḥ*

**Just as by vaidhī bhaktione can attain one’s spiritual goals*****,*** **many persons have attained suitable forms after asorbing their minds in the Lord out of lust, hatred, fear, and family relationships filled with affection, and after giving up absorption in enmity of the Lord (in the case of hatred and fear). SB 7.1.30**

Just as they attain the goal by absorbing the mind in the Lord by bhakti according to rules, many attain the goal by kāma and other bhāvas which are not according to the rules. They gave up hatred and fear (*tad-agham*) among the various bhāvas. Because fear is connected with hatred it also produces sin.

Some consider kāma to be a sin also. The following should be considered. Is kāma for the Lord itself sinful, or is it sinful if kāma means to think of Kṛṣṇa as a husband? Or is it only sinful if one thinks of Kṛṣṇa as one’s illicit lover.

If kāma for the Lord is itself sinful, is it because it is classed with hatred and other qualities, being sinful by its nature? Or is it sinful because one thinks of Kṛṣna as a lusty person or kisses him, though he is most pure? Or is kāma sinful because kāma is said to be a sin in scriptures.

It cannot be sinful by nature,because of being classed with hatred, anger etc. because it is said:

*uktaṁ purastād etat te caidyaḥ siddhiṁ yathā gataḥ*  
*dviṣann api hṛṣīkeśaṁ kim utādhokṣaja-priyāḥ*

**This point was explained to you previously. Since even Śiśupāla, who hated Kṛṣṇa, again achieved his spiritual body as an associate by thinking of him, then what to speak of the Lord’s dear gopīs. SB 10.29.13**

In this verse hatred is criticized and kāma is praised. These persons are called *priyāḥ.* The gopīs are not at fault because kāma is essentially filled with prīti or affection. The gopīs’ kāma is actually prema only.

*yat te sujāta-caraṇāmburuhaṁ staneṣu*  
*bhītāḥ śanaiḥ priya dadhīmahi karkaśeṣu*  
*tenāṭavīm aṭasi tad vyathate na kiṁ svit*  
*kūrpādibhir bhramati dhīr bhavad-āyuṣāṁ naḥ*

**O dearly beloved! Your lotus feet are so soft that we place them gently on our breasts, fearing that your feet will be hurt. Our life rests only in you. Our minds, therefore, are filled with anxiety that your tender feet might be wounded by pebbles as you roam about on the forest path. SB 10.31.19**

They have no desire for their own happiness but think only of what will make Kṛṣṇa happy. The love of Kubjā however is criticized since it lacks the gopīs’ pure intention of pleasing Kṛṣṇa alone, she being predominantly desirous of her own enjoyment. That is made clear when Śukadeva praises her love for its effects (purifying her of lust).

*sānaṅga-tapta-kucayor urasas tathākṣṇor*  
*jighranty ananta-caraṇena rujo mṛjantī*  
*dorbhyāṁ stanāntara-gataṁ parirabhya kāntam*  
*ānanda-mūrtim ajahād ati-dīrgha-tāpam*

**Simply by smelling the fragrance of Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet, Trivakrā cleansed away the burning desire Cupid had aroused in her breasts, chest and eyes. With her two arms she embraced between her breasts her lover, Kṛṣṇa, the personification of bliss, and thus she gave up her long-standing distress. SB 10.48.7**

Prīti finally manifested in her:

*sahoṣyatām iha preṣṭha dināni katicin mayā*  
*ramasva notsahe tyaktuṁ saṅgaṁ te ’mburuhekṣaṇa*

**O beloved, please stay here with me for a few days more and enjoy. I cannot bear to give up your association, O lotus-eyed one! SB 10.48.9**

*saivaṁ kaivalya-nāthaṁ taṁ prāpya duṣprāpyam īśvaram*  
*aṅga-rāgārpaṇenāho durbhagedam ayācata*

**Having thus gotten the hard-to-get Supreme Lord by the simple act of offering him body ointment, unfortunate Trivakrā submitted to the bestower of bhaktithe following request. SB 10.48.8**

*durārdhyaṁ samārādhya viṣṇuṁ sarveśvareśvaram*  
*yo vṛṇīte mano-grāhyam asattvāt kumanīṣy asau*

**Kṛṣṇa, endowed with all qualities and powers, able to bestow all benedictions, is ordinarily difficult to approach. One who has properly worshiped him and then chooses the benediction of material enjoyment is certainly of poor intelligence, for he is satisfied with an insignificant result. SB 10.48.11**

The above verses should be understood as follows. *Kaivalya-nātha*means “he who is to be served by the ekānta devotees.” Previously Kubjā was unfortunate because of her crooked body. How astonishing! Just by offering ointment, a small portion of bhagavad-dharma, her good fortunate was produced. She then asked for the good fortune of having him stay with her. (SB 10.48.8)

The good fortune of Kubjā was something like Śrīdāma’s good fortune as expressed by the citizens of Dvārakā.

*kim anena kṛtaṁ puṇyam avadhūtena bhikṣuṇā*  
*śriyā hīnena loke ’smin garhitenādhamena ca*  
*yo ’sau tri-loka-guruṇā śrī-nivāsena sambhṛtaḥ*  
*paryaṅka-sthāṁ śriyaṁ hitvā pariṣvakto ’gra-jo yathā*

**What pious acts has this unkempt, impoverished brāhmaṇa performed? People regard him as lowly and contemptible, yet the spiritual master of the three worlds, the abode of Lakṣmī, is serving him reverently. Leaving the goddess of fortune sitting on her bed, the Lord has embraced this brāhmaṇa as if he were an older brother. SB 10.80.25-26**

“Why should a lusty woman be praised?” This is answered in verse 11. The person who desires material objects (*manograhyam)* is not intelligent *(ku-manīṣī).*She however desired the Lord alone. She was most intelligent. Thus kāma is not to be classed with hatred or anger. Because of that, kāma cannot be considered sinful.

Now,the behavior by thinking of Kṛṣṇa as a lusty person or kissing him cannot considered sinful even in the ordinary world, for the Lord’s pastimes are accomplished like actions in the material world. This is their very nature. *Lokavat tu līlā-kaivalyam*: the Lord’s pastimes are similar to actions in this world. (Brahma-sūtra 2.1.33)

The Lord has similar pastimes of love with Śrī and Bhū in Vaikuṇṭha. Since these pastimes are eternal there, they should be understood to occur because those pastimes delight the Lord, who enjoys pastimes independently. Kissing the Lord is not unsuitable since the consorts have the most pure forms, not less than the Lord’s form, since they are forms of his svarūpa-śakti. And as previously stated, theLord enjoys this. And one should not find fault with the women, thinking they are material women. Only afterattaining a suitable bhāva and a form arising from his svarūpa-śakti, do these women attain him by his will.

Is kāma sinful because of scriptural statements? This kāma of the consorts is not sinful since it is never stated as such in scriptures. And thus being endowed with the mood that the Lord is the husband is not a fault. Rather it is praised in scriptures.

*yāḥ samparyacaran premṇā pāda-saṁvāhanādibhiḥ*  
*jagad-guruṁ bhartṛ-buddhyā tāsāṁ kim varṇyate tapaḥ*

**And how could one possibly describe the greatness of the worship performed by other women, who serve the Supreme Lord, thinking of him as their husband, by mentally massaging his feet. SB 10.90.27**

It is also recorded that great sages had this bhāva. Madhvācārya quotes the following from Kūrma Purāṇa:

*agni-putrā mahātmānas tapasā strītvam āpire \|*  
*bhartāraṁ ca jagad-yoniṁ vāsudevam ajaṁ vibhum \|\|*

**The saintly sons of Agni attained bodies of women by the path of** ***vaidhi-bhakti,*** **and attained as their husband (not lover), the unborn, powerful Vāsudeva, source of the universe.**

Such persons are revered in statements like the following:

*pati-putra-suhṛd-bhrātṛ-pitṛvan mātravad dharim \|*  
*ye dhyāyanti sadodyuktās tebhyo’pīha namo namaḥ \|\|*

**I pay my respects repeatedly to those who constantly and eagerly meditate upon the Lord as their husband, son, well-wisher, brother, or the meditator as father or mother. Nārāyaṇa-vyūha-stava**

Approaching the Lord as an illicit lover is also not sinful. The gopīs say:

*yat paty-apatya-suhṛdām anuvṛttir aṅga*  
*strīṇāṁ sva-dharma iti dharma-vidā tvayoktam*  
*astv evam etad upadeśa-pade tvayīśe*  
*preṣṭho bhavāṁs tanu-bhṛtāṁ kila bandhur ātmā*

**Our dear Kṛṣṇa, as an expert in religion you have advised us that the proper religious duty for women is to faithfully serve their husbands, children and other relatives. We agree that this principle is valid, but actually this service should be rendered to you who give instructions. After all, O Lord, you are the dearest friend of all embodied souls. You are their most intimate friend and their very self. SB 10.29.32**

Śukadeva says:

*gopīnāṁ tat-patīnāṁ ca sarveṣām eva dehinām*  
*yo ’ntaś carati so ’dhyakṣaḥ krīḍaneneha deha-bhāk*

**Kṛṣṇa, appearing directly in this world, moves among the gopīs and their apparent husbands as well as the other inhabitants of Vraja, in his eternal spiritual body performing his pastimes. SB 10.33.35**

The Lord himself says that the gopīs’ service is flawless:

*na pāraye ’haṁ niravadya-saṁyujāṁ*  
*sva-sādhu-kṛtyaṁ vibudhāyuṣāpi vaḥ*  
*yā mābhajan durjara-geha-śṛṅkhalāḥ*  
*saṁvṛścya tad vaḥ pratiyātu sādhunā*

**I am not able to repay my debt for your spotless service, even within a lifetime of Brahmā. Your connection with me is beyond reproach. You have worshiped me, cutting off all domestic ties, which are difficult to break. Therefore please let your own glorious deeds be your compensation. SB 10.32.22**

This bhāva is seen in others also.

*purā maharṣayaḥ sarve daṇḍakāraṇya-vāsinaḥ \|*  
*dṛṣṭvā rāmaṁ hariṁ tatra bhoktum aicchat suvigraham \|\|*  
*te sarve strītvam āpannāḥ samudbhūtās tu gokule \|*  
*hariṁ samprāpya kāmena tato muktā bhavārṇavāt \|\|*

**Previously, all the sages living in Daṇḍakāraṇya forest, who after seeing Lord Rāma, desired enjoyment with His form, attained forms of women and appeared in Gokula. Attaining the Lord by that** ***kāma,*** **they became liberated from the ocean of the material world.Padma Purāṇa 6.245.164**

Since this mood of being a woman arises even in them, and is directed to the Lord, it does not arise from material Cupid and it is not material lust.

*tāsām āvirabhūc chauriḥ smayamāna-mukhāmbujaḥ*  
*pītāmbara-dharaḥ sragvī sākṣān manmatha-manmathaḥ*

**Then Kṛṣṇa, a smile on his lotus face, appeared before the gopīs, wearing a garland and a yellow garment. He directly appeared as one who can bewilder the mind of Cupid, though Cupid himself bewilders the minds of ordinary people. SB 10.32.2**

Since the Lord is worshipped in the āgamas as Cupid, this kāma is spiritual, manifested in people by the Lord himself. Great devotees like Uddhava praise this kāma:

*etāḥ paraṁ tanu-bhṛto bhuvi gopa-vadhvo*  
*govinda eva nikhilātmani rūḍha-bhāvāḥ*  
*vāñchanti yad bhava-bhiyo munayo vayaṁ ca*  
*kiṁ brahma-janmabhir ananta-kathā-rasasya*

**Among all persons on earth, these cowherd women alone have attained prefect bodies, for they have achievedmahābhāva for Govinda, the source of all forms of the Lord. Their pure love is desired by those who fear material existence, by great sages, and by ourselves as well. For one who has tasted the narrations of the infinite Lord, what is the use of taking birth as a high-class brāhmaṇa, or even as Brahmā himself? SB 10.47.58**

What is more, it is known from Bṛhad-vāmana Purāṇa that the śrutis had this type of bhāva. It is well known that because the śrutis desired the bhāva of the nitya-siddha gopīs, they became included among them with gopī forms. The śrutis indicate this in the following:

*nibhṛta-marun-mano-’kṣa-dṛḍha-yoga-yujo hṛdi yan*  
*munaya upāsate tad arayo ’pi yayuḥ smaraṇāt*  
*striya uragendra-bhoga-bhuja-daṇḍa-viṣakta-dhiyo*  
*vayam api te samāḥ sama-dṛśo ’ṅghri-saroja-sudhāḥ*

**Simply by constantly thinking of him, the enemies of the Lord attained the same Supreme Truth whom sages fixed in yoga worship by controlling their breath, mind and senses. Similarly, we śrutis, who generally see you as all-pervading, will achieve the same nectar from your lotus feet that your consorts are able to relish because of their loving attraction to your mighty, serpentine arms, for you look upon us and your consorts in the same way. SB 10.87.23**

The meaning is clear. The entity called Brahman which the sages worship with great effort by scriptural methods was attained by the Lord’s enemies simply by remembering him without worship. The gopīs (*te*) who attached their minds to thetwo snake-like two arms of Kṛṣṇa attained the nectar of your lotus feet, the sweetness of prema generated by your touch. We śrutis, having the same bhāva (*sama-dṛśaḥ*), hvaing attained equality (*samāḥ*) with the gopīs, also attained that sweetness of your feet.

According to the context the verb *yayuḥ* should be conjugated in the first person in reference to the śrutis (*yayima* instead of *yayuh*: we attained). The word *aṅghri* (feet) indicates respect. The enemies attained the Lord quickly by remembering him. This indicates that the path of bhāva quickly yields results. Having the same bhāva *(sama-dṛśaḥ*) indicates rāgānuga, and that it is the most effective. Otherwise the śrutis, who knew all types of sādhanas and sādyas, would have undertaken a different process.

Two groups of people, each with two types, undertake remembrance in the verse. In the first group, the sages who meditate on Brahman are the main subject and the enemies are secondary.

In the second group, the gopīs are the main subject and the śrutis are secondary. The word *api* after enemies and śrutis indicates that they attained the same goal as first subjects in their group.

The word *śtriyaḥ* means the eternal gopīs. The śrutis saw these gopīs in Kṛṣṇa’s eternal abode according to Bṛhad-vāmana Purāṇa. Thus it is correctly said that among the different bhāvas, hatred and fear are regarded as undesirable and should be given up (*tad-agham hitvā*). (Kāma on the other hand has just been explained as most praiseworthy..

## Anuccheda 321

Nārada stated that many attained the goal (SB 7.1.29). That is illustrated:

*gopyaḥ kāmād bhayāt kaṁso dveṣāc caidyādayo nṛpāḥ*  
*sambandhād vṛṣṇayaḥ snehād yūyaṁ bhaktyā vayaṁ vibho*

My dear King Yudhiṣṭhira! The gopīs by their conjugal desires, Kaṁsa by his fear, Śiśupāla and other kings by envy, and the Yadus and you Pāṇḍavas by your affectionate family relationships with Kṛṣṇa, and we, by our vaidhī-bhakti, have obtained the mercy of Kṛṣṇa. SB 7.1.31

The gopīs mentioned here are particiular gopīs (not the nitya-siddhas) who previously were performing sādhana. *Vayam* indicates Nārada. He refers to his previous practice by which he attained a spiritual body.

*prayujyamāne mayi tāṁ śuddhāṁ bhāgavatīṁ tanum \|*  
*ārabdha-karma-nirvāṇo nyapatat pāñca-bhautikaḥ \|\|*

**Having been awarded a transcendental body befitting an associate of the Lord, the body made of five material elements, with karmas relating to the present body, fell away. SB 1.6.28**

He had performed vaidhī bhakti.Later having attained rāga, rāgātmikā bhakti alone remained for him without respect for rules. That status is indicated in the following:

*na mayy ekānta-bhaktānāṁ guṇa-doṣodbhavā guṇāḥ*  
*sādhūnāṁ sama-cittānāṁ buddheḥ param upeyuṣām*

**The fully dedicated devotees who see equally everywhere and who have achieved the Lord who is superior to prakṛti do not have the guṇas which give rise to qualities of good and bad. SB 11.20.36**

Nārada states that those devotees attained the goal. They attained the goal in the past. Like the gopīs, people at the present time can attain that bhāva just by hearing about their qualities. It is said:

*śruta-mātro ’pi yaḥ strīṇāṁ prasahyākarṣate manaḥ*  
*uru-gāyoru-gīto vā paśyantīnāṁ ca kiṁ punaḥ*

**The Lord, whom countless songs glorify in countless ways, forcibly attracts the minds of all women who simply hear about him. What to speak, then, of those women who see him directly? SB 10.90.26**

Śiśupāla is called a sādhaka, though he was an associate in Vaikuṇṭha previously, to show the destruction of present apparent offense by bhāva. Attachment (*snehāt)* arising from being relatives (*sambandhāt)* is illustrated in the Vṛṣṇis and the Pāṇḍavas *(yūyam*). This is one item since the next verse mentions that there are five types of persons. (sambandha and sneha are taken separately, then there would be six types in the above verse.) Since both are mentionedin the above verse, the purpose is to show speciality of the attraction as a relative. The Vṛṣṇis and Pāṇḍavas mentioned are some Vṛṣṇis (who were sādhakas, whereas other Vṛṣṇis were nitya-siddhas) and relatives of the Pāṇḍavas (the Pāṇḍavas were nitya-siddhas) in their state before achieving perfection, when they performed sādhana.“Affection through relationship”means the delight experienced in that relationship. Nārada achieved the goal by bhakti. This means by vaidhī bhakti. Vaidhi is included in the list to show that the path of bhāva is attained after following rules.

## Anuccheda 322

If one can attain the Lord by hatred, why did Veṇa go to hell. That is answered in the next verse.

*katamo 'pi na venaḥ syāt pañcānāṁ puruṣaṁ prati*

**Vena was not one of the five types. SB 7.1.32**

Among the five mood direct to the Lord, Vena is not one of them (*katamaḥ*). He had enmity in the form of a critical attitude towards the Lord, but not constant enmity. Because he lacked intense meditation on the Lord he had to suffer sinful reactions. This means that one should not rashly cultivate an inimical attitude towards the Lord because of demonic nature, in order to gain liberation.

*ye vai bhagavatā proktā upāyā hy ātma-labdhaye*  
*añjaḥ puṁsām aviduṣāṁ viddhi bhāgavatān hi tān*

**Even ignorant living entities can very easily come to know the Supreme Lord if they adopt those means prescribed by the Supreme Lord. The process recommended by the Lord is known as bhāgavata-dharma. SB 11.2.34**

## Anuccheda 323

*tasmāt kenāpy upāyena manaḥ kṛṣṇe niveśayet*

**Therefore, one should somehow think of Kṛṣṇa, by one of the favorable methods. Sb 71.32**

As with the prevous verb *yuñjyāt* the verb *niveśayet* is in the potential mood to express a desire that this should be done option not an order. Among these methods, by the one most suitable, one should think of Kṛṣṇa. Whatis achieved after a long time by vaidhī bhakti undertaken with great effort is achived quickly by accepting a particular bhāva, even by hatred. If low persons withenmity towards the Lord, who is filled with the highest qualities, attaining the goal, what low, indifferent person couḷd not develop affection for the Lord? Thus rāgānuga is accepted as the most suitable path. Nārada speaks to Yudhiṣṭhira.

## Anuccheda 324

If the general path of bhāva (even hatred) is stronger than vaidhī bhakti, what to speak then of rāgānuga as the abhidheya?

*vaireṇa yaṁ nṛpatayaḥ śiśupāla-pauṇḍra-*  
*śālvādayo gati-vilāsa-vilokanādyaiḥ*  
*dhyāyanta ākṛta-dhiyaḥ śayanāsanādau*  
*tat-sāmyam āpur anurakta-dhiyāṁ punaḥ kim*

**Inimical kings like Śiśupāla, Pauṇḍraka and Śālva, while they were lying down, sitting or engaging in other activities, enviously meditated upon the bodily movements of the Lord, his sporting pastimes, and his loving glances. Being thus always absorbed in Kṛṣṇa, they achieved positions in the spiritual world. What then can be said of the benedictions offered to those who constantly fix their minds on Lord Kṛṣṇa in a favorable, loving mood? SB 11.5.48**

*Ākṛti-dhiyaḥ* means “Those who fix their minds on the forms of the Lord.” It is thus said:

*ajñāninaḥ sura-varaṁ samadhikṣipant.*  
*yaṁ pāpino’pi śiśupāla-suyodhanādyāḥ \|*  
*muktiṁ gatāḥ smaraṇa-mātra-vidhūta-pāpāḥ*  
*kaḥ saṁśayaḥ parama-bhakti-matāṁ janānām \|\|*

**Ignorant, sinful people like Śiśupāla and Duryodhana, who criticized Kṛṣṇa, purified of their sins by remembrance of him, attained liberation. What doubt is there about the attainment of those with the highest bhakti. Garuḍa Purāṇa**

Thus one should not think that enmity is the best attitude for attaining the Lord. However it is also said:

*mayi saṁrambha-yogena nistīrya brahma-helanam*  
*pratyeṣyataṁ nikāśaṁ me kālenālpīyasā punaḥ*

**Overcoming the offense to the Kumāras by meditating on me in anger, you will quickly return to me. SB 3.16.31**

These are the words of the Lord named Vaikuṇṭha to Jaya and Vijaya. This should be explained as follows. In order to experience the result to the apparent offense, the Lord arranged for an apparent show of anger as a means of meditation since the two by their nature were perfect and had already attained the Lord and this arrangement was made to facilitate the Lord’s pastime of fighting.

Some consider hatred and fear mentioned in this verse to be a type of bhakti. That is not true, for words like bhakti and service,implying a favorable attitude, are accepted as bhakti,whereas enmity and fear, implying the opposite, are not accepted. The difference between bhakti and hatred is made clear in the following:

*yogibhir dṛśyate bhaktyā nābhaktyā dṛśyate kvacit \|*  
*draṣṭuṁ na śakyo roṣāc ca matsarād vā janārdanaḥ \|*

**The Lord is seen by yogīs through devotion and cannot be seen without devotion. The Lord cannot be seen by anger or envy. Padma Purāṇa 6.238.83**

It seems that Uddhava says the demons are devotees:

*manye ’surān bhāgavatāṁs tryadhīśe*  
*saṁrambha-mārgābhiniviṣṭa-cittān*  
*ye saṁyuge ’cakṣata tārkṣya-putram*  
*aṁse sunābhāyudham āpatantam*

**I consider that the demons who had anger towards the Lord of three energies to be devotees, because during the battle they saw Garuḍa approaching with Kṛṣṇa, carrying his cakra*****,*** **on his shoulder. SB 3.2.24**

That is not so. Theword*manye* (I consider) should not be taken literally. Thus the demons are not really devotees. Uddhava makes this statement out of enthusiasm when the demons receive a little auspiciousness just by seeing the Lord.We are unfortunate because at the time of death we cannot see the Lord’s moon like face. The demons are more fortunate for they attained the good fortune to see his moon like face. Thus, hatred is not included in bhakti. Nārada speaks to Vasudeva.

## Anuccheda 325

Thus rāgānuga-bhakti has been proved to be an authorized process. Rāgānuga-bhakti concentrates chiefly on Kṛṣṇa since it is said:

*gopyaḥ kāmād bhayāt kaṁso dveṣāc caidyādayo nṛpāḥ*  
*sambandhād vṛṣṇayaḥ snehād yūyaṁ bhaktyā vayaṁ vibho*

**My dear King Yudhiṣṭhira! The gopīs by their conjugal desires, Kaṁsa by his fear, Śiśupāla and other kings by envy, and the Yadus and you Pāṇḍavas by your affectionate family relationships with Kṛṣṇa, and we, by our vaidhī-bhakti, have obtained the mercy of Kṛṣṇa. SB 7.1.31**

All the persons mentioned concentrated on Kṛṣṇa. Even the demons concentrated on him by hatred and attained perfection. This does not happen in relation to the Lord’s expansions or even other full forms of the Lord. Therefore it is said *tasmāt kenāpy upāyena manaḥ kṛṣṇe niveśayet*: therefore by one of these means fix the mind on Kṛṣṇa. Since rāgānuga directed to Kṛṣṇa causes quick absorption, when the Lordteachesvaidhībhaktioffered to himself in the Eleventh Canto, thisbhakti isin relation the four armed form, not to the form in Gokula. Since one sees pure rāga only in Gokula, the chief form of rāgānuga is practiced in relation to Kṛṣṇa in Gokula.

In Gokula the Lord responds to them as a son etc. *ye yathā māṁ prapadyante*: as they surrender to me I respond (BG 4.11)

*mallānām aśanir nṛṇāṁ nara-varaḥ strīṇāṁ smaro mūrtimān*  
*gopānāṁ sva-jano ’satāṁ kṣiti-bhujāṁ śāstā sva-pitroḥ śiśuḥ*  
*mṛtyur bhoja-pater virāḍ aviduṣāṁ tattvaṁ paraṁ yogināṁ*  
*vṛṣṇīnāṁ para-devateti vidito raṅgaṁ gataḥ sāgrajaḥ*

**The various groups of people in the arena regarded Kṛṣṇa in different ways when He entered it with His elder brother. The wrestlers saw Kṛṣṇa as a lightning bolt, the men of Mathurā as the best of males, the women as Cupid in person, the cowherd men as their relative, the impious rulers as a chastiser, His parents as their child, the King of the Bhojas as death, the unintelligent as the Supreme Lord’s universal form, the yogīs as the Absolute Truth and the Vṛṣṇis as their supreme worshipable Deity. SB 10.43.17**

*asyāpi deva vapuṣo mad-anugrahasya*  
*svecchā-mayasya tanu-bhūta-mayasya ko ’pi*  
*neśe mahi tv avasituṁ manasāntareṇa*  
*sākṣāt tavaiva kim utātma-sukhānubhūteḥ*

**Even by controlling the mind, no one can understand the greatness of this form of the Lord of the universe, which manifested in many four-armed forms, but which is actually you, the one form of Svayam Bhagavān, who gave memercy by you own will, who spread through the universe in many spiritual forms and possess bliss which can be understood only by yourself. SB 10.14.2**

Kṛṣṇa has a sincere desire to be fed, bathed, and fanned by his devotee. Even service in generalbhakti pleases the Lord.

*patraṁ puṣpaṁ phalaṁ toyaṁ yo me bhaktyā prayacchati*  
*tad ahaṁ bhakty-upahṛtam aśnāmi prayatātmanaḥ*

**If one offers me with love and devotion a leaf, a flower, a fruit or water, I will accept it. SB 10.81.4**

Śukadeva praises rāgānuga with a desire to attain it:

*pāda-saṁvāhanaṁ cakruḥ kecit tasya mahātmanaḥ*  
*apare hata-pāpmāno vyajanaiḥ samavījayan*

**Some of the cowherd boys, who were all great souls, would then massage his lotus feet, and others, qualified by being free of all sin, would expertly fan him. SB 10.15.17**

This attitude does not destroy the Lord’s powers, for at that time his powers appear elsewhere and it is his praiseworthy nature, though he is the controller, to act according to the wishes of his devotee. Though he was bound by Yaśodā, he freed the Yamalārjuna trees. Though possessing such powers, he is praised by Śukadeva for being controlled by Yaśodā.

*evaṁ sandarśitā hy aṅga hariṇā bhṛtya-vaśyatā*  
*sva-vaśenāpi kṛṣṇena yasyedaṁ seśvaraṁ vaśe*

**In this way the attractive Lord has shown that he is under the control of the devotee, even though as Svayam Bhagavān Kṛṣṇa controls the universe and all the devatās. SB 10.9.19**

Even today those dedicated to rāgānuga worship engage in worshipping him with qualities as the son of Nanda. The Lord says to the cowherds who were amazed when he lifted Govardhana:

*yadi vo’sti mayi prītiḥ ślāghyo’haṁ bhavatāṁ yadi \|*  
*tad-ātma-bandhu-sadṛśī buddhir vaḥ kriyatāṁ mayi \|\|*

**If you have affection for me and think me worthy of praise, then think of me as your kinsman (not as the Lord). Viṣṇu Purāṇa 5.13.11**

Another version has *tadārcā bandhu-sadṛśīṁ bāndhavāḥ kriyatāṁ mayi:* O friends! You should worship me like a friend.

*nāhaṁ devo na gandharvo na yakṣo na ca dānavaḥ \|*  
*ahaṁ vo bāndhavo jāto nātaś cintyam ato’nyathā \|\|*

**I am not a devatā, a Gandharva, a Yakṣa or Dānava. I am your friend. You should not think otherwise. Viṣṇu Purāṇa 5.13.12**

*yuvāṁ māṁ putra-bhāvena brahma-bhāvena cāsakṛt*  
*cintayantau kṛta-snehau yāsyethe mad-gatiṁ parām*

**Both of you, husband and wife, constantly think of me with eternal prema as your son, and maybe you once thought of me as Brahman. You will achieve my abode which you already possess. SB 10.3.45**

Both moods (bhāva and reverence) towards the Lord are present in Vāsudeva and Devakī since they had a predominance of reverence towards the Lord (as well as being parents). It is stated that in their previous life they prominently performed austerities.

Parīkṣit did not praise Yaśodā for seeing the universe in Kṛṣṇa’s mouth but revered the Lord’s mercy (called māyā) by which she felt affection for him as a son. He praises Yaśodā’s and Nanda’s good fortune not available to Vasudeva and Devakī, for seeing Kṛṣṇa as their son, which inspired Kṛṣṇa to perform his childhood pastimes.

*nandaḥ kim akarod brahman śreya evaṁ mahodayam*  
*yaśodā ca mahā-bhāgā papau yasyāḥ stanaṁ hariḥ*

**What pious acts did Nanda perform to cause such affection and what pious acts did most fortunate Yaśodā perform so that the Lord drank from her breast?**

*pitarau nānvavindetāṁ kṛṣṇodārārbhakehitam*  
*gāyanty adyāpi kavayo yal loka-śamalāpaham*

**Great devotees sing of the activities of Kṛṣṇa in his childhood which Vasudeva and Devakī could not experience and which even today destroy the sins of all people. SB 10.8.46-47**

Śukadeva also praises that prema:

*evaṁ sandarśitā hy aṅga hariṇā bhṛtya-vaśyatā*  
*sva-vaśenāpi kṛṣṇena yasyedaṁ seśvaraṁ vaśe*

**In this way the attractive Lord has shown that he is under the control of the devotee, even though as Svayam Bhagavān Kṛṣṇa controls the universe and all the devatās. SB 10.9.19**

Nārada teaches sādhakas how to act using Vasudeva and Devakī as examples:

*darśanāliṅganālāpaiḥ śayanāsana-bhojanaiḥ*  
*ātmā vāṁ pāvitaḥ kṛṣṇe putra-snehaṁ prakurvatoḥ*

**You and your wife Devakī, having manifested love for Kṛṣṇa and accepted him as your son, have purified your selves by seeing him, embracing him, speaking with him, resting with him, sitting together with him and taking your meals with him. SB 11.5.47**
