The commentary says, “By pampering their son they accomplished everything in bhāgavad-dharma.”

*māpatya-buddhim akṛthāḥ kṛṣṇe sarvātmanīśvare*  
*māyā-manuṣya-bhāvena gūḍhaiśvarye pare ’vyaye*

**Do not think that the Supreme Lord, the soul of all beings, who conceals his powers, appears only as your son. Having human form as his svarūpa, he hides his powers but remains with undiminished powers. SB 11.5.49**

Though this statement seems to discourage thinking of Kṛṣṇa as a son, Śrīdhara Svāmī says in his commentary, “If affection for sons were a cause of liberation then everyone would be liberated. In answer to this he says this verse.” Though he is a son, and is controlled by people who think of him in this way, he also has all powers as his nature. Another explanation of the verse is to take *akṛthāḥ* as a negative “Do not think of the Lord as your son.” With the other negative particle *mā* the statement becomes positive. “Think of the Lord as your son.”

Thus without considering knowledge or lack of knowledge of the Lord’s powers performance of pure rāgānuga is best.

*jñātvājñātvātha ye vai māṁ yāvān yaś cāsmi yādṛśaḥ*  
*bhajanty ananya-bhāvena te me bhaktatamā matāḥ*

**Those who may or may not know the extent of my powers, my forms and my nature, but who worship me with great possessiveness, I consider to be the best of devotees. SB 11.11.33**

Because of the purity of rāgātmika-bhaktas in Gokula, the bhakti following them is the chief type of rāgānuga. It is established that rāgānuga is the greatest form of worshipping Kṛṣṇa, since it displays Kṛṣṇa’s full nature and has great potency which cannot be displayed anywhere else. That rāgānuga is composed of Gokula pastimes.

Now its greatness in worshipping Kṛṣṇa will be shown.

*munayaḥ sādhu pṛṣṭo ’haṁ bhavadbhir loka-maṅgalam \|*  
*yat kṛtaḥ kṛṣṇa-sampraśno yenātmā suprasīdati \|\|*

**O sages! You have asked good questions about what is beneficial for the world, because you have asked questions all about Kṛṣṇa, by which the intellect is immediately satisfied. SB 1.2.5**

The following should be said. Previously the cause of satisfaction of the mind was asked. The answer is that questions about Kṛṣṇa particularly are the cause of satisfaction.

The verse following says:

*sa vai puṁsāṁ paro dharmo yato bhaktir adhokṣaje \|*  
*ahaituky apratihatā yayātmā suprasīdati \|\|*

**The supremedharma for all human beings (sādhana bhakti) is that by which bhakti to the Lord arises, which is not caused by anything other than itself, cannot be obstructed, and which satisfies the mind completely. SB 1.2.6**

One starts with offering karmas with great effort and finally achieves steadiness in bhakti. From the worship comes satisfaction. But questions about Kṛṣṇa immediately satisfy the mind or intellect.

Discussion of other avatāras leads to absorption in Kṛṣṇa:

*harer adbhuta-vīryasya kathā loka-sumaṅgalāḥ \|\|*  
*kathayasva mahābhāga yathāham akhilātmani \|*  
*kṛṣṇe niveśya niḥsaṅgaṁ manas tyakṣye kalevaram \|\|*

**Topics of the Lord who has astonishing powers in other avatāra forms are all-auspicious for the world. O great soul! Please speak so that I can give up the body absorbing my mind, devoid of material desires, in Kṛṣṇa, who is the source of all avatāras.. SB 2.8.3-4**

*Hareḥ* means “of avatāra forms.” *Akhilātmani*means “the source of all avatāras.” Kṛṣṇa means the friend of Arjuna (not Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana Vyāsa). Parīkṣit speaks.

## Anuccheda 326

At the end of the discussion with Uddhava the same conclusion is stated. Though the Lord speaks of results generated by bhakti accompanied by jñāna at the end of the previous chapter, Uddhava, the greatest devotee, cannot accept even a little jñāna in bhakti.

*su-dustarām imāṁ manye yoga-caryām anātmanaḥ*  
*yathāñjasā pumān siddhyet tan me brūhy añjasācyuta*

**O Acyuta, I think that the method of yogadescribed by you is very difficult for one who cannot control his mind. Therefore please explain to me immediately how someone can more quickly attain perfection.**

*prāyaśaḥ puṇdarīkākṣa yuñyanto yogino manaḥ*  
*viṣīdanty asamādhānān mano-nigraha-karśitāḥ*

**O lotus-eyed Lord! Generally those yogīs who try to absorb their minds in Brahman experience frustration. Because of their inability to perfect samādhi, they become fatigued in their attempt to bring the mind under control. SB 11.29.1-2**

Because it is difficult in most cases, one will not achieve the final result and because bhakti, which he desired to hear about, is easy to execute, one will definitely achieve the results, bhakti alone should be practiced. That is the intention of his statement in the two verses. With no regard for jñāna, practicing bhakti alone, devotees practice bhakti to the form of Kṛṣṇa alone, without giving attention to the results of jñāna and yoga. That is explained in the next four verses.

*athāta ānanda-dughaṁ padāmbujaṁ*  
*haṁsāḥ śrayerann aravinda-locana*  
*sukhaṁ nu viśveśvara yoga-karmabhis*  
*tvan-māyayāmī vihatā na māninaḥ*

**O lotus-eyed Lord of the universe! Therefore, discerning men joyfully take shelter of your lotus feet, the source of bliss. They are not obstructed by actions of yoga, your illusory energy and they are not proud. SB 11.29.3**

Because some (*yogīs*) are depressed in their attempts, therefore (*athātaḥ*) those skilful at discerning the essence (*haṁsāḥ*) happily (*sukham*) serve with certainty (*nu*) your lotus feet, filled with all bliss. Since Uddhava was directly seeing Kṛṣṇa’s feet when he was speaking, he does not say “your lotus feet” but simply “the lotus feet.” These pure devotees (*amī*) are not obstructed by activities of yoga, which is your māyā, because of which they do not become proud. In this sādhana they think that they achieve results from the Lord’s unconditional mercy to the fallen, not by their own efforts in yoga.

## Anuccheda 327

The devotee receives the results of jñāna and yoga, but not only that, much more:

*kiṁ citram acyuta tavaitad aśeṣa-bandho*  
*dāseṣv ananya-śaraṇesu yad ātma-sāttvam*  
*yo ’rocayat saha mṛgaiḥ svayam īśvarāṇā.*  
*śrīmat-kirīṭa-taṭa-pīḍita-pāda-pīṭhaḥ*

**O infallible Lord! O friend of all beings! It is not very astonishing that you are dependent on your servants who have taken exclusive shelter of you. You displayed special affection for animals of Vṛndāvana whiledevatās shook your foot pedestal with the tips of their crowns. SB 11.29.4**

O friend of the completely surrendered (*aśeṣa-bandho*), or friend of all, even the demons! You give benefit to all without discrimination in the form of liberation etc. This is not astonishing for you to do. You are dependent (*ātma-sattvam*) on your completely surrendered devotees, who are averse to jñāna and yoga, such as Bali. For example you say:

*na sādhayati māṁ yogo na sāṅkhyaṁ dharma uddhava*  
*na svādhyāyas tapas tyāgo yathā bhaktir mamorjitā*

**O Uddhava! I am attained by unmixed bhakti. I cannot be attained by mystic yoga, Sāṅkhya philosophy, dharma, Vedic study, austerity or renunciation. SB 11.14.20**

You do not show any prejudice for species or qualities among the living beings who surrender. This is seen your intimate pastimes. You enjoyed sakhya bhāva (*saha*) with the animals of Vṛndāvana. But what is your other nature? Śiva, Brahmā and others (*īśvarānām)* bowed to your foot pedestal. You give liberation, which is the highest fruit for the jñānīs and yogīs, to demons. But you are dependent on your servants like the Pāṇḍavas. You act as their friend, messenger and guard. The intention of Uddhava is to say that bhakti to Kṛṣṇa alone is the process.

## Anuccheda 328

The result is described:

*taṁ tvākhilātma-dayiteśvaram āśritānāṁ*  
*sarvārtha-daṁ sva-kṛta-vid visṛjeta ko nu*  
*ko vā bhajet kim api vismṛtaye ’nu bhūtyai*  
*kiṁ vā bhaven na tava pāda-rajo-juṣāṁ naḥ*

**What person who knows the mercy that you show to your devotees could reject you, the dearest among alljīvas, the Supreme Lord of all, who gives all perfections to the devotees who take shelter of you? Who would reject you and accept something for the sake of material enjoyment or liberation, which simply leads to forgetfulness of you? And what lack is there for us who are engaged in the service of the dust of your lotus feet? SB 11.29.5**

What person who knows what favoryou have done for him (*sva-kṛta-vit*), having revealed to him your beauty as described in SB 3.28.13), could reject you, as described in the previous verse (*tam tvā)*? There are some persons like the following:

*evaṁ harau bhagavati pratilabdha-bhāvo*  
*bhaktyā dravad-dhṛdaya utpulakaḥ pramodāt*  
*autkaṇṭhya-bāṣpa-kalayā muhur ardyamānas*  
*tac cāpi citta-baḍiśaṁ śanakair viyuṅkte*

**The unfortunate yogī who has developed love for the Lord, full of all sweet qualities, whose heart is somewhat soft because of devotion, whose body hairs stand on end in ecstasy, who is constantly overcome with intense tears of joy, gradually withdraws his hook-like mind from the Lord’s form. SB 3.29.36**

But no one can give you up: he who gives you up is most ungrateful.

And who are you? You are dearer than a million lives to all beings by your nature (*akhilātma-dayita*) and are the controller *(īśvaram*). You give everything to those who take shelter of you. The word *nu* indicates conjecture. Who would accept devatās or another sādhana, and reject you, for wealth and power (*bhūtyai)* or for liberation (*vismṛtaye)?* No one would do so. All these results are included in bhakti to you that we perform. What is not achieved by those who worship your foot dust? The word *vā* in the last line indicates disinterest in these results.

*yat karmabhir yat tapasā jñāna-vairāgyataś ca yat*  
*yogena dāna-dharmeṇa śreyobhir itarair api*  
*sarvaṁ mad-bhakti-yogena mad-bhakto labhate ’ñjasā*  
*svargāpavargaṁ mad-dhāma kathañcid yadi vāñchati*

**Everything that can be achieved by karma, penance, jñāna, vairāgya, mystic yoga, charity, dharma and all other auspicious means of perfecting life is easily achieved by my devotee through bhakti. If somehow or other my devotee desires Svarga, liberation, or residence in my abode, he easily achieves such benedictions. SB 11.20.32-33**

*naivopayanty apacitiṁ kavayas taveśa*  
*brahmāyuṣāpi kṛtam ṛddha-mudaḥ smarantaḥ*  
*yo ’ntar bahis tanu-bhṛtām aśubhaṁ vidhunvann*  
*ācārya-caittya-vapuṣā sva-gatiṁ vyanakti*

**O my Lord! Intelligent persons cannot fully express their indebtedness to you, even if they were endowed with the lifetime of Brahmā, because on remembering what you have done for them, their joy increases even more: appearing externally as guru and internally as Paramātmā, you reveal the goal of prema to the devotees. SB 11.29.6**

O Lord! The wise (*kavayaḥ*), even with a life span like Brahmā’s, remembering your assistance (*kṛtam*) becoming ever increasingly blissful (*ṛddha-mudaḥ*) because of bhakti to you, cannot see (*upayanti*) how they can repay you (*apacitim*). Therefore they never give you up. What you have done is described. For some among all body bearers, those who have received your mercy, you destroy obstacles *(aśubham*) to your bhakti and reveal understanding of yourself (*sva-gatim*) externally through a guru (*ācārya-vapuṣā*) and internally as a form revealed through meditation (*caittya-vapuṣā*). Uddhava speaks.

## Anuccheda 330

The Lord then speaks about the greatness of bhakti. To devotees like Uddhava, he speaks pure bhakti in four verses starting with the following:

*hanta te kathayiṣyāmi mama dharmān su-maṅgalān*  
*yān śraddhayācaran martyo mṛtyuṁ jayati durjayam*

**Yes, I shall describe to you bhakti and jñāna*****,*** **easy to perform. By executing these, a mortal will conquer unconquerable death. SB 11.29.8**

Then for others not so inclined, he considers by his mercy other things in order to encourage his worship. Since people are generally inclined to compete with others, he considered that they couldonly worship his form as antaryāmī as a means of somehow making them favorable. In order to make them quickly give up the competitive mood and turn towards the Lord, he teaches worship of himself in the form of his portion the antaryāmī.

*atha vā bahunaitena kiṁ jñātena tavārjuna \|*  
*viṣṭabhyāham idaṁ kṛtsnam ekāṁśena sthito jagat \|\|*

**But what is the use of knowing all these individual vibhūtis, Arjuna? Please understand: by just one portion of myself, I am firmly established in this universe. BG 10.42**

Thus the Lord says:

*mām eva sarva-bhūteṣu bahir antar apāvṛtam*  
*īkṣetātmani cātmānaṁ yathā kham amalāśayaḥ*

**With a pure heart one should see me, the complete form of Paramātmā, within all beings and also within oneself, present both externally and internally, just like the ether spreads everywhere without being contaminated. SB 11.29.12**

The commentary says, “In three verses the Lord speaks about essential bhakti. One should see me situated as the Lord (*ātmānam*) in all beings (*ātmani*).”

That Lord is complete, being outside and inside (*bahiḥ antaḥ*). How can this be? He cannot be covered or restrained (*apāvrṭam*). How is this possible? He is without limbs and spreads everywhere like ether. One should see only me, the form of Kṛṣṇa, not the form of antaryāmī. In this manner, it becomes essential (antaraṅga) bhakti.

## Anuccheda 331

*iti sarvāṇi bhūtāni mad-bhāvena mahā-dyute*  
*sabhājayan manyamāno jñānaṁ kevalam āśritaḥ*  
*brāhmaṇe pukkase stene brahmaṇye ’rke sphuliṅgake*  
*akrūre krūrake caiva sama-dṛk paṇḍito mataḥ*

**O brilliant Uddhava! The person who, though taking shelter of the vision of antaryāmī, respects and considers all beings as being my form , who sees me in the brāhmaṇa and the outcaste, the thief and the giver of charity to brāhmaṇas, the sun and the sparks of fire, the gentle and the cruel, is learned. SB 11.29.13-14**

Though taking shelter of the vision of antaryāmī (*kevalaṁ jñānam*), honoring and thinking of all entities as being situated as my form (*mad-bhāvena*) in the manner previously explained, one is considered to be learned. He sees me in the brāhmaṇa and others.

## Anuccheda 332

*nareṣv abhīkṣṇaṁ mad-bhāvaṁ puṁso bhāvayato ’cirāt*  
*spardhāsūyā-tiraskārāḥ sāhaṅkārā viyanti hi*

**He who constantly meditates upon my presence within all persons quickly destroys rivalry, envy and abusiveness, along with false ego. SB 11.29.15**

This type of worship quickly destroys competition.

*visṛjya smayamānān svān dṛśaṁ vrīḍāṁ ca daihikīm*  
*praṇamed daṇḍa-vad bhūmāv ā-śva-cāṇḍāla-go-kharam*

**Disregarding friends’ ridicule, one’s bodily consciousness and embarrassment, one should offer obeisances before all—even the dogs, outcastes, cows and asses—falling flat upon the ground like a rod. SB 11.29.16**

To practice this vision one should offer respects to all beings.

*yāvat sarveṣu bhūteṣu mad-bhāvo nopajāyate*  
*tāvad evam upāsīta vāṅ-manaḥ-kāya-vṛttibhiḥ*

**Until one has fully developed the ability to see me within all living beings, one must continue to offer respects by speech, mind and body. SB 11.29.17**

The result of this worship is manifestation of Kṛṣṇa everywhere spontaneously:

*sarvaṁ brahmātmakaṁ tasya vidyayātma-manīṣayā*  
*paripaśyann uparamet sarvato mukta-saṁśayaḥ*

**By the practice of seeing the Lord everywhere, one finally realizes everything is Brahman (Kṛṣṇa). Seeing Brahman everywhere, freed from all doubts, one gives up all action. SB 11.29.18**

There is a similar usage of the word *brahman* in verse where the Lord speaks to the Pracetas:

*navyavad dhṛd aye yaj jño brahmaitad brahma-vādibhiḥ*  
*na muhyanti na śocanti na hṛṣyanti yato gatāḥ*

**Those who have attained my form which is known as Brahman by the followers of Brahman are not bewildered. They do not lament and do not rejoice, since I, full of knowledge, have entered their fresh lotus hearts. SB 4.30.20**

The commentary says that Brahman refers to a fresh manifestation of Bhagavān. Thus the result of this worship is seeing Bhagavān everywhere. Or the word Brahman is used in Gopāla-tāpanī to indicate Kṛṣṇa in the following.

*yo’vatārāṇāṁ madhye śreṣṭho’vatāraḥ ko bhavati .*  
*yena lokās tuṣṭā devās tuṣṭā bhavanti .*  
*yaṁ smṛtvā muktā asmāt saṁsārād bhavanti .*  
*kathaṁ vāsyāvatārasya brahmatā bhavati \|*

**Brahmā spoke. Who is the best among the avatāras, by whom all people and devatās become satisfied, by whose remembrance people become liberated from saṁsāra? How can this avatāra be Svayam Bhagavān (*****brahmatā*****)? Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad 2.24**

Thus the result of the process is to see parabrahman in human form. After stating this Kṛṣṇa praises such worship as topmost:

*ayaṁ hi sarva-kalpānāṁ sadhrīcīno mato mama*  
*mad-bhāvaḥ sarva-bhūteṣu mano-vāk-kāya-vṛttibhiḥ*

**I consider this process—using one’s mind, words and bodily functions for realizing me within all living beings—to be the easiest process to realize me. SB 11.29.19**

I consider this to be the correct method (*sadhrīcīnaḥ*) among all methods (*sarva-kalānām*): thinking of (*bhāvaḥ*) my form (*mat*) in all beings.

## Anuccheda 333

Worshipping the form of Kṛṣṇa is superior to worshipping the antaryāmī. That is the conclusion of Gītā:

*īśvaraḥ sarva-bhūtānāṁ hṛd-deśe ’rjuna tiṣṭhati*  
*bhrāmayan sarva-bhūtāni yantrārūḍhāni māyayā \|\|*

**O Arjuna, the Lord is situated in the heart of all living entities. He engages in action all living entities, who are mounted on a machine made of prakṛti.**

*tam eva śaraṇaṁ gaccha sarva-bhāvena bhārata*  
*tat-prasādāt parāṁ śāntiṁ sthānaṁ prāpsyasi śāśvatam \|\|*

**O descendent of Bharata, take refuge in the Paramātmā alone with all your facilities. By his mercy, you will attain the eternal spiritual abode.**

*iti te jñānam ākhyātaṁ guhyād guhyataraṁ mayā*  
*vimṛśyaitad aśeṣeṇa yathecchasi tathā kuru \|\|*

**Thus I have explained a scripture more secret than the secret knowledge. Considering this thoroughly, do as you wish to do.**

*sarva-guhyatamaṁ bhūyaḥ śṛṇu me paramaṁ vacaḥ*  
*iṣṭo ’si me dṛḍham iti tato vakṣyāmi te hitam \|\|*

**Again, hear from me the highest words, the supreme secret, since you are very dear to me and you have faith in the authority of my words. I will speak for your benefit.**

*man-manā bhava mad-bhakto mad-yājī māṁ namaskuru*  
*mām evaiṣyasi satyaṁ te pratijāne priyo ’si me \|\|65\|\|*

**Being my devotee, offer your mind to me. Offer articles to me in worship. Offer respects to me. I promise that you will come to me alone without doubt, for you are most dear to me.**

*sarva-dharmān parityajya mām ekaṁ śaraṇaṁ vraja*  
*ahaṁ tvāṁ sarva-pāpebhyo mokṣayiṣyāmi mā śucaḥ \|\|*

Giving up all dharmas, just surrender unto me alone. I will deliver you from all sins. Do not worry. BG 18.61-66

**The secret knowledge (*****guhyam*****), jñāna, was spoken in the previous chapter. The more secret knowledge (*****guhyataram*****) is knowledge of antaryāmī. The most secret knowledge is worship consisting of complete surrender andthe mind thinking of the Lord. The two elements are the same. It is also said in the ninth chapter:**

*idaṁ tu te guhyatamaṁ pravakṣyāmy anasūyave \|*  
*jñānaṁ vijñāna-sahitaṁ yaj jñātvā mokṣyase ’śubhāt \|\|*

**I will speak to you, who have no envy, the most secret knowledge, bhakti, along with realization of me, knowing which you will be freed from suffering. BG 9.1**

Kṛṣṇa then reveals the goal of what he will say later:

*rāja-vidyā rāja-guhyaṁ pavitram idam uttamam \|*  
*pratyakṣāvagamaṁ dharmyaṁ su-sukhaṁ kartum avyayam \|\|*

**This is the king of knowledge, the king of secrets, the most purifying of all methods, bringing direct realization of the Lord. It is nourished by righteous actions, easy to execute, and eternal. BG9.2**

He then criticizes person who lack faith in worship if Kṛṣṇa and praises those with faith in that process.

*avajānanti māṁ mūḍhā mānuṣīṁ tanum āśritam \|*  
*paraṁ bhāvam ajānanto mama bhūta-maheśvaram \|\|*

**The fools deride me, the Lord of all beings, thinking I have accepted a material body of human, not knowing that it is my original spiritual form, pure Brahman, higher than all else.**

*moghāśā mogha-karmāṇo mogha-jñānā vicetasaḥ \|*  
*rākṣasīm āsurīṁ caiva prakṛtiṁ mohinīṁ śritāḥ \|\|*

**Among those who cannot distinguish this truth, the devotees do not gain liberation, the karmīs do not attain their material fruits, and the jñānīs get no result from their knowledge. They assume the nature of Rākṣasas and asuras.**

*mahātmānas tu māṁ pārtha daivīṁ prakṛtim āśritāḥ \|*  
*bhajanty ananya-manaso jñātvā bhūtādim avyayam \|\|*

**Those of profound mind, taking shelter of myspiritual svarūpa, serve my human form with no distractions, knowing that my human-like body is the cause of all things, that it is indestructible and fully spiritual. BG 9.11-13**

The fools out of disrespect think I have accepted a human body (*mānuṣīṁ tanum āśritam*).

Therefore Kṛṣṇa teaches the most secret knowledge (*sarva-guhyatamam* BG18.64) afterwards, since it is superior to worship of antaryāmī. After teaching everything, he teaches finally worship of Kṛṣṇa as the highest of all. It is thus superior even to worship of the other avatāras.

Thus in the Eleventh Canto, the superiority of bhakti to Kṛṣṇa is stated by contrasting it with results given even by negative relationship with Kṛṣṇa:

*yo yo mayi pare dharmaḥ kalpyate niṣphalāya cet*  
*tad-āyāso nirarthaḥ syād bhayāder iva sattama*

**O Uddhava, greatest of saints! If dharmais offered to me without personal motivation, the effort will not be useless, just as fear related to the Lord is not useless. SB 11.29.20**

Dharma prescribed by the Vedas done as an offering to me, if offered without desire for results (*niṣphalāya kalpate*), dharma done with effort, will not be useless (*anirarthaḥ*). When the actions are done without desire for enjoyment, they do not create obstacles for bhakti and are thus not useless. Since acts are offered to Kṛṣṇa favorably, how much more useful they are than acts offered unfavorably, which also give results. This is made clear by an example. The efforts of Kaṁsa and others, related to the Lord by fear or hatred, were not useless since those efforts gave them liberation.

## Anuccheda 334

The specific form of Kṛṣṇa alone is most excellent in producing all results in sādhana and sādhya for devotees dedicated to him, like Uddhava.

*jñāne karmaṇi yoge ca vārtāyāṁ daṇḍa-dhāraṇe*  
*yāvān artho nṛṇāṁ tāta tāvāṁs te ’haṁ catur-vidhaḥ*

**Through analytical knowledge, ritualistic work, mystic yoga, mundane business and politics, people seek to advance in religiosity, economic development, sense gratification and liberation. Whatever men can accomplish in these multifarious ways I give to my devotee. SB 11.29.33**

I give all results--whatever artha, dharma, kāma and mokṣa is available through jñāna, karma or yoga. From jñāna results liberation. From karma comes dharma and kāma. From yoga arise various siddhis, which are material results *(artha*). From occupations and administration also arise material artha. The Lord speaks.

## Anuccheda 335

Again Uddhava prays:

*namo ’stu te mahā-yogin prapannam anuśādhi mām*  
*yathā tvac-caraṇāmbhoje ratiḥ syād anapāyinī*

**Obeisances unto you, O greatest of** ***yogīs*****! Please instruct me, surrendered to you, so that I may have constant attachment to your lotus feet. SB 11.29.40**

The commentary says, “Though you have done a lot, I still request as follows. Please instruct me (*anuśādhi*) what should be taught: attachment to your lotus feet, which is constantly, even in the liberated state.” Uddhava speaks.

## Anuccheda 336

The same intention is expressed in the following:

*yathā tvām aravindākṣa yādṛśaṁ vā yad-ātmakam*  
*dhyāyen mumukṣur etan me dhyānaṁ tvaṁ vaktum arhasi*

**My dear lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa, by what process should one who desires liberation meditate upon you? What are the details of the object of meditation and what is the object of meditation? Kindly explain to me this topic of meditation. I however aspire for service to you. SB 11.14.31**

The commentary says, “You should tell aboutmeditation so that those who desire liberation meditate on you. Please tell this for anyone who is inquisitive. My goal however is service (*etat*) to you. That is not accomplished by meditation. This was mentioned previously:

*tvayopabhukta-srag-gandha- vāso-’laṅkāra-carcitāḥ*  
*ucchiṣṭa-bhojino dāsās tava māyāṁ jayema hi*

**Simply by decorating ourselves with the garlands, fragrant oils, clothes and ornaments that you have already enjoyed, and by eating the remnants of your meals, we, your servants, will indeed conquer your illusory energy. SB 11.6.46”**

Uddhava speaks.

## Anuccheda 337

Uddhava remembers Kṛṣṇa, most auspiciousamong all the avatāras and sources of avatāras:

*aho bakī yaṁ stana-kāla-kūṭaṁ*  
*jighāṁsayāpāyayad apy asādhvī*  
*lebhe gatiṁ dhātry-ucitāṁ tato ’nyaṁ*  
*kaṁ vā dayāluṁ śaraṇaṁ vrajema*

**Oh! Evil Pūtanā, who offered her poisonous breast to Kṛṣṇa to drink with the intention of killing him, attained the position of a nurse in the spiritual world. How else is so merciful? I surrender to him! SB 3.2.23**

Pūtanā attained a destination suitable for a nurse (*dhārty-ucitam gatim*). Uddhava speaks.

## Anuccheda 338

The great power of worshipping Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes in Gokula has been shown in the verse above. That is also described in the following:

*pūtanā loka-bāla-ghnī rākṣasī rudhirāśanā*  
*jighāṁsayāpi haraye stanaṁ dattvāpa sad-gatim*  
*kiṁ punaḥ śraddhayā bhaktyā kṛṣṇāya paramātmane*  
*yacchan priyatamaṁ kiṁ nu raktās tan-mātaro yathā*

**Pūtanā was always hankering for the blood of human children, and with that desire she came to kill Kṛṣṇa; but because she offered her breast to the Lord, she attained Kṛṣṇa. What then is to be said of those who had faith or devotion for Kṛṣṇa and who offer something very dear, as a mother offers something to a child? SB 10.6.35**

*yena yenāvatāreṇa bhagavān harir īśvaraḥ*  
*karoti karṇa-ramyāṇi mano-jñāni ca naḥ prabho*  
*yac-chṛṇvato ’paity aratir vitṛṣṇā*  
*sattvaṁ ca śuddhyaty acireṇa puṁsaḥ*  
*bhaktir harau tat-puruṣe ca sakhyaṁ*  
*tad eva hāraṁ vada manyase cet*

**O Lord! Hearing about the Lord, full of all qualities, destroyer, of suffering, attractor of the senses, who through his appearance causes pleasure to the ears and great attraction to the mind, destroys all disinterest in the topics and all thirst for material enjoyment and produces prema for the attractive Lord and friendship with the devotees. Please speak of the Lord’s pastimes if you think I am qualified. SB 10.7.1-2**

The supreme excellence of pastimes like the rāsa-līlā with the gopīs in Gokula is described:

*vikrīḍitaṁ vraja-vadhūbhir idaṁ ca viṣṇoḥ*  
*śraddhānvito ’nuśṛṇuyād atha varṇayed yaḥ*  
*bhaktiṁ parāṁ bhagavati pratilabhya kāmaṁ*  
*hṛd-rogam āśv apahinoty acireṇa dhīraḥ*

**Anyone who faithfully and continually hears or describes the Lord’s playful affairs with the young gopīs of Vṛndāvana will attain the highest type of prema to the Lord, quickly destroy lust, the disease of the heart, and immediately become steady. SB 10.33.39**

One should with faith hear or (*atha*) describe the pastimes of Kṛṣṇa with the gopīs as well as other pastimes *(ca*). Also one could meditate on the pastimes. One attains bhakti above which nothing else exists (*parām*). One quickly gives up lust and other diseases of the heart.

Though generally this conjugal relationship is the highestbhakti, the supremebhakti is worship of Kṛṣṇain his pastimes with Rādhā, the dearest devotee. That is the natural conclusion. But these confidential pastimesare not worshipped with the senses by bhāvas of being a parent, son or servant as in the material world since these are contrary to the mood of that love. Sometimes the pastimes areslightly confidential and sometimes most confidential. Śukadeva speaks.

## Anuccheda 339

The path of bhakti has been shown in this Sandarbha. The secret which is one’s fully dedicated goal, present in sādhana and sādhya, and attained by mercy of Śrī-guru and Śrī-bhagavān, is not to be revealed to anyone.

*naitat parasmā ākhyeyaṁ pṛṣṭayāpi kathañcana*  
*sarvaṁ sampadyate devi deva-guhyaṁ susaṁvṛtam*

**O lady! Even if someone inquires, you should not disclose this fact to anyone. Secret things known only to the devatās become successful. SB 8.17.20**

*Sampadyate* means “it becomes successful.” Viṣṇu speaks to Aditi.

## Anuccheda 340

In this way sādhana bhakti has been shown. The progress to perfection is shown at the beginning teachings of Sūta starting with:

*śuśrūṣoḥ śraddadhānasya vāsudeva-kathā-ruciḥ \|*  
*syān mahat-sevayā viprāḥ puṇya-tīrtha-niṣevaṇāt \|\|*

**O brāhmaṇas! Attraction for topics concerning Kṛṣṇa will arise by service to the great devotees, followed by faith, by surrender to the feet of the pure guru, and by the desire to hear. SB 1.2.16**

Nārada described his sādhana:

*ahaṁ purātīta-bhave ’bhavaṁ mune*  
*dāsyās tu kasyāścana veda-vādinām \|*  
*nirūpito bālaka eva yogināṁ*  
*śuśrūṣaṇe prāvṛṣi nirvivikṣatām \|\|*

**In another kalpa of Brahmā in a previous life, I was born as the son of a maidservant engaged by some persons studying the Vedas. Though a child, I was also engaged in serving them during the monsoon season when they decided to stay in one place. SB 1.5.23**

Kapila says:

*satāṁ prasaṅgān mama vīrya-saṁvido*  
*bhavanti hṛt-karṇa-rasāyanāḥ kathāḥ*  
*taj-joṣaṇād āśv apavarga-vartmani*  
*śraddhā ratir bhaktir anukramiṣyati*

**From association with the best devotees, topics of my glorious pastimes become directly realized, bringing the devotee to niṣṭhā. Then the topics become an elixir for the heart and ears at the stage of ruci. By taste for these topics, āsakti, bhāva and then prema for the Lord who is the destroyer of material life, quickly develop in sequence. SB 3.25.25**

Progress in bhakti with desire for liberation is described:

*bhaktyā pumāñ jāta-virāga aindriyād*  
*dṛṣṭa-śrutān mad-racanānucintayā*  
*cittasya yatto grahaṇe yoga-yukto*  
*yatiṣyate ṛjubhir yoga-mārgaiḥ*

**The human being, being disgusted with sense happiness in this life and the next, contemplating my pastimes with devotion, and making effort to control his mind as he engages inyoga, in the future will endeavor on the correct path of yoga as taught by me. SB 3.25.26**

Pure bhakti is described:

*naikātmatāṁ me spṛhayanti kecin*  
*mat-pāda-sevābhiratā mad-īhāḥ*  
*ye 'nyonyato bhāgavatāḥ prasajya*  
*sabhājayante mama pauruṣāṇi*

**These devotees, who are engaged in service to my feet following scriptural rules, who desire only the taste of my beauty, who, gathering together, relish my pastimes amongst themselves, never desire to become one with Brahman. SB3.25.34**

In this way the sequence of progress is understood. Pure bhakti is also described by Prahlāda in teaching the demons’ sons:

*guru-śuśrūṣayā bhaktyā sarva-labdhārpaṇena ca*  
*saṅgena sādhu-bhaktānām īśvarārādhanena ca*  
*śraddhayā tat-kathāyāṁ ca kīrtanair guṇa-karmaṇām*  
*tat-pādāmburuha-dhyānāt tal-liṅgekṣārhaṇādibhiḥ*

**One performs bhakti-yoga by service to guru, offering him all that one possesses, by association with devotees of good conduct, by worship of the Lord, by faith in topics of the Lord, by chanting about the Lord’s qualities and activities, by meditation on the Lord’s lotus feet, and by seeing the deity and making offerings to the deity. SB 7.7.30-31**

The progress of bhakti is summarized with an example:

*bhaktiḥ pareśānubhavo viraktir*  
*anyatra caiṣa trika eka-kālaḥ*  
*prapadyamānasya yathāśnataḥ syus*  
*tuṣṭiḥ puṣṭiḥ kṣud-apāyo 'nu-ghāsam*

**Devotion, direct experience of the Supreme Lord, and detachment from other things—these three occur simultaneously for one who has taken shelter of Kṛṣṇa, in the same way that pleasure, fullness of the stomach and relief from hunger are experienced simultaneously, with each bite, for a person engaged in eating.**

*ity acyutāṅghriṁ bhajato 'nuvṛttyā*  
*bhaktir viraktir bhagavat-prabodhaḥ*  
*bhavanti vai bhāgavatasya rājaṁs*  
*tataḥ parāṁ śāntim upaiti sākṣāt*

**O King! The devotee who worships the lotus feet of the infallible Lord with constant endeavor achieves unflinching devotion, detachment and experience of the Lord. He achieves supreme happiness. SB 11.2.42-43**

The commentary says, “For the person who worships the Lord, bhakti in the form of prema (*bhaktiḥ),* manifestation of the form of the Lord as the shelter of prema (*pareśanubhavaḥ*), and detachment from house and other objects, since the person experiences bliss from seeing the Lord—all three simultaneously appear at the time of worship. Similarly one who eats develops happiness (*tuṣṭiḥ*), a full stomach (*puṣṭiḥ*) and loss of hunger with every bite (*anughāsam*) or every bowl of food. Performing worship once, all three appear. For a person who repeatedly worships, the highest prema, realization and detachment appear, just as a person who eats many mouthfuls of food is happier, fuller and has less hunger. By the mercy of the Lord the person becomes successful. That is stated in the second verse.”

He achieves success (*śāntim*) directly (*sākṣāt),* without obstruction, since the highest puruṣārtha manifests internally and externally. Bhakti is compared to happiness, because both give happiness. Realization is compared to full stomach because both fill up the self. Detachment is compared to loss of hunger because it produces peace. That applies to the second verse also.

Though by eating food one loses appetite, by realizing the Lord one loses appetite for anything other than the Lord. That is the difference. The example is understood to be valid in losing taste for objects except the Lord. Kavi speaks to Nimi.

Thus the abhidheya has been explained. One should consult scriptures and great devotees’ views for other details.

*guruḥ śāstraṁ śraddhā rucir anugatiḥ siddhir iti me*  
*yad etat tat sarvaṁ caraṇa-kamalaṁ rājati yayoḥ \|*  
*kṛpā-mādhvīkena snapita-nayanāmbhoja-yugalau*  
*sadā rādhā-kṛṣṇāv aśaraṇa-gatī tau mama gatiḥ \|\|*

**Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, the shelter of the shelterless, whose lotus eyes are bathed in sweet mercy, whose lotus feet are my everything-–my guru, scripture, faith, taste, surrender and perfection--are eternally my shelter.**

Here ends Bhakti Sandarbha, the fifth among the Sandarbhas which use Bhāgavatam as their source, and which contains the expert instructions of Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmīs, most revered members of the grand assembly of all Vaiṣṇavas, following the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa Caitanya-deva, who descended to distribute his worship, in order to purify Kali-yuga.

## References

Saṁsargābhāva has three cateories: atyantābhāva, absolute non-existence, not ever existing in past, present or future; pradhvaṁśābhāva, something existing but then destroyed; and prāg-abhāva, something which did not exist but manifests later. Of these, jīva’s non-existence of knowledge of the Lord is not atyantābhāva, since the jīva can obtain knowledge. It is not dhvaṁśābhāva since the jīva would have had knowledge of the Lord which was destroyed, and that knowledge cannot be created again. Something which is destroyed cannot be recreated with exactly the same form. Furthermore knowledge of the Lord if established in the jīva, should be eternal. If it is argued that the knowledge is simply unmanifested, then it is not classed as abhāva at all. The jīva’s lack of knowledge is thus prāg-abhāva, not having previously existed. This does not exclude the possibility of obtaining knowledge of the Lord in the future, which, on attainment, becomes eternal.

On hearing teachings about materialism from his teachers, he preached the glories of Viṣṇu.

Actions are related with other objects. If there is one object in existence, there is no need of action.

This means Śrīdhara Svāmī’s commentary.

vāg-yajñenārcito devaḥ prīyatāṁ me janārdanaḥ

Around the Lord’s abode are four walls. Around that are concentric walls. One each wall in the eight directions different sets of personalities are situated. Each set is called an āvaraṇa.

jñānaṁ parama-guhyaṁ me yad vijñāna-samanvitam \|

Please understand the most secret knowledge of my form, along with realization of that form, and also understand about prema-bhakti and sādhana-bhakti which will be spoken by me. SB 2.9.31

These are reactions whose effects will be experienced in future lives.

Vaivasvatam saṁgamanam janānām Ṛg Veda 10.14.1

yajante sāttvikā devān yakṣarakṣāṁsi rājasāḥ \|  
pretān bhūtagaṇāṁś cānye yajante tāmasā janāḥ \|\|

Those with sattvic nature worship the devas. Those with rajasic nature worship the Yakṣas and Rākṣasas. Those with tamasic nature worship the Pretas and Bhūtas. BG 17.4

This is bhāva, the sprout of prema.

The three verses defining kaniṣṭha (part), madhyama (part) and uttama are correlated with the eight verses describing the uttama, to show three stages of uttama: mūcchita-kaṣāya, nirdhūta-kaṣaya and nirdhūta-kaṣāya-mahā-prema. Padma Purāṇa 6.226.6

It appeas that these are jñāni-bhaktas or persons strongly fixed in vaidhī bhakti with philosophical inclination. An example of śravaṇa with prominent vicāra is givein in Anuccheda 204.

The extent of realization is not specified here. But because of knowledge of scripture, he must at least be a madhyama-bhāgavata.

iti pūrvaṁ prāṇa-buddhi-deha-dharmādhikārataḥ \|  
jāgrat-svapna-suṣupty-ākhyāvasthāsu manasā vācā \|\|  
karmaṇā hastābhyāṁ padbhyām udareṇa śiśnā yat smṛtam \|  
yad uktaṁ yat kṛtaṁ tat sarvaṁ brahmārpaṇaṁ bhavatu svāhā \|\|  
māṁ madīyaṁ ca sakalaṁ haraye’haṁ samarpaye \|  
oṁ tat saditi samprokto mantraḥ svātmārpaṇe śubhaḥ \|\|

I offer myself and everything I possess to the Lord. Om tat sat.” Nārada-pañcarātra 3.6-8

From Anuccheda 202: the śravaṇa-guru should be fixed in the Vedas (śābde brahmaṇi) by dedication to deliberation and should be fixed in the forms of the Lord (pare brahmaṇi) by inner perception.

Visvanātha’s meaning according to commentary on BRS 1.2.97: any person who seriously desires real happiness must seek a spiritual master, who is conversant with the bhakti scriptures (śābde brahmaṇi), accomplished in hearing and chanting about the Lord (pare brahmaṇi), and fully tranquil. SB 11.3.21

This implies that the mahābhāgavata mentioned here has less qualification than guru mentioned in SB 11.3.21. Mānasa-puja, performed after dhyāna, is offering all the items of worship in the mind, before the external worship.

Mūdras are using one or both hands and fingers in specific configurations to represent different objects offered in the worship. Hari-bhakti-vilāsa 8.391
